. Finally, the property (
1
) for
v|p
, along with (
2
), were established in general by Skinner
[
104
]
(except that our normalizations of Hodge-Tate weights and reciprocity maps are inverted from
loc. cit.
). The property (
3
) is an immediate corollary of (
1
).
∎

Definition 2.2.2
.

An automorphic representation
\pi
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
has (strong) coefficient field
E_{0}\subset\mathbb{C}
if
E_{0}
is a number field and, for all primes
p
and isomorphisms
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
,
\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is defined over the
p
-adic closure of
\iota^{-1}(E_{0})
. In this case, it depends only on the prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
induced by
\mathbb{C}
, and we obtain a well-defined
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}:G_{F}\to\GL_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})
such that
\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is the extension of scalars of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.

Remark 2.2.3
.

The argument of
[
23
, Proposition 3.2.5]
shows that strong coeffient fields exist in the situation of Theorem
2.2.1
.

Notation 2.2.4
.

Let
\pi
be as in Theorem
2.2.1
, and let
E_{0}
be a strong coefficient field of
\pi
. Fix a prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
with residue field
k(\mathfrak{p})
.

(1)

Write
V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
for the underlying
E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}[G_{F}]
-module of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.

(2)

Let
T_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}\subset V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
be a
G_{F}
-stable
O_{E_{0},\mathfrak{p}}
-lattice. Then the
k(\mathfrak{p})[G_{F}]
-module
\overline{T}_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
depends only on
V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
up to semisimplification. We write

\overline{\rho}_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}:G_{F}\to\GL_{2}(k(\mathfrak{p}))

for the corresponding semisimple Galois representation.

(3)

When
\mathfrak{p}
is clear from context, it may be dropped from all subscripts in the above notations.

We now turn to automorphic representations of
\GSP_{4}
.

Definition 2.2.5
.

An automorphic representation
\pi
of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
will be called
relevant
if:

•

\pi
is cuspidal and not CAP, and has unitary central character.

•

\pi_{\infty}
belongs to the discrete series
L
-packet of weight
(3,3)
.

2.2.6.

If
\pi
is an automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
, then for all
\ell
such that
\pi_{\ell}
is unramified, recall that we write the Satake parameter of
\pi_{\ell}
as a multiset of four complex numbers of the form
\left\{\alpha,\beta,\nu\alpha^{-1},\nu\beta^{-1}\right\}
. If
\pi
has central character
\omega_{\pi}
, then
\nu=\omega_{\pi}(\langle\ell\rangle)=\rec(\omega_{\pi})(\Frob_{\ell}^{-1}).

Definition 2.2.7
.

A cuspidal, non-CAP automorphic representation
\pi
of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
is
endoscopic
associated to an unordered pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of cuspidal automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
with the same central character if the following holds: for all but finitely many primes
\ell
,
if
\pi_{i,\ell}
are both unramified with Satake parameters
\left\{\alpha_{i},\nu/\alpha_{i}\right\}
, then
\pi_{\ell}
is unramified with Satake parameter
\left\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\nu/\alpha_{1},\nu/\alpha_{2}\right\}
.

2.2.8.

In this case,
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are necessarily distinct by
[
127
, Lemma 5.2]
, and the central character of
\pi
is the common
central character of the
\pi_{i}
.
In the literature, the property in Definition
2.2.7
is often called being
weakly
endoscopic, and being endoscopic requires a stronger compatibility condition of local Langlands parameters at all finite places; however, the distinction is unimportant, cf. the results of
[
127
, §5]
.

Lemma 2.2.9
.

Suppose
\pi
is an endoscopic automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
, associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
. Then
\pi
is relevant if and only if
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are unitary with discrete series archimedean components of weights 2 and 4 in some order.

Proof.

Recall
\pi
is cuspidal and not CAP by definition. Also,
\pi

clearly has unitary central character if and only if
\pi_{1}

and
\pi_{2}
are unitary, so it suffices to consider the archimedean weights.

Consider the representation
\pi_{1,\infty}\boxtimes\pi_{2,\infty}
of
\operatorname{GSO}_{2,2}(\mathbb{R})=(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{R})\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{R}))/\mathbb{R}^{\times}
;
by
[
127
, Lemma 5.6]
,
\pi_{\infty}
belongs to the local
L
-packet attached to
\pi_{1,\infty}\boxtimes\pi_{2,\infty}
via the known Langlands parametrization for real groups and the map of dual groups

{}^{L}\operatorname{GSO}_{2,2}=(\GL_{2}\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\GL_{2})(\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{C})={}^{L}\GSP_{4,\mathbb{R}}.

We conclude that
\pi_{\infty}
belongs to the desired discrete series
L
-packet if and only if
\pi_{1,\infty}
and
\pi_{2,\infty}
are discrete series of weights 2 and 4 in some order.

∎

Theorem 2.2.10
.

Let
\pi
be a relevant automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
, and fix a prime
p
along with an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
. Then there exists a semisimple Galois representation

\rho_{\pi,\iota}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\GSP_{4}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})

with the following properties.

(1)

\rho_{\pi,\iota}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is potentially semistable, and for all primes
\ell
,

\iota WD(\rho_{\pi,\iota}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}})^{F-ss}\simeq\rec_{\operatorname{GT}}(\pi_{\ell}\otimes|\cdot|^{\frac{1}{2}}).

Moreover this Weil-Deligne representation is pure of weight
-1
.

(2)

The Hodge-Tate weights of
\rho_{\pi,\iota}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
are
\left\{-1,0,1,2\right\}
.

(3)

The similitude character of
\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is
\iota^{-1}\rec(\omega_{\pi})\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}
, where
\omega_{\pi}
is the central character.

Here
\rec_{\operatorname{GT}}
is the Gan-Takeda local Langlands correspondence of
[
37
]
, which associates to an irreducible admissible representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
a
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{C})
-valued Weil-Deligne representation.

Proof.

Suppose first that
\pi
is endoscopic associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
. By Lemma
2.2.9
and Theorem
2.2.1
, we can take

\rho_{\pi,\iota}\coloneqq\rho_{\pi_{1},\iota}\oplus\rho_{\pi_{2},\iota}

under the natural embedding
\GL_{2}\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\GL_{2}\to\GSP_{4}
; this satisfies (
1
) by Theorem
2.2.1
(
1
) combined with
[
127
, Corollary 5.1, Theorem 5.2(3)]
and the construction of
\rec_{\operatorname{GT}}
in
[
37
]
. Then (
2
) and (
3
) are satisfied by Theorem
2.2.1
(
2
,
3
).

Now suppose
\pi
is not endoscopic. By
[
97
, Proposition 10.1]
combined with
[
126
, Theorem 1]
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\pi
is globally generic.
Under the extra hypothesis that
\pi_{v}
is Steinberg for some
v\neq p
, and omitting the case
\ell=p
of (
1
), the theorem then follows from the main result of
[
106
]
, except that we have twisted the
\rho_{\pi,\iota}
of
loc. cit.
by
(\chi_{p}^{{\operatorname{cyc}}})^{2}
. The extra hypothesis in
[
106
]
is needed only to appeal to the results of Taylor-Yoshida
[
110
]
,
and has since been removed by work of Caraiani
[
17
]
. Similarly, the proof in
[
106
]
of (
1
) for
\ell\neq p
extends to the case
\ell=p
with the additional input of
[
18
]
.
∎

Notation 2.2.11
.

In the setting of Theorem
2.2.10
,
we write
V_{\pi,\iota}
for the underlying four-dimensional Galois module of
\rho_{\pi,\iota}
.

Lemma 2.2.12
.

In the situation of Theorem
2.2.10
, suppose
p>3
. Then
V_{\pi,\iota}
is reducible if and only if
\pi
is endoscopic.

Proof.

This is
[
124
, Theorem 3.1]
.
∎

Definition 2.2.13
.

A relevant automorphic representation
\pi
of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
has (strong) coefficient field
E_{0}\subset\mathbb{C}
if
E_{0}
is a number field and, for all primes
p
and isomorphisms
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
,
\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is defined over the
p
-adic closure of
\iota^{-1}(E_{0})
. In this case, it depends only on the prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
induced by
\iota
, and we obtain a well-defined
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}:G_{F}\to\GSP_{4}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})
such that
\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is the extension of scalars of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.

Lemma 2.2.14
.

If
\pi
is a relevant automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
, then a strong coefficient field
E_{0}
exists for
\pi
.

Proof.

If
\pi
is endoscopic, this is clear from Remark
2.2.3
, so assume otherwise.

Let
r:\GSP_{4}\hookrightarrow\GL_{4}
be the natural embedding, and let
\overline{\iota^{-1}(E_{0})}
be the
p
-adic closure for any
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
. By the argument of
[
23
, Proposition 3.2.5]
,
there exists a number field
E_{0}
such that, for all
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
,
r\circ\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is defined over
\overline{\iota^{-1}(E_{0})}
and
\rho_{\pi,\iota}(g)
has distinct eigenvalues in
\overline{\iota^{-1}(E_{0})}^{\times}
for some
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

It suffices to check

\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is defined over
\overline{\iota^{-1}(E_{0})}
whenever
p>3
. For this, let
G
be the absolute Galois group of
\overline{\iota^{-1}(E_{0})}
. For all
\sigma\in G
, we have

\rho_{\pi,\iota}^{\sigma}=h(\sigma)\rho_{\pi,\iota}h(\sigma)^{-1}

for some
h(\sigma)\in\GL_{4}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Using the absolute irreducibility from Lemma
2.2.12
and Schur’s lemma, we conclude
h(\sigma)\in\GSP_{4}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, and
\sigma\mapsto h(\sigma)
defines a cocycle
h\in H^{1}(G,\operatorname{PGSp}_{4}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}))
. The class of
h
determines an inner form
H
of
\GSP_{4}
over
\overline{\iota^{-1}(E_{0})}
such that
\rho_{\pi,\iota}
can be conjugated to lie in
H(\overline{\iota^{-1}(E_{0})})
. However, since
\rho_{\pi,\iota}(g)
has distinct eigenvalues in

\overline{\iota^{-1}(E_{0})}
for some
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
H
must be split, and this completes the proof.
∎

2.2.15.

Analogously to Notation
2.2.4
, we make the following notations.

Notation 2.2.16
.

Let
\pi
be as in Theorem
2.2.10
, and let
E_{0}
be a strong coefficient field of
\pi
. Fix a prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
with residue field
k(\mathfrak{p})
.

(1)

Write
V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
for the four-dimensional underlying
E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-module of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.

(2)

Let
T_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}\subset V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
be any
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable
O_{\mathfrak{p}}
-lattice;
we define
\overline{T}_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}\coloneqq\left(T_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}/\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)^{ss}
, which depends only on
V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.
We also write

\overline{\rho}_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}:G_{F}\to\GL_{4}(k(\mathfrak{p}))

for the corresponding semisimple Galois representation.

(3)

When
\mathfrak{p}
is clear from context, it may be dropped from all subscripts in the above notations.

Lemma 2.2.17
.

Suppose
\pi
is a relevant automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
. Then there exists a base change
\BC(\pi)
to an automorphic representation of
\GL_{4}(\mathbb{A})
, such that for each place
v
,

\rec(\BC(\pi)_{v})=r\circ\rec_{\operatorname{GT}}(\pi_{v}),

where

r:{}^{L}\GSP_{4}=\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow\GL_{4}(\mathbb{C})={}^{L}\GL_{4}

is the natural embedding of dual groups. Moreover
\BC(\pi)
is cuspidal if and only if
\pi
is non-endoscopic.

Proof.

If
\pi
is endoscopic associated to
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
, then there exists a non-cuspidal base change, which is the isobaric sum
\pi_{1}\boxplus\pi_{2}
; the compatibility with local Langlands parameters is by the same reasoning as the endoscopic case of Theorem
2.2.10
.

In the non-endoscopic case, the lemma follows from
[
97
, Proposition 10.1]
and its proof; note that the endoscopic transfer to
\GL_{4}\times\GL_{1}
used in
loc. cit.
is compatible with the Gan-Takeda local Langlands paremeters by the main result of
[
21
]
.
∎

Lemma 2.2.18
.

Suppose
\pi
is a relevant automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
. If
\BC(\pi)
is a symmetric cube lift of a non-CM automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
, then
\pi_{0}
has discrete series archimedean component of weight 2.

Proof.

This follows from matching archimedean
L
-parameters using
[
50
, Theorem B]
and Lemma
2.2.17
.
∎

Lemma 2.2.19
.

Suppose
\pi
is a relevant automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
. If
\BC(\pi)
is the automorphic induction of a non-CM automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
with
K
real quadratic, then
\pi_{0}
has discrete series archimedean components of weights 2 and 4, in some order. Moreover the central characters
\omega_{\pi_{0}}
of
\pi_{0}
and
\omega_{\pi}
of
\pi
satisfy

\omega_{\pi}\circ\operatorname{Nm}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}=\omega_{\pi_{0}}.

Proof.

The assertion on archimedean components follows from Lemma
2.2.17
and
the compatibility of automorphic induction with local Langlands parameters
[
3
, Chapter 3, Theorem 5.1]
. To check the relation of central characters,
fix some prime
p
along with an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
. Let
\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}
denote the
\Gal(K/\mathbb{Q})
-twist.
Then we have

V_{\pi,\iota}|_{G_{K}}=\rho_{\pi_{0},\iota}\oplus\rho_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}},\iota}.

Using the identities

V_{\pi,\iota}=V_{\pi,\iota}^{\vee}\otimes\iota^{-1}\rec(\omega_{\pi})\otimes\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}},\;\;\rho_{\pi_{0},\iota}=\rho_{\pi_{0},\iota}^{\vee}\otimes\iota^{-1}\rec(\omega_{\pi_{0}})\otimes\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}},

and likewise for
\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}
, it follows that

\rho_{\pi_{0},\iota}\otimes\iota^{-1}\left(\rec(\omega_{\pi})|_{G_{K}}/\rec(\omega_{\pi_{0}})\right)\oplus\rho_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}},\iota}\otimes\iota^{-1}\left(\rec(\omega_{\pi})|_{G_{K}}/\rec(\omega_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}})\right)\cong\rho_{\pi_{0},\iota}\oplus\rho_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}},\iota}.

Since
\rho_{\pi_{0},\iota}
and
\rho_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}},\iota}
are absolutely irreducible by Theorem
C.3.2
, and have different Hodge-Tate weights by Theorem
2.2.1
(
2
), this implies
\rec(\omega_{\pi})|_{G_{K}}=\rec(\omega_{\pi_{0}})
, i.e.
\omega_{\pi}\circ\Nm_{K/\mathbb{Q}}=\omega_{\pi_{0}}
.
∎

In the next lemma, if
K
is a quadratic field, we write
\BC_{K/\mathbb{Q}}
for the base change of an automorphic representation of
\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A})
to
\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
, which exists by
[
3
]
.

Lemma 2.2.20
.

Suppose
\pi
is a relevant, non-endoscopic automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
. If

\BC(\pi)
is the automorphic induction of an automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
with
K
imaginary quadratic and
\pi_{0}
is not itself an automorphic induction, then

\pi_{0}
is of the form
\BC_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\sigma)\otimes\chi
, where
\sigma
is the unitary automorphic representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
corresponding to a non-CM classical modular form of weight
k=2
or 3, and
\chi
is a Hecke character of
K
.

Proof.

By hypothesis,
\BC_{K/\mathbb{Q}}\circ\BC(\pi)
is an isobaric sum
\pi_{0}\,\boxplus\,\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}
, where
{\operatorname{tw}}
denotes the
\Gal(K/\mathbb{Q})
-twist.

Considering archimedean
L
-parameters and using Lemma
2.2.17
combined with
[
3
, Chapter 3, Theorem 5.1]
, we see that the local
L
-parameter of
\pi_{0}
at the unique archimedean place of
K
is of the form

(2.4)

z\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}(z/\overline{z})^{\epsilon_{1}\frac{1}{2}}&\\
&(z/\overline{z})^{\epsilon_{2}\frac{3}{2}}\end{pmatrix}

for
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\in\left\{\pm 1\right\}
. Let
\omega_{\pi}:\mathbb{A}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
and
\omega_{\pi_{0}}
be the central characters of
\pi
and
\pi_{0}
, respectively; then because
\BC(\pi)\cong\BC(\pi)^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\pi}
, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{0}^{\vee}\,\boxplus\,(\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}})^{\vee}

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{0}\otimes\omega_{\pi}\circ\Nm_{K/\mathbb{Q}}\boxplus\,\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}\otimes\omega_{\pi}\circ\Nm_{K/\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{0}\otimes\omega_{\pi_{0}}^{-1}\boxplus\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}\otimes\omega_{\pi_{0}}^{-{\operatorname{tw}}}.

The archimedean
L
-parameter (
2.4
) shows that
\pi_{0}^{\vee}\not\cong\pi_{0}\otimes\omega_{\pi}\circ\Nm_{K/\mathbb{Q}}
, and hence

(2.5)

\pi_{0}^{\vee}\cong\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}\otimes\omega_{\pi}\circ\Nm_{K/\mathbb{Q}}\cong\pi_{0}\otimes\omega_{\pi_{0}}^{-1}.

On the other hand, computing the central character of
\BC(\pi)
, we have:

(2.6)

\omega_{\pi}^{2}=\omega_{\pi_{0}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{\times}}.

In particular,
(\omega_{\pi}\circ\Nm_{K/\mathbb{Q}})/\omega_{\pi_{0}}
is trivial when restricted to
\mathbb{A}^{\times}
, hence of the form
\chi/\chi^{\operatorname{tw}}
for an automorphic character
\chi
of
\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\times}
. Then
(\pi_{0}\otimes\chi^{-1})
is isomorphic to its
\Gal(K/\mathbb{Q})
-twist by (
2.5
), hence arises as the base change of a cuspidal automorphic representation
\sigma
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
by
[
3
, Chapter 3, Theorem 4.2]
. Without loss of generality, we may assume
\sigma
is unitary;
considering the archimedean local
L
-parameter of
\sigma
and again using
[
3
, Chapter 3, Theorem 5.1]
, the archimedean component of
\sigma
is discrete series of weight 2 or 3. We have
\pi_{0}=\BC_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\sigma)\otimes\chi
by construction. ∎

2.3.
Shimura varieties and Shimura sets

2.3.1.

Let
V

be a quadratic space over
\mathbb{Q}
, and recall the algebraic group
\spin(V)
from (
1.1.5
).

2.3.2.
Indefinite case

Suppose
V\otimes\mathbb{R}

has signature
(n,2)
. If
V^{-}\subset V_{\mathbb{R}}

is a negative definite
2
-plane, one obtains a map

C^{+}(V^{-})\simeq\mathbb{C}\rightarrow C^{+}(V_{\mathbb{R}}),

which induces a Shimura datum

h:\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\spin(V)_{\mathbb{R}}.

For a neat compact open subgroup
K\subset\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the resulting Shimura variety
\Shimura_{K}(V)
is a smooth quasi-projective variety over
\mathbb{Q}
.

2.3.3.
Definite case

If
V
is a positive definite quadratic space over
\mathbb{Q}
, then
\spin(V)(\mathbb{R})
is compact. For a compact open subgroup
K\subset\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, let
\Shimura_{K}(V)
denote the finite double coset space

\Shimura_{K}(V)\coloneqq\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K.

2.3.4.
Hecke algebras

Suppose
V=V_{D}
is one of the quadratic spaces from (
•
‣
1.1.6
), and let
O
be a coefficient ring.
If

K=\prod_{\ell}K_{\ell}\subset\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is a neat compact open subgroup and
S
is a finite set of primes of
\mathbb{Q}
containing all those such that
K_{\ell}
is not hyperspecial, then
{\mathbb{T}}^{S}_{O}=\mathbb{T}^{S}_{\GSP_{4},O}
acts on
H^{\ast}(\Sh_{K}(V)(\mathbb{C}),O)
; the cohomology is interpreted as
O[\Sh_{K}(V)]
in the definite case. We denote by
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{K,V_{D},O}
the quotient of
{\mathbb{T}}^{S}_{O}
defined by this action, and may drop the subscript
O
when it is clear from context.

2.4.
Automorphic representations of
\spin_{5}
groups

2.4.1.

Recall the five-dimensional quadratic spaces
V_{D}
from (
•
‣
1.1.6
).

Definition 2.4.2
.

An automorphic representation
\pi
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
is
Eisenstein
if there exists a parabolic subgroup
P\subset\GSP_{4}
, with Levi factor
L
, and an automorphic representation
\sigma
of
L(\mathbb{A})
such that
\pi
is nearly equivalent to a constituent of
\Ind_{P(\mathbb{A})}^{\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})}\sigma
.

The following generalizes Definitions
2.2.5
and
2.2.7
.

Definition 2.4.3
.

For a squarefree integer
D\geq 1
, an automorphic representation
\pi
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
will be called
relevant
if:

(1)

\pi
is not Eisenstein (in particular is cuspidal), and has unitary central character.

(2)

\pi_{\infty}
is
trivial if
\sigma(D)
is odd (in which case
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{R})
is a compact group); or
belongs to the discrete series
L
-packet of weight
(3,3)
if
\sigma(D)
is even (in which case
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{R})=\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{R})
).

A non-Eisenstein automorphic representation
\pi
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
is called
endoscopic
associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
if the condition in Definition
2.2.7
is satisfied for all but finitely many
\ell\nmid D
.

Lemma 2.4.4
.

Fix a squarefree
D\geq 1
such that
\sigma(D)
is even, and suppose
\pi
is an automorphic representation of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
that is not Eisenstein.
Then for any neat compact open subgroup
K=\prod K_{\ell}
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
:

(1)

The natural map induces an isomorphism

H^{i}_{c}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),\mathbb{C})[\pi_{f}]\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),\mathbb{C})[\pi_{f}].

(2)

We have

H^{i}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),\mathbb{C})[\pi_{f}]=\bigoplus_{\pi_{\infty}^{\prime}}m(\pi_{f}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{\prime})\cdot\pi_{f}^{K}\otimes H^{i}(\mathfrak{gsp}_{4},U(2);\pi_{\infty}^{\prime})\neq 0,

where
U(2)\subset\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{R})
is the maximal compact subgroup,
\pi_{\infty}^{\prime}
runs over cohomological representations of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{R})
, and
m(\pi_{f}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{\prime})
is the multiplicity in the discrete (equivalently cuspidal) automorphic spectrum of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
.

(3)

If
\pi
is relevant and

H^{i}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),\mathbb{C})[\pi_{f}]\neq 0
, then
i=3.

Proof.

The first part is standard, cf.
[
44
, Chapter 9]
. Then (
2
) is immediate from Matsushima’s formula and the diagram in
[
109
, p. 293]
. We now show (
3
). Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\pi_{f}^{K}\neq 0
; in particular, because
\pi
is relevant, (
2
) implies that

(2.7)

H^{3}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),\mathbb{C})[\pi_{f}]\neq 0.

Now let
S
be a finite set of places of
\mathbb{Q}
such that
K_{\ell}
is hyperspecial for
\ell\not\in S
, and fix a prime
p\not\in S\cup\left\{2,3\right\}
along with
an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
. It suffices to show

(2.8)

H^{i}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})[\pi_{f}]\neq 0\implies i=3.

For this, we argue as in
[
109
]
, with the additional input of the Fontaine-Mazur conjecture for
\GL_{2}
. Set
W^{i}
to be the
\pi^{S}
-isotypic component
7
7

7

Defined using
\iota
. We elide
\iota
from the discussion to ease the burden of notation.
of
H^{i}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
with respect to the natural action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
; then
W^{i}
is a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-module.
We have the following facts:

(1)

By Margulis’ superrigidity theorem
[
74
, Chapter IX, Corollary 7.15(iii)]
,
W^{1}=0
, and clearly
W^{0}=W^{6}=0
because
\pi_{f}
is not one-dimensional.

(2)

For each
\ell\not\in S\cup\left\{p\right\}
,
\Frob_{\ell}
satisfies the Eichler-Shimura relation on
H^{\ast}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
by
[
123
]
; in particular,
if
\pi_{\ell}
has Satake parameters
\left\{\alpha,\beta,\nu\alpha^{-1},\nu\beta^{-1}\right\}
, then

(2.9)

(\Frob_{\ell}^{-1}-\ell^{3/2}\alpha)(\Frob_{\ell}^{-1}-\ell^{3/2}\beta)(\Frob_{\ell}^{-1}-\ell^{3/2}\nu\alpha^{-1})(\Frob_{\ell}^{-1}-\ell^{3/2}\nu\beta^{-1})=0\text{ on each }W^{i}.

(3)

By Poincaré duality and part (
1
) of the theorem there are perfect pairings

W^{i}\times W^{6-i}\to\rec(\omega_{\pi})(-3)

for all
i
, where
\omega_{\pi}
is the central character of
\pi
, cf.
[
109
, p. 297]
.

(4)

Again by (
1
) of the theorem, for all
\ell\not\in S\cup\left\{p\right\}
, the eigenvalues of
\Frob_{\ell}
on
W^{i}
are Weil numbers of weight
i
.

Now suppose
W^{i}\neq 0
for some
i\neq 3
. By (
1
),
i\neq 0,1
, and then by (
3
) we conclude that
W^{2}\neq 0
. If
\ell\not\in S\cup\left\{p\right\}
is a prime such that
\Frob_{\ell}
has
n
distinct eigenvalues on
W^{2}
, it has
n
distinct eigenvalues on
W^{4}
as well by (
3
),
and at least one eigenvalue on
W^{3}
by (
2.7
).
In particular, by (
4
) and the fact that
\Frob_{\ell}
has at most 4 total eigenvalues by (
2
),
we conclude that
n=1
and
\Frob_{\ell}
also has at most two eigenvalues on
W^{3}
. Now the same argument as
[
109
, Proposition 3]
shows that there is a two-dimensional representation
R:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
– possibly reducible – with distinct Hodge-Tate weights such that
(W^{3})^{ss}=R^{\oplus e}
for some
e\geq 1
, and a character
\chi:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
such that
(W^{2})^{ss}=\chi^{\oplus d}
and
(W^{4})^{ss}=(\rec(\omega_{\pi})\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}^{-3}\chi^{-1})^{\oplus d}
for some
d\geq 1
. Note that
\omega_{\pi}
is even because the central character of
\pi_{\infty}
is trivial, so
R
is odd by (
3
) above.
Hence by
[
87
, Theorem 1.0.4]
,
R
is automorphic.
Comparing with (
2
), we see that there exists an automorphic character
\omega
of
\mathbb{A}^{\times}
and an automorphic representation
\sigma
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
such that
\pi
is nearly equivalent to a constituent of the representation

\Ind_{P(\mathbb{A})}^{\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})}\sigma\boxtimes\omega,

where
P\subset\GSP_{4}
is the Siegel parabolic subgroup with Levi factor
\GL_{2}\boxtimes\GL_{1}
.
Hence
\pi
is Eisenstein, which contradicts the assumption that
\pi
is relevant and completes the proof.
∎

We will require some information about Jacquet-Langlands transfers of relevant automorphic representations between the various
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
.

Definition 2.4.5
.

For a tempered irreducible admissible representation
\pi_{\ell}
of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
, we say
\pi_{\ell}
is
transferrable
if it does not belong to the types I, IIIa, VIa, VIb, VII, VIIIa, VIIIb, or IXa in the notation of
[
96
]
.

In particular, if
\pi_{\ell}
is unramified, it is not transferrable.

Theorem 2.4.6
.

Let
D\geq 1
be squarefree. Then:

(1)

For each relevant, non-endoscopic automorphic representation
\Pi=\otimes^{\prime}\Pi_{v}
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
,
\Pi^{D}_{f}\coloneqq\otimes^{\prime}_{\ell\nmid D}\Pi_{\ell}
can be completed to a relevant automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
.

(2)

Conversely, suppose
\pi=\otimes^{\prime}\pi_{v}
is a relevant, non-endoscopic automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
. Then
\pi^{D}_{f}\coloneqq\otimes^{\prime}_{\ell\nmid D}\pi_{\ell}
can be completed to
a relevant automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
if and only if
\pi_{\ell}
is transferrable for all primes
\ell|D
.

(3)

Let
\pi=\otimes^{\prime}\pi_{\ell}
be a relevant, non-endoscopic automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
, and assume
\pi_{\ell}
is transferrable for all
\ell|D
.
Then any relevant automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
with
\Pi^{D}_{f}\cong\pi^{D}_{f}
has automorphic multiplicity one.
The set of all such
\Pi

is a Cartesian product of local
L
-packets for
v|D\infty
, where the nonarchimedean
L
-packets are determined only by the corresponding local factors of
\pi
. The archimedean
L
-packet is the discrete series packet of weight
(3,3)

when
\sigma(D)
is even and the trivial representation when
\sigma(D)
is odd.

Proof.

When
\sigma(D)
is odd, so that
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{R})
is compact, this is
[
97
, Theorem 11.4]
.
To prove these assertions when
\sigma(D)
is even, we follow the sketch indicated in the discussion following
loc. cit.

The following fact will be used repeatedly:

(2.10)

\begin{split}\text{If $\pi_{\infty}$
is an irreducible admissible representation of $\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{R})$ with $H^{3}(\mathfrak{gsp}_{4},U(2);\pi_{\infty})\neq 0$,}\\
\text{ then $\pi_{\infty}$ lies in the discrete series $L$-packet of weight $(3,3)$.
}\end{split}

This fact follows from the calculations in
[
109
, p. 293]
.

To prove (
1
), suppose first that
\Pi=\otimes^{\prime}\Pi_{v}
is a relevant automorphic representation of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
.
By
[
97
, Corollary 7.4]
combined with Lemma
2.4.4
(
3
), we conclude that
\Pi^{D}_{f}
can be completed to a cohomological automorphic representation
\pi
of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
.
Moreover
\pi_{f}
necessarily contributes to cohomology in degree 3 by
[
97
, Proposition 8.2]
, and in particular
\pi
is relevant by (
2.10
) combined with Lemma
2.4.4
(
2
), so this proves (
1
).

To prove (
2
) and (
3
), we need some preparation. Fix a relevant automorphic representation
\pi=\otimes^{\prime}\pi_{v}
of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
.
For each prime
\ell|D
such that
\pi_{\ell}
is transferrable, there is a local
L
-packet of irreducible admissible representations of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
determined by the character relations of Lemma 11.1 of
op. cit.
Let
\mathbb{A}_{D}=\prod_{\ell|D}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
; by an irreducible admissible representation of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{D})
we mean a direct product of irreducible admissible representations of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
for
\ell|D
.

If
\pi_{\ell}
is transferrable for all
\ell|D
, let
S
be the set of irreducible admissible representations of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{D})
obtained by taking the Cartesian product of the local
L
-packets. If
\pi_{\ell}
is not transferrable for some
\ell|D
, then let
S
be the empty set.

The comparison of cohomological trace formulas from the proof of
[
97
, Theorem 11.4]
shows the following identity: for all irreducible admissible representations
\Pi_{D}
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{D})
, we have

(2.11)

\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\pi_{\infty}}m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty})\chi(\pi_{\infty})=\begin{cases}2,&\Pi_{D}\in S,\\
0,&\Pi_{D}\not\in S.\end{cases}

Here the notation is as follows:

•

The sum over
\pi_{\infty}
runs over cohomological representations of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{R})
, and
\chi(\pi_{\infty})

is the negative Euler characteristic
\sum(-1)^{i+1}\dim H^{i}(\mathfrak{gsp}_{4},U(2);\pi_{\infty})
.

•

\pi^{D}
is the representation
\otimes^{\prime}_{\ell\nmid D}\pi_{\ell}
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}^{D})
.

•

m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty})
is the multiplicity in the discrete (equivalently cuspidal, since
\pi
is relevant) automorphic spectrum of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
.

We can further manipulate the left-hand side of (
2.11
). Let
\pi_{\infty}^{W}
and
\pi_{\infty}^{H}
be the generic and holomorphic members, respectively, of the discrete series
L
-packet of weight
(3,3)
. We then claim that

(2.12)

\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\pi_{\infty}}m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty})\chi(\pi_{\infty})=m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{W})+m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{H}).

To prove (
2.12
),
suppose first that the right-hand side is positive. Then any cohomological
\pi_{\infty}
with
\Pi=\pi^{D}_{f}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty}
automorphic can have Lie algebra cohomology only in degree 3 by Lemma
2.4.4
(
2
,
3
). In particular, if
m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty})\chi(\pi_{\infty})\neq 0
then
\pi_{\infty}
is either
\pi_{\infty}^{W}
or
\pi_{\infty}^{H}
by (
2.10
), so – combined with the fact that
\chi(\pi_{\infty}^{W})=\chi(\pi_{\infty}^{H})=2
by
[
109
, p. 293]
– we have shown (
2.12
) when the right-hand side is positive. On the other hand, suppose the right-hand side of (
2.12
) vanishes; then each summand on the left-hand side of (
2.12
) is non-positive by (
2.10
) because Lie algebra cohomology vanishes for
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{R})
outside degrees 0, 2, 3, 4, and 6. But the left-hand side of (
2.12
) is also non-negative by (
2.11
), so we conclude that it is zero, hence (
2.12
) again holds.

In particular, by (
2.12
) and (
2.11
) together, we have

(2.13)

m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{W})+m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{H})=\begin{cases}2,&\Pi_{D}\in S,\\
0,&\Pi_{D}\not\in S.\end{cases}

From (
2.13
), it is clear that
\pi^{D}
can be completed to a relevant automorphic representation of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
if and only if
S
is nonempty, i.e. if and only if
\pi_{\ell}
is transferrable for all
\ell|D
; this shows (
2
).

We also see from (
2.13
) that (
3
) is equivalent to the assertion that
m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{W})=m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{H})=1
for all
\Pi_{D}\in S
. Suppose for contradiction that
m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{W})
and
m(\pi^{D}\otimes\Pi_{D}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{H})
are 0 and 2, in some order.
Let
K=\prod K_{\ell}\subset\spin(V_{D})
be a neat compact open subgroup such that
\Pi_{f}^{K}\neq 0
, and let
p>3
be a prime such that
K_{p}
is hyperspecial. Fix as
well an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
. Then by the discussion in
[
109
, p. 296]
,
H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})[\Pi_{f}^{K}]
is nonzero and has exactly two distinct Hodge-Tate weights as a representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

On the other hand, by (
2
) from the proof of Lemma
2.4.4
combined with the irreducibility of
V_{\pi,\iota}
(Lemma
2.2.12
), up to semisimplification
H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})[\Pi_{f}^{K}]
is a sum of copies of
V_{\pi,\iota}
, which is a contradiction because
V_{\pi,\iota}
has distinct Hodge-Tate weights by Theorem
2.2.10
(
2
). This completes the proof of (
3
).
∎

Remark 2.4.7
.

One can similarly prove Theorem
2.4.6
in all regular weights, by considering cohomology of Siegel threefolds with coefficients in more general local systems; we omit the details for concision.

2.5.
Relevant endoscopic automorphic representations

2.5.1.

Relevant endoscopic automorphic representations of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
can be constructed as follows. Fix unitary cuspidal automorphic representations
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
with the same central character, and whose archimedean components are discrete series of weights 4 and 2, in some order. Let
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
be squarefree positive integers, such that
\pi_{i}
admits a Jacquet-Langlands transfer
\pi_{i}^{D_{i}}
to
B_{D_{i}}^{\times}(\mathbb{A})
for
i=1,2
(notation as in (
•
‣
1.1.6
)).

Define
D_{1}\ast D_{2}\coloneqq D_{1}D_{2}/\gcd(D_{1},D_{2})^{2}
, which is a squarefree positive integer. Then as in
[
21
, §3.3]
, there is a global theta lift,

(2.14)

\Theta(\pi_{1}^{D_{1}}\boxtimes\pi_{2}^{D_{2}})=\Theta(\pi_{2}^{D_{2}}\boxtimes\pi_{1}^{D_{1}}),

which is either zero or a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\spin(V_{D_{1}\ast D_{2}})(\mathbb{A})
.

Theorem 2.5.2
.

With notation as above, relabel
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
if necessary so that
\pi_{1}
has weight 2 and
\pi_{2}
has weight 4.

(1)

The theta lift
\Theta(\pi_{1}^{D_{1}}\boxtimes\pi_{2}^{D_{2}})
is nonzero if and only if either
\sigma(D_{2})
or
\sigma(D_{1}\ast D_{2})
is even. When nonzero,
\Theta(\pi_{1}^{D_{1}}\boxtimes\pi_{2}^{D_{2}})
is always relevant and endoscopic associated to the pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
, and these representations are all distinct.

(2)

Conversely, each relevant endoscopic automorphic representation of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
arises in this way, and appears with automorphic multiplicity one in the discrete (equivalently cuspidal) spectrum.

Proof.

This follows from the results of
[
21
, §3]
, combined with the discussion of the local archimedean theta lift in §2.4 of
op. cit.
.
∎

Corollary 2.5.3
.

For any relevant automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
, there exists a relevant automorphic representation
\pi
of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
such that:

(1)

For each prime
\ell\nmid D
,
\Pi_{\ell}\cong\pi_{\ell}
.

(2)

For each prime
\ell|D
,
\pi_{\ell}
is transferrable, and
\Pi_{\ell}
lies in the corresponding local packet of representations of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
from Theorem
2.4.6
(
3
).

Proof.

If
\Pi
is not endoscopic, this follows from Theorem
2.4.6
. If
\Pi
is endoscopic, (
1
) is immediate from Theorem
2.5.2
. For (
2
),
it follows because the Langlands correspondences of
[
37
,
38
]
are constructed to be compatible with theta lifting, and the
correspondence of local representations in
[
97
, Table 3]
respects Langlands parameters by Lemma 11.1 of
op. cit.

∎

Remark 2.5.4
.

By Corollary
2.5.3
, we can associate to each relevant automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
a compatible system of Galois representations
\rho_{\Pi,\iota}
as in Theorem
2.2.10
.

2.6.
Local representations with paramodular fixed vectors

Notation 2.6.1
.

For all primes
q
(whether or not
q|D
), the
paramodular subgroup

of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
is a maximal compact subgroup described in
[
105
, p. 918]
. To avoid confusion, we denote this subgroup by
K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}
when
q\nmid D
and by
K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}
when
q|D
.

Lemma 2.6.2
.

Let
\pi
be a relevant automorphic representation of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
, and suppose
q\nmid D
is a prime such that
\pi_{q}
has a
K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}
-fixed vector. Then:

(1)

\pi_{q}
is either spherical or of type IIa in the notation of

[
96
]
.

(2)

\pi
is the unique completion of
\pi_{f}^{q}\otimes\pi_{\infty}
to an automorphic representation of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
.

(3)

If
\pi_{q}
is of type IIa, then for any
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
with
q\neq p
, the action of
I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}
on
V_{\pi,\iota}
is unipotent with monodromy of rank one. Moreover, the corresponding local packet of representations of
\spin(V_{Dq})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
in Theorem
2.4.6
(
3
) is a single representation with a unique
K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}
-fixed vector.

Proof.

By Corollary
2.5.3
,
\pi_{f}^{D}=\otimes_{\ell\nmid D}\pi_{\ell}
can be completed to a relevant automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
. In particular,
\pi_{q}
is tempered (by Theorem
2.2.10
(
1
)), so (
1
) follows from
[
96
, Tables A.1, A.13]
.

If
\pi^{q}_{f}\otimes\pi_{q}^{\prime}\otimes\pi_{\infty}
is automorphic for some
\pi_{q}^{\prime}
, then
\pi^{Dq}_{f}\otimes\pi_{q}^{\prime}
can be completed to an automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
by the same reasoning as for
\pi_{f}^{D}
. Hence by Theorem
2.2.10
(
1
),
\pi_{q}^{\prime}
and
\pi_{q}
belong to the same Gan-Takeda local
L
-packet. But the
L
-packets of type I and IIa are singletons, so this shows (
2
).

Finally, suppose

\pi_{q}
is the type IIa representation denoted
\chi\operatorname{St}_{\GL(2)}\rtimes\sigma
in
loc. cit.
Because
\pi_{q}
has a
K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}
-fixed vector,

\chi
and
\sigma
are unramified characters of
\mathbb{Q}_{q}^{\times}
. This implies the assertions on
\rho_{\pi,\iota}|_{I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}}
in (
3
) by Theorem
2.2.10
(
1
) and the explicit local Langlands paremeters found in
[
96
, Table A.7]
. The final claim in (
3
)
follows from
[
97
, Table 3]
combined with
[
105
, Theorem B]
.
∎

2.7.
Generic maximal ideals and cohomology of
\spin_{5}
Shimura varieties

2.7.1.

For this subsection, fix a
coefficient field
E\subset\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with
E
a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and let
O\subset E
be the ring of integers with uniformizer
\varpi
. Also fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
, a
squarefree
D\geq 1
, a neat compact open subgroup
K=\prod_{\ell}K_{\ell}\subset\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and a set
S
of places of
\mathbb{Q}
containing all
\ell
such that
K_{\ell}
is not hyperspecial.

Lemma 2.7.2
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}
is non-Eisenstein and
\sigma(D)
is even. Then for all
i
, the natural maps induce isomorphisms:

(2.15)

\begin{split}H^{i}_{c}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),O)_{\mathfrak{m}}&\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),O)_{\mathfrak{m}},\\
H^{i}_{c}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}&\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}.\end{split}

Proof.

We show the second isomorphism; the first follows formally. Let
P=MN\subset\spin(V_{D})
be a parabolic subgroup. Then we may fix an identification
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}^{S})\simeq\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A}^{S})
such that
P(\mathbb{A}^{S})=P_{1}(\mathbb{A}^{S})
for a parabolic subgroup
P_{1}=M_{1}N_{1}\subset\GSP_{4}
.

The Levi factor
M
is abstractly isomorphic to either
B_{D}^{\times}\times\GL_{1}
or
\GL_{1}^{3}
(the latter occurring only if
D=1
).
To any compact open subgroup
K_{M}\subset M(\mathbb{A}_{f})
we can associate a locally symmetric space
S_{K_{M}}(M)=M(\mathbb{Q})\backslash M(\mathbb{A})/K_{M}\cdot K_{\infty}
, where
K_{\infty}\subset M(\mathbb{R})
is the product of the center and a maximal compact subgroup. In particular, each connected component of
S_{K_{M}}(M)
is either a Shimura curve or an isolated point.

Using the Borel-Serre compactification of
\Sh_{K}(V_{D})
and the argument of
[
83
, §4]
, it
suffices to show the following: for any parabolic subgroup
P=MN\subset\spin(V_{D})
as above and any compact open subgroup
K_{M}=\prod_{\ell}K_{M,\ell}\subset M(\mathbb{A}_{f})
with
K_{M,\ell}
hyperspecial for
\ell\not\in S
, the support of the
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{\GSP_{4},\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}
-module

(S_{M}^{\GSP_{4}})^{\ast}H^{i}(S_{K_{M}}(M),\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is Eisenstein for all
i
. But this follows from Proposition
2.1.9
because, as
M=B_{D}^{\times}\times\GL_{1}
or
\GL_{1}^{3}
, every maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{M,\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}
in the support of
H^{i}(S_{K_{M}}(M),\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is clearly of Galois type.
∎

Definition 2.7.3
.

(1)

A maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}_{\ell,O}
is called
generic
if
p\nmid 2\ell(\ell^{4}-1)
, and the Satake parameter
\left\{\alpha,\beta,\nu/\alpha,\nu/\beta\right\}
of
\mathfrak{m}
is multiplicity-free with no two elements having ratio
\ell
.

(2)

A maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subset{\mathbb{T}}^{S}_{O}
is called
generic
if there exist infinitely many
\ell\not\in S
such that the induced maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}_{\ell,O}
is generic. For any quotient
\mathbb{T}
of
{\mathbb{T}}^{S}_{O}
, a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}
is called generic if its pullback to
{\mathbb{T}}^{S}_{O}
is so.

(3)

A Galois representation
\overline{\rho}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\GSP_{4}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
, unramified outside a finite set
S
, is called
generic
if there exists a prime
\ell\not\in S
with
p\nmid 2\ell(\ell^{4}-1)
, such that
\overline{\rho}(\Frob_{\ell})
has distinct eigenvalues, no two having ratio
\ell
.

Remark 2.7.4
.

If
\mathfrak{m}\subset{\mathbb{T}}^{S}_{O}
has an associated Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
, clearly
\mathfrak{m}
is generic if and only if
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is so.

Theorem 2.7.5
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}
is a generic maximal ideal and
\sigma(D)
is even.
Then:

(1)

For all
i<3
, we have

H^{i}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}=H^{6-i}_{c}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{m}}=0.

(2)

If
\mathfrak{m}
is also non-Eisenstein, then

H^{i}_{c}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),O)_{\mathfrak{m}}=H^{i}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),O)_{\mathfrak{m}}

is
\varpi
-torsion-free for all
i
, and vanishes unless
i=3
.

Proof.

Part (
1
) is immediate from
[
43
, Theorem 1.16]
and our definition of genericity. Using Lemma
2.7.2
, (
2
) is a standard consequence of (
1
).
∎

Lemma 2.7.6
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}_{K,V_{D},O}^{S}
is non-Eisenstein and generic, and suppose

\pi
is an automorphic representation of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
such that
\pi_{f}^{K}\neq 0
, and the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}
on
\iota^{-1}\pi_{f}^{K}
factors through
\mathbb{T}_{K,V_{D},O,\mathfrak{m}}^{S}
. Then
\pi
is not Eisenstein (in the sense of Definition
2.4.2
).

With a bit more care, the genericity assumption can be dropped; we leave the details to the reader.

Proof.

Suppose first that
D=1
, so
\pi
is an automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
, and let
\mathfrak{m}_{\pi}\subset{\mathbb{T}}^{S}_{\GSP_{4},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
be the maximal ideal determined by the Hecke action on
\iota^{-1}\pi^{S}
. Then by hypothesis,
\mathfrak{m}_{\pi}
is in the support of the Hecke module
H^{3}(\Sh_{K}(V_{1}),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}.

By Lemma
2.7.2
combined with the diagram in
[
109
, p. 293]
, we conclude that
\pi^{S}
can be completed to an automorphic representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
that appears in the discrete spectrum. Suppose for contradiction that
\pi
is Eisenstein; then
\pi^{\prime}
is either CAP or a residual representation. In either case, it follows that the Satake parameter of
\pi_{\ell}
contains a pair of the form
\left\{\alpha,\alpha\ell\right\}
for all
\ell\not\in S
: when
\pi^{\prime}
is CAP, this uses
[
89
, Theorems 2.5, 2.6]
and
[
107
, Theorem C]
, and when
\pi^{\prime}
is residual it uses well-known results on the irreducibility of principal series representations for
\GSP_{4}
. But these Satake parameters are inconsistent with the genericity of
\mathfrak{m}
, so
\pi
cannot be Eisenstein, as desired.

We now handle the case of general
D
; the argument is a mild refinement of the trace formula method used in
[
97
]
. We abbreviate
G_{D}\coloneqq\spin(V_{D})
and fix a minimal parabolic subgroup
P_{0}\subset G_{D}
(which will be all of
G_{D}
if
\sigma(D)
is odd).
Let
f_{D}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{D}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),\mathbb{C})
be a test function with regular support, and define

(2.16)

T(f_{D})=\sum_{P_{0}\subset P=MN\subset G_{D}}(-1)^{\operatorname{rank}(M)-\operatorname{rank}(G_{D})}\sum_{w\in W^{P}}(-1)^{\ell(w)}\tr\left(\overline{f}_{D}^{P}\cdot\chi_{P}^{G},\varinjlim_{K_{M}}H^{\ast}(S_{K_{M}}(M),V_{w\cdot\rho_{G_{D}}-\rho_{M}})\right).

Here
W^{P}
is the set of minimal-length coset representatives for the Weyl group of
G_{D}
modulo that of
M
,
\tr
is the supertrace,
\overline{f}^{P}
and
\chi_{P}^{G_{D}}
are defined as in
[
127
, §2.6]
,
S_{K_{M}}(M)
is the symmetric space from the proof of Lemma
2.7.2
,
\rho_{G_{D}}
and
\rho_{M}
are the half-sums of positive roots, and
V_{w\cdot\rho_{G_{D}}-\rho_{M}}
is the complex local system on
S_{K_{M}}(M)
of weight
w\cdot\rho_{G_{D}}-\rho_{M}
. Although
w\cdot\rho_{G_{D}}-\rho_{M}
might not be integral, we interpret
V_{w\cdot\rho_{G_{D}}-\rho_{M}}
as the twist of
V_{w\cdot\rho_{G_{D}}-\rho_{G_{D}}}
by the real character
\delta_{P}^{1/2}
of
M(\mathbb{R})
, i.e.

(2.17)

H^{\ast}(S_{K_{M}}(M),V_{w\cdot\rho_{G_{D}}-\rho_{M}})=\delta_{P}^{-1/2}\otimes H^{\ast}(S_{K_{M}}(M),V_{w\cdot\rho_{G_{D}}-\rho_{G_{D}}})

as Hecke modules.

For test functions
f_{1}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),\mathbb{C})
with regular support, we define
T(f_{1})
analogously to (
2.16
).
If
f_{D}
and
f_{1}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),\mathbb{C})
are matching functions in the sense of
[
97
, §5]
, then it follows from combining
[
127
, Lemma 2.10]
with
[
97
, Theorem 5.3]
that

(2.18)

T(f_{D})=T(f_{1})c_{D}

for a nonzero constant
c_{D}
depending only on
D
.

Shrinking
K_{S}
if necessary, we can fix a
K_{S}
-biinvariant test function
f_{D,S}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{D}(\mathbb{A}_{S}))
with regular support such that

(2.19)

\tr\left(f_{D,S},H^{\ast}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),\mathbb{C})[\pi^{S}])\right)\neq 0;

when
\sigma(D)
is even,
we use Theorem
2.7.5
(
2
) to ensure that contributions from
\pi
in different degrees do not cancel.Let
f_{1,S}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{S}))
be a matching function, and let
K_{1,S}\subset G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{S})
be a compact open subgroup such that
f_{1,S}
is
K_{1,S}
-biinvariant. We also fix an isomorphism
G_{D}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{S})\simeq G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{S})
,
and let
K_{1}=K^{S}K_{1,S}\subset G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, which is a compact open subgroup.
Let

\Pi(D)=\bigoplus_{P_{0}\subset P=MN\subset G_{D}}\bigoplus_{w\in W^{P}}\Ind_{P(\mathbb{A}_{f})}^{G_{D}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}\varinjlim_{K_{M}}H^{\ast}(S_{K_{M}}(M),V_{w\cdot\rho_{G_{D}}-\rho_{M}}),

and
let
{\mathcal{P}}_{D}
be the set of irreducible
G_{D}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-constituents occurring in
\Pi(D)
with a
K
-fixed vector. Likewise, we define
\Pi(1)
and
{\mathcal{P}}_{1}
, where now we consider constituents with
K_{1}
-fixed vectors, and set
{\mathcal{P}}={\mathcal{P}}_{D}\sqcup{\mathcal{P}}_{1}
.

Then
\mathcal{P}
contains finitely many near equivalence classes, cf.

[
127
, p. 45]
.
We can therefore fix a finite set
T
of primes of
\mathbb{Q}
, disjoint from
S
, so that two representations
\sigma,\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}
are nearly equivalent if and only if
\sigma_{T}\cong\tau_{T}
.
Now fix a
K_{T}=\prod_{\ell\in T}K_{\ell}
-biinvariant test function
f_{T}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{D}(\mathbb{A}_{T}),\mathbb{C})=C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{T}),\mathbb{C})
, such that for all
\tau\in{\mathcal{P}}
,
\tr(f_{T}|\tau_{T})=0
unless
\tau_{T}\cong\pi_{T}
, and
\tr(f_{T}|\pi_{T})=1
.

Also fix an auxiliary prime
v_{0}\not\in S\cup T
, and for a large constant
C>0
to be chosen later, let
f_{v_{0},C}\in\mathbb{T}_{\GSP_{4},v_{0},\mathbb{C}}
be a test function satisfying the conclusion of
[
97
, Lemma 3.9]
for the representation
\pi_{v_{0}}
; in particular,
\tr(f_{v_{0},C}|\pi_{v_{0}})=1.

We consider the global test function

f_{D}=f_{D,S}f_{T}f_{v_{0},C}f^{S\cup T\cup\left\{v_{0}\right\}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{D}(\mathbb{A}_{f})),

where
f^{S\cup T\cup\left\{v_{0}\right\}}
is the indicator function of
K^{S\cup T\cup\left\{v_{0}\right\}}
. The matching function is

f_{1}=f_{1,S}f_{T}f_{v_{0},C}f^{S\cup T\cup\left\{v_{0}\right\}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})).

Claim
.

For all
P_{0}\subset P=MN\subsetneq G_{1}
and all
w\in W^{P}
, we have

\tr\left(\overline{f}_{1}^{P}\cdot\chi_{P}^{G},\;\delta_{P}^{-1/2}\varinjlim_{K_{M}}H^{\ast}(S_{K_{M}}(M),V_{w\cdot\rho_{G_{1}}-\rho_{G_{1}}})\right)=0,

and likewise for all
P_{0}\subset P=MN\subsetneq G_{D}
.

Proof of claim.

To ease notation, we prove the claim for
G_{1}
; the proof for
G_{D}
is identical.
Let
\tau
be an irreducible constituent of
\varinjlim H^{\ast}(S_{K_{M}}(M),V_{w\cdot\rho_{G_{1}}-\rho_{G_{1}}})
.
It suffices to show that

\tr\left(\overline{f}^{P}_{1}\cdot\chi_{P}^{G_{1}},\tau\otimes\delta_{P}^{-1/2}\right)=0.

Now by the argument of
[
97
, Proposition 3.10]
, if
C
is chosen sufficiently large, then

\tr\left(\overline{f}^{P}_{1}\cdot\chi_{P}^{G},\tau\otimes\delta_{P}^{-1/2}\right)=\tr\left(\overline{f_{1,S}f_{T}f^{S\cup T\cup\left\{v_{0}\right\}}}^{P}\cdot f^{P}_{v_{0}},\tau\otimes\delta_{P}^{-1/2}\right)

for an auxiliary spherical test function
f^{P}_{v_{0}}
on
M(\mathbb{Q}_{v_{0}})
. In particular, because
\overline{f}^{P}
is invariant under
K_{1}\cap M(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
we may assume without loss of generality that
\tau
contains a fixed vector for
K_{1}\cap M(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Moreover it suffices to show

\tr\left(\overline{f}^{P}_{T},\tau_{T}\otimes\delta_{P}^{-1/2}\right)=0.

By
[
97
, Proposition 3.5(2)]
, the latter trace coincides with

(2.20)

\tr\left(f_{T},\natural-\Ind_{M(\mathbb{A}_{T})}^{G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{T})}\tau\right).

We will show (
2.20
) vanishes. Indeed, since each constituent of
\natural-\Ind_{M(\mathbb{A}_{f})}^{G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}\tau
lies in
\mathcal{P}_{1}
, if (
2.20
) is nonzero then
\pi
is nearly equivalent to a constituent of
\natural-\Ind_{M(\mathbb{A}_{f})}^{G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}\tau
by the choice of
f_{T}
. Now, the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\tau}\subset{\mathbb{T}}^{S}_{{M},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
defined by the Hecke action on
\iota^{-1}\tau
is of Galois type because
\tau
is cohomological and

M=\GL_{1}^{3}
or
\GL_{2}\times\GL_{1}
. Hence by Propositions
2.1.2
and
2.1.9
, the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{\pi}\subset{\mathbb{T}}^{S}_{\GSP_{4},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
defined by
\iota^{-1}\pi
is Eisenstein, which one can easily check contradicts the hypothesis that
\mathfrak{m}
is non-Eisenstein.
∎

In particular, the claim combined with (
2.19
)
and the choice of
f_{T}
shows that
T(f_{D})\neq 0
, so
T(f_{1})\neq 0
by (
2.18
). Using the claim again,
we see that

\tr\left(f_{1},H^{\ast}(\Sh_{K_{1}}(V_{1}),\mathbb{C})\right)\neq 0.

By Franke’s theorem
[
33
]
and our choice of
f_{T}
, we conclude there exists an automorphic representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
which is nearly equivalent to
\pi
and unramified outside
S
, such that the Hecke eigensystem of
\pi^{\prime S}
appears in
H^{\ast}(\Sh_{K_{1}}(V_{1}),\mathbb{C})
.
Expand
S
to a larger set
S^{\prime}
such that
\pi^{S^{\prime}}\cong\pi^{\prime S^{\prime}}
. Then the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}^{S^{\prime}}\subset{\mathbb{T}}^{S^{\prime}}_{\GSP_{4},O}
formed by restricting
\mathfrak{m}
descends to
\mathbb{T}^{S^{\prime}}_{K_{1},V_{1},O}
. By the case
D=1
of the lemma for
\mathfrak{m}^{S^{\prime}}
and
\pi^{\prime}
,
\pi^{\prime}
is not Eisenstein, hence
\pi
is not either.
∎

Corollary 2.7.7
.

Suppose
\sigma(D)
is even, and
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{S}_{K,V_{D},O}
is a generic, non-Eisenstein maximal ideal. Also fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
. Then

H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}=\bigoplus_{\pi_{f}}\iota^{-1}\pi_{f}^{K}\otimes\rho_{\pi_{f}},

where:

•

\pi_{f}
runs over the finite parts of relevant automorphic representations
\pi
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that the
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}
-action on
\pi_{f}^{K}
factors through
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{K,V_{D},O,\mathfrak{m}}
.

•

If
\pi
is not endoscopic, then
\rho_{\pi_{f}}=\rho_{\pi,\iota}(-2)
, cf. Remark
2.5.4
.

•

If
\pi
is endoscopic associated to a pair
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
with discrete series archimedean components of weights 2 and 4, respectively, we can write
\pi=\Theta(\pi_{1}^{D_{1}}\boxtimes\pi_{2}^{D_{2}})
by Theorem
2.5.2
. Then

\rho_{\pi_{f}}=\begin{cases}\rho_{\pi_{1},\iota}(-2),&\sigma(D_{1})\text{ even},\\
\rho_{\pi_{2},\iota}(-2),&\sigma(D_{1})\text{ odd}.\end{cases}

Proof.

It follows from Lemmas
2.7.6
and
2.4.4
(
2
)
that

H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}\cong\bigoplus_{\pi_{f}}\iota^{-1}\pi_{f}^{K}\otimes\rho_{\pi_{f}}

as Hecke modules,
where
\pi_{f}
runs over the finite parts of non-Eisenstein automorphic representations of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
with Hecke action factoring through
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{K,V_{D},O,\mathfrak{m}}
, and
\rho_{\pi_{f}}
is some Galois representation with

\dim\rho_{\pi_{f}}=\sum_{\pi_{\infty}^{\prime}}m(\pi_{f}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{\prime})\dim H^{3}(\mathfrak{gsp}_{4},U(2);\pi_{\infty}^{\prime}).

In particular, by (
2.10
), the only
\pi_{f}
with
\rho_{\pi_{f}}\neq 0
are the finite parts of
relevant
automorphic representations
\pi
.

We first consider the non-endoscopic case.
As in
[
109
, p. 296]
, we see from Theorem
2.4.6
(
3
) that
\rho_{\pi_{f}}
is four-dimensional with Hodge-Tate weights
\left\{0,-1,-2,-3\right\}
if
\pi
is not endoscopic.
Since for all but finitely many
\ell\not\in S
,
\Frob_{\ell}
satisfies the Eichler-Shimura relation (
2.9
) on
\rho_{\pi_{f}}
, we conclude that
\rho_{\pi_{f}}=\rho_{\pi,\iota}(-2)
.
It remains to consider the endoscopic case, when
\pi=\Theta(\pi_{1}^{D_{1}}\boxtimes\pi_{2}^{D_{2}}).

Then
\pi
is the unique completion of
\pi_{f}
to an automorphic representation of

\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
by Theorem
2.5.2
. Moreover, by the local archimedean theta lift described in
[
45
, Proposition 4.3.1]
,
\pi_{\infty}
is the generic or holomorphic member of the discrete series
L
-packet in the case that
\sigma(D_{1})
is even or odd, respectively. Hence we conclude as above that
\rho_{\pi_{f}}
is two-dimensional, with Hodge-Tate weights
\left\{-1,-2\right\}
or
\left\{0,-3\right\}
when
\sigma(D_{1})
is even or odd, respectively. Since we still have the Eichler-Shimura relation (
2.9
), it follows that
\rho_{\pi_{f}}
is either
\rho_{\pi_{1},\iota}(-2)
or
\rho_{\pi_{2},\iota}(-2)
depending on the Hodge-Tate weights, and the corollary follows.
∎

Corollary 2.7.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{S}_{K,V_{D},O}
be a generic and non-Eisenstein maximal ideal, and let
\mathcal{T}
be the set of relevant automorphic representations
\pi
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
such that
\pi_{f}^{K}\neq 0
and the Hecke action on
\iota^{-1}\pi_{f}^{K}
factors through
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{K,V_{D},O,\mathfrak{m}}
. Then we
have a natural embedding of
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{K,V_{D},O,\mathfrak{m}}
-algebras

\mathbb{T}^{S}_{K,V_{D},O,\mathfrak{m}}\hookrightarrow\bigoplus_{\pi\in\mathcal{T}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\pi),

where
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\pi)
is
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with Hecke action through the eigenvalues on
\iota^{-1}\pi_{f}^{K}
.

Proof.

If
\sigma(D)
is odd, this is immediate from Lemma
2.7.6
. If
\sigma(D)
is even, it follows from Theorem
2.7.5
(
2
) combined with Corollary
2.7.7
.
∎

3.
Special cycles and theta lifts

3.1.
Special cycles

3.1.1.

In this subsection, we explain the construction of special cycles
Z(T,\varphi)
, due to Kudla in the indefinite case
[
55
]
.

Construction 3.1.2
.

Let
V
be a quadratic space over
\mathbb{Q}
of signature
(m,2)
or
(m,0)
.

(1)

If
V_{0}\subset V
is a positive definite subspace, then for any
g\in\spin(V_{0})(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
,
we obtain a canonical finite morphism

(3.1)

\Sh_{K_{0,g}}(V_{0}^{\perp})\xrightarrow{\cdot g}\Sh_{K}(V),

with
K_{0,g}\coloneqq gKg^{-1}\cap\spin(V_{0}^{\perp})(\mathbb{A}_{f}).

(i)

If
V
has signature
(m,2)
and
\dim V_{0}=n
, then we write

Z(g,V_{0},V)_{K}\in\CH^{n}(\Sh_{K}(V))

for the pushforward of the fundamental class on
\Sh_{K_{0,g}}(V_{0}^{\perp})
under (
3.1
).

(ii)

If
V
has signature
(m,0)
, then we write

Z(g,V_{0},V)_{K}\in\mathbb{Z}\left[\Sh_{K}(V)\right]

for the pushforward of the constant function 1 on
\Sh_{K_{0,g}}(V_{0}^{\perp})
under (
3.1
).

(2)

For any
T\in\Sym_{n}(\mathbb{Q})_{\geq 0}
, let

\Omega_{T,V}=\left\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in V^{n}\,:\,x_{i}\cdot x_{j}=T_{ij}\;\forall\,1\leq i,j\leq n\right\},

viewed as an affine algebraic variety over
\mathbb{Q}
.

(3)

Now suppose given a neat compact open subgroup
K\subset\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, along with a test function

\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f},R)^{K}

for some
n\leq m
and some ring
R
. (The action of
K
is the natural one, factoring through the map to
\SO(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.) For any
T\in\Sym_{n}(\mathbb{Q})_{\geq 0}
, if
\Omega_{T,V}(\mathbb{Q})=\emptyset
, then the special cycle
Z(T,\varphi)_{K}
, in
\CH^{n}(\Sh_{K}(V),R)\coloneqq\CH^{n}(\Sh_{K}(V))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}R
or
R\left[\Sh_{K}(V)\right]
, is defined to vanish.
Otherwise:

(i)

If
V
is positive definite, then fix a base point
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\Omega_{T,V}(\mathbb{Q})
, and let

V_{0}=\Span_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\right\}\subset V.

Then we define

(3.2)

Z(T,\varphi)_{K}=\sum_{g\in\spin(V_{0}^{\perp})(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K}\varphi(g^{-1}x_{1},\ldots,g^{-1}x_{n})Z(g,V_{0},V)_{K}\in R[\Sh_{K}(V)].

(ii)

If
V
has signature
(m,2)
and
T
is positive definite, we define

Z(T,\varphi)_{K}\in\CH^{n}(\Sh_{K}(V),R)

by the same formula (
3.2
). If
T
is not positive definite (and still
\Omega_{T,V}(\mathbb{Q})\neq 0)
, we define
Z(T,\varphi)_{K}\in\CH^{n}(\Sh_{K}(V),R)
by the recipe in
[
55
, p. 61]
; the details will not be needed.

Remark 3.1.3
.

By transitivity of the
\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q})
-action on
\Omega_{T,V}(\mathbb{Q})
,
Z(T,\varphi)_{K}
is independent of the choice of base point for
\Omega_{T,V}(\mathbb{Q})
.

Proposition 3.1.4
.

For neat compact open subgroups
K^{\prime}\subset K\subset\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, if
\pr_{K,K^{\prime}}:\Sh_{K^{\prime}}(V)\to\Sh_{K}(V)
is the natural map, then
\pr_{K,K^{\prime}}^{\ast}Z(T,\varphi)_{K}=Z(T,\varphi)_{K^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

This is
[
55
, Proposition 5.10]
in the indefinite case; the definite case is proved in the same way.
∎

Notation 3.1.5
.

For any compact open subgroup
K\subset\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and any ring
R
, we define
{\operatorname{SC}}_{K}^{n}(V,R)
to be the
R
-span of the special cycles
Z(T,\varphi)_{K}
for
T\in\Sym_{n}(\mathbb{Q})_{\geq 0}
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f},R)
. When
R=\mathbb{Z}
it may be dropped from the notation.

Remark 3.1.6
.

Note that
{\operatorname{SC}}_{K}^{n}(V,\mathbb{Z})
contains all of the special cycles
z=Z(g,V_{0},V)_{K}
from (
3.1.1
) with
\dim V_{0}=n
. Indeed, choose a basis
\left\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\right\}
for
V_{0}
, and set
T_{ij}\coloneqq e_{i}\cdot e_{j}
. Then one can choose
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(V\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f},\mathbb{Z})^{K}
such that
\varphi|_{\Omega_{T,V}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}
is the indicator function of
K\cdot g^{-1}(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})
, and it follows that
Z(T,\varphi)_{K}=Z(g,V_{0},V)_{K}
.

3.1.7.

We will later need the following proposition to understand the double coset space appearing in (
3.2
).

Proposition 3.1.8
.

Suppose
K_{\ell}\subset\SO(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
is the stabilizer of a self-dual lattice
L\subset V\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
, and
V=V_{0}\oplus V_{1}
be an orthogonal decomposition of
V
.
Then the natural map

\displaystyle\SO(V_{0})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\backslash\SO(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})/K_{\ell}

\displaystyle\to\left\{\textrm{lattices }L_{1}\subset V_{1}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}\right\}

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\mapsto g\cdot L\cap V_{1}

is injective. If
\dim(V_{1})<\dim(V_{0})
, then its image consists of all
L_{1}
on which the pairing is
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-valued.

Proof.

This follows from (the proof of)
[
27
, Propositions 3.1.5, 3.1.6]
.
∎

3.2.
Symplectic and metaplectic groups

In this section, we set up basic notions for symplectic and metaplectic groups, mostly following the exposition of
[
35
]
.

Notation 3.2.1
.

(1)

For an integer
n\geq 1
, we define the standard symplectic lattice
W_{2n}

with basis
e_{1},\ldots,e_{n},e_{1}^{\ast},\ldots,e_{n}^{\ast}

and pairing determined by

(3.3)

\langle e_{i},e_{j}\rangle=0,\;\;\langle e_{i}^{\ast},e_{j}^{\ast}\rangle=0,\;\;\langle e_{i},e_{j}^{\ast}\rangle=\delta_{ij}.

The symplectic group
\SP_{2n}
as defined in (
1.1.4
) is the isometry group of

W_{2n}
.

(2)

The Siegel parabolic subgroup
P=MN\subset\SP_{2n}
is the stabilizer of
\Span_{\mathbb{Z}}\left\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\right\}\subset W_{2n}
.
We identify
N
with
\Sym_{n}
, the space of
n\times n
symmetric matrices, and
M
with
\GL_{n}
via the embedding

g\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}g&0\\
0&g^{-t}\end{pmatrix}\in\SP_{2n},\;\;g\in\GL_{n}.

(3)

If
k

is a local field of characteristic zero, the metaplectic group
\MP_{2n}(k)

is defined as the unique non-split central extension of
\SP_{2n}(k)

by
\mu_{2}
:

0\rightarrow\mu_{2}\rightarrow\MP_{2n}(k)\xrightarrow{g\mapsto\overline{g}}\SP_{2n}(k)\rightarrow 0.

The double cover
\MP_{2n}(k)\to\SP_{2n}(k)
splits uniquely over
N(k)
, with
P=MN
the Siegel parabolic as above. The preimage
\widetilde{P}(k)
of
P(k)
therefore has a Levi decomposition

\widetilde{P}(k)=\widetilde{M}(k)N(k)

with
\widetilde{M}(k)
a nonsplit double cover of
M(k)
.

(4)

If
k

is non-archimedean with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}

and the residue characteristic of
\mathcal{O}

is odd, let
\MP_{2n}(\mathcal{O})\subset\MP_{2n}(k)
be the unique lifting of
\SP_{2n}(\mathcal{O})

[
36
, §6]
.

(5)

Let
U(n)\hookrightarrow\SP_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
be the embedding defined by

A+iB\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
-B&A\end{pmatrix}.

We fix the Cartan decomposition

\mathfrak{sp}_{2n,\mathbb{C}}=\mathfrak{u}(n)_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{-},

such that
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is isomorphic to the symmetric square of the defining representation of
U(n)
.

(6)

Let
\widetilde{U}(n)\subset\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
be the preimage of
U(n)
. If
j^{1/2}(g,z)
is the half-integral weight automorphy factor of
[
102
, p. 25]
, then we let

\det^{1/2}:\widetilde{U}(n)\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be the restriction of
j^{1/2}(g,i)
, which is a square root of the determinant character. We set
\det^{k}\coloneqq(\det^{1/2})^{2k}
for all
k\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
.

(7)

Globally, let

\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{A})=\prod_{v}^{\prime}\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})

be the restricted product with respect to the subgroups
\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{Z}_{v})\subset\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})

for
v\neq 2,\infty
. The inclusion
\SP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow\SP_{2n}(\mathbb{A})

lifts naturally to

\SP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{A}),

by which we will view
\SP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q})
as a subgroup of
\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{A})
.

Definition 3.2.2
.

For
k\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
,
define the space

M^{2n}_{k}
of adelic Siegel modular forms of degree
2n

and weight
k
, consisting of smooth functions

f:\SP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{A})\to\mathbb{C}

such that:

(1)

f(gz)=\debt^{k}(z)f(g)

for any
z\in\widetilde{U}(n)\subset\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
.

(2)

X\cdot f(g)=0

for any
X\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}\subset\mathfrak{sp}_{2n,\mathbb{R}}
.

Remark 3.2.3
.

Note that
M_{k}^{2n}

is naturally an
\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-module.

Notation 3.2.4
.

(1)

Let
\psi

be the additive character of
\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbb{A}

which is unramified at all finite places and satisfies

\psi(x_{\infty})=e^{2\pi ix_{\infty}}

for
x_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}\subset\mathbb{A}.

(2)

For
T\in\Sym_{n}(\mathbb{Q})
, define

\psi_{T}\coloneqq\psi\circ\left(\frac{1}{2}\tr(T-)\right),

a unitary character of
N(\mathbb{Q})\backslash N(\mathbb{A})
(where
N
is the unipotent radical of the Siegel parabolic).

From basic Fourier analysis, one deduces the following proposition.

Proposition 3.2.5
.

Let
f\in M_{k}^{2n}
.
Then we have an identity of functions on
N(\mathbb{A})\subset\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{A})
:

f=\sum_{T\in\Sym_{n}(\mathbb{Q})}a_{T}(f)q_{\mathbb{A}}^{T},

where
q_{\mathbb{A}}^{T}\coloneqq e^{-\tr(T)}\cdot\psi_{T}
and

a_{T}(f)\coloneqq\frac{e^{\trace(T)}}{\volume([N])}\int_{[N]}f(n)\psi_{T}^{-1}(n)\mathrm{d}n.

Definition 3.2.6
.

For any subring
R\subset\mathbb{C}
, define

M_{k,R}^{2n}=\left\{f\in M_{k}^{2n}\,:\,a_{T}(f)\in R\text{ for all }T\in\symmetric_{n}(\mathbb{Q})\right\}.

3.3.
Weil representation

3.3.1.

Let
k

be a local field, and let
V

be a quadratic space over
k

of

odd

dimension and trivial discriminant. Also fix an even integer
2n\geq 2
. For any nontrivial additive character
\psi

of
k
, the Weil representation
\omega_{\psi}

of
\SO(V)(k)\times\MP_{2n}(k)

is realized on the complex Schwartz space
\mathcal{S}(V^{n},\mathbb{C})
, and is determined by the following formulas.

(3.4)

\begin{cases}\omega_{\psi}(g,1)\varphi(x)=\varphi(g^{-1}x),&g\in\SO(V)(k).\\
\omega_{\psi}(1,u)\varphi(x)=\psi(\frac{1}{2}u(x)\cdot x)\varphi(x),&u\in N(k)\cong\symmetric_{n}(k).\\
\omega_{\psi}(1,m)\varphi(x)=\chi_{\psi}(m)|\debt(\overline{m})|^{\frac{\dim V}{2}}\varphi(\overline{m}^{t}x),&m\in\widetilde{M}(k).\\
\omega_{\psi}(1,w)\varphi(x)=\gamma_{w}\int_{V^{n}}\varphi(y)\psi(x\cdot y)\mathrm{d}y.\end{cases}

Here, the notation is as follows:

\circ

P=MN

is the Siegel parabolic.

\circ

\chi_{\psi}

is a
\mu_{8}
-valued genuine character of
\widetilde{M}(k)

described in
[
35
, p. 1661]
.

\circ

w\in\MP_{2n}(k)

is a certain Weyl element such that
\overline{w}P\overline{w}^{-1}=P^{\text{op}}
.

\circ

\gamma_{w}

is a certain eighth root of unity.

\circ

The pairing on
V^{n}

in the second and fourth equations is

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\cdot(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})=\sum x_{i}\cdot y_{i}.

\circ

\mathrm{d}x

in the fourth equation is a self-dual Haar measure.

3.3.2.

If
k

is non-archimedean of residue characteristic
\ell

and
R\subset\mathbb{C}

is a
\mathbb{Z}\left[\frac{1}{\ell}\right]
-algebra containing all eighth and
\ell
th power roots of unity, then the same formulas give a well-defined action of
\SO(V)(k)\times\MP_{2n}(k)

on the space
\mathcal{S}(V^{n},R)

of
R
-valued Schwartz functions.

3.3.3.

Globally, if
V

is a quadratic space over
\mathbb{Q}
, we have Weil representations
\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{v},\mathbb{C})

for all places
v
, defined using the localizations of the fixed global additive character
\psi

of
\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbb{A}
. Similarly, we have the Weil representations
\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{A},\mathbb{C})
,
\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f},\mathbb{C})
, etc., defined as restricted tensor products.

3.4.
Classical theta lifting

Notation 3.4.1
.

For a Schwartz function
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{A},\mathbb{C})

and a cuspidal automorphic form
\alpha

on
\SO(V)(\mathbb{A})
, we write
\theta_{\varphi}(\alpha)

for the automorphic form on
\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{A})

defined by

(3.5)

\theta_{\varphi}(\alpha)(h)=\int_{[\SO(V)]}\alpha(g)\sum_{x\in V^{n}}\omega_{\psi}(g,h)\varphi(x)\mathrm{d}g.

The normalization depends on a choice of Haar measure.

3.4.2.

Suppose
V

is positive definite and let
K\subset\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})

be a neat compact open subgroup. For
\alpha:\Sh_{K}(V)\to\mathbb{C}
, define an automorphic form
\overline{\alpha}
on
\spin(V)(\mathbb{A})
, which descends to
\SO(V)(\mathbb{A})
, by

\overline{\alpha}(g_{f}g_{\infty})=\frac{1}{\volume(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times})}\int_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}}\alpha(g_{f}z)\mathrm{d}z,\;\;\forall g=g_{f}g_{\infty}\in\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}).

Lemma 3.4.3
.

Let
\varphi_{\infty}\in\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{R},\mathbb{C})

be the Gaussian

\varphi_{\infty}(x)=e^{-x\cdot x}.

Then for
\alpha:\Shimura_{K}(V)\to\mathbb{C}

and

\varphi_{f}\in\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f},\mathbb{C})^{K},

\theta_{\varphi_{f}\otimes\varphi_{\infty}}(\overline{\alpha})

lies in
M^{2n}_{\frac{\dim(V)}{2}}

and

a_{T}\left(\theta_{\varphi_{f}\otimes\varphi_{\infty}}(\overline{\alpha})\right)=C_{K}\cdot\alpha\left(Z(T,\varphi_{f})_{K}\right),

where the constant is

C_{K}=\frac{\volume\left(\SO(V)(\mathbb{R})\cdot\image\left(K\to\SO(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)\right)}{[K\cdot\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}:K]}.

Proof.

In the Fock model of the
\left(\mathfrak{sp}_{2n\dim(V)},\widetilde{U}(n\dim(V))\right)
-module associated to
\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{R},\mathbb{C})
,
\varphi_{\infty}
has degree zero, hence is annihilated by
\mathfrak{p}^{-}\subset\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}
; cf.
[
47
, (2.2)]
. By comparing degrees with
[
1
, Proposition 2.1(2)]
, we also conclude that
\widetilde{U}(n)
acts on
\theta_{\varphi_{f}\otimes\varphi_{\infty}}(\overline{\alpha})
by
\det^{\frac{\dim(V)}{2}},
which proves

\theta_{\varphi_{f}\otimes\varphi_{\infty}}(\overline{\alpha})\in M^{2n}_{\frac{\dim(V)}{2}}
.

It remains to compute the Fourier coefficients. We calculate:

\displaystyle a_{T}(\theta_{\varphi_{f}\otimes\varphi_{\infty}}(\overline{\alpha}))

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\trace T}}{\volume([N])}\int_{[N]}\psi_{T}^{-1}(u)\int_{[\SO(V)]}\overline{\alpha}(g)\sum_{x\in V^{n}(\mathbb{Q})}\omega_{\psi}(g,u)\varphi_{f}\otimes\varphi_{\infty}(x)\mathrm{d}g\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\trace T}}{\volume([N])}\int_{[\SO(V)]}\overline{\alpha}(g)\sum_{x\in V^{n}(\mathbb{Q})}\int_{[N]}\psi_{T}^{-1}(u)\psi\left(\frac{1}{2}u(x)\cdot x\right)\omega_{\psi}(g,1)\varphi_{f}\otimes\varphi_{\infty}(x)\mathrm{d}g\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\int_{[\SO(V)]}\overline{\alpha}(g)\sum_{x\in\Omega_{T,V}(\mathbb{Q})}\omega_{\psi}(g,1)\varphi_{f}(x)\mathrm{d}g.

Fix a base point
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\in\Omega_{T,V}(\mathbb{Q})

and let
V_{0}=\Span_{\mathbb{Q}}\left\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\right\}
. Then, since
\Omega_{T,V}(\mathbb{Q})

is a single
\SO(V)(\mathbb{Q})
-orbit, we may rewrite the final equation as

a_{T}(\theta_{\varphi_{f}\otimes\varphi_{\infty}}(\alpha))=\int_{\SO(V_{0}^{\perp})(\mathbb{A})\backslash\SO(V)(\mathbb{A})}\varphi_{f}(g^{-1}x)\int_{[\SO(V_{0}^{\perp})]}\overline{\alpha}(hg)\mathrm{d}h\mathrm{d}g.

This coincides with the claimed formula by definition of
\alpha(Z(T,\varphi_{f})_{K})
.
∎

3.4.4.

As a special case, suppose
V=\mathbb{Q}

is the one-dimensional space with quadratic form
a\mapsto a^{2}
. Then
\SO(V)

is the trivial group and we take the unit Haar measure on
\SO(V)(\mathbb{A})=\left\{\operatorname{id}\right\}
. For each prime
\ell
, let

\varphi_{\ell}\in\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},\mathbb{Z})

be the indicator function of the lattice

\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}^{n}\subset\mathbb{Q}^{n}_{\ell}=V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},

and let
\varphi_{f}=\otimes_{\ell}\varphi_{\ell}
. Let
\mathbbm{1}
be the unit automorphic form on
\SO(V)(\mathbb{A})
. We write

(3.6)

\theta_{\frac{1}{2}}\coloneqq\theta_{\varphi_{f}\otimes\varphi_{\infty}}(\mathbbm{1}).

Lemma 3.4.5
.

Let
\ell

be an odd prime, and suppose
R\subset\mathbb{C}

is a
\mathbb{Z}\left[\frac{1}{\ell}\right]
-algebra containing all eighth and
\ell
th power roots of unity. Then for any
g\in\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
,
g\cdot\theta_{\frac{1}{2}}

lies in
M^{2n}_{\frac{1}{2},R}
, and the constant term
a_{0}(g\cdot\theta_{\frac{1}{2}})

of its Fourier expansion lies in
R^{\times}
.

Proof.

By the obvious equivariance of the theta lift, we have

g\cdot\theta_{\frac{1}{2}}=\theta_{\omega_{\psi}(1,g)\varphi_{f}\otimes\varphi_{\infty}}(\mathbbm{1}).

Since

\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},R)\subset\mathcal{S}(V^{n},\mathbb{C})

is
\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
-stable, Lemma
3.4.3
shows

g\cdot\theta_{\frac{1}{2}}\in M^{2n}_{\frac{1}{2},R}.

Also,
\theta_{\frac{1}{2}}

is
\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
-invariant by construction.

By the Iwasawa decomposition

\SP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})=P(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\cdot\SP_{2n}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}),

it suffices to show
a_{0}(g\cdot\theta_{\frac{1}{2}})\in R^{\times}

for
g\in\widetilde{P}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
. Now, by Lemma
3.4.3
again,

a_{0}(g\cdot\theta_{\frac{1}{2}})=\omega_{\psi}(1,g)\cdot\varphi_{f}(0),

and it follows from the explicit formulas in (
3.3.1
) that

\omega_{\psi}(1,g)\cdot\varphi_{f}(0)\in R^{\times}

for all
g\in\widetilde{P}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
; this proves the lemma.
∎

Proposition 3.4.6
.

Let
R\subset\mathbb{C}

be a
\mathbb{Z}\left[\frac{1}{\ell}\right]
-algebra containing all eighth and
\ell
th power roots of unity. Then for all
k\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
,

M^{2n}_{k,R}\subset M^{2n}_{k}

is stable under the action of
\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
.

Proof.

If
k\in\mathbb{Z}

is integral, this is a consequence of the
q
-expansion principle for classical Siegel modular forms
[
31
, Chapter 5, Proposition 1.8]
. In general, for any
f\in M^{2n}_{k,R}

and
f^{\prime}\in M^{2n}_{k^{\prime},R}
, the product
ff^{\prime}\in M^{2n}_{k+k^{\prime},R}

has formal
q
-expansion:

(3.7)

(ff^{\prime})|_{N(\mathbb{A})}=\sum_{T\in\symmetric_{n}(\mathbb{Q})}\sum_{S\in\symmetric_{n}(\mathbb{Q})}a_{T}(f)a_{S}(f^{\prime})q_{\mathbb{A}}^{S+T}.

(This expression make sense because
a_{T}(f)

and
a_{S}(f^{\prime})

are each supported on positive semi-definite matrices with bounded denominators in their entries, see
[
102
, Proposition 1.1]
.)

We apply this to
f\in M^{2n}_{k,R}
, with
k\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
, and
f^{\prime}=\theta_{\frac{1}{2}}
. Since
k+\frac{1}{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

g(f\theta_{\frac{1}{2}})=g(f)g(\theta_{\frac{1}{2}})\in M^{2n}_{k+\frac{1}{2},R}

for any
g\in\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
.

Now, by Lemma
3.4.5
above, the
q
-expansion of
g(\theta_{\frac{1}{2}})

has an inverse power series

\sum_{T\in\symmetric_{n}(\mathbb{Q})}b_{T}q^{T}_{\mathbb{A}}

with
b_{T}\in R
. Hence, by the uniqueness of
q
-expansions, the Fourier coefficients of
g(f)

lie in
R

as well.
∎

3.5.
Formal theta lifts

3.5.1.

Suppose
V

has signature
(m,0)
or
(m,2)

for some
m\geq 1
, and let

K=\prod K_{\ell}\subset\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})

be a neat compact open subgroup.
For any subring
R\subset\mathbb{C}
, we define
\Test_{K}(V,R)

to be

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z}[\Shimura_{K}(V)],R)=\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(R[\Shimura_{K}(V)],R)

in the positive definite case, or

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\CH^{\ast}(\Shimura_{K}(V)),R)=\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(\CH^{\ast}(\Shimura_{K}(V),R),R)

in the indefinite case.

3.5.2.

Now fix
1\leq n\leq m
. For any
K
-invariant Schwartz function
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f},R)

and any
\alpha\in\Test_{K}(V,R)
, we define the formal theta lift

\Theta(\alpha,\varphi)_{K}\coloneqq\sum_{T\in\symmetric_{n}(\mathbb{Q})_{\geq 0}}\alpha\left(Z(T,\varphi)_{K}\right)q_{\mathbb{A}}^{T}.

The subscript
K

will be omitted when there is no risk of confusion.

3.5.3.

Let
\ell
be a prime
such that
K_{\ell}

has pro-order invertible in
R
, and let

\Test_{K^{\ell}}(V,R)\coloneqq\varinjlim_{K_{\ell}^{\prime}}\Test_{K^{\ell}K_{\ell}^{\prime}}(V),

where the transition maps are induced by pushforward. Note that
\Test_{K^{\ell}}(V,R)

has a natural action of
\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
, dual to the one described in
[
129
, p. 41]
. For
\alpha\in\Test_{K^{\ell}}(V)

and a
K^{\ell}
-invariant Schwartz function
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f},R)
, we define the renormalized formal theta lift

(3.8)

\Theta_{K^{\ell}}(\alpha,\varphi)\coloneqq\frac{\Theta(\alpha,\varphi)_{K^{\ell}K_{\ell}^{\prime}}}{[K_{\ell}:K_{\ell}^{\prime}]},

for any
K_{\ell}^{\prime}\subset K_{\ell}

fixing both
\alpha

and
\varphi
.
Because the cycles
Z(T,\varphi)_{K^{\ell}K_{\ell}^{\prime}}

are compatible under pullback,
\Theta_{K^{\ell}}(\alpha,\varphi)

does not depend on the choice of
K_{\ell}^{\prime}
.

Proposition 3.5.4
.

Suppose
R
is a
\mathbb{Z}[1/\ell]
-algebra containing all eighth and
\ell
th power roots of unity, and the pro-order of
K_{\ell}
is invertible in
R
. Then
\Theta_{K^{\ell}}

defines a
\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\times\MP_{2n}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
-equivariant map

\Test_{K^{\ell}}(V,R)\otimes\mathcal{S}(V^{n}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f},R)^{K^{\ell}}\rightarrow M_{\frac{\dimension(V)}{2},R}^{2n}.

Proof.

Note that both modularity and equivariance can be checked after extending scalars, so without loss of generality suppose
R=\mathbb{C}
. In the definite case, the proposition is a formal consequence of Lemma
3.4.3
above. In the indefinite case, the modularity of the formal theta lift is
[
15
, Theorem 6.2]
and the equivariance is
[
55
, Corollary 5.11]
combined with
[
129
, Corollary 2.12]
.
∎

4.
Construction of Galois cohomology classes and special periods

Before beginning this section, we establish the notation that will be in force for most of the rest of the paper.

Notation 4.0.1
.

(1)

Fix a relevant automorphic representation
\pi
of
\GSP_{4}
with trivial central character. Fix as well a strong coefficient field
E_{0}
for
\pi
(Definition
2.2.13
). If
\pi
is endoscopic associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
, then we also assume without loss of generality that
E_{0}
is a strong coefficient field for
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
.
8
8

8

In fact, it is not difficult to check that this last assumption is automatic. The main point is to use Hodge-Tate theory to verify that, for all
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
,
\rho_{\pi_{1},\iota}
and
\rho_{\pi_{2},\iota}
cannot differ by a Galois twist.

(2)

We fix a finite set
S
of finite primes, containing 2 and all
\ell
such that
\pi_{\ell}
is ramified.

(3)

For all primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
, let
O_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}
be the ring of integers, and let
\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}\in O_{\mathfrak{p}}
be a uniformizer, with residue field
k_{\mathfrak{p}}=O_{\mathfrak{p}}/\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}
. We drop the subscript
\mathfrak{p}
when there is no risk of confusion.
For a finite set of primes
S^{\prime}\supset S
, we write
\mathfrak{m}_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}^{S^{\prime}}\subset{\mathbb{T}}^{S^{\prime}}_{O_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for the maximal ideal defined by the Hecke eigenvalues of
\pi
, and drop the decorations when they are clear from context.

(4)

Notation
2.2.16
remains in force.

Remark 4.0.2
.

We assume
2\in S
so that 2 is not admissible under Definition
4.2.1
below, and to prove the (convenient but not essential) Lemma
4.4.7
.

4.1.
Assumptions on
\mathfrak{p}

We now define some assumptions on primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
. Let
p
denote the residue characteristic.

Assumption 4.1.1
.

(1)

p
does not lie in
S
.

(2)

There exists a rational prime
\ell\not\in S\cup\left\{p\right\}
such that
\ell^{4}\not\equiv 1\pmod{p}
and
\overline{\rho}_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(\Frob_{\ell})
has distinct eigenvalues, no two having ratio
\ell
.

The final assumption depends on whether
\pi
is endoscopic.
