\rho:X\times_{S}\overline{S}\to\mathbb{X}\times_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\overline{S}

such that
\rho^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}\circ\rho=c(\rho)\lambda

for some
c(\rho)\in\mathbb{Q}_{q}^{\times}
.

The functor
\mathcal{N}

is studied in
[
119
]
; in this section, we will recall the key points, and prove additional properties needed for our applications.

6.1.3.

The functor
\mathcal{N}

is represented by a formal scheme over
\SPF\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
, locally formally of finite type, which admits a decomposition into open and closed formal subschemes

(6.1)

\mathcal{N}=\sqcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{N}(i)

according to the
q
-adic valuation of
c(\rho)
.

6.1.4.

Let
\sigma:\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}\to\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}

denote the

arithmetic
Frobenius, lifting the
q
th power map on
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. The formal scheme
\mathcal{N}

is equipped with a canonical Weil descent datum
\varphi:\mathcal{N}\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{N}

over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
, which we now recall. On
S
-points,
\varphi
is given by the isomorphism
\varphi(S):\mathcal{N}(S)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{N}((\sigma^{-1})^{\ast}S)
, which sends

(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}(S)
to
((\sigma^{-1})^{\ast}X,(\sigma^{-1})^{\ast}\iota,(\sigma^{-1})^{\ast}\lambda,\rho\circ F_{X/\overline{S}}),
where

F_{X/\overline{S}}:(\sigma^{-1})^{\ast}X_{\overline{S}}\to X_{\overline{S}}

is the relative Frobenius.
Since
c(\rho\circ F_{X/\overline{S}})=c(\rho)+1
,
\varphi

restricts to an isomorphism

\varphi_{i}:\mathcal{N}(i)\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{N}(i+1)

for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

6.2.
The Bruhat-Tits stratification

6.2.1.

Let
\mathcal{M}

denote the underlying reduced scheme of
\mathcal{N}(0)
. We now recall the description in
[
119
]
of the stratification of
\mathcal{M}

in terms of lattices in the isocrystal
N

of our fixed
q
-divisible group
\mathbb{X}
. By the assumption that the involution
\ast

on
\mathcal{O}_{q}

is of unit type, we may choose coordinates

(6.2)

\mathcal{O}_{q}=\mathbb{Z}_{q^{2}}\oplus\Pi\mathbb{Z}_{q^{2}},

where
\alpha^{\ast}=\Pi\alpha\Pi^{-1}=\sigma\alpha

for
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{q^{2}}
,
\Pi^{\ast}=\Pi
, and
\Pi^{2}=q
.

6.2.2.

Label the two embeddings of
\mathbb{Z}_{q^{2}}

into
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}

by
j_{\bullet}

and
j_{\circ}
. Then we have a decomposition

(6.3)

N=N_{\bullet}\oplus N_{\circ},

where
\iota(\alpha)=j_{?}(\alpha)

on
N_{?}

for
?=\bullet,\circ
.
Each of
N_{\bullet}

and
N_{\circ}

is an isocrystal of dimension 4 and slope
\frac{1}{2}
.

The polarization
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}

induces a pairing

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:N\otimes N\to\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}=\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q},

with respect to which
N_{\bullet}

and
N_{\circ}

are each isotropic (since
\ast

is nontrivial on
\mathbb{Z}_{q^{2}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{q}
). Define a new pairing

(6.4)

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\bullet}:N_{\bullet}\otimes N_{\bullet}\to\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}

by

\langle x,y\rangle_{\bullet}=\langle x,\Pi y\rangle.

Then
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\bullet}

is symplectic and non-degenerate.

The operators
\Pi

and
V

on
N

both interchange
N_{\bullet}

and
N_{\circ}
, so the operator

(6.5)

\tau\coloneqq\Pi V^{-1}

stabilizes
N_{\bullet}
; moreover

(6.6)

\langle\tau x,\tau y\rangle_{\bullet}=\langle x,y\rangle_{\bullet}^{\sigma},

where again
\sigma

is the arithmetic Frobenius of
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
.
Hence
W\coloneqq N_{\bullet}^{\tau=1}

is a 4-dimensional
\mathbb{Q}_{q}
-vector space equipped with a
\mathbb{Q}_{q}
-valued symplectic form, such that
N_{\bullet}=W\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}.

Definition 6.2.3
.

For a lattice
\Lambda\subset W
, denote the
\mathbb{Z}_{q}
-dual lattice by
\Lambda^{\vee}
.
We define the following families of lattices in
W
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\text{lattices }\Lambda\subset W\text{ s.t. }\Lambda=\Lambda^{\vee}\right\}.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\text{lattices }\Lambda\subset W\text{ s.t. }\Lambda=q\Lambda^{\vee}\right\}.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\left\{02\right\}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\text{pairs of lattices }\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2}\subset W\text{ s.t. }q\Lambda_{0}=q\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}\subset_{2}q\Lambda_{2}^{\vee}=\Lambda_{2}\subset_{2}\Lambda_{0}\right\}.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\text{lattices }\Lambda_{1}\subset W\text{ s.t. }q\Lambda_{1}^{\vee}\subset_{2}\Lambda_{1}\subset_{2}\Lambda_{1}^{\vee}\right\}.

Here, the notation
\Lambda\subset_{n}\Lambda^{\prime}
was defined in (
•
‣
1.1.8
).

Theorem 6.2.4
.

The underlying reduced scheme
\mathcal{M}

of
\mathcal{N}(0)

admits a stratification

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\left\{0\right\}}\sqcup\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\left\{2\right\}}\sqcup\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\left\{02\right\}}\sqcup\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\left\{1\right\}},

with a decomposition into open and closed subschemes

\mathcal{M}^{0}_{?}=\bigsqcup_{y\in\mathcal{L}_{?}}\mathcal{M}^{0}_{?}(y)

for each
?=\left\{0\right\}
,
\left\{2\right\}
,
\left\{02\right\}
,
\left\{1\right\}
, satisfying the following conditions
(where
\mathcal{M}_{?}

and
\mathcal{M}_{?}(y)

denote the Zariski closures of
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{?}

and
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{?}(y)
, respectively):

(1)

For
?=\left\{0\right\}
or
\left\{2\right\}
and each
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{?}
,
\mathcal{M}_{?}(\Lambda)

is isomorphic to the smooth projective hypersurface in
\mathbb{P}^{3}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

defined by the equation

X^{q}_{3}X_{0}-X_{0}^{q}X_{3}+X_{2}^{q}X_{1}-X_{1}^{q}X_{2}.

The scheme
\mathcal{M}

is the union
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}\cup\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}
.

(2)

Given
\Lambda_{0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}

and
\Lambda_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{0})

meets
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{2})

if and only if
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2})\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{02\right\}}
, in which case the intersection is transverse and

\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{0})\cap\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{2})=\mathcal{M}_{\left\{02\right\}}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2}).

For each
(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2})\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{02\right\}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{02\right\}}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2})

is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
, and both of the resulting embeddings
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{3}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

are linear.

(3)

For each
\Lambda_{1}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}^{0}(\Lambda_{1})=\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}(\Lambda_{1})

is an isolated point.
Given
\Lambda_{0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}

and
\Lambda_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}(\Lambda_{1})

lies on
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{0})

if and only if
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{0}
,
and on
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{2})

if and only if
\Lambda_{2}\subset\Lambda_{1}
.

(4)

For a pair of distinct
\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{0})

meets
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{0}^{\prime})

if and only if
\Lambda_{0}\cap\Lambda_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}
; in this case the intersection is transverse and we have

\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{0})\cap\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{0}^{\prime})=\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}(\Lambda_{0}\cap\Lambda_{0}^{\prime}).

(5)

For a pair of distinct
\Lambda_{2},\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{2})

meets
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{2}^{\prime})

if and only if
\Lambda_{2}+\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}
; in this case the intersection is transverse and we have

\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{2})\cap\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{2}^{\prime})=\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}(\Lambda_{2}+\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}).

(6)

The stratum
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}

is precisely the nonsmooth locus of
\mathcal{N}(0)
, and the complete local ring of
\mathcal{N}(0)

at each point in
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}

is isomorphic to

\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}\llbracket X,Y,Z,W\rrbracket/q-XY+ZW.

Proof.

Each point except (6) is contained in
[
119
]
, and (6) is
[
86
, Corollary 4.2]
.
∎

6.2.5.

For later use, we recall the meaning of each of the strata in Theorem
6.2.4
on the level of
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-points.

Given
s=(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, the covariant Dieudonné module of
X

gives rise to an
\mathcal{O}_{q}
-stable lattice
M\subset N
. Such an
M

admits a decomposition

M=M_{\bullet}\oplus M_{\circ}\subset N_{\bullet}\oplus N_{\circ}

as in (
6.2.2
); here we are using
q\neq 2
.
For a lattice
\Lambda\subset W
, define
\breve{\Lambda}\coloneqq\Lambda\otimes\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}\subset M_{\bullet}.

Then we have, for any
s\in\mathcal{M}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})

and any lattices
\Lambda_{0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}
,
\Lambda_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
,
\Lambda_{1}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}
:

•

s

lies in
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}
if and only if
M_{\bullet}=\tau M_{\bullet}
, and is the point
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}(\Lambda_{1})

if and only if
M_{\bullet}=\breve{\Lambda}_{1}.

•

s

lies in
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{0})-\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}

if and only if
M_{\bullet}+\tau M_{\bullet}=\breve{\Lambda}_{0}
.

•

s

lies in
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{2})-\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}

if and only if
M_{\bullet}\cap\tau M_{\bullet}=\breve{\Lambda}_{2}
.

By Theorem
6.2.4
, at least one of these three options occurs for any point
s\in\mathcal{M}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
.

Notation 6.2.6
.

From now on, to ease the notation we shall abbreviate
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{0})
as
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{0})
, etc.

6.3.
Deformation theory and the geometry of
\mathcal{N}

6.3.1.

Let
(X,\lambda,\iota,\rho)

be an
S
-valued point of
\mathcal{N}
, for some
S\in\nilpotent
. To
X

we associate the (covariant) Dieudonné crystal
\mathbb{D}(X)

[
75
]
; thus for any thickening
S\hookrightarrow\widehat{S}

in
\nilpotent

admitting locally nilpotent divided powers, we obtain a locally free sheaf
\mathbb{D}(\widehat{S})

of
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{S}}
-modules, such that
D(X)\coloneqq\mathbb{D}(X)(S)

fits into a canonical exact sequence

(6.7)

0\to\omega_{X^{\vee}}\to D(X)\to\Lie(X)\to 0

of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules.

6.3.2.

As in (
6.2.5
) above, the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{q^{2}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{q}

on
\mathbb{D}(X)

induces a decomposition

\mathbb{D}(X)=\mathbb{D}(X)_{\bullet}\oplus\mathbb{D}(X)_{\circ},

and likewise for
D(X)
,
\omega_{X^{\vee}}
, and
\Lie X
; the action of
\Pi

interchanges the two components in each case.

The polarization
\lambda

induces a perfect alternating pairing

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:\mathbb{D}(X)\otimes\mathbb{D}(X)\to\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\operatorname{cris}}.

Since both
\mathbb{D}(X)_{\bullet}

and
\mathbb{D}(X)_{\circ}

are isotropic,
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle

identifies
\mathbb{D}(X)_{\bullet}

with the dual of
\mathbb{D}(X)_{\circ}
.
Finally, the submodule
\omega_{X^{\vee}}

of
D(X)

is also isotropic, so that
\lambda

induces perfect pairings of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules:

\displaystyle\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:\;

\displaystyle\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\otimes\Lie X_{\circ}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}

\displaystyle\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:\;

\displaystyle\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}\otimes\Lie X_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}.

If
S=\Spec\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, then
\mathbb{D}(X)

is equivalent to the data of the Dieudonné module
M

of
X
; the exact sequence (
6.7
) becomes

0\to VM/pM\to M\to M/VM\to 0.

6.3.3.

Let
S\hookrightarrow\widehat{S}

be a thickening in
\nilpotent

admitting locally nilpotent divided powers, and fix
x=(X,\lambda,\iota,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}(S)
.
Denote by
\operatorname{Lift}(x)

the set of isomorphisms classes of lifts of
x

to

\widehat{x}=(\widehat{X},\widehat{\lambda},\widehat{\iota},\widehat{\rho})\in\mathcal{N}(\widehat{S})
, and denote by
\overline{\operatorname{Lift}}(x)

the set of locally free,
\mathcal{O}_{q}
-stable, totally isotropic
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{S}}
-submodules
\widehat{\omega}_{X^{\vee}}\subset\mathbb{D}(X)(\widehat{S})

lifting
\omega_{X^{\vee}}
. From the well-known deformation theory of
q
-divisible groups
[
77
]
, one has:

Proposition 6.3.4
.

The canonical map

\widehat{x}=(\widehat{X},\widehat{\lambda},\widehat{\iota},\widehat{\rho})\mapsto\omega_{\widehat{X}^{\vee}}\subset D(\widehat{X})=\mathbb{D}(X)(\widehat{S})

defines a bijection

\Lift(x)\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\operatorname{Lift}}(x).

6.3.5.

Let
\mathcal{N}^{\smooth}

denote the formally smooth locus of
\mathcal{N}(0)
, which by Theorem

6.2.4
(6) is the complement of
\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda\end{subarray}_{1}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}}\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{1}).

Before we can calculate the tangent bundle to the mod
q

fiber of
\mathcal{N}^{\smooth}

in Theorem

6.3.7
below, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 6.3.6
.

Suppose given
(X,\lambda,\iota,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}^{\smooth}(S)
, for some
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-scheme
S
. Then
\Pi

induces isomorphisms of line bundles on
S
:

\displaystyle\Pi:\frac{\Lie X_{\bullet}}{\Pi\Lie X_{\circ}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\Pi\Lie X_{\bullet}\subset\Lie X_{\circ}

\displaystyle\Pi:\frac{\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}}{\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\subset\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ},

and likewise with
\bullet

and
\circ

reversed.

Proof.

We consider the first map; the second, and the versions with
\bullet
and
\circ
reversed, are all similar to this case. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
S=\Spf R
is affine and formally of finite type, and that
R
is a local ring with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
such that
R/\mathfrak{m}=\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.
Then
\Lie X_{\bullet}
and
\Lie X_{\circ}
are each free of rank two over
R
by the Kottwitz condition, and the map
\Pi_{\bullet}:\Lie X_{\bullet}\to\Lie X_{\circ}

is nonzero modulo
\mathfrak{m}
; indeed, this amounts to the assertion that
\Pi M_{\bullet}\neq VM_{\bullet}
for the Dieudonné module
M=M_{\bullet}\oplus M_{\circ}
corresponding to the special fiber of
X
, and this holds because we are away from the nonsmooth locus of
\mathcal{N}(0)
, cf. (
6.2.5
).

In particular, we can choose bases of
\Lie X_{\bullet}
and
\Lie X_{\circ}
such that

\Pi_{\bullet}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}\,:\,\Lie X_{\bullet}\to\Lie X_{\circ}

for some
d\in R
.
Now, we also know that
\Pi^{2}=q=0
on
\Lie(X)
, so the matrix
g
for
\Pi_{\circ}:\Lie X_{\circ}\to\Lie X_{\bullet}

must satisfy

g\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}g=0.

A direct calculation shows that
g=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&w\end{pmatrix}
for some
w\in R
, where
wd=0
; but the same reasoning as for
\Pi_{\bullet}
shows that
\Pi_{\circ}
is nonzero modulo
\mathfrak{m}
, so
w
is a unit and we conclude
d=0
. From these coordinates for
\Pi_{\bullet}
and
\Pi_{\circ}
, it is clear that
\Pi\Lie X_{\bullet}
and
\Lie X_{\bullet}/\Pi\Lie X_{\circ}
are both free of rank one over
R
and that the map in the lemma is indeed an isomorphism.
∎

Theorem 6.3.7
.

Let
\mathcal{T}

denote the tangent bundle on the mod
q

fiber
\mathcal{N}^{\smooth}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
. Then we have a canonical exact sequence:

0\to\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}(\omega_{\mathcal{X}^{\vee},\bullet},\Lie\mathcal{X}_{\bullet})\to\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}(\Pi\omega_{\mathcal{X}^{\vee},\circ},\Lie\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}/\Pi\Lie\mathcal{X}_{\circ})\to 0,

where
\mathcal{X}

is the universal
q
-divisible group.

Proof.

It suffices to consider deformations of a point
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}^{\smooth}(R)

to points of
\mathcal{N}^{\smooth}(R[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
,
for
R

an
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-algebra. Let
S=\Spec R
and
\widehat{S}=\Spec R[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
. By Proposition
6.3.4
, we need to consider lifts of
\omega_{X^{\vee}}

to locally free submodules
\widehat{\omega}_{X^{\vee}}\subset\mathbb{D}(X)(\widehat{S})

which are
\mathcal{O}_{q}
-stable and isotropic;
this is equivalent to lifting
\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\subset D(X)_{\bullet}

and
\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}\subset D(X)_{\circ}

to locally free submodules
\widehat{\omega}_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\subset\mathbb{D}(X)_{\bullet}(\widehat{S})

and
\widehat{\omega}_{X^{\vee},\circ}\subset\mathbb{D}(X)_{\circ}(\widehat{S})
, subject to the following conditions.

(i)

\langle\widehat{\omega}_{X^{\vee},\bullet},\widehat{\omega}_{X^{\vee},\circ}\rangle=0.

(ii)

\Pi\widehat{\omega}_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\subset\widehat{\omega}_{X^{\vee},\circ}.

(iii)

\Pi\widehat{\omega}_{X^{\vee},\circ}\subset\widehat{\omega}_{X^{\vee},\bullet}
.

Now, lifts of
\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}

correspond to maps of
R
-modules
f_{\bullet}:\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\to\Lie X_{\bullet}

via

f_{\bullet}\mapsto\operatorname{span}\left\{x+\epsilon f_{\bullet}(x)+\epsilon\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\,:\,x\in\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\right\},

and likewise for
\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}
. The conditions (i)-(iii) translate to:

(i)’

\langle x,f_{\bullet}(y)\rangle=\langle y,f_{\circ}(x)\rangle
, for all
x\in\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}
,
y\in\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}
.

(ii)’

\Pi f_{\bullet}(x)=f_{\circ}(\Pi x)

for all
x\in\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}
.

(iii)’

\Pi f_{\circ}(x)=f_{\bullet}(\Pi x)

for all
x\in\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}
.

If we specify
f_{\bullet}
, then (i)’ can be taken as a

definition
of
f_{\circ}
. In terms of
f_{\bullet}

only, conditions (ii)’ and (iii)’ correspond to:

(ii)”

\langle y,\Pi f_{\bullet}(x)\rangle=\langle x,\Pi f_{\bullet}(y)\rangle
, for all
x,y\in\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}
.

(iii)”

\langle y,f_{\bullet}(\Pi x)\rangle=\langle x,f_{\bullet}(\Pi y)\rangle
, for all
x,y\in\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}
.

Now, since we are outside the singular locus, Lemma

6.3.6
implies that
\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}

and
\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}

are locally rank-one direct summands of the rank-two projective
R
-modules
\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}

and
\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}
, respectively. In particular, using that (ii)” and (iii)” are clearly satisfied when
x
and
y
are linearly dependent, it suffices to check (ii)” and (iii)” for
x\in\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}

and
x\in\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}
, respectively. Using
\langle\Pi z,\Pi w\rangle=q\langle z,w\rangle=0
, we find that both conditions (ii)” and (iii)” are equivalent to
\Pi f_{\bullet}(\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ})=0
. Again by Lemma

6.3.6
, the kernel of
\Pi

on
\Lie X_{\bullet}

is
\Pi\Lie X_{\circ}
, so we are just requiring

f_{\bullet}(\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ})\subset\Pi\Lie X_{\circ},

as desired.
∎

6.3.8.
Scheme-theoretic description of the strata
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{0})
,
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{2})

Fix a lattice
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}\sqcup\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
;
we recall the construction of
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)

as a subscheme of
\mathcal{N}

given in
[
119
, §4]
.
Let
\breve{\Lambda}=\Lambda\otimes\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}\subset N_{\bullet}
, and let
Y

denote the
q
-divisible group over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}

associated to the lattice

\breve{\Lambda}\oplus q^{\delta}\Pi^{-1}\breve{\Lambda}\subset N_{\bullet}\oplus N_{\circ},

where
\delta=0
if
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}

and
\delta=1

if
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
.
The group
Y
comes with a natural quasi-isogeny

t:Y\to\mathbb{X}.

For any
(X,\lambda,\iota,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}(S)

with
S

an
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-scheme, consider the two quasi-isogenies:

\displaystyle\rho_{+}

\displaystyle:X\xrightarrow{\rho}\mathbb{X}_{S}\xrightarrow{q^{\delta}t^{-1}}Y_{S},

\displaystyle\rho_{-}

\displaystyle:Y_{S}\xrightarrow{q^{1-\delta}t}\mathbb{X}_{S}\xrightarrow{\rho^{-1}}X,

where
\delta=0

if
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}

and
\delta=1

if
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
.

Then the scheme
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)

is constructed as the locus where
\rho_{+}

and
\rho_{-}

are both isogenies.
In fact, since the dual lattice to
\breve{\Lambda}\oplus\Pi^{-1}\breve{\Lambda}

is
q^{1-2\delta}(\breve{\Lambda}\oplus\Pi^{-1}\breve{\Lambda})
,
we may identify
Y\xrightarrow{\sim}Y^{\vee}

by
q^{1-2\delta}t^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}\circ t
; with respect to this polarization,
\rho_{+}

and
\rho_{-}

are duals, so
\rho_{-}

is an isogeny if and only if
\rho_{+}

is.

Proposition 6.3.9
.

Let
\mathcal{O}(1)

be the line bundle on
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)

corresponding to the embedding into
\mathbb{P}^{3}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

of Theorem

6.2.4
(2). Then, if
(X,\lambda,\iota,\rho)
is the universal
q
-divisible group over
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)
, we have isomorphisms of line bundles on
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)^{\smooth}\coloneqq\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)\cap\mathcal{N}^{\smooth}
:

\mathcal{O}(-q)\cong\Pi\Lie X_{\circ}\cong\Lie X_{\bullet}/\Pi\Lie X_{\circ}.

Proof.

We give the proof in the case
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}
; for
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
, the roles of
\bullet
and
\circ
are interchanged. Let
S\coloneqq\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)^{\sm}
, and let
Y_{S}
be the constant
q
-divisible group
Y
on
S
.

We need to recall the construction of the projective embedding from
[
119
, §4]
. First of all, one has the Dieudonné crystal

\mathbb{D}(Y_{S})=\mathbb{D}(Y_{S})_{\bullet}\oplus\mathbb{D}(Y_{S})_{\circ},

with notation as in (
6.3.2
), and

D(Y_{S})_{\circ}=D(Y)_{\circ}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{S}

is a free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module of rank 4.
The submodule
\rho_{+,\ast}D(X)_{\circ}\subset D(Y_{S})_{\circ}

is locally a free summand of rank
1
, and this is the tautological bundle
\mathcal{O}(-1)
under the map
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{3}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
.
The canonical Verschiebung map

\mathtt{V}:D(Y_{S})_{\circ}\to D(Y_{S}^{(q)})_{\circ}\simeq D(Y_{S})_{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{O}_{S^{(q)}}

is given by the
q
th power map
\mathcal{O}_{S}\to\mathcal{O}_{S^{(q)}}
tensored with the isomorphism

V:D(Y)_{\circ}=\Pi^{-1}\breve{\Lambda}/\Pi\breve{\Lambda}\xrightarrow{\sim}\breve{\Lambda}/q\breve{\Lambda}=D(Y)_{\bullet},

so we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{O}(-q)\simeq\mathtt{V}(\rho_{+,\ast}(D(X)_{\circ}).

Now we note that the map

(6.8)

\mathtt{V}\circ\rho_{+,\ast}:D(X)_{\circ}\to D(Y_{S})_{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{O}_{S^{(}q)}

annihilates both
\Pi D(X)_{\bullet}
and
\omega_{X^{\vee},_{\circ}}
,
because
\mathtt{V}(\omega_{Y_{S}^{\vee},\circ})=\mathtt{V}\circ\Pi(D(Y_{S})_{\circ})=0
by the definition of
Y
.
In particular, (
6.8
) induces a surjection

(6.9)

\mathtt{V}\circ\rho_{+,\ast}:\frac{\Lie X_{\circ}}{\Pi\Lie X_{\bullet}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}(-q),

which is a map of line bundles by Lemma
6.3.6
and therefore an isomorphism.
Combined with Lemma
6.3.6
for the other isomorphism, this completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 6.3.10
.

For any
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}\sqcup\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}},

the normal bundle to
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)^{\smooth}

inside
\mathcal{N}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}^{\smooth}

is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}(-2q)

for the embedding into
\mathbb{P}^{3}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

given in Theorem

6.2.4
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we continue to assume
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}
; the other case is similar.

The first step is to compute the tangent bundle to
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)^{\smooth}
. We wish to consider the lifts of
(X,\lambda,\iota,\rho)\in\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)^{\smooth}(R)

to points of
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)^{\smooth}(R[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
for
R
an
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-algebra, which we may take to be reduced since
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)
is reduced. Continuing the notation of (
6.3.8
), such lifts correspond to the pairs

\begin{cases}f_{\bullet}:\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\to\Lie X_{\bullet}\\
f_{\circ}:\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}\to\Lie X_{\circ}\end{cases}

satisfying (i)’-(iii)’ from the proof of Theorem
6.3.7
, subject to the additional condition that

f_{\bullet}(\rho_{-,\ast}(\omega_{Y^{\vee}_{R},\bullet}))=f_{\circ}(\rho_{-,\ast}(\omega_{Y^{\vee}_{R},\circ}))=0.

By the definition of
Y
, we have
\omega_{Y^{\vee}_{R},\circ}=0
, so the second condition is automatic.

Claim
.

On
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)^{\smooth}

we have

(6.10)

\rho_{-,\ast}(\omega_{Y_{R}^{\vee},\bullet})=\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}.

Given the claim, we conclude by comparing with the proof of Theorem
6.3.7
that
the tangent bundle
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}
to
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)^{\smooth}

is canonically isomorphic to

\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}(\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}/\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ},\Lie X_{\bullet}).

The normal bundle is the quotient
\mathcal{T}/\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}
, which by Theorem

6.3.7
is

\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}(\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ},\Pi\Lie X_{\circ}).

Since
\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}

is dual to
\Lie X_{\bullet}/\Pi\Lie X_{\circ}

on
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)^{\smooth}
, the theorem then follows from Proposition
6.3.9
.
Now we turn to the proof of the claim.
We have

\rho_{-,\ast}(\omega_{Y_{R}^{\vee},\bullet})=\rho_{-,\ast}(D(Y_{R})_{\bullet})=\left(\ker\left(\rho_{+,\ast}:D(X)_{\circ}\to D(Y_{R})_{\circ}\right)\right)^{\perp}

because
\rho_{+}
and
\rho_{-}
are duals; the orthogonal complement is with respect to the perfect pairing on
D(X)
.
Because
R
is reduced, the Verschiebung
\mathtt{V}:D(Y_{R})_{\circ}\to D(Y_{R})_{\bullet}
is injective, so (
6.9
) implies that

\ker\left(\rho_{+,\ast}:D(X)_{\circ}\to D(Y_{R})_{\circ}\right)=\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}+\Pi D(X)_{\circ}.

Arguments similar to Lemma
6.3.6
show that
(\Pi D(X)_{\circ})^{\perp}=\Pi D(X)_{\bullet}
, and so we conclude

\rho_{-,\ast}(\omega_{Y_{R}^{\vee},\bullet})=\left(\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}+\Pi D(X)_{\bullet}\right)^{\perp}=\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\cap\Pi D(X)_{\circ}.

Now it follows from Lemma
6.3.6
and the snake lemma for the diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(X)_{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie X_{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(X)_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lie X_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi}

that
\omega_{X^{\vee},\bullet}\cap\Pi D(X)_{\bullet}=\Pi\omega_{X^{\vee},\circ}
, so the proof of the claim is complete.
∎

6.4.
Regularization of
\mathcal{M}
and intersection theory

Notation 6.4.1
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}(0)
be the blowup of
\mathcal{N}(0)
along
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}
, and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
be the strict transform of the reduced locus
\mathcal{M}
; then
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
is smooth.
We denote by
C(\Lambda_{1})

the exceptional divisor of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
above
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{1})

for each
\Lambda_{1}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}
. For any
\Lambda_{0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}

and
\Lambda_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
, let
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda_{0})

and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda_{2})

be the strict transforms of
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{0})

and
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{2})
, respectively.

Lemma 6.4.2
.

For any
\Lambda_{1}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}
, there exists an isomorphism
C(\Lambda_{1})\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

such that:

(1)

For any
\Lambda_{0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}

with
\Lambda_{1}\subset_{1}\Lambda_{0}
,
\widetilde{M}(\Lambda_{0})

meets
C(\Lambda_{1})

transversely along a divisor with class
(1,0)
.

(2)

For any
\Lambda_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}

with
\Lambda_{2}\subset_{1}\Lambda_{1}
,
\widetilde{M}(\Lambda_{2})

meets
C(\Lambda_{1})

transversely along a divisor with class
(0,1)
.

Proof.

By Theorem
6.2.4
(6), we may fix one isomorphism
C(\Lambda_{1})\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{1})

be the set of
\Lambda_{0}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}

with
\Lambda_{1}\subset_{1}\Lambda_{0}
, and likewise
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{1})
. The actions of
\operatorname{Stab}(\Lambda_{1})\subset\operatorname{Sp}(W)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

on
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{1})

and
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{1})

are transitive, and compatible with the natural
\operatorname{Sp}(W)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-action on
\mathcal{N}(0)
(see (
6.5.1
)).
For distinct
\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}(\Lambda_{1})
, it follows that the divisor classes

D_{\Lambda_{0}}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda_{0})\cap C(\Lambda_{1})

and

D_{\Lambda_{0}^{\prime}}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda_{0}^{\prime})\cap C(\Lambda_{1})

differ by an automorphism of
C(\Lambda_{1})
. In particular, if
D_{\Lambda_{0}}=(\alpha,\beta)
, then
D_{\Lambda_{0}^{\prime}}=(\alpha,\beta)

or
(\beta,\alpha)
. On the other hand, since
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{0})

meets
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{0}^{\prime})

transversely at
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{1})
, we have

D_{\Lambda_{0}}\cdot D_{\Lambda_{0}}=0

for the intersection product on
C(\Lambda_{1})
. Since
(\alpha,\beta)\cdot(\beta,\alpha)=\alpha^{2}+\beta^{2}
, which can only vanish if
\alpha=\beta=0
, it follows that
D_{\Lambda_{0}}=D_{\Lambda_{0}^{\prime}}=(\alpha,\beta)

with
\alpha\beta=0
; without loss of generality, assume
\beta=0
.
By the same reasoning, for any
\Lambda_{2}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}(\Lambda_{1})
,

D_{\Lambda_{2}}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda_{2})\cap C(\Lambda_{1})

has divisor class
(\gamma,\delta)

with
\gamma\delta=0
. However,
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{0})

meets
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{2})

transversely along
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{2})
, so we have

D_{\Lambda_{0}}\cdot D_{\Lambda_{2}}=1.

This implies
\alpha\delta=1
, so we have
D_{\Lambda_{0}}=(1,0)

and
D_{\Lambda_{2}}=(0,1)
, as desired.
∎

Notation 6.4.3
.

Let
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}\sqcup\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
.
We denote by
\mathcal{O}(1)

the line bundle on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)

arising from the pullback of
\mathcal{O}(1)

along the composite

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)\to\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{3}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}.

Lemma 6.4.4
.

For any
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}\sqcup\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
, the normal bundle to
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)

inside
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}(0)

is
\mathcal{O}(-2q)
.

Proof.

Let
\left\{\Lambda_{1}^{(0)},\ldots,\Lambda_{1}^{(n)}\right\}

be the set of lattices
\Lambda_{1}

in
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}

such that
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{1})

lies on
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)
. Then the projection
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)\to\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)

is the blowup along the points
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{1}^{(i)})
, with exceptional divisors

E_{i}\coloneqq C(\Lambda_{1}^{(i)})\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda).

Since
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)

is a smooth surface, we have
E_{i}\cdot E_{j}=-\delta_{ij}

for the intersection pairing on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)
.

Now, the normal bundle to
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)
inside
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}(0)

is locally free of rank one, and Lemma
6.3.6
implies it is isomorphic to

\mathcal{O}(-2q+\alpha_{0}E_{0}+\ldots+\alpha_{n}E_{n})

for some
\alpha_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
.
On the other hand, as
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}(0)
is formally smooth, we can compute the triple intersection number

m_{i}=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)\cdot C(\Lambda_{1}^{(i)})

in two ways, for each
0\leq i\leq n
:

\displaystyle m_{i}

\displaystyle=\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)\cdot C(\Lambda_{1}^{(i)})\right)\cdot_{C(\Lambda_{1}^{(i)})}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)\cdot C(\Lambda_{1}^{(i)})\right)=0\;\;\text{(Lemma \ref{lemma for getting divisor classes on exceptional divisor})}

\displaystyle=\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)\right)\cdot_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(\Lambda)\cdot C(\Lambda_{1}^{(i)})\right)=-\alpha_{i}.

So we find
\alpha_{i}=0

for all
i
, as desired.
∎

6.5.
The
\spin

action on
\mathcal{N}

6.5.1.

The endomorphism algebra
\End(W)

is equipped with an involution
\dagger

given by the adjoint with respect to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\bullet}
, and

V\coloneqq\End(W)^{\dagger=1,\tr=0}=\End(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}})^{\ast=1,\tr=0}

is a split orthogonal space of dimension 5, where
\ast
denotes the Rosati involution. There is a natural projection

(6.11)

\pi:\GSP(W)\to\SO(V)

inducing an isomorphism
\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})\cong\GSP(W)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
. There is also a canonical action of
\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

on
\mathcal{N}
(by modifying
\rho
); when restricted to
\operatorname{Spin}(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
, the resulting action of
\operatorname{Sp}(W)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

on
\mathcal{M}

is compatible with the natural actions on
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{02\right\}}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}
.

Definition 6.5.2
.

Define the sets of lattices

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}

\displaystyle=\left\{\Lambda\subset W\,:\,\Lambda=q^{n}\Lambda^{\vee}\text{ for some }n\in\mathbb{Z}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset W\,:\,q^{n+1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}^{\vee}\subset_{2}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{2}p^{n}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}^{\vee}\text{ for some }n\in\mathbb{Z}\right\}.

Remark 6.5.3
.

Both
\mathscr{L}
and
\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}
are homogeneous spaces for
\GSP(W)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
; the stabilizer of a point in
\mathscr{L}

is a hyperspecial subgroup, and the stabilizer of a point in
\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}

is a paramodular subgroup.

Using
\mathscr{L}

and
\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}

rather than
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}
, we can extend the combinatorial description of
\mathcal{M}

to all of
\mathcal{N}_{{\operatorname{red}}}
.

Definition 6.5.4
.

For any
\Lambda\in\mathscr{L}
, choose an arbitrary
g\in\GSP(W)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

such that
g\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}\subset\mathscr{L}
, and define

\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda)\coloneqq g^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{\left\{0\right\}}(g\Lambda).

Similarly, choose
g^{\prime}\in\GSP(W)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

such that
g^{\prime}\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}\subset\mathscr{L}
, and define

\mathcal{M}_{-}(\Lambda)\coloneqq(g^{\prime})^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}(g^{\prime}\Lambda).

For any
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}
, choose
g\in\GSP(W)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

such that
g\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}\subset\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}
, and define
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})=g^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{\left\{1\right\}}(g\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})
.

Proposition 6.5.5
.

(1)

Definition
6.5.4
yields bijections

\mathscr{L}\times\left\{\pm\right\}\xrightarrow{\sim}\left\{\text{irreducible components of }\mathcal{N}_{{\operatorname{red}}}\right\}

and

\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\left\{\text{singular points of }\mathcal{N}_{{\operatorname{red}}}\right\}.

(2)

Choose any
\Lambda\in\mathscr{L}
. For the Weil descent datum in (
6.1.4
), we have

\varphi\left(\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda)\right)=\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{M}_{-}(q\Lambda)

and

\varphi\left(\mathcal{M}_{-}(\Lambda)\right)=\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda).

Proof.

For (1), it suffices to show there are two
\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-orbits of irreducible components of
\mathcal{N}_{{\operatorname{red}}}
, and only one orbit of singular points. However, since

g\cdot\mathcal{N}(i)=\mathcal{N}(i+{\operatorname{ord}}_{q}\nu(g))

for
g\in\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
, it suffices to show that there are two
\operatorname{Spin}(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-orbits of irreducible components of
\mathcal{M}
, and one
\operatorname{Spin}(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-orbit of singular points on
\mathcal{M}
. This follows from the transitivity of the
\SP(W)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-actions on
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{\left\{1\right\}}
.

For (2), note that
\varphi^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda))=(\sigma^{2})^{\ast}\mathcal{M}_{+}(q\Lambda)
, so it suffices to show the first relation. Without loss of generality, assume
\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{0\right\}}
. By definition,
\varphi(\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda))(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, viewed as a subset of
\mathcal{N}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, is the Zariski closure of the set of points corresponding to lattices
M\subset N

such that

(V^{-1}(M+\tau M))_{\bullet}=\breve{\Lambda}

and

\Pi^{-1}M_{\circ}=qM_{\bullet}^{\vee}.

Since
V\breve{\Lambda}=\Pi\breve{\Lambda}
, the first condition is equivalent to
\Pi^{-1}M_{\circ}+\Pi^{-1}\tau M_{\circ}=\breve{\Lambda}
, or dually

M_{\bullet}\cap\tau M_{\bullet}=q\breve{\Lambda}.

Now choose any
g\in\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

with
\nu(g)=q^{-1}
; we have
qg\Lambda\in\mathcal{L}_{\left\{2\right\}}
. The locus

g\varphi(\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda))(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})\subset\mathcal{M}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})

is the Zariski closure of the set of points corresponding to lattices with
M_{\bullet}\cap\tau M_{\bullet}=qg\breve{\Lambda}

and
\Pi^{-1}M_{\circ}=M_{\bullet}^{\vee}
. But this is exactly the stratum
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{2\right\}}(qg\Lambda)(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})=\mathcal{M}_{-}(qg\Lambda)(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, so we conclude
\varphi(\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda))=\sigma^{\ast}g^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{-}(qg\Lambda)=\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{M}_{-}(q\Lambda)
, as desired.
∎

From Theorem

6.2.4
, we immediately deduce the following relations among the components
\mathcal{M}_{\pm}(\Lambda)

and the points
\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})
.

Corollary 6.5.6
.

For any
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}

and
\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}
, we have:

(1)

\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})

lies on
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda)

if and only if
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda
.

(2)

\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})

lies on
\mathcal{M}_{-}(\Lambda)

if and only if
\Lambda\subset_{1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}
.

(3)

\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda)

meets
\mathcal{M}_{-}(\Lambda^{\prime})

if and only if
q\Lambda\subset_{2}\Lambda^{\prime}\subset_{2}\Lambda
.

(4)

If
\Lambda\neq\Lambda^{\prime}
, then for
\delta=+
or
-
,
\mathcal{M}_{\delta}(\Lambda)

and
\mathcal{M}_{\delta}(\Lambda^{\prime})
can meet only in singular points of
\mathcal{N}_{{\operatorname{red}}}
.

7.
The first explicit reciprocity law: geometric inputs

7.1.
Abel-Jacobi maps for schemes with ordinary quadratic singularities

7.1.1.

Let
R_{0}

be a Henselian discrete valuation ring with uniformizer
\pi
, algebraically closed residue field
k
, and fraction field
K_{0}
. The inertia subgroup
I_{K_{0}}

of
\Gal(\overline{K}_{0}/K_{0})

has the canonical tame character
t_{p}:I_{K_{0}}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)

for any
p\neq\operatorname{char}(k)
. Let
R

be the quadratic extension
R_{0}[\pi^{1/2}]
, and
K

its field of fractions, with inertia subgroup
I_{K}\subset I_{K_{0}}.
We write
s_{0},s,\eta_{0},\eta

for the closed points and the generic points of
\Spec R_{0}
and
\Spec R
, with corresponding geometric points
\overline{\eta}_{0}=\overline{\eta}
. Let
X

be an irreducible scheme of finite type and pure relative dimension
2r-1

over
\Spec R_{0}
, for some integer
r\geq 1
. We assume
X

has
ordinary quadratic singularities: this means that
X

is smooth outside a finite set of closed points
\left\{x_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

in
X_{s_{0}}
, and,
étale locally near each
x_{i}
,
X

is isomorphic to
\Spec R_{0}[y_{0},\ldots,y_{2r-1}]/(Q-\pi)
, with
Q

the equation of a smooth quadric in
\mathbb{P}_{R_{0}}^{2r-1}
.

7.1.2.

The blowup
Y

of
X_{R}

at the points
\left\{x_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}

is strictly semistable in the sense of
[
98
]
, with a particularly simple form
[
48
]
. The irreducible components of the special fiber
Y_{s}

of
Y

are
\widetilde{X}_{s}
, the strict transform of
X_{s}
, and the exceptional divisors
D_{i}
. Each
D_{i}

is isomorphic to the smooth projective quadric in
\mathbb{P}^{2r}_{k}=\operatorname{Proj}(k[y_{0},\ldots,y_{2r-1},t])
cut out by
Q-t^{2}
, and
the intersection
C_{i}=D_{i}\cap\widetilde{X}_{s}

is the hyperplane section
t=0
, so that
C_{i}
is a smooth quadric in
\mathbb{P}^{2r-1}_{k}
. Since
Y

is semistable, we have

(7.1)

\mathcal{N}_{C_{i}/\widetilde{X}_{s}}=-\mathcal{N}_{C_{i}/D_{i}}=\mathcal{O}(-1)

in the Picard group of
C_{i}
.

7.1.3.

Let
O
be a finite flat extension of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with
p
odd and
p\neq\Char(k)
, and let
\varpi\in O
be a uniformizer. We fix a coefficient ring
\Lambda=O

or
O/\varpi^{m}

for some
m\geq 1.

We recall the definition of the nearby cycles complex: let
\overline{j}:Y_{\overline{\eta}}\to Y

and
\overline{i}:Y_{s}\to Y

denote the inclusions of the geometric generic and special fibers, respectively. Then
R\Psi\Lambda=\overline{i}^{\ast}\circ\overline{j}_{\ast}\circ\overline{j}^{\ast}\Lambda
, an element of the bounded derived category of sheaves on
Y_{s}
; it has a canonical action of
I_{K}\subset I_{K_{0}}

factoring through the tame character
t_{p}
, and the (increasing) monodromy filtration
M_{\bullet}R\Psi\Lambda
. Fix
T\in I_{K}

such that
t_{p}(T)

generates
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
; then the monodromy operator
T-1

on
R\Psi\Lambda

induces compatible maps

T-1:M_{i}R\Psi\Lambda\to M_{i-2}R\Psi\Lambda

for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proposition 7.1.4

(Saito)
.

Let

i_{0}:\widetilde{X}_{s}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{i\in I}D_{i}\to Y_{s}

and

i_{1}:\bigsqcup_{i\in I}C_{i}\to Y_{s}

be the natural maps. Then the graded pieces of
M_{\bullet}R\Psi\Lambda

are given by:

\displaystyle\GR_{i}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle|i|>1,

\displaystyle\GR_{1}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda

\displaystyle=i_{1\ast}\Lambda(-1)[-1],

\displaystyle\GR_{0}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda

\displaystyle=i_{0\ast}\Lambda,

\displaystyle\GR_{-1}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda

\displaystyle=i_{1\ast}\Lambda[-1].

Proof.

This is
[
98
, Proposition 2.2.3]
.
∎

Lemma 7.1.5
.

The following composite map is an isomorphism:

H^{2r}(Y_{s},M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda)\to H^{2r}(Y_{s},\GR_{0}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda)=H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda)\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}H^{2r}(D_{i},\Lambda)\twoheadrightarrow H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda).

Proof.

First, consider the tautological distinguished triangle

(7.2)

M_{-1}R\Psi\Lambda\to M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda\to\GR_{0}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda\to M_{-1}R\Psi\Lambda[+1].

This yields an exact sequence

H^{2r}(Y_{k},M_{-1}R\Psi\Lambda)\to H^{2r}(Y_{k},M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda)\to H^{2r}(Y_{k},\GR^{M}_{0}R\Psi\Lambda)\to H^{2r+1}(Y_{k},M_{-1}R\Psi\Lambda).

Applying Proposition
7.1.4
and using
H^{2r-1}(C_{i},\Lambda)=0

for all
i\in I
, we obtain

0\to H^{2r}(Y_{k},M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda)\to H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{k},\Lambda)\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}H^{2r}(D_{i},\Lambda)\to H^{2r}(C_{i},\Lambda).

However,
H^{2r}(D_{i},\Lambda)\to H^{2r}(C_{i},\Lambda)

is an isomorphism for each
i\in I

by the Lefschetz hyperplane theorem, and the lemma follows.
∎

7.1.6.

Let
R\Psi_{X}\Lambda
be the nearby cycles complex for
X
, defined as in (
7.1.3
); it also coincides with the nearby cycles complex for
X_{R}
. From now on, we will assume:

(
\text{BC}_{X}
)

\text{the base change map }H^{i}(X_{\overline{\eta}_{0}},\Lambda)\to H^{i}(X_{s_{0}},R\Psi_{X}\Lambda)\text{ is an isomorphism for all }i.

Since the blowup map
f:Y\to X_{R}

is proper and is an isomorphism on generic fibers, we have a canonical isomorphism

f_{\ast}R\Psi\Lambda=R\Psi_{X}\Lambda

by
[
29
, §2.1.7]
. In particular, (

BC
X

) implies:

(
\text{BC}_{Y}
)

\begin{split}\text{the base change map }H^{i}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)=H^{i}(Y_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)\to H^{i}(Y_{s},R\Psi\Lambda)\text{ is an isomorphism for all }i.\end{split}

Lemma 7.1.7
.

Let
j:\bigsqcup_{i\in I}C_{i}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}_{s}

be the natural embedding. Then the monodromy operator
T-1

on
H^{2r-1}(Y_{s},R\Psi\Lambda)

fits into a commutative diagram with exact rows:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{i\in I}H^{2r-2}(C_{i},\Lambda(-1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2r}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{i\in I}H^{2r-2}(C_{i},\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2r-2}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2r-2}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T-1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\ast}}

Here,
t

is the isomorphism
-\otimes t_{p}(T)
.

Proof.

From the vanishing of
\GR_{i}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda

for
|i|>1

(Proposition
7.1.4
), we have a canonical factorization

(7.3)

T-1:R\Psi\Lambda\to\GR_{1}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda\to M_{-1}R\Psi\Lambda\to R\Psi\Lambda;

taking cohomology and applying Proposition
7.1.4
, (
7.3
) induces a commutative diagram:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2r-1}(Y_{s},R\Psi\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{i\in I}H^{2r-2}(C_{i},\Lambda(-1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2r-1}(Y_{s},R\Psi\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{i\in I}H^{2r-2}(C_{i},\Lambda).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T-1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

The description of
t

is
[
98
, Corollary 2.2.4.2]
.

We now explain the exactness of the top row

(7.4)

H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\alpha}\oplus_{i\in I}H^{2r-2}(C_{i},\Lambda(-1))\xrightarrow{j_{\ast}}H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda)\to H^{2r}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)

of the diagram in the lemma. From the tautological distinguished triangle

(7.5)

M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda\to R\Psi\Lambda\to\GR^{M}_{1}R\Psi\Lambda\to M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda[+1],

we deduce the exact sequence

(7.6)

H^{2r-1}(Y_{s},R\Psi\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\alpha}\oplus_{i\in I}H^{2r-2}(C_{i},\Lambda(-1))\xrightarrow{\delta}H^{2r}(Y_{s},M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda)\to H^{2r}(Y_{s},R\Psi\Lambda).

Combined with Lemma
7.1.5
and (
\text{BC}_{Y}
), it suffices to show that the composite

(7.7)

\oplus_{i\in I}H^{2r-2}(C_{i},\Lambda(-1))\xrightarrow{\delta}H^{2r}(Y_{s},M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda)

is the pushforward map, and this follows from
[
98
, Proposition 2.2.6]
.
The argument for the exactness of the bottom row

H^{2r-2}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)\to H^{2r-2}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda)\xrightarrow{j^{\ast}}\bigoplus_{i\in I}H^{2r-2}(C_{i},\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\beta}H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)

is essentially identical.
∎

7.1.8.

By
(\text{BC}_{Y})
, the monodromy filtration
M_{\bullet}

of
R\Psi\Lambda

induces filtrations
M_{\bullet}
on

H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)

and on

H^{1}(I_{K_{0}},H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda))=H^{1}(I_{K},H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda))

(using
p\neq 2
).

Proposition 7.1.9
.

The diagram in Lemma
7.1.7
induces an exact sequence

0\to M_{-1}H^{1}(I_{K_{0}},H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda))\xrightarrow{\zeta}\frac{H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda)}{\image(j_{\ast}\circ t^{-1}\circ j^{\ast})}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\frac{H^{2r}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)}{\image(\gamma\circ j_{\ast}\circ t^{-1}\circ j^{\ast})},

such that
\zeta(c)=j_{\ast}\circ t^{-1}(y)
for any
y\in\oplus_{i\in I}H^{2r-2}(C_{i},\Lambda)
such that
c(T)=\beta(y)
.

Proof.

By definition,
M_{-1}H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)

is the image of

\beta:\bigoplus_{i\in I}H^{2r-2}(C_{i},\Lambda)\to H^{2r-1}(Y_{s},R\Psi\Lambda)\cong H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda).

Then, by the left half of the commutative diagram in Lemma
7.1.7
, the map
c\mapsto c(T)

identifies

M_{-1}H^{1}(I_{K_{0}},H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda))\simeq\frac{\image\beta}{\image(\beta\circ t\circ\alpha)}.

Using the exactness of the rows in Lemma
7.1.7
, we also have the exact sequence

0\to\frac{\image\beta}{\image(\beta\circ t\circ\alpha)}\xrightarrow{j_{\ast}\circ t^{-1}}\frac{H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda)}{\image(j_{\ast}\circ t^{-1}\circ j^{\ast})}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\frac{H^{2r}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)}{\image(\gamma\circ j_{\ast}\circ t^{-1}\circ j^{\ast})},

and the proposition follows.
∎

7.1.10.

Let
\CH^{r}(X_{\eta_{0}})^{0}

denote the Chow group of cohomologically trivial algebraic cycles of pure codimension
r
. We have the Abel-Jacobi map

\partial_{\operatorname{AJ}}:\CH^{r}(X_{\eta_{0}})^{0}\to H^{1}(I_{K_{0}},H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda(r))),

which we are now ready to compute in terms of the geometry of the special fiber of
X
.

If
Z_{\eta_{0}}

is a closed irreducible subvariety of
X_{\eta_{0}}
, then write
Z

for its Zariski closure in
X
,
Z_{Y}

for the strict transform of
Z_{R}

under the blowup
Y\to X_{R}
, and
Z_{Y_{s}}

for
Z_{Y}\times_{Y}Y_{s}
. The intersection
Z_{Y_{s}}\times_{Y}\widetilde{X}_{s}

is
\widetilde{Z}_{s}
, the strict transform of
Z_{s_{0}}

under the blowup
\widetilde{X}_{s}\to X_{s_{0}}
. Extending this construction linearly, for any algebraic cycle
z_{\eta}=\Sigma n_{j}Z_{\eta}^{(j)}

of pure codimension
r
, we obtain a codimension-
r

algebraic cycle

\widetilde{z}_{s}=\Sigma n_{j}\widetilde{Z}_{s}^{(j)}

on
\widetilde{X}_{s}
.

Theorem 7.1.11
.

Let
z_{\eta_{0}}

be an algebraic cycle of codimension
r

on
X_{\eta_{0}}
, whose class in
\CH^{r}(X_{\eta_{0}})

is cohomologically trivial, and assume (

BC
X

).
Then
\partial_{\operatorname{AJ}}(z)

lies in
M_{-1}H^{1}(I_{K_{0}},H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda(r)))
. If, for each irreducible component
Z_{\eta}

of the support of
z_{\eta}
,
Z_{Y_{s}}

is generically smooth, then

\zeta\left(\partial_{\operatorname{AJ}}(z)\right)\in\frac{H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda(r))}{j_{\ast}\circ t^{-1}\circ j^{\ast}\left(H^{2r-2}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda(r-1))\right)}

coincides with the algebraic cycle class of
\widetilde{z}_{s}
, where
\zeta
is the map from Proposition
7.1.9
.

Proof.

In the proof of
[
68
, Theorem 2.18]
there is constructed:

•

An element
F

of the bounded derived category of abelian sheaves on
Y_{s}

fitting into a commutative diagram:

(7.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Psi\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Psi\Lambda/M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Psi\Lambda/M_{-2}R\Psi\Lambda.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T-1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T-1}

•

A class
[z^{\sharp}]^{\prime}_{0}\in H^{2r-1}(Y_{s},F(r))

such that
\partial_{\operatorname{AJ}}(z)|_{I_{K}}

is represented by the cocycle that factors through
t_{p}:I_{K}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)

and satisfies

\partial_{\operatorname{AJ}}(z)(T)=(T-1)[z^{\sharp}]^{\prime}_{0}\in H^{2r-1}(Y_{s},R\Psi\Lambda(r))=H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda(r)).

In our context, since
\GR_{i}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda=0

for
|i|>1
, the diagram (
7.8
) becomes
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Psi\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Psi\Lambda/M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{-1}R\Psi\Lambda.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T-1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T-1}

In particular,
(T-1)H^{2r-1}(Y_{s},F(r))

lies inside
M_{-1}H^{2r-1}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda(r)),

and by construction
\zeta\left(\partial_{\operatorname{AJ}}(z)\right)

is represented by the image of
[z^{\sharp}]^{\prime}_{0}

under the composite map

(7.9)

\begin{split}H^{2r-1}(Y_{s},F(r))\to H^{2r-1}(Y_{s},\GR_{1}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda(r))\to H^{2r}(Y_{s},\GR_{0}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda(r))\\
=H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda(r))\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}H^{2r}(D_{i},\Lambda(r)\twoheadrightarrow H^{2r}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda(r)).\end{split}

By
[
68
, Proposition 2.19]
, under the generic smoothness assumption of the theorem, this image is exactly the cycle class of
\widetilde{z}_{s}
.
∎

7.1.12.

We conclude this section with a related lemma.

Lemma 7.1.13
.

In addition to (

BC
X

), assume

(
\text{BC}_{X,c}
)

\text{the base change map }H^{i}_{c}(X_{\overline{\eta}_{0}},\Lambda)\to H^{i}_{c}(X_{s_{0}},R\Psi_{X}\Lambda)\text{ is an isomorphism for all }i.

Suppose
\mathcal{H}
is a commutative
O
-algebra (not necessarily finitely generated) of correspondences on
X
, such that the singular locus of
X
is stable under
\mathcal{H}
. Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathcal{H}
be a maximal ideal such that the natural map induces an isomorphism

H^{i}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}\to H^{i}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}

for all
i
. Then for all
i
, the natural map

H^{i}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}\to H^{i}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{s},\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}

is an isomorphism as well.

Proof.

The tautological distinguished triangle (
7.5
) gives a commutative diagram of long exact sequences
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{c}(Y_{s},M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{c}(Y_{s},R\Psi\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{c}(Y_{s},\operatorname{gr}_{1}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}(Y_{s},M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}(Y_{s},R\Psi\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}(Y_{s},\operatorname{gr}_{1}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

The first marked isomorphism is by (

BC
X

) and (

BC

X
,
c

), and the second is by Proposition
7.1.4
and the compactness of each
C_{i}
. By the five lemma, we have an isomorphism

H^{i}_{c}(Y_{s},M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}(Y_{s},M_{0}R\Psi\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}

for all
i
. Arguing similarly with the distinguished triangle (
7.2
), we find
a natural isomorphism

H^{i}_{c}(Y_{s},\operatorname{gr}_{0}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}(Y_{s},\operatorname{gr}_{0}^{M}R\Psi\Lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}}

for all
i
, which implies the lemma by Proposition
7.1.4
once again.
∎

7.2.
Semistable reduction of
\spin_{5}
Shimura varieties

7.2.1.

Let
D\neq 1
be a squarefree product of an even number of primes, and fix an odd prime
q|D
. With
V_{D}
as in (
•
‣
1.1.6
), we suppose fixed a
q
-adic uniformization datum
(\ast,A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},i_{D},i_{D/q})
for
V_{D}
(Definition
1.4.4
(
2
)); we will choose this uniformization datum more precisely in Construction
7.6.4
below.

Let
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathscr{D}
be the associated PEL data and self-dual
q
-integral refinement from Definition

1.4.2
.

7.2.2.

For the entirety of this section, we fix a neat level subgroup

K^{q}=\prod_{\ell\neq q}K_{\ell}\subset\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q})\cong\prod_{\ell\neq q}\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q}),

with the isomorphism arising from Remark
1.4.5
(
2
);
then we obtain a flat, quasi-projective scheme
X=\mathcal{M}_{K^{q}}

over
\mathbb{Z}_{(q)}

representing the PEL-type moduli problem defined by
\mathscr{D}

at level
K^{q}
.
The generic fiber
X_{\mathbb{Q}}

of
X

is isomorphic to
\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}}(V_{D})
, where
K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}\subset\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

is a paramodular subgroup in the sense of Notation
2.6.1
.

Lemma 7.2.3
.

Let
R\Psi_{X}O

denote the nearby cycles complex on
X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
. Then the natural maps

H^{i}_{c,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O)\to H^{i}_{c,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},R\Psi_{X}O)

and

H^{i}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O)\to H^{i}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},R\Psi_{X}O)

are isomorphisms for all
i
.

Proof.

This is a special case of
[
58
, Theorem 6.8]
.
∎

Let
O_{D}\subset B_{D}
be the unique maximal
\mathbb{Z}_{(q)}
-order. We now take the
q
-divisible group
\mathbb{X}\coloneqq\overline{A}_{0}[q^{\infty}]
, with its induced polarization and
(O_{D}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q})
-action, to be the base point for the Rapoport-Zink space
\mathcal{N}

(Definition
6.1.2
).

Theorem 7.2.4
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{X}

be the completion of
X

along the supersingular locus
X^{{ss}}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
. Then we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathcal{X}\cong\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q})\times\mathcal{N}/K^{q},

where
\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

acts on
\mathcal{N}

as described in §
6.5
.

(2)

The singular locus of
X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

is the discrete set of points

X^{{\operatorname{sing}}}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}=\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q})\times\mathcal{N}^{{\operatorname{sing}}}_{{\operatorname{red}}}/K^{q}.

The complete local ring of
X

at any point of
X^{{\operatorname{sing}}}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

is isomorphic to

\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}\llbracket x,y,z,w\rrbracket/(xy-zw-q).

Proof.

Part (1) is the Rapoport-Zink uniformization theorem for
X
; part (2) follows from (1) and Theorem
6.2.4
(
6
), after noting that all singularities of
X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

lie in the supersingular locus by
[
86
, Theorem 7.5]
.
∎

7.2.5.

In particular, Theorem
7.2.4
(2) asserts that
X

has ordinary quadratic singularities, so that the results of §
7.1
apply. Following the notation therein, let
\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

be the blowup of
X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

along the singular locus;
note that
\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

inherits an action of the full prime-to-
q

Hecke algebra.

7.3.
Tate classes

The goal of this subsection is to show that the full cohomology group
H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O)

is generated by Tate classes from the supersingular locus, after a Hecke localization.

Notation 7.3.1
.

Recall the sets
\mathscr{L}
and
\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}
from Definition
6.5.2
.

(1)

For

g=(g^{q},\Lambda)\in\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q})\times\mathscr{L}/K^{q},

let
B_{\pm}(g)

be the image of
(g^{q},\mathcal{M}_{\pm}(\Lambda))

under the uniformization in Theorem
7.2.4
, and let
\widetilde{B}_{\pm}(g)
be its strict transform under the blowup
\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\to X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
.

(2)

For

g=(g^{q},\Lambda_{\text{Pa}})\in\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q})\times\mathscr{L}_{\text{Pa}}/K^{q},

let
y(g)\in X^{{\operatorname{sing}}}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

be the image of
(g^{q},\mathcal{M}(\Lambda_{\text{Pa}}))
, and let
C(g)
be the exceptional divisor of
\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
over the point
y(g)
.

(3)

Recall that
\mathscr{L}

and
\mathscr{L}_{\text{Pa}}

are homogeneous spaces for
\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})\cong\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
, with the stabilizer of any point a hyperspecial or paramodular subgroup, respectively. We will therefore abbreviate the two sets in (
1
) and (
2
)
by
\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})

and
\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q}),
respectively,
even though the identifications actually depend on a non-canonical choice of base point which we do not need to make.

Remark 7.3.2
.

Notation
7.3.1
identifies
\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\times\left\{\pm\right\}
and
\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{D/q})
with the set of irreducible components of
X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}^{{ss}}
and the set of points of
X^{{\operatorname{sing}}}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
, respectively.

To study the intersections of the divisors
\widetilde{B}_{\pm}(g)

and
C(g)
, we need to
define some additional Hecke operators.

Definition 7.3.3
.

Let

\delta_{\pm}:O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})]\to O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})]

be the maps defined by

\delta_{+}:(g^{q},\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})\mapsto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda\\
\Lambda\in\mathscr{L}\end{subarray}}(g^{q},\Lambda),

\delta_{-}:(g^{q},\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})\mapsto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda\subset_{1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\\
\Lambda\in\mathscr{L}\end{subarray}}(g^{q},\Lambda).

Similarly, let

\theta_{\pm}:O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})]\to O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})]

be the maps defined by

\theta_{+}\,:\,(g^{q},\Lambda)\mapsto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda\\
\Lambda_{\operatorname{Pa}}\in\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Pa}}\end{subarray}}(g^{q},\Lambda_{\operatorname{Pa}}),\;\;\;\;\theta_{-}\,:\,(g^{q},\Lambda)\mapsto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda\subset_{1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\\
\Lambda_{\operatorname{Pa}}\in\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Pa}}\end{subarray}}(g^{q},\Lambda_{\operatorname{Pa}}).

These are incarnations of the level-lowering and level-raising operators in
[
96
, §3]
.

Definition 7.3.4
.

We define the natural composite maps

(7.10)

\inc^{\ast}:H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))\;\;\longrightarrow\\
\bigoplus_{g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})}H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(C(g),O(2))\;\;\;\;\oplus\bigoplus_{g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})}\left(H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{B}_{+}(g),O(2))\oplus H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{B}_{-}(g),O(2))\right)\\
\xrightarrow{\sim}O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]^{\oplus 2}

and

(7.11)

\inc_{c,\ast}:O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]^{\oplus 2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\\
\bigoplus_{g\in\Shimura_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})}H^{0}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(C(g),O)\oplus\bigoplus_{g\in\Shimura_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})}\left(H^{0}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{B}_{+}(g),O)\oplus H^{0}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{B}_{-}(g),O)\right)\\
\longrightarrow H^{2}_{c,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1)).

We also denote by
\inc_{c}^{\ast}

the composite

\inc_{c}^{\ast}:H^{4}_{c,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))\rightarrow H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))\xrightarrow{\inc^{\ast}}O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]^{\oplus 2}

and likewise by
\inc_{\ast}

the composite

\inc_{\ast}:O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]^{\oplus 2}\xrightarrow{\inc_{c,\ast}}H^{2}_{c,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))\longrightarrow H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1)).

Notation 7.3.5
.

For
\alpha\in O\left[\Shimura_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]

and
g\in\Shimura_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})
, let
m(g;\alpha)\in O

denote the coefficient of
g

in
\alpha
, and similarly for
\Shimura_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})
.

Lemma 7.3.6
.

Fix
g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{D/q})
.

(1)

There exists an isomorphism
C(g)\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

with the following property: for
h\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})
, the intersection
\widetilde{B}_{+}(h)\cdot C(g)

has the cycle class
(m(h;\delta_{+}(g)),0)

on
C(g)
; and the intersection
\widetilde{B}_{-}(h)\cdot C(g)

has the cycle class
(0,m(h;\delta_{-}(g)))
.

(2)

Let
\iota_{g}:C(g)\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

be the natural inclusion. If we fix an isomorphism
C(g)\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

as in (1), then we have

\inc^{\ast}(\iota_{g})_{\ast}[(1,0)]=(-g,0,\delta_{-}(g))

and

\inc^{\ast}(\iota_{g})_{\ast}[(0,1)]=(-g,\delta_{+}(g),0).

Proof.

(1) is immediate from Lemma

6.4.2
and Corollary
6.5.6
; (2) follows from (1), using that the Chern class of the normal bundle on
C(g)

is
(-1,-1)

(by (
7.1
)) to compute the first coordinate.
∎

Notation 7.3.7
.

For any
g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})
, let

[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{B}_{\pm}(g)}(1)]\in H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))

denote the pushforward of the class of the line bundle
\mathcal{O}(1)

on
\widetilde{B}_{\pm}(g)
(which is the one induced by Notation
6.4.3
).

Recall the explicit Hecke algebra generators from (
2.1.10
).

Lemma 7.3.8
.

For all
g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})
, we have

\inc_{c}^{\ast}[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)}(1)]=(0,-2q(q+1)\cdot g,T_{q,2}\cdot g)

and

\inc_{c}^{\ast}[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{B}_{-}(g)}(1)]=(0,\langle q\rangle^{-1}T_{q,2}\cdot g,-2q(q+1)\cdot g).

Proof.

The two calculations are similar, so we consider
\left[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)}(1)\right]
. We must calculate the intersection pairings with divisor classes
\left[C(h)\right]

and
\left[\widetilde{B}_{\pm}(g^{\prime})\right]
, for
h\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})

and
g^{\prime}\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})
.

Since
C(h)\cdot\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)

always lies in the exceptional divisor of the blowup
\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)\to B_{+}(g)
, we have

(7.12)

\left[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)}(1)\right]\cdot\left[C(h)\right]=\left[\mathcal{O}(1)\right]\cdot_{\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)}\left[C(h)\cdot\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)\right]=0.

Now,
\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)

and
\widetilde{B}_{+}(g^{\prime})

meet only if
g=g^{\prime}
, in which case we find

(7.13)

\left[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)}(1)\right]\cdot\left[\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)\right]=[\mathcal{O}(1)]\cdot_{\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)}\left[\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)/\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}}\right]=-2q(q+1),

since the normal bundle to
\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)

is
\mathcal{O}(-2q)
(Lemma

6.4.4
)
and
B_{+}(g)

has degree
q+1
(Theorem

6.2.4
(
1
)).
Finally, we compute

(7.14)

\left[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)}(1)\right]\cdot\left[\widetilde{B}_{-}(g^{\prime})\right]=\left[\mathcal{O}(1)\right]\cdot_{\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)}\left[\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)\cdot\widetilde{B}_{-}(g^{\prime})\right]=m(g^{\prime};T_{q,2}\cdot g),

since
B_{+}(g)\cap B_{-}(g^{\prime})

consists of
m(g^{\prime};T_{q,2}\cdot g)

linearly embedded copies of
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
inside
B_{+}(g)\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
(by Theorem
6.2.4
(
2
) and Corollary
6.5.6
).
Combining these calculations gives

\inc_{c}^{\ast}\left[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)}(1)\right]=(0,-2q(q+1)\cdot g,T_{q,2}\cdot g),

as desired.
∎

Definition 7.3.9
.

Fix a finite set
S
of places of
\mathbb{Q}
containing
q
, and all primes
\ell\neq q
such that
K_{\ell}
is not hyperspecial.
A maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}

will be called

weakly

q
-generic
if the map

\langle q\rangle^{-1}T_{q,2}^{2}-4q^{2}(q+1)^{2}:O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\to O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}

is an isomorphism.

Remark 7.3.10
.

Using (
2.3
), one calculates the following:
if
\pi
is a relevant automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
unramified at
q
with trivial central character and
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
is any isomorphism with
p\neq q
, then
\langle q\rangle^{-1}T_{q,2}^{2}-4q^{2}(q+1)^{2}
acts on the spherical vector of
\pi_{q}
with eigenvalue

q^{2}\left(\iota\tr(\Frob_{q}|V_{\pi,\iota})^{2}-4(q+1)^{2}\right).

Lemma 7.3.11
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}

be a weakly
q
-generic maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}
. Then the map

\inc^{\ast}_{c,\mathfrak{m}}:H^{4}_{c,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\to O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]^{\oplus 2}_{\mathfrak{m}}

is surjective.

We note that Lemma

7.3.11
is slightly stronger than the corresponding statement for
\inc^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.3.6
, it suffices to show that the image of
\inc^{\ast}_{c,\mathfrak{m}}

contains
O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}
. Define a map

(7.15)

\mu=(\mu_{+},\mu_{-}):O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}\to H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}

by linearly extending

(7.16)

\mu_{\pm}(g)=\left[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{B}_{\pm}(g)}(1)\right].

Then by Lemma
7.3.8
, the composite
\inc^{\ast}_{c,\mathfrak{m}}\circ\mu

is given by the matrix map

\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
-2q(q+1)&\langle q\rangle^{-1}T_{q,2}\\
T_{q,2}&-2q(q+1).\end{pmatrix}

Since

\debt\begin{pmatrix}-2q(q+1)&\langle q\rangle^{-1}T_{q,2}\\
T_{q,2}&-2q(q+1)\end{pmatrix}=4q^{2}(q+1)^{2}-\langle q\rangle^{-1}T^{2}_{q,2}

and
\mathfrak{m}

is weakly
q
-generic, we have

\image(\inc^{\ast}_{c,\mathfrak{m}})\supset\image(\inc^{\ast}_{c,\mathfrak{m}}\circ\mu)=O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]^{\oplus 2}_{\mathfrak{m}},

as desired.
∎

In Theorem
7.3.14
, we will see that
\inc^{\ast}_{c,\mathfrak{m}}

also has torsion kernel.

Lemma 7.3.12
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}

be a generic and non-Eisenstein maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}
. There is a canonical injection induced by pullback

H^{2}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))_{\mathfrak{m}}\hookrightarrow\bigoplus_{g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})}H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(C(g),O(1))\simeq O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}

and a canonical surjection induced by pushforward

O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}\simeq\bigoplus_{g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})}H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(C(g),O(1))_{\mathfrak{m}}\twoheadrightarrow H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
7.1.7
and Theorem
2.7.5
(
2
).
∎

Corollary 7.3.13

(Ihara’s Lemma)
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}

be a generic, non-Eisenstein, and weakly
q
-generic maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}
. Then the degeneracy map

(\delta_{+},\delta_{-}):O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\longrightarrow O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}

is surjective.

Proof.

Combining Lemmas
7.3.12
and
7.3.11
, we see that the composite map

\begin{split}O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}\simeq\oplus H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(C(g),O(1))_{\mathfrak{m}}\longrightarrow H_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}^{4}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{\inc^{\ast}_{c,\mathfrak{m}}}\\
O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]^{\oplus 2}_{\mathfrak{m}}\end{split}

is surjective. On the other hand, by Lemma

7.3.6
, this composite is given as a matrix by

\begin{pmatrix}-1&0&\delta_{-}\\
-1&\delta_{+}&0\end{pmatrix},

and the corollary follows by restricting to the preimage of
O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}.

∎

Theorem 7.3.14
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}

be a generic, non-Eisenstein, and weakly
q
-generic
maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}
. Then

\inc^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{m}}:H_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{4}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\to O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}

and

\inc_{\ast,\mathfrak{m}}:O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}\to H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))_{\mathfrak{m}}

are both surjective. Moreover
\inc_{\ast,\mathfrak{m}}

is injective, and
\inc^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{m}}

is injective modulo
O
-torsion.

In fact, only the surjectivity of
\inc_{\ast,\mathfrak{m}}

is needed for the main result.

Proof.

We claim that it suffices to show

(7.17)

\dim H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}\leq\dim\left(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)+2\dim\left(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\right);

indeed, this combined with Lemma
7.3.11
implies that
\inc^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{m}}

is injective modulo torsion as well as surjective, and the other assertions follow by duality along with Lemma
7.1.13
and Theorem
2.7.5
(
2
). (We also use that
H^{2}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O)_{\mathfrak{m}}

is
O
-torsion-free by Lemma

7.3.12
.)
Inspecting the diagram in Lemma
7.1.7
and using Theorem
2.7.5
(
2
), we see that

\dim H^{4}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}=2\dim\left(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)-\operatorname{rank}\left(T-1|H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}\left(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\right)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right),

where
T\in I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}
is a generator of tame inertia, so we wish to show

(7.18)

\dim\left(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)\leq 2\dim\left(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)+\operatorname{rank}\left(T-1|H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}\left(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\right)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right).

Fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
. Applying
Lemma
2.7.6
, it suffices to show

(7.19)

\begin{split}\dim\left(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]\left[\iota^{-1}\pi_{f}^{q}\right]\right)\leq 2\dim\left(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]\left[\iota^{-1}\pi_{f}^{q}\right]\right)+\\
\operatorname{rank}\left(T-1|H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}\left(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\right)\left[\iota^{-1}\pi_{f}^{q}\right]\right)\end{split}

for all relevant automorphic representations
\pi
of
\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{A})
such that
\pi^{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}_{f}\neq 0
and the Hecke action on
\iota^{-1}\pi^{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}_{f}
factors through
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O,\mathfrak{m}}
.

By Lemma
2.6.2
(
1
,
2
),
\pi_{q}

is uniquely determined by
\pi^{q}_{f}
, and is either spherical or of type IIa. Assume first that
\pi_{q}

is spherical. Then
\pi_{f}^{q}

cannot be completed to a relevant automorphic representation of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
(by Corollary
2.5.3
), so by Corollary
2.7.7
the final term in (
7.19
) vanishes. Since
\dim\pi_{q}^{K_{q}}=1
and
\dim\pi_{q}^{K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}=2
by
[
96
, Table A.13]
, both sides of (
7.19
) are 2.

On the other hand, suppose that
\pi_{q}

is of type IIa.

Case 1:

\pi

is non-endoscopic. Then
\pi_{f}^{q}

can be completed to a relevant automorphic representation
\pi^{\prime}

of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})

by Theorem
2.4.6
.
By Lemma
2.6.2
(
3
),
\pi^{\prime}_{q}

has a unique fixed vector for
K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}\subset\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
, so by Corollary
2.7.7
,

H^{3}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\left[\iota^{-1}\pi_{f}^{q}\right]=\rho_{\pi,\iota}(-2)\otimes\iota^{-1}(\pi_{f}^{q})^{K^{q}}.

By Lemma
2.6.2
(
3
),
\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is tamely ramified at
q

with monodromy of rank one. On the other hand,
\pi_{q}
has a unique paramodular fixed vector, and
\pi
has automorphic multiplicity one for
\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{A})
by Theorem
2.4.6
(
3
). So in this case we see that both sides of (
7.19
) are 1.

Case 2:

\pi

is endoscopic, associated to a pair of cuspidal automorphic representations
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})

of
\GL_{2}

with discrete series archimedean components of weights 2 and 4, respectively. Theorem
2.5.2
implies that there exist (uniquely determined) quaternion algebras
B_{1}

and
B_{2}

such that
\pi

is the theta lift
\Theta(\pi_{1}^{B_{1}}\boxtimes\pi_{2}^{B_{2}})
, with
\pi_{i}^{B_{i}}

the Jacquet-Langlands transfers. Moreover,
B_{1}\otimes\mathbb{R}

is ramified and
B_{2}\otimes\mathbb{R}

is split. Since
\pi_{q}

is of type IIa with a paramodular fixed vector, we can conclude from Lemma
2.6.2
(
3
) and Theorem
2.2.1
(
1
) that exactly one of
\pi_{i,q}

is a twist of a Steinberg representation, and the other is unramified. Let
B_{i}^{\prime}

be the quaternion algebras obtained from
B_{i}

by changing invariants at
q

and
\infty
. Then
\pi_{f}^{q}

has a unique completion to an automorphic representation of
\spin(V_{D})
, which is

(7.20)

\begin{cases}\Theta(\pi_{1}^{B_{1}^{\prime}}\boxtimes\pi_{2}^{B_{2}}),&\pi_{1,q}\text{ ramified},\\
\Theta(\pi_{1}^{B_{1}}\boxtimes\pi_{2}^{B_{2}^{\prime}}),&\pi_{2,q}\text{ ramified.}\end{cases}

We therefore have (applying Corollary
2.7.7
)

H_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}^{3}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\left[\pi_{f}^{q}\right]\cong(\pi_{f}^{q})^{K^{q}}\otimes\rho,

with

\rho=\begin{cases}\rho_{\pi_{1},\iota}(-2),&\pi_{1,q}\text{ ramified},\\
\rho_{\pi_{2},\iota}(-2),&\pi_{2,q}\text{ ramified.}\end{cases}

In particular, the monodromy at
q

has rank one in either case, so again both sides of (
7.19
) are 1.∎

7.4.
Level-raising and potential map

Lemma 7.4.1
.

The Hecke operators
\theta_{\pm}
,
\delta_{\pm}

satisfy:

\displaystyle\delta_{+}\circ\theta_{+}

\displaystyle=\delta_{-}\circ\theta_{-}=T_{q,1}+(q+1)(q^{2}+1)

\displaystyle\delta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}

\displaystyle=(q+1)T_{q,2}

\displaystyle\delta_{+}\circ\theta_{-}

\displaystyle=\langle q\rangle^{-1}(q+1)T_{q,2}.

Proof.

By definition,
\delta_{+}\circ\theta_{+}

is induced by

\displaystyle(g^{q},\Lambda)

\displaystyle\mapsto\delta_{+}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda\\
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\end{subarray}}(g^{q},\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda^{\prime}\\
\Lambda^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda\\
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\end{subarray}}(g^{q},\Lambda^{\prime})
