\displaystyle=\sum_{\Lambda^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}}e(\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime})(g^{q},\Lambda^{\prime}),

where

e(\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime})=\#\left\{\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\,:\,\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda,\,\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda^{\prime}\right\}.

If
e(\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime})\neq 0
, then either
\Lambda=\Lambda^{\prime}

or
\Lambda^{\prime}\in T_{q,1}\cdot\Lambda
. In the latter case,
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}=\Lambda\cap\Lambda^{\prime}

is uniquely determined, so
e(\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime})=1.

On the other hand,
e(\Lambda,\Lambda)

is the number of lattices
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda
, or equivalently the number of rational 3-planes in the symplectic space
\Lambda/q\Lambda
. Thus

e(\Lambda,\Lambda)=\#\mathbb{P}^{3}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=(q+1)(q^{2}+1).

This shows

\delta_{+}\circ\theta_{+}=T_{q,1}+(q+1)(q^{2}+1),

and the calculation for
\delta_{-}\circ\theta_{-}

is similar. We now compute
\delta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}
, which is induced by

\displaystyle(g^{q},\Lambda)

\displaystyle\mapsto\delta_{-}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda\\
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\end{subarray}}(g^{q},\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda\\
\lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda^{\prime}\subset_{1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\\
\Lambda^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}\end{subarray}}(g^{q},\Lambda^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\Lambda^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}}e^{\prime}(\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime})(g^{q},\Lambda^{\prime}),

where

e^{\prime}(\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime})=\#\left\{\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\,:\,\Lambda^{\prime}\subset_{1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda\right\}.

If
e^{\prime}(\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime})\neq 0
, then
\Lambda^{\prime}\in T_{q,2}\cdot\Lambda.

On the other hand, given
\Lambda^{\prime}\in T_{q,2}\cdot\Lambda
, then the choices of
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}

with
\Lambda^{\prime}\subset_{1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda

are in bijection with rational lines in the 2-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector space
\Lambda/\Lambda^{\prime}
; hence

e^{\prime}(\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime})=\#\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=q+1.

This shows
\delta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}=(q+1)T_{q,2}
, and the computation of
\delta_{+}\circ\theta_{-}

is similar.
∎

7.4.2.

Recall from Lemma

7.1.7

the natural embedding

j:\bigsqcup C(g)\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}.

Lemma 7.4.3
.

The composite map

\begin{split}\inc^{\ast}\circ j_{\ast}\circ j^{\ast}\circ\inc_{\ast}:O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]^{\oplus 2}\longrightarrow\\
O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]^{\oplus 2}\end{split}

is given by the matrix

\begin{pmatrix}2&-\theta_{+}&-\theta_{-}\\
-\delta_{+}&0&(q+1)\langle q\rangle^{-1}T_{q,2}\\
-\delta_{-}&(q+1)T_{q,2}&0.\end{pmatrix}

Proof.

We begin by calculating
\inc^{\ast}\circ j_{\ast}\circ j^{\ast}\left[C(g)\right]
, for
g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})
. Let
\iota_{g}:C(g)\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

be the natural embedding. Since the
C(g)

are all disjoint,

j_{\ast}\circ j^{\ast}\left[C(g)\right]\in H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))

is the pushforward of the class of the normal bundle, i.e.
\iota_{g\ast}[(-1,-1)]

in the notation of Lemma

7.3.6
. Then

\inc^{\ast}\iota_{g\ast}[(-1,-1)]=-\inc^{\ast}\iota_{g\ast}[(1,0)]-\inc^{\ast}\iota_{g\ast}[(0,1)]=(2\cdot g,-\delta_{+}(g),-\delta_{-}(g))

by Lemma

7.3.6
(2), which gives the first column of the matrix.

For the second column, we must calculate
\inc^{\ast}\circ j_{\ast}\circ j^{\ast}\left[\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)\right]
, for
g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})
.
By Lemma

7.3.6
(1), the class

j_{\ast}\circ j^{\ast}\left[\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)\right]\in H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))

is

\sum_{h\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})}m(h;\theta_{+}(g))\iota_{h\ast}\left[(1,0)\right].

Then by Lemma

7.3.6
(2),

\inc^{\ast}\circ j_{\ast}\circ j^{\ast}\left[\widetilde{B}_{+}(g)\right]=\left(-\theta_{+}(g),0,\delta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}(g)\right).

By Lemma

7.4.1
,
\delta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}=(q+1)T_{q,2},

so this gives the second column of the matrix; the third column is similar.
∎

Definition 7.4.4
.

Define the potential map

\nabla:H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))\to O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]

as the composite

H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))\xrightarrow{\inc^{\ast}}O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]^{\oplus 2}\xrightarrow{M}O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right],

with
M

the matrix map

\begin{pmatrix}\delta_{+}+\delta_{-}&2&2\end{pmatrix}.

Definition 7.4.5
.

The level-raising Hecke operator
\mathtt{T}_{q}^{\operatorname{lr}}

is defined by

\mathtt{T}_{q}^{\operatorname{lr}}\coloneqq T_{q,1}+(q+1)(q^{2}+1)-T_{q,2}(q+1).

Remark 7.4.6
.

Using (
2.3
), one calculates the following:
If
\pi
is a relevant automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A})
unramified at
q
with trivial central character and
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
is any isomorphism with
p\neq q
, then
\mathtt{T}_{q}^{\operatorname{lr}}
acts on the spherical vector of
\pi_{q}
with eigenvalue

q^{-1}\iota\det(\Frob_{q}-q|V_{\pi,\iota}).

Theorem 7.4.7
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}

be a generic, non-Eisenstein, and weakly
q
-generic maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}
. The composite map

\nabla\circ j_{\ast}\circ t^{-1}\circ j^{\ast}:\,H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\to O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}

has image contained in

\left(\langle q\rangle^{-1}-1,\mathtt{T}_{q}^{\operatorname{lr}}\right)\cdot O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}.

In particular,
\nabla\circ\zeta

gives a well-defined surjection

\nabla\circ\zeta:\,M_{-1}H^{1}\left(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},H^{3}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)\twoheadrightarrow\frac{O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}}{\left(\langle q\rangle^{-1}-1,\mathtt{T}_{q}^{\operatorname{lr}}\right)},

where
\zeta
is the map from Proposition

7.1.9
.

Proof.

By Theorem
7.3.14
, the image of
\nabla\circ j_{\ast}\circ t^{-1}\circ j^{\ast}

coincides with the image of

\nabla\circ j_{\ast}\circ j^{\ast}\circ\inc_{\ast,\mathfrak{m}}:O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}\oplus O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right)_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus 2}\to O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}.

By Lemma
7.4.3
, this is the composite of matrix maps

\begin{split}\begin{pmatrix}\delta_{+}+\delta_{-}&2&2\end{pmatrix}\circ\begin{pmatrix}2&-\theta_{+}&-\theta_{-}\\
-\delta_{+}&0&(q+1)\langle q\rangle^{-1}T_{q,2}\\
-\delta_{-}&(q+1)T_{q,2}&0\end{pmatrix}\\
=\begin{pmatrix}0&-\mathtt{T}_{q}^{\operatorname{lr}}&-\mathtt{T}_{q}^{\operatorname{lr}}+\left(\langle q\rangle^{-1}-1\right)(q+1)T_{q,2}\end{pmatrix}\end{split}

(using Lemma
7.4.1
to compute
\delta_{\pm}\circ\theta_{\pm}
).
∎

7.5.
Siegel cycles on the special fiber

Definition 7.5.1
.

Recall the set
\mathscr{L}
from Definition
6.5.2
.
We define

\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}\coloneqq\left\{\text{pairs }(\Lambda_{+},\Lambda_{-})\in\mathscr{L}^{2}\,:\,q\Lambda_{+}\subset_{2}\Lambda_{-}\subset_{2}\Lambda_{+}\right\}.

Notation 7.5.2
.

Note that
\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}

is a homogeneous space for
\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
, where the stabilizer of any point is a Siegel parahoric subgroup. As in Notation
7.3.1
, we abbreviate

(7.21)

\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Sie}}}(V_{D/q})=\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q})\times\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}/K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Sie}},

although the identification depends on a choice of base point of
\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}
which is not necessary for our discussion.

Definition 7.5.3
.

(1)

We define degeneracy maps

\delta_{\pm}^{\operatorname{Sie}}:\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}\to\mathscr{L}

by

\delta_{\pm}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(\Lambda_{+},\Lambda_{-})=\Lambda_{\pm}.

(2)

We define the operator

\theta_{\operatorname{Sie}}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}:\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}\to\mathbb{Z}\left[\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\right]

by

\theta_{\operatorname{Sie}}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}(\Lambda_{+},\Lambda_{-})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\\
\Lambda_{-}\subset\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset\Lambda_{+}\end{subarray}}\left[\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\right].

(3)

We extend these maps linearly to

\delta_{\pm}^{\operatorname{Sie}}:O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Sie}}}(V_{D/q})\right]\to O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right]

and

\theta_{\operatorname{Sie}}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}:O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Sie}}}(V_{D/q})\right]\rightarrow O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})\right].

Notation 7.5.4
.

For each
g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Sie}}}(V_{D/q})
, let

D(g)=B_{+}(\delta_{1}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(g))\cap B_{-}(\delta_{2}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(g)),

which is a closed subscheme of
X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
isomorphic to

\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
.
We write
\widetilde{D}(g)\hookrightarrow\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

for the strict transform of
D(g)
under the blowup
\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\to X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
, and
[\widetilde{D}(g)]\in H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))
for its algebraic
cycle class.

Lemma 7.5.5
.

For
g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Sie}}}(V_{D/q})
, we have

\nabla\left[\widetilde{D}(g)\right]=(\delta_{+}+\delta_{-})\circ\theta_{\operatorname{Sie}}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}(g)-4q\delta_{+}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(g)-4q\delta_{-}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(g).

Proof.

We first calculate
\inc^{\ast}\left[\widetilde{D}(g)\right].

For
g^{\prime}\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})
, we have

\left[\widetilde{D}(g)\right]\cdot\left[\widetilde{B}_{+}(g^{\prime})\right]=\left[\widetilde{B}_{+}(\delta_{1}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(g))\right]\cdot\left[\widetilde{B}_{-}(\delta_{2}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(g))\right]\cdot\left[\widetilde{B}_{+}(g^{\prime})\right]=0

unless
g^{\prime}=\delta_{1}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(g)
, in which case the intersection number is
-2q

(cf. the proof of Lemma

7.3.8
).
Similarly,

\left[\widetilde{D}(g)\right]\cdot\left[\widetilde{B}_{-}(g^{\prime})\right]=\begin{cases}-2q,&g^{\prime}=\delta_{2}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(g),\\
0,&\text{else}.\end{cases}

Now consider the intersections with
C(h)
, for
h\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}(V_{D/q})
. We see from Lemma

7.3.6
that
\widetilde{D}(g)

meets
C(h)

transversely with multiplicity
m(h;\theta_{\operatorname{Sie}}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}(g))
. Hence

\inc^{\ast}\left[\widetilde{D}(g)\right]=\left(\theta_{\operatorname{Sie}}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}(g),-2q\delta_{1}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(g),-2q\delta_{2}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(g)\right).

The claimed formula then follows from the formula for
\nabla

in Definition
7.4.4
.
∎

7.6.
Special cycles on ramified
\spin_{5}
Shimura varieties

The goal of this section is to compute the local ramification of Abel-Jacobi images of special cycles
Z(T,\varphi)
on the generic fiber of
X
, by applying the results of §
7.1
. However, our first task is to make a good choice of the uniformization datum from (
7.2.1
).

Notation 7.6.1
.

Fix a matrix
T\in\symmetric_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{(q)})_{\geq 0}

such that

T\equiv\begin{pmatrix}0&\alpha\\
\alpha&0\end{pmatrix}\pmod{q},

for some
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}.

Then we make the following notations.

(1)

Let
V_{\star}
be the two-dimensional quadratic space over
\mathbb{Q}_{q}
with basis
\left\{e_{1}^{\star},e_{2}^{\star}\right\}
and pairing matrix given by
T
.

(2)

Let
B_{\star}
be the quaternion algebra
C(V_{\star})
, with its natural positive nebentype involution
\ast
.

(3)

Let
d

be the discriminant of the unique quaternion algebra
B_{d}

such that
B_{D}\otimes B_{\star}\simeq M_{2}(B_{d})
, and let
O_{d}\subset B_{d}
be the unique maximal
\mathbb{Z}_{(q)}
-order.

Remark 7.6.2
.

Because
B_{\star}
is split at
q
and
\infty
,
B_{d}
is ramified at
q
and split at
\infty
. In particular,
O_{d}
is well-defined.

Proposition 7.6.3
.

Fix a nebentype involution
\ast
on
O_{D}
, of unit type. Then there exists an isomorphism

\beta:B_{D}\otimes B_{\star}\xrightarrow{\sim}M_{2}(B_{d})

such that:

(1)

\beta(O_{D})\subset M_{2}(O_{d})
.

(2)

The induced involution
\dagger

on
M_{2}(B_{d})

stabilizes
M_{2}(O_{d})
, and is of non-unit type.

(3)

If
\Pi_{D}\in O_{D}

and
\Pi_{d}\in O_{d}

are uniformizers, then

\beta(\Pi_{D})\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\Pi_{d}^{-1}&0\\
0&\Pi_{d}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}

lies in
\GL_{2}(O_{d})
.

(4)

The
\mathbb{Z}_{(q)}
-lattice
\Lambda_{\star}\coloneqq M_{2}(O_{d})^{\dagger=1,\trace=0}\cap B_{\star}\subset V_{\star}

has basis
\left\{e_{1}^{\star},qe_{2}^{\star}\right\}
.

Proof.

Let

X=\operatorname{Isom}(B_{D}\otimes B_{\star},M_{2}(B_{d})),

viewed as algebraic variety over
\mathbb{Q}
;
X

is a (split) torsor for the algebraic group
\GL_{2}(B_{d})
. Note that all the conditions of the proposition can be checked after tensoring with
\mathbb{Z}_{q}
, and define an open subset
U\subset X(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

in the
q
-adic topology. Since
X(\mathbb{Q})

is dense in
X(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
, it suffices to show
U\neq\emptyset
.

Since the involution
\ast
on
B_{D}
is nebentype and of unit type, we can fix a unit
j\in O_{D}^{\times}
such that
j^{\ast_{D}}=-j^{\ast_{D}}
and
\alpha^{\ast}=j(\alpha^{\ast_{D}})j^{-1}
for
\alpha\in B_{D}
(recall
\ast_{D}
is the canonical involution on
B_{D}
).
Also choose a uniformizer
\Pi\in O_{D}
satisfying
\tr\Pi=0
and
\Pi j=-j\Pi
, and let
K\coloneqq\mathbb{Q}(\Pi)\subset B_{D}
. We have a decomposition

B_{D}=K\oplus j\cdot K,

which
defines an embedding

\iota:B_{D}\hookrightarrow M_{2}(K)\hookrightarrow M_{2}(B_{D}),

satisfying
\alpha(O_{D})\subset M_{2}(O_{D})
. Now let
\dagger

be the non-unit type involution on
O_{D}

defined by

j^{\dagger}=j,\;\Pi^{\dagger}=\Pi,\;\text{and}\,(\Pi j)^{\dagger}=-\Pi j.

We extend
\dagger

to an involution of non-unit type on
M_{2}(B_{D})

by

\begin{pmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
\gamma&\delta\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}\alpha^{\dagger}&-\gamma^{\dagger}j^{2}\\
-\beta^{\dagger}/j^{2}&\delta^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix}.

A simple calculation shows
\iota(\alpha)^{\dagger}=\iota(\alpha^{\ast}),

for all
\alpha\in B_{D}
. Moreover, the centralizer
Z

of
\iota(B_{D})

inside
M_{2}(B_{D})

satisfies

L\coloneqq Z\cap M_{2}(O_{D})^{\dagger=1,\trace=0}=\mathbb{Z}_{(q)}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\Pi&0\\
0&\Pi\end{pmatrix}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{(q)}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}0&\Pi j\\
\Pi j^{-1}&0\end{pmatrix}.

We have a natural quadratic form

x\mapsto x^{2}
on
L
,
which is represented by
\begin{pmatrix}q\alpha&0\\
0&-q\alpha\end{pmatrix}
in the basis above,
for a unit
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{(q)}^{\times}
. Let
L_{\mathbb{Q}}=L\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(q)}}\mathbb{Q}
.

We may then choose the following two isomorphisms:

(i)

An isomorphism
\beta_{1,q}:Z\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}=C(L_{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}\xrightarrow{\sim}C(V_{\star})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}=B_{\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}
compatible with the involutions,
such that
\beta_{1,q}(L\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q})\subset V_{\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}

is the lattice spanned by
e_{1}^{\star}
and
qe_{2}^{\star}
.

(ii)

An isomorphism
\beta_{2,q}:M_{2}(O_{D})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q}\xrightarrow{\sim}M_{2}(O_{d})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q}
.

. One checks readily that the induced isomorphism

\beta_{q}:(B_{D}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q})\otimes(B_{\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q})\xrightarrow[\sim]{\operatorname{id}\otimes\beta_{1,q}^{-1}}B_{D}\otimes Z\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\iota}M_{2}(B_{D})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\beta_{2,q}}M_{2}(B_{d})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}

lies in
U
, so indeed
U\neq\emptyset
.∎

Now we use Proposition
7.6.3
to construct some particular integral models of special cycles on
X_{\mathbb{Q}}=\Sh_{K}(V_{D})
.

Construction 7.6.4
.

Fix once and for all a positive, unit type involution on
B_{D}
, and a choice of
\beta
as in Proposition
7.6.3
for this choice. We will now also write
\ast
for the induced involution on
M_{2}(B_{d})
.

(1)

By Corollary
1.3.5
and Remark
7.6.2
, we fix an abelian scheme
A_{0}
over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
with supersingular reduction,
equipped with an embedding
\iota_{0}^{\diamond}:M_{2}(O_{d})\hookrightarrow\End(A_{0})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{(q)}

and a polarization
\lambda_{0}:A_{0}\to A_{0}^{\vee}

such that

\iota_{0}^{\diamond}(\alpha)^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{0}\circ\iota_{0}^{\diamond}(\alpha^{\ast}).

We choose our
q
-uniformization datum for
V_{D}
in (
7.2.1
) to be of the form
(\ast,A_{0},\iota_{0}^{\diamond}\circ\beta,\lambda_{0},i_{D},i_{D/q})
.
With notation as in Definition
1.4.4
, we also obtain the PEL datum
\mathcal{D}^{\diamond}=(M_{2}(B_{d}),\dagger,H,\psi)
, with self-dual
q
-integral refinement
\mathscr{D}^{\diamond}=(M_{2}(O_{d}),\dagger,\Lambda,\psi)
.

(2)

We observe that, following Remark
1.4.5
(
2
), our choice of
q
-adic uniformization datum defines an orthogonal decomposition

V_{D}\xrightarrow[\sim]{i_{D}}\End(H,B_{D})^{\dagger=1,\tr=0}=V_{\star}\oplus V_{d}^{\diamond}

where

V_{d}^{\diamond}\coloneqq\End(H,M_{2}(B_{d}))^{\dagger=1,\tr=0}.

(3)

Now fix an element

g^{q}=\prod_{\ell\neq q}g_{\ell}\in\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}^{q}_{f}).

Let

K^{\diamond}_{\ell}\coloneqq g_{\ell}K_{\ell}g_{\ell}^{-1}\cap\spin(V_{d}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})

for each
\ell\neq q
; we define

K^{q\diamond}=\prod_{\ell\neq q}K^{\diamond}_{\ell},

a neat compact open subgroup by
[
90
, §0.1]
. We let
\bm{Z}(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})
be the
\mathbb{Z}_{(q)}
-scheme representing the moduli functor
\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}_{K^{q\diamond}}
associated to
\mathscr{D}^{\diamond}
at level
K^{q\diamond}
, with special fiber
Z(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
.

Remark 7.6.5
.

Note that (by Corollary
1.3.4
combined with Proposition
1.2.4
),
\bm{Z}(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})
is the usual integral model of the quaternionic Shimura curve associated to
B_{d}
at level
K^{q\diamond}
, with maximal compact level structure at
q
.

7.6.6.

We have the obvious forgetful finite morphism

(7.22)

j:\bm{Z}(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})\rightarrow X

defined on the moduli problems by
(A,\iota^{\diamond},\lambda,\eta^{q})\mapsto(A,\iota^{\diamond}\circ\beta,\lambda,g^{q}\cdot\eta^{q}).

Lemma 7.6.7
.

The generic fiber of (
7.22
)
coincides with the special cycle

Z(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}}\to\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}}(V_{D}).

Proof.

See the proof of
[
57
, Proposition 2.5]
.
∎

Notation 7.6.8
.

We now consider the special fiber
Z(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
.

(1)

Similarly to Construction
7.6.4
(
2
), we obtain a natural orthogonal decomposition

V_{D/q}\xrightarrow[\sim]{i_{D/q}}\End^{0}(\overline{A}_{0},B_{D})^{\dagger=1,\trace=0}=V_{\star}\oplus V_{d/q}^{\diamond},

with

V_{d/q}^{\diamond}\coloneqq\End^{0}(\overline{A}_{0},M_{2}(B_{d}))^{\dagger=1,\trace=0}

a three-dimensional quadratic space whose Hasse invariant coincides with that of
B_{d/q}
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{N}^{\diamond}
be the Rapoport-Zink space parametrizing framed, polarized deformations of the
q
-divisible group
A_{0}[q^{\infty}]
, with action of
M_{2}(O_{d}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q})
. (The details are analogous to Definition
6.1.2
). Let
j^{\loc}:\mathcal{N}^{\diamond}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}
be the natural closed immersion induced by
\beta\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q}:O_{D}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q}\hookrightarrow M_{2}(O_{d}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q})
, and let

\mathcal{N}^{\diamond}=\sqcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{N}^{\diamond}(i)

be the decomposition defined analogously to (
6.1
), or equivalently defined by
\mathcal{N}^{\diamond}(i)=\mathcal{N}^{\diamond}\cap\mathcal{N}(i).

Remark 7.6.9
.

By the local analogue of Corollary
1.3.4
,
\mathcal{N}^{\diamond}
is isomorphic to the formal scheme considered in
[
12
, §I.3]
, which is the one encountered in the well-known Čerednik-Drinfeld uniformization of quaternionic Shimura curves.

Lemma 7.6.10
.

The image of
\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}\coloneqq\mathcal{N}^{\diamond}_{{\operatorname{red}}}(0)

under
j^{\operatorname{loc}}

is contained in
\mathcal{M}_{\left\{02\right\}}

(cf. Theorem

6.2.4
).

Proof.

It suffices to consider
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-valued points, so suppose given
x=(X_{x},\iota_{x},\lambda_{x},\rho_{x})\in\mathcal{M}^{\diamond}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, with
\iota:M_{2}(O_{d})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q}\hookrightarrow\End(X_{x})

an embedding compatible with involutions.
By (
6.2.5
), it suffices to show the Dieudonné module
M

of
X_{x}

satisfies
M+\tau M=\tau M+\tau^{2}M

for
\tau=(\iota\circ\beta(\Pi_{D}))\cdot V^{-1}.

(Since
M

is self-dual,
M\cap\tau M

is then
\tau
-stable as well.)
If
\tau^{\prime}=\iota\begin{pmatrix}\Pi_{d}&0\\
0&\Pi_{d}\end{pmatrix}\cdot V^{-1}
, then we have

(\tau^{\prime})^{n}M=\tau^{n}M

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}

by Proposition
7.6.3
(3). So it suffices to show
M+\tau^{\prime}M

is
\tau^{\prime}
-stable. The action of

M_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{q})\subset M_{2}(O_{d})\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q}

on
M

defines a decomposition
M=M_{0}\oplus M_{0}
, where
M_{0}

inherits an action
\iota_{0}

of
O_{d}
. We can further decompose
M_{0}=M_{0,\bullet}\oplus M_{0,\circ}
according to the eigenvalues of the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{q^{2}}\subset O_{d}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q}
. Then
M_{0,\bullet}
and
M_{0,\circ}
are both free of rank two over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
and stable under
\tau_{0}\coloneqq\iota_{0}(\Pi_{d})V^{-1}
. Hence
M_{0}+\tau_{0}M_{0}
is
\tau_{0}
-stable by
[
94
, Proposition 2.17]
; so
M+\tau^{\prime}M

is
\tau^{\prime}
-stable, as desired.
∎

Definition 7.6.11
.

We define a subset
\mathscr{L}^{\diamond}\subset\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}
by

j^{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathcal{N}^{\diamond}_{{\operatorname{red}}})=\bigcup_{(\Lambda_{+},\Lambda_{-})\in\mathscr{L}^{\diamond}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda_{+})\cap\mathcal{M}_{-}(\Lambda_{-})\right),

which makes sense
by Lemma
7.6.10
.

By
[
12
, Proposition III.2]
(and Remark
7.6.5
), the special fiber
Z(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
is purely supersingular. Hence by Rapoport-Zink uniformization, we have:

Proposition 7.6.12
.

The special fiber
Z(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

is isomorphic to

\spin(V_{d/q}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\spin(V_{d/q}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{A}^{q}_{f})\times\mathcal{N}^{\diamond}_{{\operatorname{red}}}/K^{q\diamond}_{g^{q}},

in such a way that the special fiber of
j:\bm{Z}(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})\to X

is given by
(h^{q},x)\mapsto(h^{q}g^{q},j^{\operatorname{loc}}(x))

under the uniformization of Theorem

7.2.4
.

Notation 7.6.13
.

Let

(7.23)

\widetilde{j}:\widetilde{Z}(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\to\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

be the strict transform of

Z(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\to X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},

and

(7.24)

\left[\widetilde{Z}(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\right]\in H^{4}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(\widetilde{X}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))

its algebraic cycle class.

Corollary 7.6.14
.

We have

\nabla\left[\widetilde{Z}(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\right]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[(h^{q},\Lambda)]\in\\
\spin(V_{d/q}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\spin(V^{\diamond}_{d/q})(\mathbb{A}^{q}_{f})\times\mathscr{L}/K^{q\diamond}_{g^{q}}\end{subarray}}m(\Lambda)[(h^{q}g^{q},\Lambda)],

where

m(\Lambda)=\sum_{y\in\mathscr{L}^{\diamond}}\operatorname{mult}(\Lambda,-4q\delta_{+}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(y)-4q\delta_{-}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(y)+(\delta_{+}+\delta_{-})\circ\theta^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}_{\operatorname{Sie}}(y)).

Proof.

This is immediate from Lemma
7.5.5
, Proposition
7.6.12
, and
Definition
7.6.11
.
∎

To compute the multiplicities
m(\Lambda)

in the Corollary
7.6.14
above, it is better to work with lattices in the split 5-dimensional quadratic space
V\coloneqq V_{D/q}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}

rather than the symplectic space
W

(see (
6.5.1
)).

Definition 7.6.15
.

(1)

A
vertex lattice
L\subset V

is a
\mathbb{Z}_{q}
-lattice satisfying

L^{\vee}\supset L\supset qL^{\vee}.

For
0\leq i\leq 2,

set

\VL(2i)\coloneqq\left\{\text{vertex lattices }L\subset V\,:\,\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}L^{\vee}/L=2i\right\}.

(The analogous sets
\VL(2i+1)

are all empty.)

(2)

For any

\mathbb{Z}_{q}
-lattice
\Lambda\subset W
, we define

L_{\Lambda}=\End_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}}(\Lambda)\cap V,

which makes sense because
V=\End(W)^{\dagger=1,\trace=0}.

Lemma 7.6.16
.

The map
\Lambda\mapsto L_{\Lambda}

induces an isomorphism

\mathscr{L}/q^{\mathbb{Z}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\VL(0)

and a surjection

\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}/q^{\mathbb{Z}}\twoheadrightarrow\VL(4).

Moreover, the map

(\Lambda_{+},\Lambda_{-})\mapsto L_{\Lambda_{+}}\cap L_{\Lambda_{-}}

induces a surjection

\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}/q^{\mathbb{Z}}\twoheadrightarrow\VL(2).

Proof.

For each
0\leq i\leq 2,
\VL(2i)

is a homogeneous space for
\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
. Since the maps

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}/q^{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\VL(0)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}/q^{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\VL(2)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}/q^{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\VL(4)

are all
\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-equivariant, they are automatically surjective. Finally, for the injectivity of the first map, it suffices to note that the stabilizers coincide, i.e. the hyperspecial subgroup of
\SO(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

is the image of a hyperspecial subgroup of
\spin(V)(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
.
∎

Lemma 7.6.17
.

The projections of Lemma
7.6.16
fit into the following commutative diagrams:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\mathscr{L}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\VL(4)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\VL(0)]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{+}+\delta_{-}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\mathscr{L}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\VL(2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\VL(0)]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{+}^{\operatorname{Sie}}+\delta_{-}^{\operatorname{Sie}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\delta}^{\operatorname{Sie}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{L}_{\operatorname{Sie}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\VL(2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\VL(4)]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\operatorname{Sie}}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\theta}_{\operatorname{Sie}}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}

Here, the bottom maps are:

\displaystyle\overline{\delta}(L_{4})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{0}\in\VL(0)\\
L_{0}\supset L_{4}\end{subarray}}[L_{0}],

\displaystyle\text{for }L_{4}\in\VL(4),

\displaystyle\overline{\delta}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{0}\in\VL(0)\\
L_{0}\supset L_{2}\end{subarray}}[L_{0}],

\displaystyle\text{for }L_{2}\in\VL(2),

\displaystyle\overline{\theta}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}_{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{4}\in\VL(4)\\
L_{4}\subset L_{2}\end{subarray}}[L_{4}],

\displaystyle\text{for }L_{2}\in\VL(2).

Proof.

Given
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\in\mathscr{L}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}

with

q^{n+1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}^{\vee}\subset_{2}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{2}q^{n}\Lambda^{\vee}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}

and
\Lambda\in\mathscr{L}

with
\Lambda\in\delta_{+}(\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})
, we first claim
L_{\Lambda}\supset L_{\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}.

Indeed, for any
\ell\in L_{\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}
,
\ell

induces a self-adjoint, trace-zero endomorphism of the two-dimensional symplectic space
q^{n}\Lambda^{\vee}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}/\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}
; hence
\ell(q^{n}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}^{\vee})\subset\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}
. Since
\Lambda

fits into a chain

\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset_{1}\Lambda=q^{n}\Lambda^{\vee}\subset_{1}q^{n}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}^{\vee},

we conclude

\ell(\Lambda)\subset\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}\subset\Lambda,

so
\ell\in L_{\Lambda}
.
Similarly, we see that, for
\Lambda\in\mathscr{L}

appearing in
\delta_{-}(\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})
, we have
L_{\Lambda}\supset L_{\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}
. Now to prove the first diagram commutes, it suffices to show

(7.25)

\degree\overline{\delta}(L_{\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}})=\degree(\delta_{+}(\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}))+\degree(\delta_{-}(\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}})).

The left-hand side is the number of lattices
L\in\VL(0)

containing
L_{\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}
; such lattices are in bijection with isotropic planes in the split 4-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-quadratic space
L^{\vee}_{\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}/L_{\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}}
, hence there are
2(q+1)

of them. Meanwhile
\degree(\delta_{\pm}(\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}))=q+1,

since lattices
\Lambda\supset_{1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}

(resp.
\Lambda\subset_{1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}
)
are in bijection with lines in the 2-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-symplectic space
q^{n}\Lambda^{\vee}_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}/\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}

(resp.
\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}/q^{n+1}\Lambda_{{\operatorname{Pa}}}^{\vee}
). This proves (
7.25
), hence the commutativity of the first diagram; and the rest are similar.
∎

Notation 7.6.18
.

(1)

For convenience, we now abbreviate

V_{\star,q}=V_{\star}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}.

Let
L_{\star}\subset V_{\star,q}

be the
\mathbb{Z}_{q}
-lattice spanned by
\left\{e_{1}^{\star},qe_{2}^{\star}\right\}
, and let
L^{(0)}_{\star}=\Span_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}}\left\{q^{-1}e_{1}^{T},qe_{2}^{T}\right\}

and
L^{(1)}_{\star}=\Span_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}}\left\{e_{1}^{T},e_{2}^{T}\right\}

be the two self-dual lattices in
V_{\star,q}

containing
L_{\star}
.

(2)

We write
\VL(2)^{\diamond}\subset\VL(2)

for the subset consisting of lattices of the form

L_{2}=L_{\star}\oplus L_{\diamond},

where

L_{\diamond}\subset V_{\diamond}\coloneqq V_{d/q}^{\diamond}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}

is self-dual.

Lemma 7.6.19
.

\mathscr{L}^{\diamond}

consists of two
\spin(V^{\diamond}_{d/q})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-orbits; if
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathscr{L}^{\diamond}

is one orbit, then the map of Lemma
7.6.16
induces an isomorphism

\mathcal{O}/q^{\mathbb{Z}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\VL(2)^{\diamond}.

Proof.

By
[
12
, Théorème 9.3]
and Remark
7.6.5
,
\mathscr{L}^{\diamond}

consists of two
\spin(V_{d/q}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-orbits, and the stabilizer of any point in
\mathscr{L}^{\diamond}

is a hyperspecial subgroup. On the other hand, for any point in
\mathscr{L}^{\diamond}

with image
L_{2}\in\VL(2)
, we know

L_{2}\cap V_{\star,q}\supset L_{\star}

by Proposition
7.6.3
(4) and the definition of the strata
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda_{+})

and
\mathcal{M}_{-}(\Lambda_{-}).

Since the stabilizer of
L_{2}

in
\SO(V_{d/q}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

is hyperspecial, this forces

L_{2}=L_{\star}\oplus L_{\diamond},

for some
L_{\diamond}\subset V_{\diamond}

self-dual. Hence the image of either orbit
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathscr{L}^{\diamond}

in
\VL(2)

is contained in
\VL(2)^{\diamond}
; since
\VL(2)^{\diamond}

is a single orbit, we have a surjection
\mathcal{O}/q^{\mathbb{Z}}\twoheadrightarrow\VL(2)^{\diamond}.

Finally, we see that this map is an isomorphism because the stabilizers coincide.
∎

Lemma 7.6.20
.

The multiplicity
m(\Lambda)

in Corollary
7.6.14
depends only on
L_{\Lambda}\in\VL(0)
, and is given by:

m(\Lambda)=\begin{cases}4,&\text{if }L_{\Lambda}\cap V_{\star,q}=L_{\star},\\
4-4q,&\text{if }L_{\Lambda}\cap V_{\star,q}=L_{\star}^{(0)}\text{ or }L^{(1)}_{\star},\\
0,&\text{else}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{O}

be one of the two
\spin(V_{d/q}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-orbits in
\mathscr{L}^{\diamond}
; we will compute

m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{O}}\operatorname{mult}\left(\Lambda,-4q\delta_{+}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(y)-4q\delta_{-}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(y)+(\delta_{+}+\delta_{-})\circ\theta_{\operatorname{Sie}}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}(y)\right).

Let
m(\Lambda,y)

be the summand above, so that
m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{O}}m(\Lambda,y)
. Fix some
y\in\mathcal{O}

corresponding to
L_{y}\in\VL(2)
.
Then

m(\Lambda,q^{n}y)=m(q^{-n}\Lambda,y)

is nonzero for at most one
n
, so

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}m(\Lambda,q^{n}y)=\operatorname{mult}(L_{\Lambda},-4q\overline{\delta}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{y})+\overline{\delta}\circ\overline{\theta}_{\operatorname{Sie}}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}(L_{y}))

by Lemma
7.6.17
. Since
\mathcal{O}/q^{\mathbb{Z}}

maps isomorphically to
\VL(2)^{\diamond}

by Lemma
7.6.19
, we find

m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)=\sum_{L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}}\operatorname{mult}\left(L_{\Lambda},-4q\overline{\delta}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{2})+\overline{\delta}\circ\overline{\theta}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}_{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{2})\right).

Next, we calculate, for any
L_{2}\in\VL(2)
,

\displaystyle\overline{\delta}\circ\overline{\theta}^{{\operatorname{Pa}}}_{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{4}\in\VL(4)\\
L_{4}\subset L_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{0}\in\VL(0)\\
L_{0}\supset L_{4}\end{subarray}}[L_{0}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{0}\in\VL(0)\\
L_{0}\supset L_{2}\end{subarray}}\#\left\{L_{4}\in\VL(4)\,:\,L_{4}\subset L_{2}\right\}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{0}\in\VL(0)\\
L_{0}\cap L_{2}\in\VL(4)\end{subarray}}[L_{0}]

\displaystyle=(q+1)\overline{\delta}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{2})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{0}\in\VL(0)\\
L_{0}\cap L_{2}\in\VL(4)\end{subarray}}[L_{0}].

(The
q+1

choices of
L_{4}\subset L_{2}

correspond to flags

qL_{2}^{\vee}\subset qL_{4}^{\vee}\subset L_{4}\subset L_{2},

hence to complete isotropic flags in the split 3-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-quadratic space
L_{2}/qL_{2}^{\vee}
.)
Hence

(7.26)

m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)=\sum_{L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}}\operatorname{mult}\left(L_{\Lambda},(1-3q)\overline{\delta}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{2})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{0}\in\VL(0)\\
L_{0}\cap L_{2}\in\VL(4)\end{subarray}}[L_{0}]\right).

Now observe that, for any
L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}

and
L_{4}\in\VL(4)

with
L_{4}\subset L_{2}
, we have

L_{4}\supset qL_{4}^{\vee}\supset qL_{2}^{\vee}\supset L_{\star}.

Hence, if
L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}
, all
L_{0}

appearing in

(1-3q)\overline{\delta}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{2})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{0}\in\VL(0)\\
L_{0}\cap L_{2}\in\VL(4)\end{subarray}}[L_{0}]

have
L_{0}\cap V_{\star,q}\supset L_{\star}
.
In particular, if
m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)\neq 0
, then

L_{\Lambda}\cap V_{\star,q}=L_{\star}^{(0)},\,L_{\star}^{(1)},\,\text{or}\,L_{\star},

since these are the only lattices containing
L_{\star}

on which the pairing is
\mathbb{Z}_{q}
-valued.

Suppose first
L_{\Lambda}\cap V_{\star,q}=L_{\star}^{(i)}
, for
i=0

or 1. Then we may write

L_{\Lambda}=L_{\diamond}\oplus L_{\star}^{(i)}

with
L_{\diamond}\subset V_{\diamond}

self-dual, and

m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)=(1-3q)\#\left\{L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}\,:\,L_{\Lambda}\in\overline{\delta}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{2})\right\}+\#\left\{L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}\,:\,L_{2}\cap L_{\Lambda}\in\VL(4)\right\}.

Recall that all
L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}

are of the form
L_{\diamond}^{\prime}\oplus L_{\star}
, with
L_{\diamond}^{\prime}\subset V_{\diamond}

self-dual, and

\overline{\delta}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{\diamond}^{\prime}\oplus L_{\star})=\left[L_{\diamond}^{\prime}\oplus L_{\star}^{(0)}\right]+\left[L_{\diamond}^{\prime}\oplus L_{\star}^{(1)}\right].

Hence

\displaystyle m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle=(1-3q)+\#\left\{L^{\prime}_{\diamond}\subset V_{\diamond}\,\text{self-dual}\,:\,(L^{\prime}_{\diamond}\cap L_{\diamond})\oplus L_{\star}\in\VL(4)\right\}

\displaystyle=(1-3q)+(q+1)

\displaystyle=2-2q;

the
(q+1)

choices of
L_{\diamond}^{\prime}

correspond bijectively to the isotropic lines in the 3-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-space
L_{\diamond}/qL_{\diamond}
.

Now suppose
L_{\Lambda}\cap V_{\star,q}=L_{\star}
. Then
L_{\Lambda}

does not appear in
\overline{\delta}^{\operatorname{Sie}}(L_{2})
for any
L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}
, so by (
7.26
), we have

m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)=\#\left\{L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}\,:\,L_{\Lambda}\cap L_{2}\in\VL(4)\right\}.

Recall that any
L_{4}

contained in
L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}

satisfies
qL_{4}^{\vee}\supset L_{\star}
; we first show that there is a unique such
L_{4}

contained in
L_{\Lambda}
.
Indeed, any such
L_{4}

fits in a chain

qL_{\Lambda}\subset_{2}qL_{4}^{\vee}\subset_{1}L_{4}\subset_{2}L_{\Lambda},

and so we must have

qL_{4}^{\vee}=L_{\star}+qL_{\Lambda},

which determines
L_{4}
. Now, for this
L_{4}
, we claim there are exactly two
L_{2}\in\VL(2)^{\diamond}

with
L_{2}\supset L_{4}
. Such an
L_{2}

fits into a chain

L_{4}\subset_{1}L_{2}\subset_{2}L_{2}^{\vee}\subset_{1}L_{4}^{\vee}.

Since
L_{\star}\subset qL_{4}^{\vee}
, we have

\frac{1}{q}L_{\star}\subset L_{4}^{\vee}.

Thus

L_{4}^{\vee}/L_{4}\cong\frac{1}{q}L_{\star}/L_{\star}\oplus\mathbb{H},

with
\mathbb{H}

a hyperbolic plane over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. For
L_{2}

to lie in
\VL(2)^{\diamond}

is equivalent to
\frac{1}{q}L_{\star}\subset L_{2}^{\vee}
, so choices of
L_{2}

correspond to choices of isotropic lines in
L_{4}^{\vee}/L_{4}

orthogonal to
\frac{1}{q}L_{\star}/L_{\star}
; these are isotropic lines in
\mathbb{H}
, so there are exactly two. Hence
m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)=2

if
L_{\Lambda}\cap V_{\star,q}=L_{\star}.

We have now shown

m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)=\begin{cases}2,&\text{if}\,L_{\Lambda}\cap V_{\star,q}=L_{\star},\\
2-2q,&\text{if}\,L_{\Lambda}\cap V_{\star,q}=L_{\star}^{(0)}\text{or}\,L_{\star}^{(1)},\\
0,&\text{else.}\end{cases}

Since
m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda)

is evidently independent of the choice of orbit
\mathcal{O}
, we have

m(\Lambda)=2m_{\mathcal{O}}(\Lambda),

which completes the proof.∎

Combining Lemma
7.6.20
and Corollary
7.6.14
gives the following simple formula.

Corollary 7.6.21
.

We have

\begin{split}&\nabla\left[{\widetilde{Z}}(g^{q},V_{\star},V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\right]=4Z(g^{q}g_{q}^{\star},V_{\star},V_{D/q})_{K^{q}K_{q}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+4(1-q)\left(Z(g^{q}g^{(0)},V_{\star},V_{D/q})_{K^{q}K_{q}})+Z(g^{q}g^{(1)},V_{\star},V_{D/q})_{K^{q}K_{q}}\right)\in O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})\right],\end{split}

where
g_{q}^{\star}
,
g_{q}^{(0)}
,
g_{q}^{(1)}\in\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})

represent the cosets in

\spin(V_{d/q}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})\backslash\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})/K_{q}

corresponding to the lattices
L_{\star}
,
L_{\star}^{(0)}
,
L_{\star}^{(1)}
under Proposition
3.1.8
.

7.7.
Interpretation in terms of test functions

7.7.1.

We now define a specific test function
\varphi_{q}^{{\operatorname{tot}}}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D/q}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q},\mathbb{Z})

as follows. First, define a subset
X\subset V^{2}_{D/q}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}

by

(7.27)

X=\left\{(x,y)\in V_{D/q}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}\,|\,x\cdot x\in q\mathbb{Z}_{q},\,y\cdot y\in q\mathbb{Z}_{q},\,x\cdot y\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{\times}\right\}.

Let
L\subset V_{D/q}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}

be a self-dual lattice, and let
\varphi_{q}^{(0)}
,
\varphi_{q}^{(1)}
,
\varphi_{q}^{\star}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D/q}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q},\mathbb{Z})

be indicator functions of the following compact open subsets of
V_{D/q}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q}
:

\displaystyle X^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\left\{(x,y)\in X\,:\,x,y\in L-qL\right\}

\displaystyle X^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\left\{(x,y)\in X\,:\,x\in qL-q^{2}L,\,y\in q^{-1}L-L\right\}

\displaystyle X^{\star}

\displaystyle=\left\{(x,y)\in X\,:\,x\in L-qL,\,y\in q^{-1}L-L\right\}.

Let

\varphi_{q}^{{\operatorname{tot}}}=\varphi_{q}^{\star}+(1-q)\left(\varphi_{q}^{(0)}+\varphi_{q}^{(1)}\right).

Corollary
7.6.21
can then be reformulated as follows:

Theorem 7.7.2
.

Let
K=\prod K_{v}\subset\spin(V_{D/q})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be neat with
K_{q}
hyperspecial, and let

\varphi^{q}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D/q}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q},O)

be a
K^{q}
-invariant Schwartz function. Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{S}_{O}
be a generic, non-Eisenstein, and weakly
q
-generic maximal ideal.

Then, for all
T\in\symmetric^{2}(\mathbb{Q})_{\geq 0}
,
there exists a choice of uniformization datum for
V_{D}

and a test function
\varphi_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q},\mathbb{Z})^{K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}}

such that

\nabla\circ\zeta\circ\res_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}\circ\;\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}\left(Z(T,\varphi^{q}\otimes\varphi_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}})_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}}\right)=4Z(T,\varphi^{q}\otimes\varphi_{q}^{\operatorname{tot}})_{K^{q}K_{q}}\in\frac{O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})(V_{Q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}}{(\langle q\rangle^{-1}-1,\mathtt{T}_{q}^{\operatorname{lr}})}.

Remark 7.7.3
.

(1)

The map
\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}
is defined as in (
4.4.2
), and we are using Theorem
7.1.11
to apply
\zeta
on the left-hand side of the identity.

(2)

The choice of uniformization datum intervenes in two points in the displayed equation: first in the definition of
\nabla
, and second in the isomorphism
V_{D/q}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q}\cong V_{D}\otimes\mathbb{A}^{q}_{f}
from Remark
1.4.5
(
2
), which we are using to view
\varphi^{q}
as a test function in
\mathcal{S}(V_{D}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q},O)
and
K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}
as a compact open subgroup of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

(3)

Without any great difficulty,
\varphi_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}

can be chosen not to depend on
T
. Since this is not used in the proofs of the main results, we omit the details.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume
T

is of the form considered in Notation
7.6.1
; otherwise
Z(T,\varphi^{q}\otimes\varphi_{q}^{{\operatorname{tot}}})_{K}=0,

so
\varphi_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}=0

satisfies the conclusion of the theorem. We fix the uniformization datum as in Construction
7.6.4
(
1
);
in particular, we are identifying
V_{\star}

with a subspace of
V_{D}
, so we may choose
\varphi_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{q},\mathbb{Z})

such that
\varphi_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}|_{\Omega_{T,V_{D}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q})}

is the indicator function of the coset
K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}\cdot(e_{1}^{\star},e_{2}^{\star}).

For any
\varphi^{q}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D/q}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q},O)
, write

{\operatorname{supp}}(\varphi^{q})\cap\Omega_{T,V_{D/q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q})=\sqcup\spin(V_{D/q}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q})g_{i}^{q}K^{q}.

Then we have

Z(T,\varphi^{q}\otimes\varphi_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}})_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}}=\sum_{i}Z(g^{q}_{i},V_{\star},V_{D})_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}}\varphi^{q}((g_{i}^{q})^{-1}e_{1}^{\star},(g_{i}^{q})^{-1}e_{2}^{\star}).

Now, by Theorem
7.1.11
, we conclude that

\nabla\circ\zeta\circ\res_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}\circ\;\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}\left(Z(T,\varphi^{q}\otimes\varphi_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}})_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}}\right)\in\frac{O\left[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}}(V_{D/q})(V_{Q})\right]_{\mathfrak{m}}}{(\langle q\rangle^{-1}-1,\mathtt{T}_{q}^{\operatorname{lr}})}

coincides with

\sum_{i}\varphi^{q}((g_{i}^{q})^{-1}e_{1}^{\star},(g_{i}^{q})^{-1}e_{2}^{\star})\cdot\nabla\left[\widetilde{Z}(g^{q}_{i},V_{\star},V_{D})_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{ram}}}\right].

Then the theorem follows from Corollary
7.6.21
and the construction of
\varphi_{q}^{\operatorname{tot}}
.
∎

8.
First explicit reciprocity law

For this section, let
\pi
,
S
, and
E_{0}
be as in Notation
4.0.1
, fix a
prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
satisfying Assumption
4.1.1
, and put
\mathfrak{m}\coloneqq\mathfrak{m}_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
as usual. Our goal for this section is to combine Theorem
7.7.2
with Corollary
5.6.3
to prove Theorems
8.5.1
and
8.5.2
below. First we make some deformation-theoretic preparations in §
8.1
-§
8.3
; then we check the criterion from Corollary
5.6.3
in §
8.4
; and we complete the proofs in §
8.5
.

8.1.
Typic modules

The following definition is a generalization of
[
99
, Definition 5.2]
.

Definition 8.1.1
.

Let
G
be a group,
R
a Noetherian local ring with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
, and
\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m}
a finite collection of representations
\sigma_{i}:G\to\GL_{n_{i}}(R)
such that the residual representations
\overline{\sigma}_{i}\coloneqq\sigma\otimes_{R}R/\mathfrak{m}_{R}
are distinct and absolutely irreducible for each
i=1,\cdots,m
.

An
R[G]
-module
M
is called
\sigma_{i}
-typic if it is isomorphic to
\sigma_{i}\otimes_{R}M_{0}
for an
R
-module
M_{0}
, and
(\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m})
-typic if it is isomorphic to a direct sum
\oplus M_{i}
with each
M_{i}

\sigma_{i}
-typic.

Proposition 8.1.2
.

With notation as in Definition
8.1.1
, let
N\subset M
be an inclusion of
R[G]
-modules.

(1)

If
M
is
\sigma_{i}
-typic for some
i\in\left\{1,\cdots,m\right\}
, then
N
is
\sigma_{i}
-typic.

(2)

If
M
is
(\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m})
-typic, then
N
is
(\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m})
-typic.

Proof.

Part (
1
) is proved in
[
99
, Proposition 5.4]
. For (
2
), as in
loc. cit.
we may assume without loss of generality that
M
and
N
are finitely generated. Let
M=\oplus M_{i}
be the decomposition of
M
into
\sigma_{i}
-typic parts, and let
\pi_{i}:M\to M_{i}
be the projection map for each
i=1,\cdots,m
. Without loss of generality, we may assume
\pi_{i}(N)=M_{i}
. We claim that the natural injection

\oplus\pi_{i}:N\hookrightarrow\oplus M_{i}

is an isomorphism, i.e.

\oplus\pi_{i}
is surjective. Let
\overline{N}\coloneqq N\otimes_{R}R/\mathfrak{m}_{R}
and
\overline{M}_{i}\coloneqq M_{i}\otimes_{R}R/\mathfrak{m}_{R}
; by Nakayama’s lemma, it suffices to show the induced map

\oplus\overline{\pi}_{i}:\overline{N}\to\oplus\overline{M}_{i}

is surjective. Because the residual representations
\overline{\sigma}_{i}
are all distinct, any
R[G]
-stable submodule of
\oplus\overline{M}_{i}
is a direct sum
\oplus\overline{M}_{i}^{\prime}
for some
\overline{M}_{i}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}_{i}
. So if
\oplus\overline{\pi}_{i}
is not surjective, then
\overline{\pi}_{i}
is not surjective for some
i=1,\cdots,m
; but this contradicts our assumption that
\pi_{i}(N)=M_{i}
for all
i
, so
\oplus\pi_{i}
is surjective, which shows (
2
).
∎

8.2.
Deformation theory: non-endoscopic case

We assume for this subsection that
\pi
is not endoscopic. We will apply the results and notations of Appendix
B
to
\rho_{\pi}=\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
, which we view as valued in
\GSP_{4}(O)
via Remark
4.1.4
. First note:

Lemma 8.2.1
.

The representation
\rho_{\pi}

satisfies Assumptions
B.1.3
and
B.1.5
from Appendix
B
.

Proof.

By Assumption
4.1.1
,
\overline{\rho}_{\pi}

is absolutely irreducible, so
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q},\ad^{0}\overline{\rho}_{\pi})=0.

Also,
\overline{\rho}_{\pi}\not\cong\overline{\rho}_{\pi}(1)

by considering the similitude characters (since
p>3
). So, again using the absolute irreducibility,

H^{0}(\mathbb{Q},\ad^{0}\overline{\rho}_{\pi}(1))\subset\Home_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\overline{T}_{\pi},\overline{T}_{\pi}(1))=0

as well. This shows Assumption
B.1.3
(
1
). Assumptions
B.1.3
(
2
,
3
) are clear from Theorem
2.2.10
.
We now consider Assumption
B.1.5
. By Lemma
B.1.6
it suffices to show

H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},\WD(\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi}))=0
for all non-archimedean
v
.
But

H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},\WD(\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi}))\subset\Hom_{\WD_{v}}(\WD(V_{\pi}),\WD(V_{\pi}(1))),

with
\WD_{v}
the local Weil-Deligne group,
which vanishes by purity (Theorem
2.2.10
(
1
)).
∎

8.2.2.

Suppose
q
is an admissible prime, and let
\mathcal{D}_{q}
and
R_{q}
be as in Notation
B.1.4
.
For any
A\in\CNL_{O}
and
\rho_{A}\in\mathcal{D}_{q}(A)
, let
M_{A}

be the free, rank-four
A
-module with
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}

action determined by
\rho_{A}
. Then
by
[
66
, Lemma 3.21]
,

M_{A}

admits a
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}
-stable decomposition

(8.1)

M_{A}=M_{0}\oplus M_{1},

where:

•

Each of
M_{0}

and
M_{1}

is free of rank two over
A
.

•

M_{0}\otimes_{A}k

has
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}
eigenvalues
1

and
q
.

•

M_{1}\otimes_{A}k

has generalized
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}

eigenvalues
a

and
q/a
, with
a\neq 1,q
.

(Here
k=O/\varpi
, and
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}
is any lift of Frobenius.)

Definition 8.2.3
.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{q}^{\operatorname{ord}}\subset\mathcal{D}_{q}

be the subfunctor of lifts
\rho_{A}

such that

\debt(\rho_{A}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{q})-T|M_{0})=(1-T)(q-T).

Lemma 8.2.4
.

The functor
\mathcal{D}_{q}^{\operatorname{ord}}

is represented by a formally smooth quotient
R_{q}^{\operatorname{ord}}

of
R_{q}
, of relative dimension 10 over
O
.

Proof.

This follows the same argument as
[
66
, Proposition 3.35]
(cf. also
[
122
, Proposition 3.8]
).
∎

8.2.5.

In light of Lemma
8.2.4
, we take the admissible primes in Notation
B.2.4
to be the ones of Definition
4.2.1
, and the notion of
R_{q}^{\operatorname{ord}}

to be the one from Lemma
8.2.4
; the definitions of
n
-admissible primes in Definition
4.2.1
and Definition
B.2.5
(
3
) then coincide.

Notation 8.2.6
.

(1)

Let
Q\geq 1

be admissible, and denote by
\widetilde{R}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}

the global
\GSP_{4}
-valued deformation ring of
\overline{\rho}_{\pi}

as a representation of
G_{\mathbb{Q},S\cup\div(Qp)}
, with fixed similitude character
\chi_{p}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
.
Let
R^{Q}
and
R_{Q}
be the quotients of
\widetilde{R}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}
defined in Notation
B.4.4
(identifying
Q
with
\div(Q)
for notational convenience).

(2)

Let

\rho_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\GSP_{4}(R_{Q})

be a framing of the universal deformation, and let
M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}
be the free
R_{Q}
-module of rank four with
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-action defined by
\rho_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}.

8.2.7.

Let
\pr_{p}:I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
be the maximal pro-
p
quotient.

Lemma 8.2.8
.

Suppose
Q
is admissible and
q|Q
is a prime. In the decomposition
M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}}=M_{0}\oplus M_{1}
of (
8.1
),
M_{1}
is unramified. Moreover, there exists a basis of
M_{0}
and an element
t_{q}\in R_{Q}
such that

\rho_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}|_{M_{0}}=\begin{pmatrix}\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}&\ast\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},

and

\rho_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}(g)|_{M_{0}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&t_{q}\pr_{p}(g)\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\;\forall g\in I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}.

Proof.

Since
M_{0}
and
M_{1}
are
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}
-stable, this follows from
[
103
, Propositions 5.3, 5.5]
.
∎

Definition 8.2.9
.

Suppose
Q
and
q\nmid Q
are admissible. Then:

(1)

We set
P_{q}(T)=\det(\rho_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}(\Frob_{q})-T|M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}})\in R_{Q}[T].

(2)

We set
R_{Q,q}^{\operatorname{cong}}\coloneqq R_{Q}\otimes_{R^{Qq}}R_{Qq}.

Lemma 8.2.10
.

Suppose
Q\geq 1
is admissible, and
q\nmid Q
is an admissible prime. Then

R_{Q,q}^{\operatorname{cong}}=R_{Q}/(P_{q}(q))=R_{Qq}/(t_{q}).

Proof.

We have
R_{Q,q}^{\operatorname{cong}}=R_{Q}/(P_{q}(q))
because an unramified deformation of
\overline{\rho}_{\pi}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}}
is ordinary if and only if
q
is an eigenvalue of
\Frob_{q}
; on the other hand, it is clear from Lemma
8.2.8

that
R_{Q,q}^{\operatorname{cong}}=R_{Qq}/(t_{q})
.
∎

Lemma 8.2.11
.

Suppose
q

is
n
-admissible. Then:

(1)

H^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})+H^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n}).

(2)

The quotients

\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}{H^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}\>,\;\;\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}{H^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}

are both free of rank one over
O/\varpi^{n}
.

In particular,
q
is standard in the sense of Definition
B.4.7
.

Proof.

First note that

\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}{H^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}=\Home_{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1),\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})

is free of rank one over
O/\varpi^{n}

since
q

is
n
-admissible. On the other hand,
H^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})

clearly surjects onto this quotient by definition, which shows (
1
). Since
Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})

and
Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})

both contain all coboundaries, we see that

\frac{Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}{Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})\cap Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}=\frac{H^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}{H^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})\cap H^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}=\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}{H^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}

is free of rank 1 over
O/\varpi^{n}
.
On the other hand,
Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})

and
Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})

are both free of rank 10 over
O/\varpi^{n}

because
R_{q}^{\operatorname{ord}}
and the unramified local deformation ring
R_{q}^{\operatorname{unr}}
are formally smooth, so we conclude that
Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})\cap Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})

is free of rank 9 over
O/\varpi^{n}
. In particular,

\frac{Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}{Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})\cap Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}=\frac{H^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}{H^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})\cap H^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}=\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}{H^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,n})}

is also free of rank one over
O/\varpi^{n}
, as desired.
∎

8.2.12.

To state the next lemma, we establish some temporary notation.
Suppose
Q
is admissible and let
K
be an
S
-tidy level structure
K
for
\spin(V_{DQ})
(Definition
4.3.1
).
Abbreviate
\mathbb{T}\coloneqq\mathbb{T}^{S\cup{\operatorname{div}}(Q)}_{K,V_{DQ},O,\mathfrak{m}},
which may be the zero ring. Also fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
inducing
\mathfrak{p}
.
Then we write
\mathcal{T}
for the set of relevant automorphic representations
\Pi
of
\spin(V_{DQ})(\mathbb{A})

with
\Pi^{K}_{f}\neq 0
such that the Hecke action on
\iota^{-1}\Pi_{f}^{K}
factors through
\mathbb{T}
.
Recall from Corollary
2.7.8
that we have an embedding of
\mathbb{T}
-algebras

(8.2)

\mathbb{T}\hookrightarrow\bigoplus_{\Pi\in\mathcal{T}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\Pi),

where
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\Pi)
is
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with Hecke action through the eigenvalues on
\iota^{-1}\Pi_{f}^{K}
.
By the same argument as
[
13
, Theorem 7.9.4]
, there exists a Galois representation

\rho:G_{\mathbb{Q},S\cup\div(Qp)}\rightarrow\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{T})

such that, for each
\Pi\in\mathcal{T}
, the composite

G_{\mathbb{Q},S\cup\div(Qp)}\xrightarrow{\rho}\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{T})\rightarrow\GSP_{4}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\Pi))

is conjugate to the Galois representation
\rho_{\Pi,\iota}
from Remark
2.5.4
.

Lemma 8.2.13
.

With notation as in (
8.2.12
), we have:

(1)

The
composite

G_{\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{\rho}\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\nu}\mathbb{T}^{\times}

is given by
\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}
, and the corresponding
O
-algebra map
r_{\rho}:\widetilde{R}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\mathbb{T}
factors through
R_{Q}
.

(2)

Suppose
\sigma(DQ)
is even.
Then
H^{3}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}

is
\rho_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}
-typic when viewed as a
R_{Q}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-module via (
1
).

Proof.

We have
\nu\circ\rho=\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}
because
each
\Pi\in\mathcal{T}
has trivial similitude character by Lemma
4.3.2
. To complete the proof of (
1
), it suffices to show that all the composite maps

\widetilde{R}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{r_{\rho}}\mathbb{T}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\Pi)

factor through
R_{Q}
for
\Pi\in\mathcal{T}
. Because
K_{p}
is hyperspecial,
each
\rho_{\Pi,\iota}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is crystalline with Hodge-Tate weights
\left\{-1,0,1,2\right\}
by Theorem
2.2.10
(
2
). So it suffices to check that
\rho_{\Pi,\iota}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}}
is ordinary for all
q|Q
.
Indeed,
\rho_{\Pi,\iota}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}}
is tamely ramified because
\overline{\rho}_{\pi}
is unramified, but ramified by Corollary
2.5.3
(
2
) and Theorem
2.2.10
(
1
).
In particular, for any lift of
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}
,

\rho_{\Pi,\iota}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{q})

has a pair of eigenvalues of ratio
q
. It follows that
\rho_{\Pi,\iota}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}}
is ordinary, and this shows (
1
).

For (
2
), by Proposition
8.1.2
(
1
) and Theorem
2.7.5
(
2
) it suffices to show
H^{3}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}

is
\rho^{{\operatorname{univ}}}_{Q}
-typic. However, this is immediate from Corollary
2.7.7
and the construction of the map in (
1
); note that each
\Pi\in\mathcal{T}
is non-endoscopic because
\overline{\rho}_{\pi}
is absolutely irreducible.
∎

Definition 8.2.14
.

For any admissible
Q
with
\sigma(DQ)
even and any
S
-tidy level structure
K
for
\spin(V_{DQ})
, we define
H_{Q}(K)=\Hom_{R_{Q}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]}\left(M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)
.

Remark 8.2.15
.

In the context of Definition
8.2.14
:

(1)

By
[
99
, Proposition 5.3]
and Lemma
8.2.13
(
2
), we have

H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\simeq M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}\otimes_{R_{Q}}H_{Q}(K)

as
R_{Q}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-modules.

(2)

Under the isomorphism of (
1
), we have, for all
q|Q
:

H^{1}\left(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)\simeq H^{1}(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}})\otimes_{R_{Q}}H_{Q}(K).

Lemma 8.2.16
.

Suppose
Q
is admissible with
\sigma(DQ)
even, and
K
is an
S
-tidy level structure for
\spin(V_{DQ})
. Then for any
q|Q
, the
\varpi
-power-torsion of
H^{1}\left(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)
is contained in

H^{1}\left(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)^{\Frob_{q}=1}\simeq H^{1}(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}})^{\Frob_{q}=1}\otimes_{R_{Q}}H_{Q}(K)\simeq H_{Q}(K)/(t_{q}).

(The element
t_{q}\in R_{Q}
was defined in Lemma
8.2.8
.)

Proof.

By Lemma
8.2.8
, we see that

H^{1}(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},M^{{\operatorname{univ}}}_{Q})=R_{Q}/(t_{q})\oplus R_{Q}(-1)\oplus M_{1}(-1)

as
R_{Q}
-modules with
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}
-action,
with
M_{0}\oplus M_{1}
the decomposition of (
8.1
) for
M^{{\operatorname{univ}}}_{Q}
. In particular,
\Frob_{q}-1
acts invertibly on
R_{Q}(-1)\oplus M_{1}(-1)
.
Since
M_{1}

is free over
R_{Q}

and
H_{Q}(K)

is
\varpi
-torsion-free by Theorem
2.7.5
(
2
), it follows that the
\varpi
-torsion part of
H^{1}(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},M^{{\operatorname{univ}}}_{Q})\otimes_{R_{Q}}H_{Q}(K)

is contained in

\left(H^{1}(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}})\otimes_{R_{Q}}H_{Q}(K)\right)^{\Frob_{q}=1}=H^{1}(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},M^{{\operatorname{univ}}}_{Q})^{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}=1}\otimes H_{Q}(K)=H_{Q}(K)/(t_{q}),

as claimed.

∎

Lemma 8.2.17
.

Suppose
Q
is
n
-admissible with
\sigma(DQ)
even, and
K
is an
S
-tidy level structure for
\spin(V_{DQ})
. Then for all
q|Q
, all
\alpha_{0}\in\Hom_{R_{Q}}(H_{Q}(K)/(t_{q}),O/\varpi^{n})
, and all
z\in{\operatorname{SC}}^{2}_{K}(V_{DQ},O)
, we have

\alpha_{0}\circ\res_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}\circ\,\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}(z)\in\partial_{q}(\kappa_{n}(Q;K)).

Here
\res_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}\circ\,\partial{\operatorname{AJ}}_{\mathfrak{m}}(z)
is viewed as an element of
H^{1}\left(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)^{\Frob_{q}=1}
, which we identify with
H_{Q}(K)/(t_{q})
by Lemma
8.2.16
;
O/\varpi^{n}

is viewed as an
R_{Q}
-algebra via the map corresponding to
\rho_{\pi,n}
; and
\partial_{q}
was defined in Notation
4.2.9
.

Proof.

By construction,
T_{\pi,n}
and
M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}\otimes_{R_{Q}}O/\varpi^{n}
are isomorphic as
O[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-modules.
Given a map
\alpha_{0}:H_{Q}(K)/(t_{q})\rightarrow O/\varpi^{n}
, we obtain a corresponding map of Galois modules

\alpha={\operatorname{id}}\otimes\alpha_{0}:H^{3}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\Shimura_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\simeq M^{{\operatorname{univ}}}_{Q}\otimes_{R_{Q}}H_{Q}(K)\rightarrow M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}\otimes_{R_{Q}}O/\varpi^{n}\simeq T_{\pi,n}.

Let
\alpha_{\ast}:H^{1}\left(\mathbb{Q},H^{3}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\Shimura_{K}(V_{DQq})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi,n})

be the map induced by
\alpha
. For any
z\in{\operatorname{SC}}_{K}^{2}(V_{DQq},O),

\kappa_{n}(Q;K)

contains
\alpha_{\ast}(\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}})(z)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi,n})
. So the lemma follows from the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\left(\mathbb{Q},H^{3}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\Shimura_{{K}}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\left(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},H^{3}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\Shimura_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)^{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}=1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},M_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}})^{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}=1}\otimes_{R_{Q}}H_{Q}(K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{Q}(K)/(t_{q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi,n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},T_{\pi,n})^{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}=1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}},M^{{\operatorname{univ}}}_{Q})^{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}=1}\otimes_{R_{Q}}O/\varpi^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O/\varpi^{n}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

∎

8.3.
Deformation theory: endoscopic case

For this subsection, we assume
\pi

is endoscopic, associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})

of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}

with discrete series archimedean components of weights 2 and 4, in some order. In particular, we have
\rho_{\pi}=\rho_{\pi_{1}}\oplus\rho_{\pi_{2}}
.

Notation 8.3.1
.

(1)

Set
S_{\pi}\coloneqq T_{\pi_{1}}\otimes T_{\pi_{2}}(-1)

with the diagonal Galois action; for any
n\geq 1
, we also write
S_{\pi,n}\coloneqq S_{\pi}\otimes_{O}O/\varpi^{n}
.
Let

(8.3)

H^{1}_{{\operatorname{cris}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},S_{\pi,n})\subset H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},S_{\pi,n})

be the subspace of cocycles corresponding to extensions

0\rightarrow T_{\pi_{1},n}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\rightarrow T_{\pi_{2},n}\rightarrow 0

such that
\mathcal{E}

is torsion crystalline with Hodge-Tate weights in
[-1,2]
, cf. (
1.5.4
).

(2)

For any squarefree
Q\geq 1
with
p\nmid Q
, define

\Sel_{\mathcal{G}^{Q}}(\mathbb{Q},S_{\pi,n})\coloneqq\ker\left(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},S_{\pi,n})\to\prod_{\ell\not\in S\cup\div(Qp)}H^{1}(I_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}},S_{\pi,n})\times\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},S_{\pi,n})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{cris}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},S_{\pi,n})}\right).

When
Q=1
we drop it from the notation.

Lemma 8.3.2
.

Suppose
Q\geq 1
is admissible and let
n\geq 1

be any integer. Then
{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{G}^{Q}}(\mathbb{Q},S_{\pi,n})={\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{Q},S_{\pi,n}).

Proof.

Fix
q|Q
. Since
q

is admissible, the eigenvalues of
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{q}

on
S_{\pi,n}\otimes O/\varpi=\overline{T}_{\pi_{1}}\otimes\overline{T}_{\pi_{2}}(-1)

are of the form
\left\{\alpha,\alpha^{-1},q\alpha^{-1},\alpha q^{-1}\right\}

for some
\alpha\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}

with
\alpha\neq 1,q,q^{2},q^{-1}
.
Thus
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},S_{\pi,n})=H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},S_{\pi,n}(1))=0

for all
n
, so
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},S_{\pi,n})=0

for all
n

by the local Euler characteristic formula, and the lemma follows.
∎

Notation 8.3.3
.

For an integer
n\geq 1
,
recall the notion of pseudorepresentations of degree
n
from
[
117
, Definition 2.1.1]
. We use
op. cit.
as our basic reference for pseudodeformation theory, although some of the relevant results are due to Bellaïche and Chenevier
[
5
]
.

(1)

Let
G
be a group and
R
be a ring. If
\rho:G\to\GL_{n}(R)
is any representation, we write
D_{\rho}:G\to R
for the associated degree-
n
pseudorepresentation. A pseudorepresentation
D:G\to R
of degree
n
is called
reducible
if it is equal to
D_{\rho}
for a reducible representation
\rho:G\to\GL_{n}(R)
.

(2)

If
Q\geq 1
is squarefree, let

\widetilde{\operatorname{PsDef}}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}:\CNL_{O}\to\operatorname{Set}

be the functor defined by

A\mapsto\left\{\text{pseudorepresentations }D:G_{\mathbb{Q},S\cup\div(Qp)}\rightarrow A\text{ of degree }4\,:\,D\otimes_{A}k=D_{\overline{\rho}_{\pi}}\right\},

where
k=O/\varpi
and
\overline{\rho}_{\pi}
is viewed as valued in
\GL_{4}(k)
.
Let
{\operatorname{PsDef}}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}\subset\widetilde{\operatorname{PsDef}}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}
be the subfunctor of pseudorepresentations that are torsion crystalline at
p

with Hodge-Tate weights in
[-1,2]
in the sense of
[
117
, Definition 2.5.4]
.
By Theorems 2.2.5 and 2.5.5 of
op. cit.
,
\widetilde{\operatorname{PsDef}}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}
and
{\operatorname{PsDef}}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}
are representable by universal pseudodeformation rings
\widetilde{R}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}
and
R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}
, respectively.

(3)

Let
\widetilde{J}^{Q}_{\operatorname{red}}\subset\widetilde{R}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}
be the reducibility ideal defined in
[
117
, Proposition 4.2.2(2)]
, and
J^{Q}_{\operatorname{red}}\subset R_{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}
its image under the natural projection
\widetilde{R}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}\twoheadrightarrow R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Remark 8.3.4
.

(1)

The ideal
\widetilde{J}^{Q}_{\operatorname{red}}
is characterized by the property that, for any morphism
f:\widetilde{R}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}\to A
in
\CNL_{O}
corresponding to a pseudorepresentation
D:G_{\mathbb{Q},S\cup\div(Qp)}\to A
,
D
is reducible if and only if
f(\widetilde{J}^{Q}_{\operatorname{red}})=0
.

(2)

In what follows, we we will apply the results of
[
117
, §4]
. Although the discussion there is carried out for residual representations which are a sum of two characters, as noted in Remark 4.3.6 of
op. cit.
, the results also apply for any residual representation which is multiplicity free with exactly 2 irreducible constituents, which includes
\overline{\rho}_{\pi}
by Lemma
4.1.8
.

For all squarefree
Q\geq 1
, let
p_{\pi}^{Q}:R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}\to O
be the augmentation corresponding to
\rho_{\pi}
.

Lemma 8.3.5
.

Suppose the Bloch-Kato Selmer group
H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},V_{\pi_{1}}\otimes V_{\pi_{2}}(-1))

vanishes.
Then there is a constant
C_{\operatorname{RS}}\geq 0

such that, for any admissible
Q\geq 1
, there exists
j\in{\operatorname{Ann}}_{R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(J_{\operatorname{red}}^{Q})

with
p_{\pi}^{Q}(j)\not\equiv 0\pmod{\varpi^{C}}.

Proof.

We know
{\operatorname{Fitt}}_{R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(J_{\operatorname{red}}^{Q})\subset{\operatorname{Ann}}_{R_{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}}(J_{\operatorname{red}}^{Q})
. Then since Fitting ideals are stable under base change, it suffices to show there exists
C

with
\varpi^{C}\in{\operatorname{Fitt}}_{O}(J^{Q}_{\operatorname{red}}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{Q}}O)

for all
Q
, or equivalently that
J^{Q}_{\operatorname{red}}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{Q}}O

is finite with uniformly bounded cardinality.

Let
B^{Q}
and
C^{Q}
be the finitely generated
R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-modules appearing in
[
117
, p. 38]
for the deformation problem
{\operatorname{PsDef}}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}
. By construction in
[
117
, Proposition 4.2.2]
,
we have a surjection
B^{Q}\otimes C^{Q}\twoheadrightarrow J^{Q}_{\operatorname{red}}
, so it suffices to show in turn that
B^{Q}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{Q}}O

and
C^{Q}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{Q}}O

are finite of uniformly bounded cardinality.
Let
M

be a finitely generated
O
-module. Because
p_{\pi}^{Q}

corresponds to the reducible Galois representation
\rho_{\pi}
, we have
p_{\pi}^{Q}(J^{Q}_{\operatorname{red}})=0
, so
[
117
, Theorem 4.3.5]
gives canonical isomorphisms

(8.4)

\begin{split}\Home_{O}(B^{Q}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{Q}}O,M)&\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}_{O[G_{\mathbb{Q}}],\mathcal{C}^{Q}}(T_{\pi_{2}},T_{\pi_{1}}\otimes_{O}M)\\
\Home_{O}(C^{Q}\otimes_{q^{Q}_{\pi}}O,M)&\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}_{O[G_{\mathbb{Q}}],\mathcal{C}^{Q}}(T_{\pi_{1}},T_{\pi_{2}}\otimes_{O}M).\end{split}

Here
\mathcal{C}^{Q}

is the full subcategory of finitely generated
O[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-modules which are unramified outside
S{\cup}\div(Qp)

and all of whose finite subquotients are torsion crystalline with Hodge-Tate weights in
[-1,2]
.

In particular, for all
n\geq 1
,

\Home_{O}(B^{Q}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{Q}}O,O/\varpi^{n})=\Home_{O}(C^{Q}\otimes_{p^{Q}_{\pi}}O,O/\varpi^{n})=\Sel_{\mathcal{G}^{Q}}(\mathbb{Q},S_{\pi,n}).

We conclude that
B^{Q}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{Q}}O

and
C^{Q}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{Q}}O

are isomorphic, and by Lemma
8.3.2
, they are also independent of
Q
; so take
Q=1
without loss of generality.
Then by Proposition
1.5.5
,

\Hom_{O}(B^{1}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{1}}O,O)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}={\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}_{O[G_{\mathbb{Q}}],\mathcal{C}^{1}}(T_{\pi_{2}},T_{\pi_{1}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}=H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},V_{\pi_{1}}\otimes V_{\pi_{2}}(-1))=0.

Since
B^{1}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{1}}O
is a finitely generated
O
-module, it follows that
B^{1}\otimes_{p_{\pi}^{1}}O
is finite, as desired.
∎

8.3.6.

We now study deformations of each
\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{i}}
. As in the non-endoscopic case, we will apply the results and notations of Appendix
B
to
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
, which we view as valued in
\GL_{2}(O)
.
In the same way as Lemma
8.2.1
, we obtain:

Lemma 8.3.7
.

For
i=1,2
, the representation
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
satisfies Assumptions
B.1.3
and
B.1.5
from Appendix
B
.

8.3.8.

For each prime
\ell
, let
R_{\ell,i}
be the local deformation ring for
\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{i}}
as in Notation
B.1.4
.
If
q

is BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
(Definition
4.2.5
), let
R_{q,i}^{\operatorname{ord}}

be the Steinberg quotient of
R_{q,i}
in the sense of
[
73
, §2]
.

Lemma 8.3.9
.

The ring
R_{q,i}^{\operatorname{ord}}
is formally smooth over
O
of dimension 3.

Proof.

Immediate from
[
103
, Proposition 5.5]
.
∎

8.3.10.

In light of Lemma
8.3.9
,
we take the “admissible” primes in Notation
B.2.4
to be the BD-admissible ones for
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
. Then the notion of
n
-admissible in Definition
B.2.5
(
3
) coincides with the notion of
n
-BD-admissible from Definition
4.2.5
; we will always say
n
-BD-admissible to avoid confusion.

Notation 8.3.11
.

(1)

For
i=1,2

and a squarefree integer
Q\geq 1
coprime to
p
, let
\widetilde{R}_{\mathfrak{m},i}^{Q}

be the global deformation ring of

\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{i}}:G_{\mathbb{Q},S{\cup}{\operatorname{div}}(Qp)}\rightarrow\GL_{2}(O/\varpi),

with fixed determinant
\chi_{p}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
.

(2)

Let
R^{Q}_{i}
and – when
Q
is BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
–
R_{Q,i}

be the quotients of
\widetilde{R}_{\mathfrak{m},i}^{Q}
defined in Notation
B.4.4
(
2
,
3
). (We are identifying
Q
with
\div(Q)
for notational convenience.)

(3)

Let
\rho_{Q,i}^{\operatorname{univ}}:G_{\mathbb{Q},S\cup\div(Qp)}\to\GL_{2}(R_{Q,i})
be a framing of the universal deformation, with underlying
R_{Q,i}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-module
M_{Q,i}^{\operatorname{univ}}
.

8.3.12.

As in the non-endoscopic case,
let
\pr_{p}:I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
be the maximal pro-
p
quotient. By the construction of
R_{q,i}^{\operatorname{ord}}
, we have:

Lemma 8.3.13
.

Suppose
Q\geq 1
is BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
, and
q|Q
is a prime. Then there exists a basis for
M_{Q,i}^{\operatorname{univ}}
and an element
t_{q}\in R_{Q,i}
such that

\rho^{\operatorname{univ}}_{Q,i}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\chi_{p}^{\operatorname{cyc}}&\ast\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},

and

\rho^{\operatorname{univ}}_{Q,i}(g)=\begin{pmatrix}1&\pr_{p}(g)t_{q}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\;\forall g\in I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}.

Definition 8.3.14
.

If
Q
and
q\nmid Q
are BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
:

(1)

Define
P_{q,i}(T)=\det(\rho_{Q}^{\operatorname{univ}}(\Frob_{q})-T)\in R_{Q,i}[T].

(2)

Define

R_{Q,q,i}^{\operatorname{cong}}\coloneqq R_{Q,i}\otimes_{R^{Qq}_{\mathfrak{m},i}}R_{Qq,i}.

The same proof as Lemma
8.2.10
shows:

Lemma 8.3.15
.

Suppose
Q
and
q\nmid Q
are BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
. Then

R_{Q,q,i}^{\operatorname{cong}}=R_{Q,i}/(P_{q,i}(q))=R_{Qq,i}/(t_{q}).

Lemma 8.3.16
.

Suppose
q
is
n
-BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
. Then

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi_{i},n})=H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi_{i},n})\oplus H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi_{i},n}),

in which each factor is free of rank one over
O/\varpi^{n}
.
In particular,
q
is standard in the sense of Definition
B.4.7
.

Proof.

Let
\Frob_{q}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}
be a lift of Frobenius, and let
\tau_{q}\in I_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}
be an element such that
\pr_{p}(\tau_{q})=1
. Then, with respect to the basis in Lemma
8.3.13
,
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi_{i},n})
is spanned by the following two cocycles:

\displaystyle\Frob_{q}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix},\;\;\tau_{q}\mapsto 0

\displaystyle\Frob_{q}

\displaystyle\mapsto 0,\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\tau_{q}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}.

The first generates
H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi_{i},n})
and the second
H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi_{i},n})
; also, both cocycles are clearly not
\varpi^{n-1}
-torsion, and the lemma follows.
∎

8.3.17.

For the next two lemmas, we introduce some temporary notation. Let
Q\geq 1

be admissible, let
K
be an
S
-tidy level structure for
\spin(V_{DQ})
, and abbreviate
\mathbb{T}\coloneqq\mathbb{T}^{S\cup\div(Q)}_{K,V_{DQ},O,\mathfrak{m}}
, which may be the zero ring.
Also fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
inducing the prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
.
Then we write
\mathcal{T}
for the set of relevant automorphic representations
\Pi
of
\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A})
such that
\Pi_{f}^{K}\neq 0
, and the Hecke action on
\iota^{-1}\Pi_{f}^{K}
factors through
\mathbb{T}
.
By Corollary
2.7.8
, we have the embedding of
\mathbb{T}
-algebras

(8.5)

\mathbb{T}\hookrightarrow\bigoplus_{\Pi\in\mathcal{T}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\Pi),

where
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\Pi)
has Hecke action through the eigenvalues on
\iota^{-1}\Pi_{f}^{K}
.
Then by
[
22
, Corollary 1.14]
, there is a canonical pseudorepresentation

(8.6)

D_{Q}:G_{\mathbb{Q},S{\cup}{\operatorname{div}}(Qp)}\rightarrow\mathbb{T},

such that for all
\Pi\in\mathcal{T}
, the composite of
D_{Q}
with the character
\lambda_{\Pi}:\mathbb{T}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(\Pi)
is the pseudorepresentation associated to
\rho_{\Pi,\iota}
.
Finally, let
\mathcal{T}_{\textrm{end}}\subset\mathcal{T}
be the subset of endoscopic representations, and
let
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
be the quotient of
\mathbb{T}
defined by the actions on
\Pi\in\mathcal{T}_{\textrm{end}}
.

Lemma 8.3.18
.

(1)

The pseudodeformation
D_{Q}

is induced by an
O
-algebra morphism
R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}
.

(2)

The composite
R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\mathbb{T}\to\overline{\mathbb{T}}
factors through
R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}/J^{Q}_{\operatorname{red}}
.

Proof.

By definition,
D_{Q}

is induced by an
O
-algebra morphism
d:\widetilde{R}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}
.
By (
8.5
), to prove (
1
) it suffices to show each composite map

\widetilde{R}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{d}\mathbb{T}\xrightarrow{\lambda_{\Pi}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
factors through
R_{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}
, with
\lambda_{\Pi}
as in (
8.3.17
); but this is clear because
K_{p}
is hyperspecial, so
\rho_{\Pi,\iota}
is crystalline at
p
for all such
\Pi
.
Similarly, for (
2
) it suffices to note that
\lambda_{\Pi}\circ d
annihilates
J_{\operatorname{red}}^{Q}
for all
\Pi\in\mathcal{T}_{\textrm{end}}
, because
\rho_{\Pi,\iota}
is reducible.
∎

8.3.19.

Let

(8.7)

R_{\mathfrak{m}}^{Q}/J_{\operatorname{red}}^{Q}\to R_{Q_{1},1}\otimes_{O}R_{Q_{2},2}

be the canonical map, defined on moduli problems by sending a pair of deformations
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}
of
\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{1}}
and
\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{2}}
to the pseudorepresentation
D_{\rho_{1}\oplus\rho_{2}}
. It follows from
[
117
, Proposition 4.2.6]
that (
8.7
) is surjective.

Lemma 8.3.20
.

Write
Q=Q_{1}\cdot Q_{2}
such that each
q|Q_{i}
is BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
(Remark
4.2.6
(
2
)). Then:

(1)

The map
R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}/J^{Q}_{\operatorname{red}}\to\overline{\mathbb{T}}
induced by Lemma
8.3.18
(
2
) factors through the surjection (
8.7
).
In particular,
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
is an
R_{Q_{i},i}
-module for
i=1,2
.

(2)

Assume
\sigma(DQ)
is even. For any
j\in\Ann_{R^{Q}_{\mathfrak{m}}}({J_{{\operatorname{red}}}^{Q}})
, the

\mathbb{T}
-action on

jH^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Shimura_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}

factors through
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
, and as a
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
-module,
jH^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Shimura_{K}(V_{DQ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}

is

(\rho^{\operatorname{univ}}_{Q_{1},1}\otimes_{R_{Q_{1},1}}\overline{\mathbb{T}},\rho^{\operatorname{univ}}_{Q_{2},2}\otimes_{R_{Q_{2},2}}\overline{\mathbb{T}})\text{-typic}.

Proof.

Arguing as in Lemma
8.3.18
,
for (
1
) it suffices to consider the pseudorepresentations attached to
\Pi\in\mathcal{T}_{\textrm{end}}
. Suppose
\Pi
is associated to a pair
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
, and fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
inducing
\mathfrak{p}
. Then
\rho_{\Pi,\iota}=\rho_{\tau_{1},\iota}\oplus\rho_{\tau_{2},\iota}
. We have
