).

10.3.2.

We have natural closed embeddings

(10.5)

\mathcal{N}^{\diamondsuit}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}

compatible with the actions of

\spin(V_{Dq}^{\diamondsuit})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})\subset\spin(V_{Dq}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})\subset\spin(V_{Dq})(\mathbb{Q}_{q}).

From the Rapoport-Zink uniformization theorem, we deduce:

Proposition 10.3.3
.

For each
?=\diamondsuit,\circ
, or
\emptyset
, let
X^{{ss}?}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
denote the supersingular locus. Then there is a canonical isomorphism

X^{{ss}?}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\cong\spin(V_{Dq}^{?})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\spin(V_{Dq}^{?})(\mathbb{A}^{q}_{f})\times\mathcal{M}^{?}/K^{q?},

Here

(

).

Proposition 10.3.4
.

(1)

Each irreducible component of
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}
or
\mathcal{M}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
; in particular,
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}
is a union of irreducible components of

.

(2)

The group
\spin(V_{Dq})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
acts transitively on the set of irreducible components of

(3)

There are two
\spin(V_{Dq}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-orbits of irreducible components of
\mathcal{M}
interchanged by
\varphi
, and the stabilizer of each component is a hyperspecial subgroup.

(4)

For any irreducible component
A\subset\mathcal{M}
, we have
\varphi^{2}(A)=(\sigma^{2})^{\ast}\left(\langle q\rangle\cdot A\right).

Proof.

See
[
57
, §4]
and
[
56
, §4]
for the structure of
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}
, respectively.
∎

Notation 10.3.5
.

(1)

Fix an irreducible component
\mathcal{M}(1)\subset\mathcal{M}^{\circ}
as a basepoint, and let
K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}\subset\spin(V_{Dq})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
be the stabilizer of

.

(2)

For all

By Proposition

(
2
), this defines a bijection between

and the irreducible components of
\mathcal{M}
.

(3)

Let

be an element normalizing

such that

and

\varphi(\mathcal{M}(g))=\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{M}(gF)
for all

; such an

(
4
).

(4)

Let
K_{q}^{\circ}\coloneqq K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}\cap\spin(V_{Dq}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
, which
is hyperspecial by Proposition
10.3.4
(
3
).
11
11

11

Since the subgroup

from (

) is also hyperspecial, we hope this will not produce any confusion.
We also set
K^{\circ}=K^{q\circ}K_{q}^{\circ}\subset\spin(V_{Dq}^{\circ})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Remark 10.3.6
.

By Proposition

(

), under Notation

10.4.
Tate classes on
X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}

Definition 10.4.1
.

(1)

We label the irreducible components of
X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}^{{ss}\circ}
as
B_{\delta}^{\circ}(g)
for

(g,\delta)\in\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\times\left\{0,1\right\},

by defining

to be the image of

under the uniformization of Proposition

.

(2)

We define the incidence map

(10.6)

\begin{split}\inc^{\circ\ast}:H^{2}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))\to\bigoplus_{(g,\delta)\in\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\times\left\{0,1\right\}}H^{2}(B_{\delta}^{\circ}(g),O(1))\\
\cong O[\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})]^{\oplus 2}\end{split}

and dually

(10.7)

\begin{split}\inc^{\circ}_{\ast}:O[\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})]^{\oplus 2}\cong\bigoplus_{(g,\delta)\in\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\times\left\{0,1\right\}}H^{2}(B_{\delta}^{\circ}(g),O(1))\\
\to H^{2}_{c}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1)).\end{split}

10.4.2.

Let
S^{\circ}
be the set of primes
\ell
such that

is not hyperspecial.
For the rest of this section, we shall apply the results and notations of Appendix
A
, with the added superscript

for consistency. In particular, we obtain a compact open subgroup
\widetilde{K}^{\circ}=\prod\widetilde{K}^{\circ}_{\ell}\subset C^{+}(V_{D}^{\circ})^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, where

is hyperspecial for

.

.

The integral model
{X}^{\circ}
for

over

.

Under the condition (
3
) from Appendix

, this follows from the construction of the embedding on generic fibers in

.
∎

Lemma 10.4.4
.

For each
i
, there are canonical

compatible with those of Lemma

.

, Lemma 4.4]
.

∎

10.4.5.

The Clifford algebra

is a totally definite quaternion algebra over

, whose local invariants coincide with those of

at all finite places; let

be the

. Then by

, Theorem 3.13]
, we have a uniformization

(10.9)

\widetilde{X}^{{ss}\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\simeq Q^{\times}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Q^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q})\times\mathcal{M}^{\circ}/\widetilde{K}^{q\circ}

compatible with Proposition

for

. Here

is viewed as a compact open subgroup of

using the isomorphism

that follows from the choice of

(

).

We may therefore extend

to a map

fitting into the following commutative diagram:

(10.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\widetilde{X}^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\inc^{\circ\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ\ast}}

On the bottom right, the level subgroup is

, where

, and by definition

10.4.6.
Hecke actions

Recall the local and global Hecke algebras
\mathbb{T}_{\ell}^{\circ}
,
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell}^{\circ}
,
\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}
, and
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\circ S^{\circ}}
from (
A.1.3
).
We define actions of the local Hecke algebras
\mathbb{T}_{q}^{\circ}\cong\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{q}^{\circ}
on
H^{i}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O)
,
H^{i}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O)
,
H^{i}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O)
, and
H^{i}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(\widetilde{X}^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O)
via the isomorphisms of Lemmas
10.2.9
and
10.4.4
.

Lemma 10.4.7
.

(1)

The maps
\inc^{\circ\ast}
and
\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ\ast}
are equivariant for
\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\circ S^{\circ}}
, respectively.

(2)

The maps
\inc^{\circ}_{\ast}
and
\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ}_{\ast}
are equivariant for
\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\circ S^{\circ}}
, respectively, after extending scalars to
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Proof.

It is clear that
\inc^{\circ\ast}
,
\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ\ast}
,
\inc^{\circ}_{\ast}
, and
\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ}_{\ast}
are equivariant for all prime-to-
q
Hecke operators, so it suffices to
consider the action of
\mathbb{T}_{q}^{\circ}\cong\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{q}^{\circ}
.
Also, by the commutative diagrams (
10.10
) and (
10.11
), it suffices to consider
\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ\ast}
and
\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ}_{\ast}
.
The final reduction is that we may prove both statements of the lemma after extending scalars to
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, since the target of
\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ\ast}
is
O
-torsion-free.

Applying Lemma
10.4.4
and the étale comparison theorem, for (
1
) it therefore suffices to show that any map

H^{2}(\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\circ}}(V_{D})(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{C})\to\mathbb{C}\left[\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right]

which is equivariant for
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\circ S^{\circ}\cup\left\{q\right\}}
is also equivariant for
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{\circ}_{q}
; but this is clear from the Jacquet-Langlands correspondence and strong multiplicity one for
\GL_{2}
.
The proof of (
2
) is the same.
∎

Definition 10.4.8
.

Let
T_{q}^{\circ}\in\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{q}^{\circ}\cong\mathbb{T}_{q}^{\circ}
be the double coset operator represented by
\begin{pmatrix}q&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
in any basis such that
\widetilde{K}_{q}^{\circ}=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{q^{2}})
.

Lemma 10.4.9
.

(1)

The composite maps

O\left[\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right]^{\oplus 2}\xrightarrow{\inc^{\circ}_{\ast}}H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))\to H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))\xrightarrow{\inc^{\circ\ast}}O\left[\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right]^{\oplus 2}

and

O\left[\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right]^{\oplus 2}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ}_{\ast}}H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(\widetilde{X}^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))\to H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\widetilde{X}^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ\ast}}O\left[\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right]^{\oplus 2}

are both given by the matrix

\begin{pmatrix}-2q&T_{q}^{\circ}\langle q\rangle^{-1}\\
T_{q}^{\circ}&-2q\end{pmatrix}.

(2)

The restricted map

\inc^{\circ\ast}:(T_{q}^{\circ 2}\langle q\rangle^{-1}-4q^{2})H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))^{\Frob_{q}^{2}=\langle q\rangle}\to O\left[\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right]^{\oplus 2}

has
\varpi
-power-torsion kernel.

Proof.

For (
1
), see
[
67
, Proposition 2.21(4)]
; the off-diagonal entries follow from the intersection combinatorics of
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}
described in
[
56
, §4]
. For (
2
), it suffices to prove the analogous statement for
\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ\ast}
due to the commutative diagram (
10.10
). Let us fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
. Extending
scalars to
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and applying Lemma
10.4.7
and Proposition
A.1.4
, it suffices to show that

\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ\ast}:H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{X}^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))^{\Frob_{q}^{2}=\langle q\rangle}[\iota^{-1}\tau_{f}]\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\left[\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right][\iota^{-1}\tau_{f}]^{\oplus 2}

is injective for all discrete automorphic representations
\tau
of
C^{+}(V_{D}^{\circ})^{\times}(\mathbb{A})
such that

(
\operatorname{Hecke}_{q}
)

T_{q}^{\circ 2}\langle q\rangle^{-1}-4q^{2}\neq 0\text{ on }\tau_{f}^{\widetilde{K}^{\circ}}.

Now note that
\Frob_{q}^{2}=\langle q\rangle
on the image of

\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ}_{\ast}:O\left[\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right]^{\oplus 2}\to H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(\widetilde{X}^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1));

this follows from Proposition
10.3.4
(
4
). In particular, we have a well-defined composite map

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\left[\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right]^{\oplus 2}[\iota^{-1}\tau_{f}]\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ}_{\ast}}H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{X}^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))^{\Frob_{q}^{2}=\langle q\rangle}[\iota^{-1}\tau_{f}]\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\inc}^{\circ\ast}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\left[\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right]^{\oplus 2}[\iota^{-1}\tau_{f}],

given by the matrix in part (
1
), which is invertible by the assumption (
\operatorname{Hecke}_{q}
).

It therefore suffices to show that

\dim_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{X}^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))^{\Frob_{q}^{2}=\langle q\rangle}[\iota^{-1}\tau_{f}]\leq 2\dim\mathbb{Q}_{p}\left[\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right][\iota^{-1}\tau_{f}]

for all
\tau
satisfying (
\operatorname{Hecke}_{q}
). This dimension count follows from
[
112
, Proposition 2.25]
when
\tau
is cuspidal; when
\tau
is not cuspidal, it is clear from Proposition
A.1.4
.
∎

10.4.10.

Consider the algebraic cycle class

(10.12)

[X^{\diamondsuit}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}]\in H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1)).

which makes sense by Lemma
10.2.5
.

Lemma 10.4.11
.

There exists a special cycle
Z\in{\operatorname{SC}}_{K^{\circ}}^{1}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})
such that

\inc^{\circ\ast}([X^{\diamondsuit}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}])=(Z,Z)\in O\left[\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right].

Proof.

Since
[X^{\diamondsuit}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}]
is Frobenius-invariant, it suffices to consider the first coordinate of
\inc^{\circ\ast}([X^{\diamondsuit}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}])
, which we write as
\inc^{\circ\ast}_{1}([X^{\diamondsuit}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}])
. Since
\mathcal{M}^{\diamondsuit}
is zero-dimensional, all the intersections of
X^{\diamondsuit}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
with supersingular curves on
X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
are proper. Hence
\inc^{\circ\ast}_{1}([X^{\diamondsuit}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}])
is computed by

(10.13)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[(g^{q},g_{q})]\in\spin(V_{Dq}^{\diamondsuit})(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\spin(V_{Dq}^{\diamondsuit})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{q})\times\spin(V_{Dq}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})/K^{\diamondsuit q}\times K_{q}^{\circ}\end{subarray}}m(g_{q})[(g^{q},g_{q})],

where, for
g_{q}\in\spin(V_{Dq}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
,
m(g_{q})
is the degree of the divisor
\mathcal{N}^{\diamondsuit}\cap\mathcal{M}(g_{q})
on
\mathcal{M}(g_{q})\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
. In particular,
m(g_{q})
depends only on the
\spin(V_{Dq}^{\diamondsuit})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
-orbit of
g_{q}
. Moreover,
g_{q}\mapsto m(g_{q})
is a compactly supported function on
\spin(V_{Dq}^{\diamondsuit})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})\backslash\spin(V_{Dq}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})
since each point of
\mathcal{M}
lies on only finitely many irreducible components.
12
12

12

One can see this using that
\mathcal{M}
is locally of finite type over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, or more concretely from
[
56
, §4]
.
Hence (
10.13
) is a finite linear combination of special cycles

Z(h_{q}^{(i)},V_{T}\cap V_{Dq}^{\circ},V_{Dq}^{\circ})_{K^{\circ}}\in\mathbb{Z}\left[\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\right]

for some elements

h_{q}^{(i)}\in\spin(V_{Dq}^{\diamondsuit})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})\backslash\spin(V_{Dq}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{q})/K_{q}^{\circ},

which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 10.4.12
.

It would not be difficult to make Lemma
10.4.11
more explicit, but it is unnecessary for the main results.

Theorem 10.4.13
.

For any
h\in\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}_{O}
, there exists a cycle
Z_{Dq}^{\circ}\in{\operatorname{SC}}^{1}_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ},O)
such that

\inc^{\circ}_{\ast}[(Z_{Dq}^{\circ},Z_{Dq}^{\circ})]-(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)^{2}h[X^{\diamondsuit}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}]\in H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},c}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))

has
\varpi
-power-torsion image in
H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))
.

Proof.

Let
Z\in{\operatorname{SC}}^{1}_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})
be the special cycle in Lemma
10.4.11
, so that

\inc^{\circ\ast}([X^{\diamondsuit}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}])=(Z,Z),

and define

Z_{Dq}^{\circ}\coloneqq(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)\cdot(T_{q}^{\circ}+2q)\cdot h\cdot Z\in{\operatorname{SC}}^{1}_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ},O).

By Lemma
10.4.9
(
2
), it suffices to show that

(10.14)

\inc^{\circ\ast}\inc^{\circ}_{\ast}[(Z_{Dq}^{\circ},Z_{Dq}^{\circ})]=\inc^{\circ\ast}(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)^{2}h[X^{\diamondsuit}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}].

By Lemmas
10.4.9
(
1
) and
10.4.7
, the left-hand side of (
10.14
) is

(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)\cdot(T_{q}^{\circ}+2q)\cdot h\cdot\begin{pmatrix}-2q&T_{q}^{\circ}\langle q\rangle^{-1}\\
T_{q}^{\circ}&-2q\end{pmatrix}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}Z\\
Z\end{pmatrix}\\
=(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)^{2}\cdot h\cdot\begin{pmatrix}Z\\
Z\end{pmatrix}

because the Hecke operator
\langle q\rangle
acts trivially on special cycles. This coincides with the right-hand side of (
10.14
) by Lemma
10.4.7
and the definition of
Z
, so the proof is complete.
∎

10.5.
Pushing forward from
X^{\circ}
to
X

10.5.1.

Now let
S
be a finite set of primes of
\mathbb{Q}
such that
K_{\ell}
is hyperspecial for
\ell\not\in S
, and let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{S\cup\left\{q\right\}}_{O}
be a generic, non-Eisenstein maximal ideal.

Notation 10.5.2
.

We denote by
j
the closed embedding
X^{\circ}\hookrightarrow X
of (
10.4
).

Notation 10.5.3
.

Let

\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m},\mathbb{F}_{q}}:\CH^{2}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}})\to H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}})

denote the local Abel-Jacobi map constructed analogously to (
4.4.2
).

Lemma 10.5.4
.

The composite map

\CH^{1}(X^{\circ}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}})\xrightarrow{j_{\ast}}\CH^{2}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}})\xrightarrow{\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m},\mathbb{F}_{q}}}H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}})

factors through the specialization map

H^{2}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}^{\circ},O(1))\to H^{2}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}^{\circ},O(1))^{\Frob_{q}=1}.

Proof.

For any variety
Y
defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, we have the Hochschild-Serre spectral sequence:

(10.15)

E^{i,j}_{2}=H^{i}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{j}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(n))\implies H^{i+j}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,O(n)),\;\;\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}.

Since
\mathbb{F}_{q}
has cohomological dimension one, it follows immediately that the map

H^{i}(Y,O(n))\to H^{i}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(n))^{\Frob_{q}=1}

is surjective. Let
H^{i}(Y,O(n))^{0}\subset H^{i}(Y,O(n))
be the kernel of this map, and let

\partial:H^{i}(Y,O(n))^{0}\to H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{i-1}(Y_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(n))

be the edge map from (
10.15
).

It then suffices to show that the map

H^{2}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}^{\circ},O(1))\xrightarrow{j_{\ast}}H^{4}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}},O(2))\xrightarrow{\partial}H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{3}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}})

factors through the surjection
H^{2}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}^{\circ},O(1))\twoheadrightarrow H^{2}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}^{\circ},O(1))^{\Frob_{q}=1},
i.e. is trivial on
H^{2}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}^{\circ},O(1))^{0}
.

Consider the commutative diagram arising from the functoriality of (
10.15
):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\circ}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}},O(1))^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{1}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{4}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}},O(2))^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{4}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}},O(2))^{0}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\loc_{\mathfrak{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\loc_{\mathfrak{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}

Now note that the composite map

H^{1}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))\xrightarrow{j_{\ast}}H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))\xrightarrow{\loc_{\mathfrak{m}}}H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))_{\mathfrak{m}}

is identically zero: indeed, the source is
\varpi
-power-torsion by
[
74
, Chapter IX, Corollary 7.15(iii)]
, and the target is
\varpi
-torsion-free by Theorem
2.7.5
(
2
). In particular, the composite from the top left to the bottom right of the commutative diagram vanishes, which proves the lemma.
∎

10.5.5.

We now construct a map

\partial_{{ss},\mathfrak{m}}:O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})]_{\mathfrak{m}}\to H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}})

in several steps.

Construction 10.5.6
.

Assume
\langle q\rangle=1
in
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{K,V_{D},O,\mathfrak{m}}
.

(1)

For
g\in\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})
, let
B(g)\subset X^{{ss}}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
be the image of
(g^{q},\mathcal{M}(g_{q}))
under the uniformization of Proposition
10.3.3
.

(2)

Define an action of
\Frob_{q}
on
\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})
by
g\mapsto gF
. Then using Proposition
10.3.4
(
4
), we obtain a map

O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})]^{\Frob_{q}=1}\to\CH^{2}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}},O).

(3)

By our assumption
\langle q\rangle=1
, the natural map gives an isomorphism

O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\Frob_{q}^{2}=1}\xrightarrow{\sim}O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})]_{\mathfrak{m}}.

(4)

Finally, we define the map

\partial_{ss,\mathfrak{m}}:O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})]_{\mathfrak{m}}\to H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}})

to be the composite

\begin{split}O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})]_{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{\text{(\ref{constr:partial_ss_2ERL_iso})}^{-1}}O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\Frob_{q}^{2}=1}\xrightarrow{[g]\mapsto[g]+[gF]}O[\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})]_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\Frob_{q}=1}\\
\xrightarrow{\text{(\ref{constr:partial_ss_2ERL_cycle_class})}}\CH^{2}(X_{\mathbb{F}_{q}},O)_{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathbb{F}_{q},\mathfrak{m}}}H^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}})\xrightarrow[\operatorname{BC}_{X}]{\sim}H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}).\end{split}

Theorem 10.5.7
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbb{T}^{S\cup\left\{q\right\}}_{O}
satisfies:

(1)

\mathfrak{m}
is non-Eisenstein and generic.

(2)

\langle q\rangle=1
in
\mathbb{T}^{S}_{K,V_{D},O,\mathfrak{m}}
.

Let
h\in\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}_{O}
be any element, and let
C\geq 0
be an integer such that
\varpi^{C}
annihilates the
\varpi
-power-torsion in
H^{2}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(1))
. Then there exists a special cycle

Z_{Dq}\in{\operatorname{SC}}^{2}_{K}(V_{Dq},O)

such that

\varpi^{C}\cdot\Res_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}\left(j_{\ast}\left((T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)^{2}\cdot h\cdot[X^{\diamondsuit}]\right)\right)=\varpi^{C}\cdot\partial_{{ss},\mathfrak{m}}(Z_{Dq})\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}).

Proof.

Let

\overline{j}:\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\xrightarrow{\cdot g_{0}^{q}}\Sh_{K^{q}K_{q}^{\operatorname{Pa}}}(V_{Dq})

be the natural map, induced by the embedding
V_{Dq}^{\circ}\hookrightarrow V_{Dq}
from Notation
10.3.1
(
1
).
In particular, we have

(10.16)

j(B_{\delta}^{\circ}(g))=B(\overline{j}(g)F^{\delta}),\;\;(g,\delta)\in\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})\times\left\{0,1\right\}.

Then we take

Z_{Dq}^{\circ}=\sum_{g\in\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})}n(g)[g]\in{\operatorname{SC}}^{1}_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ},O)

to be the special cycle provided by Theorem
10.4.13
, and let

Z_{Dq}=\overline{j}\left(Z_{Dq}^{\circ}\right)\in{\operatorname{SC}}_{K}^{2}(V_{Dq},O)

be the pushforward.
If we let

\operatorname{cl}_{ss}^{\circ}(Z_{Dq}^{\circ})=\sum_{g\in\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{Dq}^{\circ})}n(g)\left(\left[B_{0}^{\circ}(g)\right]+\left[B_{1}^{\circ}(g)\right]\right)\in\CH^{1}(X^{\circ}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}},O),

then by (
10.16
),

\partial_{{ss},\mathfrak{m}}(Z_{Dq})=\operatorname{BC}_{X}\left(\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m},\mathbb{F}_{q}}j_{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{ss}^{\circ}(Z_{Dq}^{\circ})\right)\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},H^{3}(X_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}).

In light of Lemma
10.5.4
and the local-global compatibility of the Abel-Jacobi map, it then suffices to show that

\operatorname{cl}_{ss}^{\circ}(Z_{Dq}^{\circ})-(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)^{2}\cdot h\cdot[X^{\diamondsuit}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}]\in\CH^{1}(X^{\circ}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}})

has
\varpi
-power-torsion image in
H^{2}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X^{\circ}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}},O(1))
. But this is precisely the content of Theorem
10.4.13
.
∎

10.5.8.

Now let
\pi
,
S
, and
E_{0}
be as in Notation
4.0.1
, and let
\mathfrak{p}
be a prime of

satisfying Assumption
4.1.1
. We set

as usual.

Corollary 10.5.9
.

Suppose
q\nmid D
is
n
-admissible and
K
is an
S
-tidy level structure for
\spin(V_{D})
. Then
for any
\alpha\in\Test_{K}(V_{D},\pi,O/\varpi^{n})
and any
h\in\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}_{O}
,

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\lambda_{n}^{D}(q)\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q}\circ\alpha_{\ast}\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}\left(j_{\ast}\left((T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)^{2}\cdot h\cdot[X^{\diamondsuit}]\right)\right)-C,

where
C
is a constant independent of

and

.

Proof.

from Theorem

are satisfied by Lemmas

and

.
Let

be the composite map

.
Then

, where

11.
Second explicit reciprocity law

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
11.2.6
below.

11.1.
Setup and notation

Let
\pi
,
S
, and
E_{0}
be as in Notation
4.0.1
, and fix the following data:

\circ

A prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
satisfying Assumption
4.1.1
, with residue characteristic
p
.

\circ

A squarefree product
D\geq 1
of primes in
S
, with
\sigma(D)
even.

\circ

An
S
-tidy level structure
K
for
\spin(V_{D})
.

\circ

A Schwartz function
\varphi=\bigotimes^{\prime}_{\ell}\varphi_{\ell}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f},O)^{K}
.

11.1.1.

For all
\ell\not\in S
,
let
L_{\ell}\subset V_{D}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
be the unique self-dual lattice stabilized by
K_{\ell}
.

11.2.
Modifying the test function

In several steps, we now construct a new Schwartz function
\varphi^{\prime}
which coincides with
\varphi
at cofinitely many primes.

Construction 11.2.1
.

(1)

Label the finitely many imaginary quadratic fields contained in
\rho_{\pi}=\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
as
E_{1},\ldots,E_{s}
for some
s\geq 0
.

(2)

For each
1\leq i\leq s
, fix an odd prime
\ell_{i}\not\in S{\cup}\left\{p,\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{i-1}\right\}
inert in
E_{i}
such that
\rho_{\pi}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{\ell_{i}})
has distinct eigenvalues (possible
a fortiori
by Assumption
4.1.1
(
2
)).

(3)

Let

X_{\ell_{i}}=\left\{(x,y)\in V_{D}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}}\,:\,x\cdot x\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell_{i}}^{\times})^{2},\,x\cdot y\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ell_{i}},\,y\cdot y\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ell_{i}}^{\times}-(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell_{i}}^{\times})^{2}\right\}.

(4)

We define a test function

\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{i}}=(\ell_{i}+1)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{(x,y)\in L_{\ell_{i}}^{2}\cap X_{\ell_{i}}\right\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{(x,y)\in L_{\ell_{i}}\times(\ell_{i}^{-1}L-L_{\ell_{i}})\cap X_{\ell_{i}}\right\}}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}},\mathbb{Z})^{K_{\ell_{i}}}.

(5)

Now fix a prime
\ell_{0}\not\in S{\cup}\left\{p,\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{s}\right\}
such that
\rho_{\pi}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{\ell_{0}})
has distinct eigenvalues.

(6)

Let

\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{0}}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{0}},\mathbb{Z})^{K_{\ell_{0}}}

be the indicator function of the set

\left\{(x,y)\in L_{\ell_{0}}^{2}\,:\,x\cdot x\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{\ell_{0}}-(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell_{0}}^{\times})^{2},\,x\cdot y\in\ell_{0}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell_{0}},\,y\cdot y\in\ell_{0}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell_{0}}^{\times}\right\}.

(7)

Finally, we define

\varphi^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigotimes_{\ell\neq\ell_{i}}\varphi_{\ell}\otimes\bigotimes_{i=0}^{s}\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{i}}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f},O)^{K}.

Lemma 11.2.2
.

Suppose
\kappa^{D}(1,\varphi;K)\neq 0
.
Then
there exists a test function
\alpha\in\Test_{K}(V_{D},\pi,O)
and a matrix

T\in\symmetric_{2}(\mathbb{Q})_{>0}
satisfying
(T1)
and
(T2)
of (
10.1.1
) such that

\alpha_{\ast}\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}\left(Z(T,\varphi^{\prime})_{K}\right)\neq 0.

Moreover, for all
1\leq i\leq s
,
\ell_{i}
is split in the quadratic field
F\coloneqq\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{T_{11}})
and
T
lies in
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{(\ell_{i})})
.

Proof.

Repeatedly applying Corollary
5.6.6
, we conclude

\kappa^{D}(1,\varphi^{\prime};K)\neq 0
; i.e.,
there exists
T\in\symmetric_{2}(\mathbb{Q})_{\geq 0}
and
\alpha\in\Test_{K}(V_{D},\pi,O)
such that

\alpha_{\ast}\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}\left(Z(T,\varphi^{\prime})_{K}\right)\neq 0.

By definition of
Z(T,\varphi^{\prime})_{K}
, it follows that, for all
0\leq i\leq s
, there exists
x,y\in\Omega_{T,V_{D}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})
such that
\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{i}}(x,y)\neq 0
. Now the choice of
\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{0}}
implies that
T
is positive definite,
T_{11}
is not a rational square, and the quadratic space defined by
T
has nontrivial Hasse invariant at
\ell_{0}\nmid D
, and this proves the first claim of the lemma. Similarly, for each
1\leq i\leq s
,
\ell_{i}
is split in
F
and
T
lies in
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{(\ell_{i})})
by the choice of
\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{i}}
.
∎

11.2.3.

We will now assume
\kappa^{D}(1,\varphi;K)\neq 0
, and fix
\alpha
and
T
satisfying the conclusion of Lemma
11.2.2
.
We choose a base point
(e_{1}^{T},e_{2}^{T})\in\Omega_{T,V_{D}}(\mathbb{Q})
such that
e_{1}^{T},e_{2}^{T}\in L_{\ell_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq s
(possible by the last point of Lemma
11.2.2
), and we adopt the setup of §
10
for this choice of
T
,
(e_{1}^{T},e_{2}^{T})
, and
K
; the choice of
g_{0}
is postponed to Proposition
11.2.5
.

Definition 11.2.4
.

For each
\ell
split in
F
and each hyperspecial subgroup
K_{\ell}^{\circ}\subset\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
, we define a Hecke operator
T_{\ell}^{\circ}\in\mathbb{T}_{\ell}^{\circ}
as follows. Choose an isomorphism

\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\simeq G_{\ell}\coloneqq\left\{(g_{1},g_{2})\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\,:\,\det g_{1}=\det g_{2}\right\}

mapping
K_{\ell}^{\circ}
to
G_{\ell}\cap\left(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\right)
. Then
T_{\ell}^{\circ}
is the double coset operator represented by

\left(\begin{pmatrix}\ell&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\ell&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\right)\in G_{\ell}.

Proposition 11.2.5
.

There exists an element
g_{0}\in\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\left\{\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{s}\right\}})
such that, adopting the notation of (
10.1.3
) for this choice of
g_{0}
:

(1)

The compact open subgroups
K^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\subset\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})
are hyperspecial for
1\leq i\leq s
.

(2)

We have

\alpha_{\ast}\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}j_{\ast}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{s}T^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\cdot\left[\shimmer_{K^{\diamond}}(V_{D}^{\diamond})\right]\right)\neq 0,

where
\left[\shimmer_{K^{\diamond}}(V_{D}^{\diamond})\right]\in\CH^{1}(\shimmer_{K^{\circ}}(V^{\circ}_{D}))
is the class of the cycle from (
10.2
).

Proof.

By definition of
Z(T,\varphi^{\prime})_{K}
and the assumption
\alpha\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}(Z(T,\varphi^{\prime})_{K})\neq 0
, there exists
g_{0}\in\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\left\{\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{s}\right\}})
such that

(11.1)

Z\coloneqq\sum_{g=\prod g_{\ell_{i}}\in\prod_{i=1}^{s}\spin(V_{D}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})\backslash\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})/K_{\ell_{i}}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{i}}(g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}\cdot(e_{1}^{T},e_{2}^{T}))\right)\cdot Z(g_{0}g,V_{T},V_{D})_{K}

satisfies
\alpha_{\ast}\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}(Z)\neq 0
. We will check the claimed properties for this choice of
g_{0}
. First, if
\varphi_{\ell_{i}}^{\prime}(g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}(e_{1}^{T},e_{2}^{T}))\neq 0
, then
g_{\ell_{i}}^{-1}e_{1}^{T}\cdot g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{1}^{T}=e_{1}^{T}\cdot e_{1}^{T}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{(\ell_{i})}
, so in particular

L_{\ell_{i}}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}}g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{1}^{T}=\mathbb{Z}_{\ell_{i}}g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{1}^{T}.

On the other hand, we chose
(e_{1}^{T},e_{2}^{T})
so that
L_{\ell_{i}}\cap e_{1}^{T}=\mathbb{Z}_{\ell_{i}}e_{1}^{T}
.
Hence by Proposition
3.1.8
, we have
g_{\ell_{i}}\in\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})\cdot K_{\ell_{i}}
. In particular, we can rewrite (
11.1
) as

(11.2)

\begin{split}Z&=\sum_{g=\prod g_{\ell_{i}}\in\prod_{i=1}^{s}\spin(V_{D}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})\backslash\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})/K_{\ell_{i}}\cap\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})}\prod_{i=1}^{s}\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{i}}(g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}(e_{1}^{T},e_{2}^{T}))\cdot Z(g_{0}g,V_{T},V_{D})_{K}\\
&=\sum_{g=\prod g_{\ell_{i}}\in\prod_{i=1}^{s}\spin(V_{D}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})\backslash\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})/K_{\ell_{i}}\cap\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})}\prod_{i=1}^{s}\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{i}}(g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}(e_{1}^{T},e_{2}^{T}))\cdot j_{\ast}Z(g,V_{T}\cap V_{D}^{\circ},V_{D}^{\circ})_{K^{\circ}}.\end{split}

Again because
e_{1}^{T}\cdot e_{1}^{T}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{(\ell_{i})}
,
L^{\circ}\coloneqq L_{\ell_{i}}\cap(V^{\circ}_{D}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})
is self-dual, and so its stabilizer
K^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\coloneqq K_{\ell_{i}}\cap\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})
in
\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})
is hyperspecial, which proves (
1
). To prove (
2
), by definition we have

(11.3)

\begin{split}\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{i}}(e_{1}^{T},g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{2}^{T})&=\begin{cases}\ell_{i}+1,&g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{2}^{T}\in L_{\ell_{i}},\\
1,&g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{2}^{T}\in\ell^{-1}_{i}L_{\ell_{i}}-L_{\ell_{i}},\\
0,&g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{2}^{T}\not\in\ell_{i}^{-1}L_{\ell_{i}}\end{cases}\\
&=\begin{cases}\ell_{i}+1,&g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{2}\in L^{\circ},\\
1,&g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{2}\in\ell^{-1}_{i}L^{\circ}-L^{\circ},\\
0,&g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{2}\not\in\ell^{-1}_{i}L^{\circ},\end{cases}\end{split}

where

e_{2}\coloneqq e_{2}^{T}-\frac{e_{2}^{T}\cdot e_{1}^{T}}{e_{1}^{T}\cdot e_{1}^{T}}e_{1}^{T}\in V_{D}^{\circ}.

Note that
e_{2}\cdot e_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{(\ell_{i})}^{\times}
because, for
(e_{1}^{T},g^{-1}_{\ell_{i}}e_{2}^{T})
to lie in the support of
\varphi^{\prime}_{\ell_{i}}
, we automatically have
e_{1}^{T}\cdot e_{1}^{T}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{(\ell_{i})}-(\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{\ell_{i}})^{2}
,
e_{1}^{T}\cdot e_{2}^{T}\in\mathbb{Z}_{(\ell_{i})}
, and
e_{2}^{T}\cdot e_{2}^{T}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{(\ell_{i})}\cap(\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{\ell_{i}})^{2}
.

Let
\varphi^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\in\mathcal{S}(V_{D}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}},\mathbb{Z})^{K^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}}
be the indicator function of
L^{\circ}
; by our specification
e_{2}^{T}\in L_{\ell_{i}}
and Proposition
3.1.8
, we see that
\varphi^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}(g_{\ell_{i}}^{-1}e_{2})
is the indicator function of
\spin(V_{D}^{\diamond})(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}})\cdot K_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ}
.
The proposition therefore follows from (
11.2
), (
11.3
), and the following:

Claim
.

When restricted to
\left\{y\in V^{\circ}_{D}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}}\,:\,y\cdot y\in\mathbb{Z}^{\times}_{\ell_{i}}\right\},
we have
T^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\cdot\varphi^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}=(\ell_{i}+1)\varphi^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\ell_{i}^{-1}L^{\circ}-L^{\circ}}.

To prove the claim, observe first that

T^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\cdot\varphi^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}=\sum_{L^{\prime}\sim L^{\circ}}\mathbbm{1}_{L^{\prime}},

where
L^{\prime}
runs over self-dual lattices in
V^{\circ}_{D}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}}
such that

(11.4)

\ell_{i}L^{\circ}\subset_{1}\ell_{i}L^{\prime}+\ell_{i}L^{\circ}\subset_{2}L^{\prime}\cap L^{\circ}\subset_{1}L^{\circ}.

Note that such a chain (
11.4
) uniquely determines
L^{\prime}
because
L^{\prime}
and
L^{\circ}
correspond to the two isotropic lines in the split two-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{\ell_{i}}
-space
L^{\circ}+L^{\prime}/L^{\circ}\cap L^{\prime}
. We can also write the chain (
11.4
) as

(11.5)

\ell_{i}L^{\circ}\subset_{1}L_{1}\subset_{2}L_{1}^{\vee}\subset_{1}L^{\circ},

i.e. such chains correspond bijectively to isotropic lines
L_{1}/{\ell_{i}}L^{\circ}
in the
\mathbb{F}_{\ell_{i}}
-quadratic space
L^{\circ}/\ell_{i}L^{\circ}
.

Now for any
y\in V_{D}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell_{i}}
with
y\cdot y\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ell_{i}}^{\times}
, we wish to calculate

T^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\cdot\varphi^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}(y)=\#\left\{L^{\prime}\sim L^{\circ}\,:\,y\in L^{\prime}\right\}.

If
y\in L^{\circ}
, then the choices of
L^{\prime}
correspond to isotropic lines orthogonal to
y
in the four-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{\ell_{i}}
-space
L^{\circ}/\ell_{i}L^{\circ}
; there are
\ell_{i}+1
such lines because
y^{\perp}\subset L^{\circ}/\ell_{i}L^{\circ}
is a non-degenerate three-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{\ell_{i}}
-space. Hence
T^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\cdot\varphi^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}(y)=\ell_{i}+1

for
y\in L^{\circ}
.

On the other hand, since any
L^{\prime}\sim L^{\circ}
is contained in
\ell^{-1}_{i}L^{\circ}
, if
T^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\cdot\varphi_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ}(y)\neq 0
we must have
y\in\ell_{i}^{-1}L^{\circ}
. So suppose
y\in\ell^{-1}_{i}L^{\circ}-L^{\circ}
. Now, lattices
L^{\prime}\sim L^{\circ}
with
y\in L^{\prime}
(equivalently
\ell_{i}y\in\ell_{i}L^{\prime}
) are in bijection with chains (
11.5
) satisfying
\ell_{i}y\in L_{1}
; since
\ell_{i}y\not\in\ell_{i}L^{\circ}
, the unique such chain is given by
L_{1}=\ell_{i}L^{\circ}+\ell_{i}y
, and this makes sense because
\ell_{i}y
is isotropic in
L^{\circ}/\ell_{i}L^{\circ}
.
We therefore conclude that
T^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\cdot\varphi^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}(y)=1
for
y\in\ell_{i}^{-1}L^{\circ}-L^{\circ}
, which completes the proof of the claim.
∎

Theorem 11.2.6
.

Suppose
\pi
is non-endoscopic,
\kappa^{D}(1)\neq 0
, and admissible primes exist for
\rho_{\pi}
. Then there exists a constant
C\geq 0
such that, for all
N
, there exist infinitely many admissible primes
q
with
n(q)\geq N
,

\order_{\varpi}\operatorname{loc}_{q}\kappa^{D}_{n(q)}(1)\geq n(q)-C,

and

\order_{\varpi}\lambda^{D}_{n(q)}(q)\geq n(q)-C.

Proof.

Since
\kappa^{D}(1)\neq 0
, we can choose the data
\varphi
and
K
in the beginning of this section so that
\kappa^{D}(1,\varphi;K)\neq 0
(where
K
can be made
S
-tidy by Lemma
4.4.7
).
Then fix
g_{0}\in\spin(V_{D})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\left\{\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{s}\right\}})
as in Proposition
11.2.5
, so that

(11.6)

\alpha_{\ast}\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}j_{\ast}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{s}T^{\circ}_{\ell_{i}}\left[\shimmer_{K^{\diamond}}(V_{D}^{\diamond})\right]\right)\neq 0;

equivalently, by Lemma
4.1.6
, (
11.6
) is non-torsion. Let
S^{\circ}
be the set of primes
\ell
such that
K^{\circ}_{\ell}
is not hyperspecial.
Without loss of generality, we may replace
O
by a finite extension such that all the eigenvalues of
\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}_{O}
acting on
H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}(\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{D}^{\circ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
lie in
O
; let
\bm{h}_{1},\ldots,\bm{h}_{m}:\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}_{O}\to O
be the finitely many characters that appear in the action on
H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{D}^{\circ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}
, cf. Corollary
A.2.2
. Then we may choose elements
h_{j}\in\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}_{O}
such that
\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}_{O}
acts on
h_{j}H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{D}^{\circ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}
through
\bm{h}_{j}
, and moreover

\sum_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}=\varpi^{C_{0}}\text{ on }H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{D}^{\circ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}

for a constant
C_{0}\geq 0
.
In particular, by Lemma
10.5.4
and Proposition
11.2.5
,

c\coloneqq\alpha\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}j_{\ast}\left(h_{j}\cdot\left(\prod_{i=1}^{s}T_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ}\right)\left[\Sh_{K^{\diamond}}(V_{D}^{\diamond})\right]\right)

is non-torsion for some
1\leq j\leq m
. Write
\bm{h}\coloneqq\bm{h}_{j}
and
h\coloneqq h_{j}
. Let us fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
. By Corollary
A.2.2
, we are in one of the following two cases:

Case 1
.

The character
\iota\circ\bm{h}
is given by the action of
\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}
on a global newform in an automorphic representation
\sigma
of
B_{F}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\times}
unramified outside primes above
S^{\circ}
. Moreover
\JL(\sigma)=\BC_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(\sigma_{0})\otimes\chi
where
\sigma_{0}
is a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
of weight 2 and
\chi
is a finite order character of
F^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
.

Case 2
.

The character
\iota\circ\bm{h}
is given by the action of
\mathbb{T}^{\circ S^{\circ}}
on the automorphic representation
\chi_{0}\circ\nu
, where
\nu:\spin(V_{D}^{\circ})\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
is the restriction of the norm character in (
A.1.1
) and
\chi_{0}
is a quadratic character of
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{\times}/\nu(K^{\circ})
.

Claim 1
.

In Case 1,
\sigma_{0}
does not have CM by any imaginary quadratic field
K_{0}\subset F(\rho_{\pi})
.

Proof of claim 1.

If so, then
K_{0}\neq F
, and
K_{0}
is contained in the compositum of
F
and one of the quadratic fields
E_{i}\subset\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})
from Construction
11.2.1
; in particular, since
\ell_{i}
is inert in
K_{i}
but split in
F
by Lemma
11.2.2
,
\ell_{i}
is inert in
K_{0}
.
It follows that
\tr\rho_{\sigma,\iota}(\Frob_{\ell_{i}})=0
. On the other hand, it is not difficult to compute using the Satake transform and Theorem
2.2.1
(
1
) that

T_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ}=\tr\rho_{\sigma,\iota}(\Frob_{\ell_{i}})\cdot\tr\rho_{\sigma,\iota}(\tau\Frob_{\ell_{i}}\tau^{-1})

on a local newform in
\sigma_{\ell_{i}}
, where
\tau\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
projects to the nontrivial element of
\Gal(F/\mathbb{Q})
. Hence
h\cdot T_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ}=\bm{h}(T_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ})\cdot h=0
, which contradicts the nontriviality of
c
.
∎

Now fix an element
g\in\Gal(F(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q})
such that:

(1)

g
is admissible for
\rho_{\pi}
and has nontrivial image in
\Gal(F/\mathbb{Q})
.

(2)

c(g)
has nonzero component in the 1-eigenspace for
g
for any cocycle representative of
c
.

(3)

In Case 1 above,
\rho_{\sigma_{0},\iota}(g^{2})
has distinct eigenvalues.

This is possible by Proposition
C.5.3
because
c
is nontorsion. Now fix constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}\geq 0
such that:

(1)

The component of
c(g)
in the 1-eigenspace for
g
is nonzero modulo
\varpi^{C_{1}}
.

(2)

In Case 1 above,
\tr(\rho_{\sigma,\iota}(g^{2}))-4\det\rho_{\sigma,\iota}(g^{2})\neq 0\pmod{\varpi^{C_{2}}}
.
In Case 2,
C_{2}=0
.

(3)

\varpi^{C_{3}}
annihilates the
O
-torsion in
H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\Sh_{K^{\circ}}(V_{D}^{\circ})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O)
.

Now fix
N\geq C_{1}+C_{2}+C_{3}
and let
c_{N}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi,N})
be the reduction of
c
modulo
\varpi^{N}
. All but finitely many primes with Frobenius conjugate to
g
in
\Gal(F(T_{\pi,N},c_{N})/\mathbb{Q})
are
N
-admissible and satisfy Assumption

10.2.1
; let
q
be one such and
abbreviate
n\coloneqq n(q)\geq N
. If
\alpha_{n}\in\Test_{K}(V_{D},\pi,\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{n})
is the image of
\alpha
, then:

(11.7)

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q}\alpha_{n,\ast}\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}j_{\ast}\left(h\cdot\left(\prod_{i=1}^{s}T_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ}\right)\left[\Sh_{K^{\diamond}}(V_{D}^{\diamond})\right]\right)\geq n-C_{1}.

We have:

Claim 2
.

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\bm{h}(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)\leq C_{2}.

Proof of Claim 2.

In Case 1, we have

\bm{h}(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)=\tr\rho_{\sigma,\iota}(\Frob_{q}^{2})-4\det\rho_{\sigma,\iota}(\Frob_{q}^{2})\equiv\tr\rho_{\sigma,\iota}(g^{2})-4\det\rho_{\sigma,\iota}(g^{2})\pmod{\varpi^{n}},

so this follows from the choice of
C_{2}
. In Case 2, we have
\bm{h}(T_{q}^{\circ})=(q^{2}+1)\chi_{0}(\langle q\rangle)
and
\bm{h}(\langle q\rangle)=\chi_{0}(\langle q\rangle)
, so
\bm{h}(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)=(q^{2}-1)^{2}\chi_{0}(\langle q\rangle)
, which is a
\varpi
-adic unit because
q
is admissible.
∎

Now, by Corollary
10.5.9
we have

\displaystyle{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\lambda_{n}^{D}(q)

\displaystyle\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q}\alpha_{n,\ast}\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}j_{\ast}\left((T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)h\left(\prod_{i=1}^{s}T_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ}\right)\left[\Sh_{K^{\diamond}}(V_{D}^{\diamond})\right]\right)-C_{3}

\displaystyle={\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q}\alpha_{n,\ast}\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}j_{\ast}\left(\bm{h}(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle)h\left(\prod_{i=1}^{s}T_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ}\right)\left[\Sh_{K^{\diamond}}(V_{D}^{\diamond})\right]\right)-C_{3}

\displaystyle\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q}\alpha_{n,\ast}\circ\partial_{{\operatorname{AJ}},\mathfrak{m}}j_{\ast}\left(h\left(\prod_{i=1}^{s}T_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ}\right)\left[\Sh_{K^{\diamond}}(V_{D}^{\diamond})\right]\right)-C_{3}-{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\bm{h}(T_{q}^{\circ 2}-4q^{2}\langle q\rangle).

By Claim
2
and (
11.7
), we conclude that

(11.8)

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\lambda_{n}^{D}(q)\geq n-C_{1}-C_{2}-C_{3}.

Since
j_{\ast}\left(h\cdot\left(\prod_{i=1}^{s}T_{\ell_{i}}^{\circ}\right)\left[\Sh_{K^{\diamond}}(V_{D}^{\diamond})\right]\right)\in\CH^{2}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D}),O)
lies in
{\operatorname{SC}}^{2}_{K}(V_{D},O)
by Remark
3.1.6
, (
11.7
) and (
11.8
) together show the theorem.
∎

In the endoscopic case, we similarly obtain:

Theorem 11.2.7
.

Suppose
\pi
is endoscopic associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
, such that
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are not both CM for the same imaginary quadratic field. For
j=1
or 2, assume

\kappa^{D}(1)^{(j)}\neq 0
and admissible primes exist which are BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{j}}
. Then there exists a constant
C\geq 0
such that, for all
N
, there exist infinitely many admissible primes
q
which are
N
-BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{j}}
, such that

\order_{\varpi}\operatorname{loc}_{q}\kappa^{D}_{n(q)}(1)^{(j)}\geq n(q)-C

and

\order_{\varpi}\lambda^{D}_{n(q)}(q)\geq n(q)-C.

Proof.

By using Corollary
5.6.7
in place of Corollary
5.6.6
, we can refine Lemma
11.2.2
to obtain the same conclusion where
\alpha:H^{3}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\Sh_{K}(V_{D})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},O(2))_{\mathfrak{m}}\to T_{\pi}
is required to factor through
T_{\pi_{j}}
. From here, the rest of the proof follows that of Theorem
11.2.6
, substituting Proposition
C.5.4
for Proposition
C.5.3
.
∎

12.
Main result: rank one case

12.1.
Setup and notation

Let
\pi
,
S
, and
E_{0}
be as in Notation
4.0.1
, and fix a prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
satisfying Assumption
4.1.1
, with residue characteristic
p
.

Definition 12.1.1
.

An automorphic representation
\pi
of
\GL_{4}(\mathbb{A})
is of
general type
if it is neither an automorphic induction nor a symmetric cube lift.

For this section, we shall assume:

Hypothesis

(
\bigstar
)
.

If
\pi
is non-endoscopic, then
\BC(\pi)
is
either of general type, or a symmetric cube lift of a non-CM automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
, or induced from a non-CM automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
with
K/\mathbb{Q}
real quadratic; in the latter two cases
E_{0}
is also a strong coefficient field of
\pi_{0}
.

12.2.
Choosing Chebotarev primes

12.2.1.

Let
L_{\pi}\subset\End_{O}(T_{\pi})
be the underlying
O
-module of
\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi}=\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
, which is free of rank 10 over
O
. In general,
L_{\pi}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is not absolutely irreducible, even if
V_{\pi}
is. In the endoscopic case, we also let
L_{\pi_{i}}
be the underlying
O
-module of
\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi_{i}}
, for
i=1,2
.

Proposition 12.2.2
.

Suppose
\pi
is not endoscopic. We have the following cases for
L_{\pi}
:

(1)

If
\BC(\pi)
is an automorphic induction of
\pi_{0}
as in Hypothesis
(\bigstar)
, then

L_{\pi}=\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}\ad^{0}T_{\pi_{0}}\oplus\left(\otimes-\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}T_{\pi_{0}}\right)(-1).

Both direct summands are absolutely irreducible after inverting
p
.

(2)

If
\BC(\pi)
is a symmetric cube lift of
\pi_{0}
as in Hypothesis
(\bigstar)
, then

L_{\pi}=\ad^{0}T_{\pi_{0}}\oplus\Sym^{6}(T_{\pi_{0}})(-3),

with each summand absolutely irreducible after inverting
p
.

(3)

If
\BC(\pi)
is of general type, then
L_{\pi}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is absolutely irreducible.

Proof.

In the first case,
T_{\pi_{0}}
exists by Lemma
2.2.19
, and
we have

T_{\pi}=\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}T_{\pi_{0}};

so the claimed decomposition follows from, e.g., the discussion in
[
13
, §7.5.16]
. For the irreducibility, if
\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}
is the
\Gal(K/\mathbb{Q})
twist, then one checks using Hodge-Tate weights that
V_{\pi_{0}}\not\simeq V_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}}\otimes\chi
for any character
\chi
of
G_{K}
.
It follows that
\otimes-\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}V_{\pi_{0}}
is absolutely irreducible, since if it reduces it must have a one-dimensional constituent. Similarly, if
\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}\ad^{0}V_{\pi_{0}}
is not absolutely irreducible, then
\ad^{0}V_{\pi_{0}}\cong\ad^{0}V_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}}
, so by Corollary
C.3.7
,
V_{\pi_{0}}|_{G_{L}}=V_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}}|_{G_{L}}
for a finite extension
L/K
, and this is a contradiction by Lemma
C.3.4
.

In the second case,
T_{\pi_{0}}
exists by Lemma
2.2.18
, and by definition,
T_{\pi}=\Sym^{3}T_{\pi_{0}}(-1)
. Then we have

\End(T_{\pi})=\Sym^{6}(T_{\pi_{0}})(-3)\oplus\Sym^{4}(T_{\pi_{0}})(-2)\oplus\Sym^{2}(T_{\pi_{0}})(-1)\oplus O

as an
O[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-module, and by dimension counting,
L_{\pi}
consists of the first and third summands.
The irreducibility of each summand of
L_{\pi}
after inverting
p
is clear because the
Zariski closure (over
E
) of the projective image of
\rho_{\pi_{0}}
is
\PGL_{2}(E)
by Theorem
C.3.2
.

The third case follows from Lemma
C.4.3
, noting that
p>3
by Remark
4.1.2
.
∎

12.2.3.

We write
L_{\pi,n}=L_{\pi}/\varpi^{n}L_{\pi}
for all
n\geq 1
and likewise for
L_{\pi_{i},n}
when
\pi
is endoscopic.
We will be applying the results and notations of Appendix
B
to
\rho_{\pi}
(and
\rho_{\pi_{i}}
when
\pi
is endoscopic), using Lemmas
8.2.1
and
8.3.7
to check the relevant assumptions.
The notion of admissible primes for Notation
B.2.4
is as explained in (
8.2.5
) and (
8.3.10
).
In particular, if
\pi
is not endoscopic and
q
is an
n
-admissible prime for
\rho_{\pi}
, let
H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,n})\subset H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi})
be the subspace defined in Definition
B.2.5
.

Lemma 12.2.4
.

Suppose
\pi
satisfies Hypothesis
(\bigstar)
and is not endoscopic. There exists a
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable decomposition
L_{\pi}=L_{\pi}^{\heartsuit}\oplus L_{\pi}^{\circ}
such that
L_{\pi}^{\heartsuit}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
L_{\pi}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
are absolutely irreducible and distinct, and moreover, if
q
is an
n
-admissible prime:

(1)

We have
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,n}^{\heartsuit})+H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,n})=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,n})
.

(2)

We have
H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,n}^{\circ})=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,n}^{\circ}).

(3)

If
T_{\pi,n}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{q}}}=M_{0,n}\oplus M_{1,n}
is the decomposition of Lemma
4.2.2
, then the composite

L_{\pi,n}^{\heartsuit}\hookrightarrow L_{\pi,n}\twoheadrightarrow\ad^{0}M_{0,n}

is surjective.

Here
L_{\pi,n}^{\heartsuit}=L_{\pi}^{\heartsuit}/\varpi^{n}L_{\pi}^{\heartsuit}
, and likewise for
L_{\pi,n}^{\circ}
.

Proof.

In case (
3
) of Proposition
12.2.2
, the lemma is clear, taking
L_{\pi}^{\circ}=0
.
Suppose we are in case (
1
) of Proposition
12.2.2
.
Then if
q
is admissible for
\rho_{\pi}
, we have
\tr\rho_{\pi}(\Frob_{q})\not\equiv 0\pmod{\varpi}
, which implies
q
splits in
K
.
Let
\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}
be the
\Gal(K/\mathbb{Q})
-twist. Because
\det\rho_{\pi_{0}}=\chi_{p}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
, comparing the decomposition
T_{\pi,n}=M_{0,n}\oplus M_{1,n}
with
T_{\pi}|_{G_{K}}=T_{\pi_{0}}\oplus T_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}}
shows that, up to replacing
\pi_{0}
with
\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}
, we have
M_{0,n}=T_{\pi_{0},n}.

Now note that
H^{1}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\left(\otimes-\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}T_{\pi_{0},n}\right)(-1)\right)=0
because the
\Frob_{q}
eigenvalues on
\left(\otimes-\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}\overline{T}_{\pi_{0}}\right)(-1)
are
q/\alpha
,
\alpha/q
,
\alpha
, and
1/\alpha

for some
\alpha\neq q^{2},q^{-1},\pm 1,\pm q
.
Hence the lemma holds in this case with
L_{\pi}^{\heartsuit}=\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}\ad^{0}T_{\pi_{0}}
and
L_{\pi}^{\circ}=\left(\otimes-\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}T_{\pi_{0}}\right)(-1)
.

Now suppose we are in case (
2
) of Proposition
12.2.2
, and take
L_{\pi}^{\heartsuit}=\Sym^{6}(T_{\pi_{0}})(-3)
,
L_{\pi}^{\circ}=\ad^{0}T_{\pi_{0}}
.
If
\Frob_{q}
acts on
\overline{T}_{\pi_{0}}
with generalized eigenvalues
\left\{\alpha,\beta\right\}
, then the admissibility of
\Frob_{q}
implies that (up to reordering) we have
\beta^{3}=q
and
\alpha=\beta^{2}
.
Choose a basis for
T_{\pi}
such that

\Frob_{q}=\begin{pmatrix}\alpha^{2}/\beta&&&\\
&\alpha&&\\
&&\beta&\\
&&&\beta^{2}/\alpha\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}q&&&\\
&\beta^{2}&&\\
&&\beta&\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}.

One sees immediately that the eigenvalues of
\Frob_{q}
on
\ad^{0}\overline{T}_{\pi_{0}}
are
\left\{1,\beta,\beta^{-1}\right\}
, which are all distinct from
q^{-1}
, so
H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,n}^{\circ}(1))=0
, and this implies (
2
) by local Poitou-Tate duality.

To prove (
1
) and (
3
), we use the following:

Claim
.

The two decompositions into one-dimensional
O/\varpi
-vector spaces

L_{\pi,1}^{\Frob_{q}=1}=(L_{\pi,1}^{\circ})^{\Frob_{q}=1}\oplus(L_{\pi,1}^{\heartsuit})^{\Frob_{q}=1}=(\ad^{0}M_{0,1})^{\Frob_{q}=1}\oplus(\ad^{0}M_{1,1})^{\Frob_{q}=1}

are both orthogonal with respect to the Killing form, and not the same.

Before proving the claim, we show it implies (
1
) and (
3
).
Indeed, by (
2
), we can rewrite (
1
)
as asserting the surjectivity of

H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},\ad^{0}M_{1,n})=H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,n})\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,n})\to H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,n}^{\circ}).

Since
\Frob_{q}
acts on
L_{\pi}
with eigenvalues that are all either 1 or not congruent to 1 modulo
\varpi
, this is equivalent to the surjectivity of

(\ad^{0}M_{1,1})^{\Frob_{q}=1}\to(L_{\pi,1}^{\circ})^{\Frob_{q}=1},

which follows from the claim.

For (
3
), we can take
n=1
.
Since
\Frob_{q}
acts with distinct eigenvalues
\left\{1,q,q^{-1}\right\}
on
\ad^{0}M_{0,1}
, and the eigenvalues
q
and
q^{-1}
do not appear in
L_{\pi,1}^{\circ}=\ad^{0}\overline{T}_{\pi_{0}}
, it again suffices to consider the
\Frob_{q}=1
eigenspaces. In particular, it suffices to show that
(L_{\pi,1}^{\heartsuit})^{\Frob_{q}=1}
is not contained in the kernel of the map
L_{\pi,1}^{\Frob_{q}=1}\to(\ad^{0}M_{0,1})^{\Frob_{q}=1}
, which again follows from the claim.

Now we return to the proof of the claim. In our basis of
T_{\pi}
,
(\ad^{0}M_{1,1})^{\Frob_{q}=1}
consists of the matrices
\begin{pmatrix}0&&&\\
&x&&\\
&&-x&\\
&&&0\end{pmatrix}
, and
(\ad^{0}M_{0,1})^{\Frob_{q}=1}
consists of the matrices

\begin{pmatrix}x&&&\\
&0&&\\
&&0&\\
&&&x\end{pmatrix}
. This decomposition is plainly orthogonal for the Killing form.

Meanwhile,
(L_{\pi,1}^{\circ})^{\Frob_{q}=1}=(\ad^{0}\overline{T}_{\pi_{0}})^{\Frob_{q}=1}
consists
of the matrices of the form

\begin{pmatrix}3y&&&\\
&y&&\\
&&-y&\\
&&&-3y\end{pmatrix}.

The decomposition
L_{\pi}^{\circ}\oplus L_{\pi}^{\heartsuit}
is necessarily orthogonal for the Killing form because the form is Galois-invariant, and the claim follows.

∎

Lemma 12.2.5
.

Suppose
\pi
is not endoscopic and satisfies Hypothesis
(\bigstar)
. Then:

(1)

The action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
on
T_{\pi}
contains a scalar of infinite order.

(2)

The projective image of
\rho_{\pi}
is a compact
p
-adic Lie group with semisimple Lie algebra.

Proof.

If
\BC(\pi)
is not an automorphic induction, then the lemma follows from Corollary
C.2.6
combined with Lemma
C.2.2
(
4
,
5
).
If
\BC(\pi)
is an automorphic induction of the kind in
Hypothesis (
\bigstar
), it follows from Corollary
C.3.7
.
∎

Lemma 12.2.6
.

Suppose
\pi
satisfies Hypothesis
(\bigstar)
. Then:

(1)

If
\pi
is not endoscopic, there exists a constant
C\geq 0
such that

\varpi^{C}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,n})=\varpi^{C}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,n}(1))=0

for all
n\geq 1
.

(2)

If
\pi
is endoscopic, then for
j=1
or 2 such that
\pi_{j}
is non-CM, there exists a constant
C\geq 0
such that

\varpi^{C}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi_{j},n})=\varpi^{C}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi_{j},n}(1))=0

for all
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

We start with the non-endoscopic case.
For
i=0
or 1, consider the inflation-restriction exact sequence:

(12.1)

\begin{split}0\to H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,n}(i)^{G_{\mathbb{Q}(\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi})}})\to H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,n}(i))\to\\
\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\Gal(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q}(\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi})),L_{\pi,n}(i)).\end{split}

We claim the third term vanishes. Indeed, the
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
action on
\Gal(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q}(\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi}))
by conjugation is trivial, so the third term
is

\Hom(\Gal(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q}(\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi})),L_{\pi,n}(i)^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}})=0

because the absolute irreducibility of
\overline{T}_{\pi}
implies
\overline{L}_{\pi}(i)^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}=0
.
If
i=1
, the first term is uniformly bounded in
n
by Lemma
12.2.5
(
1
), because any
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
that acts as a scalar
z
on
T_{\pi}
lies in
G_{\mathbb{Q}(\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi})}
and acts by
z^{2}
on
L_{\pi}(1)
, so the lemma is proved in this case.

For the case
i=0
, we have
L_{\pi,n}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}(\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi})}}=L_{\pi,n}
. By Lemma
12.2.5
(
2
) combined with
[
30
, Lemma B.1]
, the first term in (
12.1
) is uniformly bounded in
n
, which proves the lemma when
\pi
is non-endoscopic.

Now consider the endoscopic case. Using Theorem
C.3.2
to replace Lemma
12.2.5
, the same argument as above shows

\varpi^{C}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi_{j}})/\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi_{j},n})=\varpi^{C}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi_{j}})/\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi_{j},n}(1))=0

for some constant
C\geq 0
. By inflation-restriction, it suffices to show

\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\Gal(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi_{j}})),L_{\pi_{j},n}(i))

is uniformly bounded in
n
for
i=0,1
.
By the same argument as for the
claim in Lemma
9.2.2
, any Galois-invariant group homomorphism
\Gal(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})/\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi_{j}}))\to L_{\pi_{j},n}(i)
lies in the proper subspace on which complex conjugation acts by
-1
; since
L_{\pi_{j}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is absolutely irreducible by Theorem
C.3.2
again, this suffices by
[
70
, Lemma 2.3.3]
.
∎

Lemma 12.2.7
.

Let
L=L_{1}\oplus L_{2}
be a free
O
-module of finite rank with
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
action, where
L_{i}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
are absolutely irreducible and distinct. Then there is a constant
C\geq 0
with the following property: for any
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable
O
-submodule
H\subset L/\varpi^{n}L
, we have

H\supset\varpi^{C}\pr_{1}(H)\oplus\varpi^{C}\pr_{2}(H).

Here
\pr_{i}:L/\varpi^{n}L\to L_{i}/\varpi^{n}L_{i}
is the natural projection.

Proof.

Write
L_{i,n}=L_{i}/\varpi^{n}
and
note that we have isomorphisms

\frac{\pr_{1}(H)}{H\cap L_{1,n}}\xleftarrow{\sim}\frac{H}{H\cap L_{2,n}\oplus H\cap L_{1,n}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\frac{\pr_{2}(H)}{H\cap L_{2,n}}.

In particular, it is enough to show that any isomorphic
O[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]
-module subquotients of
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are
\varpi^{C}
-torsion for some universal constant
C
. Suppose on the contrary that for all integers
m\geq 0
, there exist submodules
B_{i}^{m}\subset A_{i}^{m}\subset L_{i}
with
A_{1}^{m}/B_{1}^{m}\cong A_{2}^{m}/B_{2}^{m}
not
\varpi^{m}
-torsion.
Rescaling, we may assume without loss of generality that
A_{i}^{m}
has nonzero image in
\varpi L_{i}
, so by
[
70
, Lemma 2.3.3]
we know
\varpi^{C_{0}}L_{i}\subset A_{i}^{m}
for some constant
C_{0}
depending on
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
.

For each
m
, let
N_{i}(m)
be the maximal integer such that

B_{i}^{m}\subset\varpi^{N_{i}(m)}L,

which implies

(12.2)

\varpi^{N_{i}(m)+C_{0}}L\subset B_{i}^{m}\subset\varpi^{N_{i}(m)}L.

In particular, for
A_{i}^{m}/B_{i}^{m}
not to be
\varpi^{m}
-torsion, we must have

(12.3)

N_{i}(m)+C_{0}>m

for all
m
.
Now consider the chain of maps:

(12.4)

\varpi^{C_{0}}L_{1}/\varpi^{N_{1}(m)+C_{0}}L_{1}\twoheadrightarrow\varpi^{C_{0}}L_{1}/(B_{1}^{m}\cap\varpi^{C_{0}}L_{1})\hookrightarrow A_{1}^{m}/B_{1}^{m}\xrightarrow{\sim}A_{2}^{m}/B_{2}^{m}\hookrightarrow L_{2}/B_{2}^{m}\twoheadrightarrow L_{2}/\varpi^{N_{2}(m)}L_{2}.

The two injections in the diagram have cokernel annihilated by
\varpi^{C_{0}}
, so in particular the composite has cokernel annihilated by
\varpi^{2C_{0}}
. By (
12.3
), after reindexing, we may assume without loss of generality that
N_{i}(m)
is increasing in
m
. Then by compactness of
\Hom_{O}(L_{1},L_{2})
, up to passing to a subsequence, the maps in (
12.4
) fit together and give in the inverse limit a Galois-equivariant map

\varpi^{C_{0}}L_{1}\to L_{2}

with cokernel annihilated by
\varpi^{2C_{0}}
. This is absurd because there are no nontrivial maps
L_{1}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to L_{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, so we have a contradiction and the lemma is proved.
∎

Lemma 12.2.8
.

Suppose
\pi
is not endoscopic and satisfies Hypothesis
(\bigstar)
, and admissible primes exist for
\rho_{\pi}
. There is a constant
C\geq 0
with the following property:
suppose given integers
n,m
and cocycles

c\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi,n})
,
\varphi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m})
,
\psi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m}(1))
. Let
(\varphi^{\heartsuit},\varphi^{\circ})
be the decomposition of
\varphi
with respect to

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m})=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m}^{\heartsuit})\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m}^{\circ}),

and likewise for
\psi
.
Then for any
N\geq\max\left\{n,m\right\}
, there are infinitely many
N
-admissible primes
q
such that all of the cocycles are unramified at
q
and:

•

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q}c\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}c-C.

•

We have

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\left(\Res_{q}\varphi,\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m})\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m})}\right)\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\varphi^{\heartsuit}-C.

•

We have

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\left(\Res_{q}\psi,\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m}(1))}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m}(1))\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m}(1))}\right)\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\psi^{\heartsuit}-C.

Proof.

By Lemma
12.2.6
(
1
), Corollary
C.2.8
, and inflation-restriction, the restrictions of
c
,
\varphi
, and
\psi
correspond to
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-invariant homomorphisms

\displaystyle\Res(c)

\displaystyle:G_{\mathbb{Q}(T_{\pi,N})}\to T_{\pi,n},

\displaystyle\Res(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\Res(\varphi^{\heartsuit})\oplus\Res(\varphi^{\circ}):G_{\mathbb{Q}(T_{\pi,N})}\to L_{\pi,m},

\displaystyle\Res(\psi)

\displaystyle=\Res(\psi^{\heartsuit})\oplus\Res(\psi^{\circ}):G_{\mathbb{Q}(T_{\pi,N})}\to L_{\pi,m}(1)

satisfying

(12.5)

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\Res(c)\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}(c)-C_{0},\;\;{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\Res(\varphi^{?})\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}(\varphi^{?})-C_{0},\;\;{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\Res(\psi^{?})\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}(\psi^{?})-C_{0}

where
?=\heartsuit
or
\circ
and
C_{0}\geq 0
is a constant.
We combine these homomorphisms into a map

H:G_{\mathbb{Q}(T_{\pi,N})}\to T_{\pi,n}\oplus L_{\pi,m}\oplus L_{\pi,m}(1).

Let
\pr_{1},

\pr_{2}
,
\pr_{3}
be the projections onto each of the three factors.

For any
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
that acts on
T_{\pi}
as a scalar
z
, we have

\displaystyle\im(H)

\displaystyle\supset(g-1)(g-z^{2})\im(H)+(g-z)(g-z^{2})\im(H)+(g-1)(g-z)\im(H)

\displaystyle=(z-1)(z^{2}-1)\pr_{1}\im(H)\oplus(z-1)(z^{2}-1)\pr_{2}\im(H)\oplus(z^{2}-1)(z-1)\pr_{3}\im(H)

\displaystyle=(z-1)(z^{2}-1)\left(\im\Res(c)\oplus\im\Res(\varphi)\oplus\im\Res(\psi).\right)

By Lemma
12.2.7
combined with Lemma
12.2.5
(
1
), this also implies

\im(H)\supset\varpi^{C_{1}}\left(\im\Res(c)\oplus\im\Res(\varphi^{\heartsuit})\oplus\im\Res(\varphi^{\circ})\oplus\im\Res(\psi^{\heartsuit})\oplus\im\Res(\psi^{\circ})\right)

for some constant
C_{1}\geq 0
indepedent of
n
amd
m
.
In particular, by (
12.5
) combined with
[
70
, Lemma 2.3.3]
, there exists a constant
C\geq 0
independent of
n
and
m
, such that

(12.6)

O\cdot\im(H)\supset\varpi^{n-{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}(c)+C}T_{\pi,n}\oplus\varpi^{m-{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}(\varphi^{\heartsuit})+C}L^{\heartsuit}_{\pi,m}\oplus\varpi^{m-{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}(\psi^{\heartsuit})+C}L^{\heartsuit}_{\pi,m}(1).

Now fix an admissible element
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
, which is possible by Lemma
4.2.3
. By repeatedly raising
g
to
p
th powers and taking the limit, we may assume without loss of generality that

\rho_{\pi}(g)
has finite order coprime to
p
. Choose a basis
\left\{e_{1},e_{2},e_{3},e_{4}\right\}
for
T_{\pi}
with respect to which we have

\rho_{\pi}(g)=\begin{pmatrix}\chi_{p}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(g)&&&\\
&1&&\\
&&\ast&\ast\\
&&\ast&\ast\end{pmatrix},

and set
M_{0}=\Span_{O}\left\{e_{1},e_{2}\right\}
,
M_{1}=\Span_{O}\left\{e_{3},e_{4}\right\}
.
Note that, by Lemma

12.2.4
(
3
),

L_{\pi}^{\heartsuit}
surjects onto the direct summand
\ad^{0}M_{0}

of
L_{\pi}
.

Hence, using (
12.6
), we may choose
h\in G_{\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})}
as follows:

(1)

The
e_{2}
-component of
c(h)
has order at least
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}(c)-C_{.}

(2)

The component of
\varphi(h)
in the
g
-invariant line

\left\{\begin{pmatrix}x&&&\\
&-x&&\\
&&0&\\
&&&0\end{pmatrix}\right\}\subset L_{\pi}
has order at least
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}(\varphi^{\heartsuit})-C
.

(3)

The component of
\psi(h)
in the
g
-invariant line

\left\{\begin{pmatrix}0&0&&\\
\ast&0&&\\
&&&\\
&&&\end{pmatrix}\right\}\subset L_{\pi}(1)

has order at least
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}(\psi^{\heartsuit})-C
.

In particular, because
g
has finite order coprime to
p
, the same is also true for the corresponding components of
c(gh)
,
\varphi(gh)
, and
\psi(gh)
, with respect to any choice of cocycle representatives.

Now suppose
q\not\in S\cup\left\{p\right\}
has Frobenius conjugate to
g
in
\Gal(\mathbb{Q}(T_{\pi,N},c,\varphi,\psi))
. To show that
q
satisfies the conclusion of the lemma, it suffices to observe that the
O
-modules

\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m})\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m})},\;\;\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m}(1))}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m}(1))\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q},L_{\pi,m}(1))},

which are both free of rank one over
O/\varpi^{m}
, are generated by the cocyles

\Frob_{q}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}1&&&\\
&-1&&\\
&&0&\\
&&&0\end{pmatrix},\;\;\Frob_{q}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}0&0&&\\
1&0&&\\
&&&\\
&&&\end{pmatrix},

respectively.
∎

The Selmer groups in the next lemma (and the rest of this section) are the ones from Definition
B.2.5
.

Lemma 12.2.9
.

Suppose
\pi
is non-endoscopic and satisfies Hypothesis
(\bigstar)
, and let
C
be the constant of Lemma
12.2.8
. If

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m})=0

for some
m\geq\max\left\{1,C\right\}
, and
q_{1}
is an
N
-admissible prime for some
N\geq 5m
, then either:

(1)

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})=0
; or:

(2)

For any cocycle
c\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi,n})
with
n\leq N
, and any
M\geq N
, there exist infinitely many
M
-admissible primes
q_{2}
such that

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1}q_{2})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})=0

and

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{2}}c\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}c-C.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume (
1
) does not hold,
and let
\varphi\in\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})
be an element whose image in
\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})
is nonzero; it follows from Lemma
B.2.7
(
1
,
2
) that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\varphi=5m
. Similarly, by Lemma
B.2.8
, there exists an element
\psi\in\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m}(1))
whose image in
\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m}(1))
is nonzero, and we have
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\psi=5m
.
Write

\varphi=\varphi^{\heartsuit}\oplus\varphi^{\circ},\;\;\psi=\psi^{\heartsuit}\oplus\psi^{\circ}

as in the statement of Lemma
12.2.8
.

Claim 1
.

We have
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\varphi^{\heartsuit}\geq 4m
and
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\psi^{\heartsuit}\geq 4m
.

Proof of claim 1.

Suppose that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\varphi^{\heartsuit}<4m
; then by Lemma
B.2.7
(
1
,
2
), the images of
\varphi
and
\varphi^{\circ}
coincide in
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m})
. However, by Lemma
12.2.4
(
2
) we have

H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{1}},L_{\pi,m}^{\circ})=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{1}},L_{\pi,m}^{\circ}),

so it follows that the image of
\varphi
lies in
\Sel_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m})
; this contradicts the assumption
\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m})=0
.

Similarly, if
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\psi^{\heartsuit}<4m
, then
\psi
and
\psi^{\circ}
have the same image in
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m}(1))
. However, the local Tate dual of Lemma
12.2.4
(
1
) shows that
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{1}},L_{\pi,m}^{\circ}(1))\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{1}},L_{\pi,m}(1))=0,
so then the image of
\psi
lies in
\Sel_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,m}(1))
, and this contradicts Lemma
B.2.8
.
∎

By Claim 1 combined with Lemma
12.2.8
, there are infinitely many
M
-admissible primes
q_{2}
such that:

•

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{2}}c\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}c-C.

•

We have

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\left(\Res_{q_{2}}\varphi,\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})}\right)\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\varphi^{\heartsuit}-C\geq 3m.

•

We have

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\left(\Res_{q_{2}}\psi,\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m}(1))}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m}(1))\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m}(1))}\right)\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\psi^{\heartsuit}-C\geq 3m.

Put

\Sel_{\mathcal{F}_{q_{2}}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})=\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})\cap\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1}q_{2})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})

and

\Sel_{\mathcal{F}_{q_{1}}}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})=\Sel_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})\cap\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m}).

Our next claim is:

Claim 2
.

We have

\varpi^{3m-1}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}_{q_{2}}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})=0.

Proof of claim 2.

First, we have the exact sequence

(12.7)

0\to\Sel_{\mathcal{F}_{q_{1}}}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})\to\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})\to\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{1}},L_{\pi,5m})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{1}},L_{\pi,5m})\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{1}},L_{\pi,5m})}\simeq O/\varpi^{5m},

where the final isomorphism is by Lemma
8.2.11
.
Because
\Sel_{\mathcal{F}_{q_{1}}}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})
is
\varpi^{m-1}
-torsion by Lemma
B.2.7
(
3
), but
\varpi^{5m-1}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})\neq 0
by assumption, from (
12.7
) we have an isomorphism of
O
-modules

(12.8)

\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})\simeq O/\varpi^{5m}\oplus T

where
T
is
\varpi^{m-1}
-torsion. In particular, for any
x\in\Sel_{\mathcal{F}_{q_{2}}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})
,
\varpi^{m-1}x=\varpi^{a}\varphi
for some
a\geq 0
. We conclude

(12.9)

0={\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\left(\Res_{q_{2}}(\varpi^{m-1}x),\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})}\right)\geq 3m-a,

so
a\geq 3m
, hence
\varpi^{3m-1}x=0.

∎

Now we are ready to complete the proof of the lemma. We have the exact sequence

(12.10)

0\to\Sel_{\mathcal{F}_{q_{2}}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})\to\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1}q_{2})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})\to\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})}\simeq O/\varpi^{5m}.

On the other hand, for any
\varphi^{\prime}\in\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1}q_{2})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})
, we can compute the global Tate pairing

0=\sum_{v}\langle\varphi^{\prime},\psi\rangle_{v}=\langle\varphi^{\prime},\psi\rangle_{q_{2}}

by the definition of the local conditions for
\mathcal{F}(q_{1}q_{2})
and
\mathcal{F}(q_{1})
. Now, the induced local Tate pairing

\begin{split}&O/\varpi^{5m}\times O/\varpi^{5m}\simeq\\
&\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})}\times\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m}(1))+H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m}(1))}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m}(1))}\to O/\varpi^{5m}\end{split}

is perfect, so we conclude

\begin{split}{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}&\left(\Res_{q_{2}}\varphi^{\prime},\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m})}\simeq O/\varpi^{5m}\right)\\
&\leq 5m-{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\left(\Res_{q_{2}}\psi,\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m}(1))+H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m}(1))}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{2}},L_{\pi,5m}(1))}\right)\leq 2m.\end{split}

In particular, the image of the final map in (
12.10
) is
\varpi^{2m}
-torsion, so we have

\varpi^{5m-1}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1}q_{2})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})=0

by Claim 2. Hence by Lemma
B.2.7
(
1
,
2
),
\overline{\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1}q_{2})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m})}=0
, as desired.
∎

We also have an endoscopic analogue:

Lemma 12.2.10
.

Suppose
\pi
is endoscopic associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
, and let
j=1
or 2. Suppose
\pi_{j}
is non-CM.
Then there exists a constant
C
with the following property: if

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi_{j},m})=0

for some
m\geq\max\left\{1,C\right\}
, and
q_{1}
is an
N
-admissible prime for some
N\geq 5m
which is BD-admissible for
\pi_{j}
, then either:

(1)

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi_{j},5m})=0
; or:

(2)

For any cocycle
c\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi_{j},n})
with
n\leq N
, and for any
M\geq N
, there exist infinitely many
M
-admissible primes
q_{2}
, BD-admissible for
\pi_{j}
, such that

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1}q_{2})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi_{j},5m})=0

and

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{2}}c\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}c-C.

Proof.

The same argument used to
prove Lemma
12.2.9
applies formally, taking
L_{\pi}^{\circ}\coloneqq 0
and
L_{\pi}^{\heartsuit}\coloneqq L_{\pi_{j}}
. When proving the appropriate analogue of Lemma
12.2.8
, one uses the claim in the proof of Lemma
9.2.2
in place of Corollary
C.2.8
, Lemma
12.2.6
(
2
) in place of Lemma
12.2.6
(
1
), and Theorem
C.3.2
in place of Lemma
12.2.5
(
1
).
∎

12.3.
Proof of the main results

Theorem 12.3.1
.

Suppose
\pi
is non-endoscopic and satisfies Hypothesis
(\bigstar)
,
and let
\mathfrak{p}
be a prime of
E_{0}
satisfying Assumption
4.1.1
, such that admissible primes exist for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.
Then for all
D\geq 1

squarefree with
\sigma(D)
even,

\kappa^{D}(1)_{\mathfrak{p}}\neq 0\implies\dim H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}})=1.

Remark 12.3.2
.

To deduce Theorem
C
as stated in the introduction from Theorem
12.3.1
, one needs the following additional observation. Let
\widetilde{\kappa}^{D}(1)_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}})
be defined like
\kappa^{D}(1)_{\mathfrak{p}}
, but allowing all neat level structures, not only those hyperspecial outside our fixed set
S
of Notation
4.0.1
: then
\widetilde{\kappa}^{D}(1)_{\mathfrak{p}}\neq 0\implies\kappa^{D}(1)_{\mathfrak{p}}\neq 0
. This is a consequence of Proposition
3.5.4
and Corollary
2.7.7
.

Proof.

Let
N
be a large integer to be specified later, and let
M\geq N
be the number from Lemma
1.6.3
(
3
) applied to
T_{\pi}
and
n=N
. By Theorem
11.2.6
, there exists a constant
C_{0}\geq 0
independent of
N
and an
M
-admissible prime
q_{1}
such that

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{1}}\kappa^{D}_{n(q_{1})}(1)\geq n(q_{1})-C_{0}

and

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\lambda^{D}_{n(q_{1})}(q_{1})\geq n(q_{1})-C_{0}.

Because
\kappa^{D}(1)\subset H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi})
is a free
O
-module by Lemma
4.1.6
(
1
), we may fix a class
\kappa^{D}(1)_{0}\in\kappa^{D}(1)
, with image
\kappa^{D}_{m}(1)_{0}
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi,m})
for all
m\geq 1
, such that

(12.11)

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{1}}\kappa^{D}_{m}(1)_{0}\geq m-C_{0}

for all
m\leq n(q_{1})
.

Claim
.

Suppose
\dim H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},V_{\pi})>1
. Then there exists a class
c\in H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi})
, with images
c_{m}\in H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi,m})
for all
m\geq 1
, such that:

(1)

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}c_{m}=m
for all
m
.

(2)

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{1}}c_{m}\leq C_{0}
for all
m\leq n(q_{1})
.

Proof of claim.

By the assumption
\dim H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},V_{\pi})>1
, we may choose
c\in H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi})
such that

(12.12)

c+\alpha\kappa^{D}(1)_{0}\not\in\varpi H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi}),\;\;\forall\alpha\in O.

Adjusting
c
by an
O
-multiple of
\kappa^{D}(1)_{0}
and using (
12.11
), we can ensure that (
2
) holds.
By definition, we have an injection

\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi})}{H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi})}\hookrightarrow\prod_{v}\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},V_{\pi})}{H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q}_{v},V_{\pi})},

hence the quotient
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi})/H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi})
is
O
-torsion-free, and in particular
c\not\in\varpi H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi})
by (
12.12
).
Then (
1
) holds as well by Lemma
4.1.6
(
2
).

∎

By Theorem
9.1.3
and Lemma
B.3.6
, there exists a constant
m_{0}\geq 1
such that
\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},\ad^{0}\rho_{m_{0}})=0
. Without loss of generality, we assume
N\geq 10m_{0}
and
m_{0}\geq\max\left\{1,C\right\}
, for the constant
C
of Lemma
12.2.8
. Now consider the following two cases:

(1)

If
\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,10m_{0}-1})=0
, then we choose (by Lemma
9.1.2
and (
1
) of the claim) an
n(q_{1})
-admissible prime
q_{2}
such that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{2}}c_{n(q_{1})}\geq n(q_{1})-C_{1}
for a constant
C_{1}\geq 0
independent of
N
,
q_{1}
, and
q_{2}
.

(2)

If
\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,10m_{0}-1})\neq 0
, then
a fortiori
we have

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m_{0}})\neq 0
.
We choose (by Lemma
12.2.9
) an
(n(q_{1})+5m_{0})
-admissible prime
q_{2}\neq q_{1}
such that

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1}q_{2})}(\mathbb{Q},L_{\pi,5m_{0}})=0
and
