{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{2}}c_{n(q_{1})}\geq{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}n(q_{1})-C_{1}

for a constant
C_{1}\geq 0
independent of
N
,
q_{1}
, and
q_{2}
.

By Theorem
8.5.1
combined with Corollary
B.4.3
,
in either case we can conclude – as long as
N
is sufficiently large in a manner depending only on
\pi
,
\mathfrak{p}
, and
m_{0}
– that

\partial_{q_{2}}\kappa_{n(q_{1})}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})\supset\lambda_{n(q_{1})}^{D}(q_{1})\cdot(\varpi^{C_{2}})

for a constant
C_{2}
that is independent of
N
,
q_{1}
, and
q_{2}
. Hence we may choose an element
\kappa_{n(q_{1})}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})_{0}\in\kappa_{n(q_{1})}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})
,
with images
\kappa_{m}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi,m})
for all
m\leq n(q_{1})
,
such that

(12.13)

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\partial_{q_{2}}\kappa_{m}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})_{0}\geq m-C_{0}-C_{2}.

We now compute the Tate pairing

(12.14)

\langle c_{N},\kappa_{N}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})_{0}\rangle=\sum_{v}\langle c_{N},\kappa_{N}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})_{0}\rangle_{v}=0\in O/\varpi^{N}.

Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
9.1.4
and using that
M\leq n(q_{1})
,
there is a constant
C_{3}\geq 0

so that

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\langle c_{N},\kappa_{N}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})_{0}\rangle_{v}\leq C_{3},\;\;\forall v\not\in\left\{q_{1},q_{2}\right\}.

By (
2
) of the claim, we also have

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\langle c_{N},\kappa_{N}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})_{0}\rangle_{q_{1}}\leq C_{0}.

In particular, (
12.14
) implies

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\langle c_{N},\kappa_{N}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})_{0}\rangle_{q_{2}}\leq\max\left\{C_{0},C_{3}\right\}.

However, by Proposition
4.2.8
, (
12.13
) combined with the choice of
q_{2}
implies

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\langle c_{N},\kappa_{N}^{D}(q_{1}q_{2})_{0}\rangle_{q_{2}}\geq N-C_{0}-C_{1}-C_{2}.

This is a contradiction if we choose
N>C_{0}+C_{1}+C_{2}+\max\left\{C_{0},C_{3}\right\},
and the proof of the theorem is complete.
∎

Theorem 12.3.3
.

Suppose
\pi
is endoscopic, associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A})
(in any order), and
\mathfrak{p}
is a prime of
E_{0}
satisfying Assumption
4.1.1
, such that
H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},V_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}\otimes V_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(-1))=0
.
Assume as well that
\kappa^{D}(1)_{\mathfrak{p}}^{(1)}\neq 0
for some squarefree
D
with
\sigma(D)
even. Then the following hold:

(1)

If
\pi_{1}
is non-CM and there exist admissible primes which are BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}
, then

\dim H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},V_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}})=1.

(2)

If for each
j=1,2
, there exist admissible primes which are BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{j},\mathfrak{p}}
, then

\dim H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},V_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}})=0.

In particular, if for each
j=1,2
,
\pi_{j}
is non-CM and there exist admissible primes which are BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{j},\mathfrak{p}}
, then

\kappa^{D}(1)_{\mathfrak{p}}\neq 0\implies\dim H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}})=1.

Remark 12.3.4
.

The existence of admissible primes which are BD-admissible for each
\rho_{\pi_{j},\mathfrak{p}}
is considered in Proposition
C.4.11
.

Proof.

Let
N
be a large integer to be specified later, and let
M\geq N
be the number from Lemma
1.6.3
(
3
) for
T_{\pi}
and
n=N
. By Theorem
11.2.7
, there exists a constant
C_{0}\geq 0
independent of
N
and an
M
-admissible prime
q_{1}
, BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{1}}
, such that

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{1}}\kappa_{n(q_{1})}^{D}(1)^{(1)}\geq n(q_{1})-C_{0}

and

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\lambda^{D}_{n(q_{1})}(q_{1})\geq n(q_{1})-C_{0}.

As in Theorem
12.3.1
, we fix a class

\kappa^{D}(1)_{0}\in\kappa^{D}(1)^{(1)}
, with image
\kappa_{m}^{D}(1)_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi_{1},m})
for all
m\geq 1
, such that

(12.15)

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{1}}\kappa_{m}^{D}(1)_{0}\geq m-C_{0}

for all
m\leq n(q_{1})
.

Now we suppose we are in case (
1
) of the theorem; assume for contradiction
\dim H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},V_{\pi_{1}})>1
.
By the same argument as for the claim in Theorem
12.3.1
, we have a class
c\in H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi_{1}})
, with images
c_{m}\in H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi_{1},m})
, such that:

(1)

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}c_{m}=m
for all
m\geq 1.

(2)

{\operatorname{ord}}\loc_{q_{1}}c_{m}\leq C_{0}
for all
1\leq m\leq n(q_{1}).

By Proposition
9.2.3
and Lemma
B.3.6
, there exists a constant
m_{0}\geq 1
such that

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi_{1},m_{0}})=0.

Increasing
N
if necessary, we assume
N>10m_{0}
.
Now consider the following cases:

(1)

If
\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},\ad^{0}T_{\pi_{1},10m_{0}-1})=0
,
then choose (by Lemma
9.2.2
)
q_{2}
to be
n(q_{1})
-admissible and BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{1}}
such that

{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{2}}c_{n(q_{1})}\geq n(q_{1})-C_{1}

for a constant
C_{1}\geq 0
independent of
N
,
q_{1}
, and
q_{2}
.

(2)

If
\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},\ad^{0}T_{\pi_{1},10m_{0}-1})\neq 0
, then
a fortiori
we have

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1})}(\mathbb{Q},\ad^{0}T_{\pi_{1},5m_{0}})\neq 0.

We choose, by Lemma
12.2.10
, an
(n(q_{1})+5m_{0})
-admissible prime
q_{2}\neq q_{1}
, BD-admissible for
\pi_{1}
, such that

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(q_{1}q_{2})}(\mathbb{Q},\ad^{0}T_{\pi_{1},5m_{0}})=0

and
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{2}}c_{n(q_{1})}\geq n(q_{1})-C_{1}
for a constant
C_{1}\geq 0
independent of
N
,
q_{1}
, and
q_{2}
.

By Theorem
8.5.2
and Corollary
B.4.3
, in either case we can conclude

\partial_{q_{2}}\kappa^{D}_{n(q_{1})}(q_{1}q_{2})\supset\lambda_{n(q_{1})}^{D}(q_{1})\cdot(\varpi^{C_{2}})

for all
N
sufficiently large in a manner depending only on
\pi
,
\mathfrak{p}
, and
m_{0}
, and
for a constant
C_{2}\geq 0
that is independent of
N
,
q_{1}
, and
q_{2}
. The remainder of the proof of (
1
) is now identical to Theorem
12.3.1
.

Now we suppose we are in case (
2
), and assume for contradiction that there exists a non-torsion class
c\in H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi_{2}})
with images
c_{m}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi_{2},m})
for all
m\geq 1
. By the proof of the claim in Theorem
12.3.1
, we may assume
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}c_{m}=m
for all
m
.
Then we choose (by Lemma
9.2.2
)
q_{2}
to be
n(q_{1})
-admissible and BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{2}}
, such that
{\operatorname{ord}}_{\varpi}\loc_{q_{2}}c_{n(q_{1})}\geq n(q_{1})-C_{1}
for a constant
C_{1}\geq 0
independent of
N
,
q_{1}
, and
q_{2}
.
By Proposition
9.2.3
and Lemma
B.3.6
, there exists a constant
m_{0}\geq 1
such that

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbb{Q},\ad^{0}\rho_{\pi_{2},m_{0}})=0.

By Theorem
8.5.2
(
1
) and Corollary
B.4.3
, we can conclude that

\partial_{q_{2}}\kappa^{D}_{N}(q_{1}q_{2})\supset\lambda_{N}^{D}(q_{1})\cdot(\varpi^{C_{2}})

for all
N
sufficiently large and for a constant
C_{2}\geq 0
that is independent of
N
,
q_{1}
, and
q_{2}
.
The remainder of the proof of (
2
) now follows the proof of Theorem
12.3.1
, using that
\loc_{q_{1}}c_{N}=0
because
H^{1}_{f}(\mathbb{Q}_{q_{1}},T_{\pi_{2},N})=0
.

∎

Appendix A
Cohomology of
\spin_{4}
Shimura varieties

A.1.
The auxiliary quaternionic Shimura variety

A.1.1.

For this section, let
V
be a quadratic space over
\mathbb{Q}
of signature
(2,2)
and nontrivial discriminant character
\chi
. Then by
[
51
, Appendix A]
,
C^{+}(V)=B\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F\eqqcolon B_{F}
, where
B
is an indefinite quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
and
F/\mathbb{Q}
is the real quadratic field associated to
\chi
.
We abbreviate
G\coloneqq\spin(V)
,
\widetilde{G}\coloneqq\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}B_{F}^{\times}
.
Then we have an exact sequence of algebraic groups over
\mathbb{Q}
:

(A.1)

1\rightarrow G\rightarrow\widetilde{G}\rightarrow(\Restriction_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m})/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow 1.

Let
K=\prod K_{\ell}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be a neat compact open subgroup, and let
S
be a nonempty set of primes such that
K_{\ell}
is hyperspecial for
\ell\not\in S
.
Fix a neat compact open subgroup
\widetilde{K}=\prod\widetilde{K}_{\ell}\subset\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

For all
\ell\not\in S
,
\widetilde{K}_{\ell}
is hyperspecial.

(2)

\widetilde{K}\cap\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{f})=K
.

(3)

We have
\nu(\widetilde{K})\cap O_{F,+}^{\times}=(O_{F}^{\times}\cap\widetilde{K})^{2}
, where
\nu:\widetilde{G}\to\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
is the norm character and
O_{F,+}^{\times}
is the group of totally positive units of
F
.

Such a
\widetilde{K}
exists because
S
is nonempty. Let
\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(V)
be the Shimura variety for
\widetilde{G}
at level
\widetilde{K}
.

Proposition A.1.2
.

Under conditions (
1
) - (
3
) above, the natural map

(A.2)

\Shimura_{K}(V)\rightarrow\widetilde{\Shimura}_{\widetilde{K}}(V)

is an open and closed embedding.

Proof.

This follows from
[
69
, Proposition 2.10, Remark 2.11]
.
∎

A.1.3.
Hecke algebras

For a prime
\ell\not\in S
and a ring
R
, let
\mathbb{T}_{\ell,R}

(resp.
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell,R}
) denote the spherical Hecke algebra of
K_{\ell}
-biinvariant (resp.
\widetilde{K}_{\ell}
-biinvariant)
R
-valued functions on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
(resp.
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
). If
S^{\prime}\supset S
is a finite set of primes, then we set

\mathbb{T}_{R}^{S^{\prime}}\coloneqq\otimes^{\prime}_{\ell\not\in S^{\prime}}\mathbb{T}_{\ell,R},\;\;\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{R}^{S^{\prime}}\coloneqq\otimes^{\prime}_{\ell\not\in S^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell,R}.

When
R=\mathbb{Z}
we drop it from the notation.

Proposition A.1.4
.

Fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
.
There is a decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}^{S}
-modules:

H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(V)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\bigoplus_{\pi_{f}}H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(V)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})_{\pi_{f}}\otimes\iota^{-1}\pi_{f}^{\widetilde{K}}\oplus\bigoplus_{\chi}H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(V)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})_{\chi\circ\det}\otimes\iota^{-1}(\chi\circ\det)^{\widetilde{K}},

where
\pi_{f}
runs over finite parts of cuspidal, infinite-dimensional automorphic representations
\pi
of
B_{F}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\times}
with discrete series archimedean components of parallel weight 2, and
\chi
runs over finite order characters of
F^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
.
As
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-representations, we have

H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(V)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})_{\pi_{f}}^{ss}=\otimes-\Ind_{G_{F}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}\rho_{\JL(\pi),\iota}

and

H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(V)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})_{\chi\circ\det}^{ss}=\iota^{-1}\rec(\chi|_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}})(-1)\oplus\iota^{-1}\rec(\chi|_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}})\cdot\omega_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(-1),

where
\omega_{F/\mathbb{Q}}
is the quadratic character of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
associated to
F
.

Proof.

When
B_{F}
is split, this follows from the discussion in
[
113
, §XI.2]
. In the nonsplit case, the Hecke module decomposition is clear from Matsushima’s formula, and the Galois actions follow from
[
59
]
.
∎

A.1.5.

Let
\ell\not\in S
be a prime;
then we may identify
B\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}\simeq M_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
in such a way that
\widetilde{K}_{\ell}=\GL_{2}(O_{F}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
and

G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\subset\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\simeq\GL_{2}(F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
consists of those matrices having determinant in
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}^{\times}
.
Let
T(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
and
\widetilde{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
be the standard diagonal tori in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
and
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
, respectively, and let
B(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
and
\widetilde{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
be the upper triangular Borel subgroups.

Proposition A.1.6
.

Let
\chi_{0}:\widetilde{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be an unramified character. Then
\left(\Ind_{\widetilde{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})}^{\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})}\chi_{0}\right)|_{G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})}
has a unique
K_{\ell}
-spherical constituent, which is isomorphic to the unique spherical constituent of
\Ind_{B(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})}^{G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})}(\chi_{0}|_{T(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})})
.

Proof.

This is clear from the observation that
\widetilde{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\cdot K_{\ell}=\widetilde{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\cdot\widetilde{K}_{\ell}=\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
; indeed,
K_{\ell}\cdot(\widetilde{K}_{\ell}\cap\widetilde{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}))=\widetilde{K}_{\ell}
because
\nu(\widetilde{T}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\cap\widetilde{K}_{\ell})=\nu(\widetilde{K}_{\ell})
.
∎

It follows immediately that:

Corollary A.1.7
.

Let
\widetilde{\pi}
be an irreducible admissible representation of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
which is
\widetilde{K}_{\ell}
-spherical, and let
\chi_{\widetilde{\pi}}:\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell}\to\mathbb{C}
be the character giving the Hecke action on
\widetilde{\pi}^{\widetilde{K}_{\ell}}
. Then, viewing
\widetilde{\pi}
as an admissible representation of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
,
\mathbb{T}_{\ell}
stabilizes the one-dimensional space
\widetilde{\pi}^{\widetilde{K}_{\ell}}
and acts on it via the composite of
\chi_{\widetilde{\pi}}
with the homomorphism
\mathbb{T}_{\ell}\to\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell}
determined by the Satake transform and the map of dual groups
{}^{L}\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}B_{F}^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow{}^{L}\spin(V).

A.2.
Hecke action on Tate classes for
\spin_{4}

For an automorphic represention
\pi
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, let
\BC_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi)
denote the base change to
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
.

Lemma A.2.1
.

Continue the notation of Proposition
A.1.4
.

(1)

If
H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(V)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\pi_{f}}\neq 0
, then there exists an automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, with
\pi_{0,\infty}
discrete series of weight 2, and a finite-order character
\chi
of
F^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
, such that
\JL(\pi)=\BC_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{0})\otimes\chi
.

(2)

If
H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(V)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\chi\circ\det}\neq 0
, then
\chi|_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}}=\omega_{F/\mathbb{Q}}
or
\mathbbm{1}
.

Proof.

Part (
2
) is obvious from Proposition
A.1.4
. Part (
1
) follows from the proof of
[
113
, Theorem XI.4.6(i)]
, except for the assertion about
\pi_{0,\infty}
; but this is clear by
[
3
, Chapter 3, Theorem 5.1]
and the archimedean condition on
\pi
in Proposition
A.1.4
.
∎

Corollary A.2.2
.

The
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-module
H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\Sh_{K}(V),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}
decomposes completely into a direct sum of characters
\bm{h}:\mathcal{\mathbb{T}}^{S}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, each of which arises from the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
on either:

•

A newform in an automorphic representation
\pi
of
B_{F}(\mathbb{A}_{F})^{\times}
, unramified outside
S
, such that
\pi
satisfies the conclusion of Lemma
A.2.1
(
1
).

•

The automorphic character
\chi_{0}\circ\nu
of
\spin(V)(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, where
\chi_{0}
is either trivial or the Hecke character associated to
F/\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

Because (
A.2
) is an open and closed embedding, we have a split inclusion of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-modules

H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\Sh_{K}(V)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))\subset H^{2}_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}},!}(\widetilde{\Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(V)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))

and so the corollary is immediate from Lemma
A.2.1
and Corollary
A.1.7
.
∎

Appendix B
Relative deformation theory and level-raising

In this appendix, we recall the relative deformation theory of Fakhruddin, Khare, and Patrikis
[
30
]
in a format useful for characteristic zero level-raising. In the hope that this discussion will be helpful in future work, we work with representations valued in more general groups than are needed for the main text.

B.1.
Notation

B.1.1.
The group

Let
G

be a smooth, split group scheme over the ring of integers
O

of a finite extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, such that the neutral component of
G

is a connected reductive group. Let
G^{\operatorname{der}}

be the derived subgroup of
G
.
Write
\varpi

for the uniformizer of
O
. We suppose
p>2

and
G

satisfy
[
30
, Assumption 2.1]
. When
G=\GSP_{4}
or
\GL_{2}
, which are the cases relevant for the main text,
loc. cit.
is satisfied for all odd
p
. Let
d_{G}
be the dimension of
\Lie G^{\operatorname{der}}
.

B.1.2.
The Galois representation

Let
k

be a number field, and fix a Galois representation
\rho:G_{k}\rightarrow G(O).

For an integer
n\geq 1
, let
\rho_{n}:G_{k}\rightarrow G(O/\varpi^{n})

be the reduction of
\rho
, and let
\overline{\rho}\coloneqq\rho_{1}
. Also write
{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho
,
{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}
, and
{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho}

for the natural
G_{k}
-representations on
\operatorname{Lie}G^{\operatorname{der}}
,
\operatorname{Lie}G^{\operatorname{der}}\otimes_{O}O/\varpi^{n}
, and
\operatorname{Lie}G^{\operatorname{der}}\otimes_{O}O/\varpi
, respectively. Let
\Sigma_{p}
and
\Sigma_{\infty}
be the set of places of
k
lying above
p
and
\infty
, respectively. We will always suppose fixed a finite set
S
of nonarchimedean places of
k
such that
\Sigma_{p}\cap S=\emptyset
and
\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}}
is unramified for
v\not\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}
. We
make the following assumptions on
\rho
:

Assumption B.1.3
.

(1)

H^{0}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho})=H^{0}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho}(1))=0
.

(2)

\rho

is odd in the sense of
[
30
, Definition 1.2]
.

(3)

For all primes
v\in\Sigma_{p}
,
\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}}

is potentially semistable with regular Hodge-Tate cocharacter
\mu_{v}:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to G
.

Notation B.1.4
.

Recall the category
\CNL_{O}
from (
1.1.3
).
Let
\mu:G\to H=G/G^{\operatorname{der}}
be the maximal abelian quotient of
G
, and let

\chi:G_{k}\xrightarrow{\rho}G(O)\xrightarrow{\mu}H(O)

be the multiplier character of
\rho
.
For all primes
v

of
k
, let
\mathcal{D}_{v}
be the functor on
\CNL_{O}
defined by

(B.1)

\mathcal{D}_{v}(A)=\left\{\rho_{A}:G_{k_{v}}\to G(A)\,:\,\rho_{A}\otimes_{A}(O/\varpi)=\overline{\rho},\,\mu\circ\rho_{A}=\chi\right\}.

The functor
\mathcal{D}_{v}
is represented by a universal deformation ring
\widetilde{R}_{v}
. For
v\in\Sigma_{p}
,
let
R_{v}
be the quotient of
\widetilde{R}_{v}
corresponding to potentially semistable deformations with fixed Hodge type
\mu_{v}

[
4
, Proposition 3.0.12]
; for
v\not\in\Sigma_{p}
, set
R_{v}\coloneqq\widetilde{R}_{v}
.

Our final assumption on
\rho

is:

Assumption B.1.5
.

For all primes
v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}
, the point
y_{v}
of
\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]
defined by

\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}}:G_{k_{v}}\rightarrow G(O)\rightarrow G(E)

is formally smooth.

In particular, Assumption
B.1.5
implies that
y_{v}

lies on a unique irreducible component of
\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]
. Let
R_{v}\twoheadrightarrow\overline{R}_{v}

be the quotient corresponding to the Zariski closure of this irreducible component.
We have the following simple criterion for Assumption
B.1.5
to hold:

Lemma B.1.6
.

For all
v
,
y_{v}
is a formally smooth point of
\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]
if and only if

H^{0}(\WD(\ad^{0}\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}})(1))=0.

Proof.

This is immediate from
[
6
, Corollary 3.3.4]
; note that, in the notation of
loc. cit.
,
\ad\WD(\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}})
is by definition the Weil-Deligne representation associated to
\ad\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}}
, cf.
[
6
, §2]
.
∎

B.2.
Selmer groups and relative deformation theory

Recall that a
Selmer structure

\mathcal{F}
for an
O[G_{k}]
-module
M
is a collection of
O
-submodules (the “local conditions”)

H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},M)\subset H^{1}(k_{v},M)

for all nonarchimedean
13
13

13

Since
p\neq 2
, for all
v|\infty
we have
H^{1}(k_{v},M)=0
.
places
v
of
k
, such that
H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},M)=H^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(k_{v},M)
for all but finitely many
v
. The associated
Selmer group
is

H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k,M)=\ker\left(H^{1}(k,M)\to\prod_{v}\frac{H^{1}(k_{v},M)}{H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},M)}\right).

If
M
is finite and
M^{\prime}=\Hom(M,(E/O)(1))
is the Cartier dual, then
H^{1}(k_{v},M)
and
H^{1}(k_{v},M^{\prime})
are dual under the local Tate pairing.
The
dual Selmer structure

\mathcal{F}^{\ast}
to
\mathcal{F}
is the Selmer structure for
M^{\prime}
defined by the orthogonal complement local conditions.

Proposition B.2.1
.

Let
v

be a nonarchimedean place of
k
. There exists a nonempty open set
Y_{v}\subset\Spec\overline{R}_{v}(O)

containing the point corresponding to
\rho_{v}
, and a collection of submodules
Z_{r,v}\subset Z^{1}(G_{k_{v}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{r})

with the following properties.

(1)

Z_{r,v}
is free
over
O/\varpi^{r}

of rank
\dimension\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]
(
=d_{G}
if
v\not\in\Sigma_{p}
).

(2)

Let
Y_{n}^{v}

be the image of
Y_{v}

in
\Spec R_{v}(O/\varpi^{n})

and denote by
\varphi^{Y_{v}}_{n,r}:Y^{v}_{n+r}\rightarrow Y^{v}_{n}

the reduction maps for
n,r\geq 1
.
Then given
r_{0}\geq 1
, there exists
n_{0}\geq 1

such that, for all
n\geq n_{0}

and all
0\leq r\leq r_{0}
, the fibers of
\varphi^{Y_{v}}_{n,r}

are nonempty principal homogeneous spaces for
Z_{r,v}
.

(3)

The natural
O
-module maps
{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{r}\twoheadrightarrow{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{r-1}

and
{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{r-1}\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{r}

induce surjections
Z_{r,v}\twoheadrightarrow Z_{r-1,v}

and inclusions
Z_{r-1,v}\hookrightarrow Z_{r,v}
.

(4)

Z_{r,v}

contains all coboundaries in
Z^{1}(G_{k_{v}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{r})
.

Proof.

See
[
30
, Proposition 4.7]
.
∎

Remark B.2.2
.

Although
Y_{v}\subset\Spec\overline{R}_{v}(O)

is not uniquely determined by the properties in Proposition
B.2.1
, the property (
2
) shows that
Z_{r,v}

depends only on
\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}}
(by considering the fiber over
\rho_{n}|_{G_{k_{v}}}
).

Definition B.2.3
.

For all
n\geq 1
, we define a Selmer structure
\mathcal{F}
for
\ad^{0}\rho_{n}
by

H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})=\begin{cases}\im\left(Z_{n,v}\to H^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})\right),&v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p},\\
H^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho_{n}),&v\not\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\Sigma_{\infty}.\end{cases}

Notation B.2.4
.

Now suppose given a set
\mathfrak{Q}

of finite primes
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}

of
k

called

admissible
, and, for each
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}\in\mathfrak{Q}
, a quotient
R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}^{\operatorname{ord}}

of
R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}

with the following properties:

(1)

R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}^{\operatorname{ord}}

is formally smooth of dimension
d_{G}
.

(2)

R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}^{\operatorname{ord}}

is stable under the conjugation action by

\kernel\left(G(O)\rightarrow G(O/\varpi)\right).

We also suppose
\mathfrak{Q}\cap(S\cup\Sigma_{p})=\emptyset
.

Definition B.2.5
.

(1)

A lift
\tau_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}:G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}\rightarrow G(A)

of
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}}
, for a complete local Noetherian
O
-algebra
A
, is called

ordinary

if the corresponding map
R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}\rightarrow A

factors through
R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}^{\operatorname{ord}}
.

(2)

For an admissible prime
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}\in\mathfrak{Q}
, a global lift
\tau:G_{k}\rightarrow G(A)

of
\overline{\rho}

is called
\mathdutchbcal q
-ordinary if
\tau|_{G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}}

is ordinary.

(3)

For
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}\in\mathfrak{Q}
, we say
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}

is
n
-
admissible

if
\rho_{n}

is
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}
-ordinary.

(4)

If
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}

is
n
-admissible, then we define
Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})\subset Z^{1}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})

as the relative tangent space to
\Spec R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}^{\operatorname{ord}}\otimes_{O}O/\varpi^{n}

at the point corresponding to
\rho_{n}|_{G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}}
. Let

H^{1}_{{\operatorname{ord}}}({k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})=\image\left(Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})\rightarrow H^{1}({k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})\right),

and let
H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}({k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{n}(1))\subset H^{1}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},\ad^{0}\rho_{n}(1))
be the orthogonal complement of
H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})
.

(5)

If
\mathdutchbcal Q

is a finite set of
n
-admissible primes, then we define a Selmer structure
\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)

for
{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}

by

H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})=\begin{cases}H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}),&v\not\in\mathdutchbcal Q,\\
H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}),&v={\mathdutchbcal{q}}\in\mathdutchbcal Q.\end{cases}

(6)

If
\mathdutchbcal Q
is a finite set of
n
-admissible primes, then we define the
relative Selmer groups
by

\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})=\image\left({\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})\rightarrow{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho})\right)

and, dually,

\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}(1))=\image\left({\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}(1))\rightarrow{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho}(1))\right).

Proposition B.2.6
.

Suppose
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}
is
n
-admissible.

(1)

Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})

is free of rank
d_{G}

over
O/\varpi^{n}

and contains all coboundaries.

(2)

For all
1<r\leq n
, the natural maps
\ad^{0}\rho_{r}\twoheadrightarrow\ad^{0}\rho_{r-1}
and
\ad^{0}\rho_{r-1}\hookrightarrow\ad^{0}\rho_{r}
induce surjections
Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{r})\twoheadrightarrow Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{r-1})
and injections
Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{r-1})\hookrightarrow Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{r})
.

(3)

Let
Y_{{\mathdutchbcal{q}},n,{\operatorname{ord}}}\subset\Spec R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}^{\operatorname{ord}}(O)
be the set of points reducing to
\rho_{n}|_{G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}}
modulo
\varpi^{n}
, and let
Y_{m,n,{\operatorname{ord}}}^{{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}
be the image in
\Spec R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}^{\operatorname{ord}}(O/\varpi^{m})
for all
m\geq n
. Then for any
1\leq r\leq n
, the fibers of
Y_{m+r,n,{\operatorname{ord}}}^{{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}\to Y_{m,n,{\operatorname{ord}}}^{{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}
are nonempty principal homogeneous spaces over
Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{r}).

Proof.

Parts (
1
) and (
3
) are immediate from the conditions on
R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}^{\operatorname{ord}}
in Notation
B.2.4
. For (
2
), it is clear from the definition that

Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{r-1})\supseteq\image\left(Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{r})\to Z^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{r-1})\right),

and equality holds by (
1
) and counting. A similar argument shows the compatibility with

Z^{1}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{r-1})\hookrightarrow Z^{1}(G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}},\ad^{0}\rho_{r}).

∎

Lemma B.2.7
.

Let
\mathdutchbcal Q
be a finite set of
n
-admissible primes. Then:

(1)

For all
a,b\geq 0
with
a+b\leq n
, there are natural exact sequences

0\to{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{a})\to{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{a+b})\to{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{b})

and

0\to{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{a}(1))\to{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{a+b}(1))\to{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{b}(1)).

(2)

The exact sequences in (
1
) identify

\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{a})=\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})[\varpi^{a}]

and

\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{a}(1))=\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n}(1))[\varpi^{a}]

for all
a\leq n
.

(3)

If
\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})=0
for some integer
m\leq n
, then for all
m^{\prime}
with
m-1\leq m^{\prime}\leq n
,
the natural map induces an isomorphism

{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m-1})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m^{\prime}}).

Proof.

Using Proposition
B.2.6
(
1
,
2
), the first part is
[
30
, Lemma 6.1]
, except for the injectivity on the left, which follows from Assumption
B.1.3
(
1
).
Part (
2
) is a corollary of (
1
), because the kernel of
\varpi^{a}:\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})\to\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})
coincides with the kernel of
\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})\to\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n-a})
, and likewise for dual Selmer groups.
So we show (
3
). For any
m^{\prime}
with
m-1<m^{\prime}\leq n
, the map

{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m^{\prime}})\to{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\overline{{\operatorname{ad}}}^{0}\rho)

factors through
\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})
, hence vanishes; in particular, we have an isomorphism

{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m^{\prime}-1})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m^{\prime}})

by (
1
). The claim follows by downwards induction on
m^{\prime}
.

∎

Lemma B.2.8
.

Suppose
\mathdutchbcal Q

is a finite set of
n
-admissible primes. Then for all
m\leq n
,

\dimension_{O/\varpi}\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})=\dimension_{O/\varpi}\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m}).

Proof.

This is
[
30
, Lemma 6.3]
. Note that the local conditions are balanced in the sense of

loc. cit.
: for
v\not\in\mathdutchbcal Q
, this is
[
30
, Proposition 4.7(3)]
, and for
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}\in\mathdutchbcal Q

the same calculation applies because by Proposition
B.2.6
(
1
).
∎

Remark B.2.9
.

The proof of
[
30
, Lemma 6.3]
uses Assumption
B.1.3
(
1
).

Definition B.2.10
.

For any finite set of primes
\mathdutchbcal Q
disjoint from
S\cup\Sigma_{p}
, we define the Shafarevich-Tate groups:

(B.2)

\Sha^{2}_{\mathdutchbcal Q}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})\coloneqq\kernel\left(H^{2}(k^{S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\mathdutchbcal Q}/k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})\rightarrow\prod_{v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\mathdutchbcal Q}H^{2}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})\right)

and

(B.3)

\Sha_{\mathdutchbcal Q}^{1}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}(1))\coloneqq\kernel\left(H^{1}(k^{S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\mathdutchbcal Q}/k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}(1))\rightarrow\prod_{v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\mathdutchbcal Q}H^{1}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}(1))\right),

for all
n\geq 1
.

Lemma B.2.11
.

Suppose given a finite set
\mathdutchbcal Q

of
n
-admissible primes such that

\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})=0.

Then the natural map

\Sha^{2}_{\mathdutchbcal Q}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho})\rightarrow\Sha^{2}_{\mathdutchbcal Q}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})

is identically zero.

Proof.

In the commutative diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sha^{1}_{\mathdutchbcal Q}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sha^{1}_{\mathdutchbcal Q}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho}(1)),}

the bottom map is identically zero because
\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n}(1))=0

by Lemma
B.2.8
. Hence the top map is identically zero as well. But by global Poitou-Tate duality, the top map is canonically dual to the map

\Sha_{\mathdutchbcal Q}^{2}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho})\rightarrow\Sha_{\mathdutchbcal Q}^{2}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho}_{n}),

so this shows the lemma.
∎

Theorem B.2.12

(Fakhruddin-Khare-Patrikis,
[
30
]
)
.

Fix
m\geq 1
. Then there exists a constant
n_{0}=n_{0}(m,\rho)\geq 1

with the following property. For any
N\geq n_{0}
, if
\mathdutchbcal Q

is a finite set of
N
-admissible primes and

\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})=0,

then there exists a representation

\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}:G_{k}\rightarrow G(O)

satisfying:

(1)

\rho\equiv\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}\pmod{\varpi^{N-m+1}}.

(2)

\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

is unramified outside
S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\mathdutchbcal Q
.

(3)

For all
v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}
, the points of
\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]

corresponding to
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}|_{G_{k_{v}}}

and
\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}}

lie on the same irreducible component.

(4)

For all
\mathdutchbcal q\in\mathdutchbcal Q
,
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

is
\mathdutchbcal q
-ordinary.

Proof.

For all primes
v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}
, apply Proposition
B.2.1
with
r_{0}=m

to obtain an integer
n_{0,v}
and a subset
Y_{v}\subset\overline{R}_{v}(O)
. Then let

n_{0}=\Max\left\{\Max_{v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}}\left\{n_{0,v}\right\}+m,2m\right\}.

We construct
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

as the inverse limit of representations
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n}:G_{k}\rightarrow G(O)
, compatible under reduction maps, with the following properties for all
n\geq 1
:

(1)

For all
v\not\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\mathdutchbcal Q
,
\rho_{n}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}|_{G_{k_{v}}}

is unramified.

(2)

For
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}\in\mathdutchbcal Q
,
\rho_{n}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}|_{G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}}

is ordinary.

(3)

For
v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}
,
\rho_{n}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}|_{G_{k_{v}}}

lies in the set
Y_{n}^{v}
(cf. Proposition
B.2.1
(
2
)).

This suffices because
Y_{v}\subset\Spec\overline{R}_{v}(O)\subset\Spec R_{v}(O)
and
\Spec R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}^{\operatorname{ord}}(O)\subset\Spec R_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}(O)
are both closed in the
\varpi
-adic topology, for all
v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}
and
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}\in\mathdutchbcal Q
.
The representations
\rho_{n}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

are constructed inductively,

but
, when constructing
\rho_{n+1}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}
, we will allow ourselves to modify the representations

\rho_{n-m+2}^{\mathdutchbcal Q},\cdots,\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n-1},\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n}.

The base case of the induction is
\rho_{n}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}\equiv\rho_{n}

for
n\leq N
.

For the inductive step, first fix local lifts

\rho_{n+1,v}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

of
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n-m+1}|_{G_{k_{v}}}

for
v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\mathdutchbcal Q
, with the following property: if
v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}
, then
\rho_{n+1,v}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

lies in
Y^{v}_{n+1}
, and if
v={\mathdutchbcal{q}}\in\mathdutchbcal Q
, then
\rho_{n+1,{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

lies in
\Spec R^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}(O/\varpi^{n+1})
. Such choices are possible by Proposition
B.2.1
(
2
) and the formal smoothness of
\Spec R^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}
. Now let
c\in H^{2}(k^{S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\mathdutchbcal Q}/k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho})

denote the obstruction class defined by choosing a set-theoretic lift

\widetilde{\rho}_{n+1}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}:G_{k,S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\mathdutchbcal Q}\rightarrow G(O/\varpi^{n+1})

of
\rho_{n}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}
; we have

(B.4)

c\in\Sha_{\mathdutchbcal Q}^{2}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\overline{\rho})

since the local lifts
\rho_{n+1,v}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

exist for all
v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\mathdutchbcal Q
.

Then by Lemma
B.2.11
,
c

has trivial image
c_{m}

in
\Sha_{\mathdutchbcal Q}^{2}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})
. But, because
n-m+1\geq N-m+1\geq m
,
c_{m}

is precisely the obstruction to lifting
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n-m+1}

modulo
\varpi^{n+1}
. Thus we may choose a lift

\widetilde{\rho}_{n+1}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}:G_{k,S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\cup\mathdutchbcal Q}\rightarrow G(O/\varpi^{n+1})

with

\widetilde{\rho}_{n+1}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}\equiv\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n-m+1}\pmod{\varpi^{n-m+1}}.

For each place
v\in S\cup\mathdutchbcal Q\cup\Sigma_{p}
, comparing
\widetilde{\rho}_{n+1}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}|_{G_{k_{v}}}

to
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n+1,v}

as lifts of
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n-m+1}|_{G_{k_{v}}}

produces a collection of local classes

(f_{v})\in\prod_{v\in S\cup\mathdutchbcal Q\cup\Sigma_{p}}\frac{H^{1}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})}{H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})}.

Now consider the commutative diagram with exact rows coming from the Poitou-Tate long exact sequence:

(
\ast
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(k^{S\cup\mathdutchbcal Q\cup\Sigma_{p}}/k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{v\in S\cup\mathdutchbcal Q\cup\Sigma_{p}}\frac{H^{1}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})}{H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m}(1))^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(k^{S\cup\mathdutchbcal Q\cup\Sigma_{p}}/k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{v\in S\cup\mathdutchbcal Q\cup\Sigma_{p}}\frac{H^{1}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m-1})}{H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m-1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m-1}(1))^{\vee},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{loc}_{m-1}}

where the superscript
\vee

denotes Pontryagin duality. The injectivity of the rightmost map, or equivalently the surjectivity of its dual
{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m-1}(1))\rightarrow{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m}(1))
, follows from Lemma
B.2.7
(
1
), Lemma
B.2.8
, and the vanishing of
\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m}(1))
.

Our next claim is that the image of
(f_{v})

under the central vertical map of (
\ast
) lies in the image of
\operatorname{loc}_{m-1}
. Indeed, because
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n}

satisfies (i)-(iii) above, it follows from Propositions
B.2.1
(
2
) and
B.2.6
(
3
) that the image of
(f_{v})

coincides with the image of the global cocycle formed by comparing
\widetilde{\rho}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n+1}\pmod{\varpi^{n}}

and
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n}

as lifts of
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n-m+1}
. Then because the rows of (
\ast
) are exact, we conclude that
(f_{v})

lies in the image of
\operatorname{loc}_{m}
. Picking a preimage, we may then modify
\widetilde{\rho}^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n+1}

to a representation
\rho^{\prime}_{n+1}:G_{k}\rightarrow G(O/\varpi^{n+1})

with

\rho^{\prime}_{n+1}\equiv\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n-m+1}\pmod{\varpi^{n-m+1}}.

Properties (i)-(iii) hold for
\rho_{n+1}^{\prime}

by Propositions
B.2.1
(
2
),
B.2.6
(
3
) again.
To complete the inductive step, we set
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n+1}\coloneqq\rho_{n+1}^{\prime}
, and

relabel

\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n-m+2},\cdots,\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}_{n}

to be the reductions of
\rho^{\prime}_{n+1}
.
∎

B.3.
Relation to Bloch-Kato Selmer groups

Notation B.3.1
.

Let
\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}\subset\Sigma_{p}
be the set of places
v|p
of
k
such that
\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}}
is crystalline. For
v\in\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
, we write
R_{v}^{\operatorname{cris}}
for the crystalline quotient of
R_{v}
(constructed in
[
4
]
).

Remark B.3.2
.

Recall that
\Spec R_{v}^{\operatorname{cris}}[1/\varpi]
is a union of irreducible components of
\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]
; in particular,
\overline{R}_{v}
is a quotient of
R_{v}^{\operatorname{cris}}
, and
\Spec R_{v}^{\operatorname{cris}}[1/\varpi]
is equidimensional of the same dimension as
\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]
.

Definition B.3.3
.

For all finite places
v
of
k
, and all
r\geq 0
, define

Z^{{\operatorname{rel}}}_{r,v}\subset Z^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho_{r})

to be the subspace of cocycles
c
corresponding to lifts
\rho_{c}:G_{k_{v}}\to G(O[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2},\varpi^{r}\epsilon))
such that the corresponding map
f_{c}:\widetilde{R}_{v}\to O[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2},\varpi^{r}\epsilon)
factors through
R_{v}
(resp.
R_{v}^{\operatorname{cris}}
) if
v\not\in\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
(resp.
v\in\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
).
In particular,
Z^{{\operatorname{rel}}}_{r,v}=Z^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho_{r})
if
v\not\in\Sigma_{p}
.

Proposition B.3.4
.

Fix a place
v
of
k
.

(1)

For all
r\geq 1
, we have
Z_{r,v}\subset Z^{\operatorname{rel}}_{r,v}.

(2)

The cardinality of
Z^{{\operatorname{rel}}}_{r,v}/Z_{r,v}
is uniformly bounded in
r
.

Proof.

Let
I\subset\widetilde{R}_{v}
be the kernel of the map to
R_{v}
(resp.
R_{v}^{\operatorname{cris}}
) if
v\not\in\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
(resp.
v\in\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
).
For (
1
), suppose given a cocycle
c\in Z_{r,v}
which corresponds to a lift

f_{c}:\widetilde{R}_{v}\to O[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2},\varpi^{r}\epsilon)

of the map
f:\widetilde{R}_{v}\to O
determined by
\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}}
. Because
c\in Z_{r,v}
, for all
n
sufficiently large the map

\widetilde{R}_{v}\xrightarrow{f_{c}}O[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2},\varpi^{r}\epsilon)\xrightarrow{\epsilon\mapsto\varpi^{n}}O/\varpi^{n+r}

factors through
\widetilde{R}_{v}/I
; hence

f_{c}(I)\subset(\varpi^{n}-\epsilon)\cap(\epsilon)\text{ in }O[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2},\varpi^{r}\epsilon)

for all
n
sufficiently large. We conclude
f_{c}(I)=0
, hence
c
lies in
Z^{\operatorname{rel}}_{r,v}
, which proves (
1
).

For (
2
), let
\mathfrak{p}\subset\widetilde{R}_{v}
be the kernel of
f
, and note that

Z^{\operatorname{rel}}_{r,v}
is canonically identified with

\Hom_{O}(\mathfrak{p}/(\mathfrak{p}^{2},I),O/\varpi^{r}).

Since
\widetilde{R}_{v}
is Noetherian,
\mathfrak{p}/(\mathfrak{p}^{2},I)
is a finitely-generated
O
-module, hence

\lg_{O}Z^{\operatorname{rel}}_{r,v}=r\cdot\rank_{O}(\mathfrak{p}/(\mathfrak{p}^{2},I))+\textrm{O}(1)

as
r
varies. But because
\rho|_{G_{k_{v}}}
defines a formally smooth point of
\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]
, the
O
-rank of
\mathfrak{p}/(\mathfrak{p}^{2},I)
is also
\dim\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]
(which equals
\dim\Spec R_{v}^{\operatorname{cris}}[1/\varpi]
for
v\in\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
). Moreover

\lg_{O}Z_{r,v}=r\dim\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]

by Proposition
B.2.1
(
1
), so (
2
) follows.

∎

Proposition B.3.5
.

For all places
v
of
k
,
let

H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho)=\varprojlim_{n}H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})\subset H^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho).

Then we have

H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho)\otimes_{O}E=H^{1}_{f}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E).

Proof.

Suppose first that
v\not\in\Sigma_{p}
. Then

\dim H^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)-\dim H^{1}_{f}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)=\dim H^{0}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho(1)\otimes_{O}E)=0

by the local Euler characteristic formula, local duality, and Lemma
B.1.6
(under Assumption
B.1.5
). By Proposition
B.2.1
(
1
,
3
), we also have

\begin{split}\dim H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho)\otimes_{O}E&=d_{G}-\dim H^{0}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)\\
&=\dim_{E}\Lie G^{\operatorname{der}}\otimes_{O}E-\dim H^{0}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)\\
&=\dim H^{1}_{f}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E),\end{split}

and the proposition follows.

Now we consider the case
v\in\Sigma_{p}
. By Proposition
B.2.1
(
1
), we have

\displaystyle\dim H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho)\otimes_{O}E

\displaystyle=\dim\Spec R_{v}[1/\varpi]+\dim\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E-\dim H^{0}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)

\displaystyle=\dim D_{\operatorname{dR}}(\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)/\Fil^{0}D_{\operatorname{dR}}(\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)-\dim H^{0}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E),

where the latter equality is by the proof of
[
6
, Theorem 3.3.2]
and by Assumption
B.1.5
. In particular, by
[
9
, Corollary 3.8.4]
, we have

\dim H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho)\otimes_{O}E=\dim H^{1}_{f}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E).

It therefore suffices to show that
H^{1}_{f}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)\subset H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho)\otimes_{O}E
.

By
Proposition
B.3.4
, we have

(\varprojlim_{r}Z_{r,v})\otimes_{O}E=(\varprojlim_{r}Z_{r,v}^{\operatorname{rel}})\otimes_{O}E\subset Z^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E).

In particular, by the definition of
Z^{\operatorname{rel}}_{r,v}
,
H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho)\otimes_{O}E
consists of cocycles
c
such that the corresponding
G
-valued deformation
\rho_{c}
of
\rho

to
E[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2})
is crystalline (resp. potentially semistable) with Hodge type
\mu_{v}
if
v\in\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
(resp.
v\in\Sigma_{p}-\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
).
By
[
4
, Proposition 2.3.2]
, it suffices to check this condition for the representation
\sigma\circ\rho_{c}:G_{k_{v}}\to\GL_{n}(E[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2}))
obtained by composing
\rho_{c}
with any faithful algebraic representation
\sigma:G\to\GL_{n,E}
.

Consider the case when
v\in\Sigma_{p}-\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
.
Suppose given a cocycle
c\in H^{1}_{f}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)
;
a fortiori
,
c
lies in the kernel of the map

H^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)\to H^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}B_{{\operatorname{dR}}}),

hence the cocycle corresponding to the deformation
\sigma\circ\rho_{c}
of
\sigma\circ\rho
lies in the kernel of the map

H^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}(\sigma\circ\rho))\to H^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}(\sigma\circ\rho)\otimes B_{{\operatorname{dR}}}).

In particular,
\sigma\circ\rho_{c}
is potentially semistable by the argument of
[
2
, Lemma 1.2.5]
, and this completes the proof.
When
v\in\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
, an analogous argument applies, using that
c
lies in the kernel of the map

H^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)\to H^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}B_{{\operatorname{cris}}})

by definition of
H^{1}_{f}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)
.
∎

Lemma B.3.6
.

Suppose
H^{1}_{f}(k,\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)=0
. Then for all
n
sufficiently large,

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})=0.

Proof.

We first claim:

Claim
.

We have
\Sel_{\mathcal{F}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho)=\varprojlim_{n}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n}).

Proof of claim.

Set
Z_{n,v}=Z^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(G_{k_{v}},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})
for all finite
v\not\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}
, and set
Z_{v}\coloneqq\varprojlim_{n}Z_{n,v}
for all finite
v
. It follows from Proposition
B.2.1
(
1
,
3
) and a direct calculation in the unramified case that
Z^{1}(G_{k_{v}},\ad^{0}\rho)/Z_{v}
is torsion-free and
Z_{n,v}
is the image of the map
Z_{v}\to Z^{1}(G_{k_{v}},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})
for all
n\geq 1
. Then the claim follows from
[
76
, Lemma 3.7.1]
.
∎

Now we return to the proof of the lemma.
By Proposition
B.3.5
, the assumption
H^{1}_{f}(k,\ad^{0}\rho\otimes_{O}E)=0
implies that
\Sel_{\mathcal{F}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho)
is torsion, hence trivial because
H^{1}(k,\ad^{0}\rho)
is
\varpi
-torsion-free by Assumption
B.1.3
(
1
).
So by the claim, we have

\varprojlim_{n}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})=0,

which implies

\varprojlim_{n}\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})=\cap_{n}\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})=0.

Hence
\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})=0
for
n
sufficiently large. ∎

B.4.
Controlling congruences for level-raised representations

Definition B.4.1
.

(1)

For each place
v
of
k
, we define

C_{v}\coloneqq\sup_{r}\#\left(Z_{r,v}^{\operatorname{rel}}/Z_{r,v}\right),

which is finite by Proposition
B.3.4
.

(2)

If
\mathdutchbcal Q
is a finite set of
n
-admissible primes for some
n\geq 1
, define a Selmer structure
\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\operatorname{rel}}
for
\ad^{0}\rho_{n}
by

H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\operatorname{rel}}}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})=\begin{cases}\im\left(Z^{\operatorname{rel}}_{n,v}\to H^{1}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho_{n})\right),&v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p},\\
H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k_{v},\ad^{0}\rho_{n}),&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proposition B.4.2
.

Let
C_{1}=\sum_{v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}}C_{v}
, where
C_{v}
is as in Definition
B.4.1
.
Then

\lg_{O}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\operatorname{rel}}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})\leq\lg_{O}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})+C_{1}

for all
n\geq 1
and all
n
-admissible
\mathdutchbcal Q
.

Proof.

This follows from the exactness of the sequence

(B.5)

0\rightarrow{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})\rightarrow{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})\rightarrow\prod_{v\in S\cup\Sigma_{p}}\frac{H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})}{H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n})}.

∎

Corollary B.4.3
.

Fix
c\geq 0
. There exists a constant
C_{2}\geq 0
, depending only on
c
and
\rho
, with the following property: for all
n\geq m-1\geq 0
and all
n
-admissible
\mathdutchbcal Q
with
|\mathdutchbcal Q|=c
,

\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{m})=0\implies\lg_{O}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\operatorname{rel}}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})\leq C_{2}(m-1)+C_{1},

where
C_{1}
is the constant in Proposition
B.4.2
.

Proof.

By Lemma
B.2.7
(
3
), if
\overline{\Sel}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{m})=0
then

\varpi^{m-1}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})=0.

Hence

(B.6)

\begin{split}\lg_{O}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})&\leq\left(\dim_{O/\varpi}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})[\varpi]\right)(m-1)\\
&=\left(\dim_{O/\varpi}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\overline{\rho})\right)(m-1)\\
&\leq\left(\dim_{O/\varpi}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}}(k,\ad^{0}\overline{\rho})+\sum_{\mathdutchbcal q\in\mathdutchbcal Q}\dim_{O/\varpi}\frac{H^{1}(k_{\mathdutchbcal q},\ad^{0}\overline{\rho})}{H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal q},\ad^{0}\overline{\rho})}\right)(m-1);\end{split}

in the second line we have used Lemma
B.2.7
(
2
).
By the local Euler characteristic formula,
\dim_{O/\varpi}H^{1}(k_{\mathdutchbcal q},\ad^{0}\overline{\rho})
is uniformly bounded in
\mathdutchbcal q
, so (
B.6
) becomes

(B.7)

\lg_{O}\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n})\leq C_{2}(m-1)

for a constant
C_{2}
depending only on
c=|\mathdutchbcal Q|
and
\rho
. Combined with Proposition
B.4.2
, this proves the corollary.
∎

Notation B.4.4
.

Let
\Sigma
be a finite set of places of
k
.

(1)

Let
A\in\CNL_{O}
.
A lift
\rho_{A}:G_{k}\to G(A)
of
\overline{\rho}
is called
\Sigma
-good
if:

(i)

\mu\circ\rho_{A}=\chi
(notation as in Notation
B.1.4
);

(ii)

\rho_{A}
is
unramified outside
S\cup\Sigma_{p}\cup\Sigma_{\infty}\cup\Sigma
;

(iii)

For all
v\in\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
(resp.
v\in\Sigma_{p}-\Sigma_{p}^{\operatorname{cris}}
), the map
\widetilde{R}_{v}\to A
defined by
\rho_{A}|_{G_{k_{v}}}
factors through
R_{v}^{\operatorname{cris}}
(resp.
R_{v}
).

(2)

Let
\mathcal{D}_{\Sigma}^{\operatorname{global}}
be the functor on
\CNL_{O}
defined by

\mathcal{D}_{\Sigma}^{\operatorname{global}}(A)=\left\{\rho_{A}:G_{k}\to G(A)\,:\,\rho_{A}\otimes_{A}(O/\varpi)=\overline{\rho}\text{ and }\rho_{A}\text{ is }\Sigma\text{-good}\right\}/\sim,

where the equivalence relation is
\ker\left(G(A)\to G(O/\varpi)\right)
-conjugacy.
By (the same argument of)
[
25
, Proposition 2.2.9]
,
\mathcal{D}_{\Sigma}^{\operatorname{global}}
is represented by a global deformation ring which we denote
R^{\Sigma}
.

(3)

Now suppose
\Sigma=\mathdutchbcal Q
for some finite subset
\mathdutchbcal Q\subset\mathfrak{Q}
. Let
\mathcal{D}_{\mathdutchbcal Q\text{-ord}}^{\operatorname{global}}\subset\mathcal{D}_{\mathdutchbcal Q}^{\operatorname{global}}
be the subfunctor consisting of deformations which are
\mathdutchbcal q
-ordinary for all
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}\in\mathdutchbcal Q
.
By (the same argument of)
[
25
, Proposition 2.2.9]
,
\mathcal{D}_{\mathdutchbcal Q\text{-ord}}^{\operatorname{global}}
is represented by the
\mathdutchbcal Q
-
ordinary quotient
of
R^{\mathdutchbcal Q}
, which we denote
R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}
.

(4)

Given a homomorphism
f_{\mathdutchbcal Q}:R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}\rightarrow O
, define the

congruence ideal

(B.8)

\eta_{f_{\mathdutchbcal Q}}\subset O\coloneqq f_{\mathdutchbcal Q}({\operatorname{Ann}}_{R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}}(\kernel f_{\mathdutchbcal Q}))\subset O.

Lemma B.4.5
.

Let
m\geq 1
be an integer. Then we may choose the integer
n_{0}(m,\rho)\geq 1
in Theorem
B.2.12
such that the following holds:
suppose
n\geq n_{0}(m,\rho)
, and
\mathdutchbcal Q
is an
n
-admissible set
such that

\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})=0.

Let

f_{\mathdutchbcal Q}:R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}\rightarrow O

be the homomorphism corresponding to the representation
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}
from Theorem
B.2.12
.
Then

\Ord_{\varpi}\eta_{f_{\mathdutchbcal Q}}\leq{\operatorname{lg}}_{O}{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\operatorname{rel}}}({\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n-m+1}).

Proof.

Let
n_{1}(m,\rho)\geq 1
satisfy the conclusion of Theorem
B.2.12
, and let
C_{1}
be the constant from Proposition
B.4.2
. We set
n_{0}(m,\rho)\coloneqq\max\left\{n_{1}(m,\rho),C_{1}+2m-1\right\}
, and check the claimed property. Write
I\coloneqq\kernel f_{\mathdutchbcal Q}
. We have

{\operatorname{Fitt}}_{R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}}(I)\subset{\operatorname{Ann}}_{R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}}(I),

so by base change for Fitting ideals,

{\operatorname{Fitt}}_{R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}/I}(I/I^{2})\subset\eta_{f_{\mathdutchbcal Q}}.

Because
R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}/I=O
, it therefore suffices to bound
{\operatorname{lg}}_{O}I/I^{2}.

Now note that
O
-module maps
I/I^{2}\rightarrow O/\varpi^{s}
, for any integer
s\geq 1
, are canonically in bijection with lifts

R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}\rightarrow O[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2},\varpi^{s}\epsilon)

of
f_{\mathdutchbcal Q}
. Taking
s=n-m+1
and using that
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}\equiv\rho\pmod{\varpi^{n-m+1}}
, such lifts are in bijection with classes in
{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\operatorname{rel}}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n-m+1})
. Hence

(B.9)

\Hom(I/I^{2},O/\varpi^{n-m+1})\cong\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\operatorname{rel}}}(k,\ad^{0}\rho_{n-m+1}).

Now, by Lemma
B.2.7
(
3
),

{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n-m+1})

is
\varpi^{m-1}
-torsion. In particular, (
B.5
) shows that
{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\operatorname{rel}}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{n-m+1})

is
\varpi^{m+C_{1}-1}
-torsion, hence
a fortiori

\varpi^{n-m}
-torsion. Since
I
is finitely generated over
R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}
, we conclude that
I/I^{2}
is
\varpi^{n-m}
-torsion.

Thus

\lg_{O}I/I^{2}={\operatorname{lg}}_{O}\Home_{O}(I/I^{2},O/\varpi^{n-m+1}),

and the lemma follows from (
B.9
).
∎

We remark that essentially the same argument shows:

Remark B.4.6
.

The map
f_{\mathdutchbcal Q}:R_{\mathdutchbcal Q}\to O
is an isomorphism if and only if
\Sel_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\operatorname{rel}}}(k,\ad^{0}\overline{\rho})=0
.

Definition B.4.7
.

Let
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}

be
n
-admissible. We say that
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}

is

standard
if:

(1)

There exists a representation
\tau_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}:G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}}\rightarrow G(O)

which is both ordinary and unramified.

(2)

For all
m\leq n
, both

\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})+H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})}

and

\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})+H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})}

are free of rank one over
O/\varpi^{m}
.

Lemma B.4.8
.

Fix an integer
m\geq 1
and let
n_{0}=n_{0}(m,\rho)

be the integer of Theorem
B.2.12
. Let
n\geq\max\left\{n_{0},3m\right\}
be an integer and suppose given a finite set
\mathdutchbcal Q

of
(n+m)
-admissible primes and two additional
n
-admissible primes
{\mathdutchbcal{p}},{\mathdutchbcal{q}}\not\in\mathdutchbcal Q
, such that:

(1)

{\mathdutchbcal{q}}

is standard and

not

(n+1)
-admissible.

(2)

\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})=\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}}{\mathdutchbcal{p}})}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m})=0

but
\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}})}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1})\neq 0.

Then the representation
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}}{\mathdutchbcal{p}}}

constructed in Theorem
B.2.12
is ramified at
{\mathdutchbcal{p}}

modulo
\varpi^{n+m}
.

Proof.

Let
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

and
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}}{\mathdutchbcal{p}}}

be the representations afforded by Theorem
B.2.12
, so that
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}\equiv\rho\pmod{\varpi^{n+1}}

and
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}}{\mathdutchbcal{p}}}\equiv\rho\pmod{\varpi^{n-m+1}}.

Modulo
\varpi^{n+m}
,
\rho_{\mathdutchbcal Q}

and
\rho_{\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}}{\mathdutchbcal{p}}}

differ by a cocycle
c\in H^{1}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1})
. (This make sense because
2m-1\leq n-m+1
.) Also let
d\in{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}})}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))

be an element whose image in
\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}})^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))

is nonzero, which exists Lemma
B.2.8
. By global Poitou-Tate duality, we have

(B.10)

\sum_{v}\langle c,d\rangle_{v}=0,

where
\langle c,d\rangle_{v}

is the local Tate pairing.
By Proposition
B.2.1
(
2
) and by the choice of
n_{0}
,
\operatorname{loc}_{v}c

lies in
H^{1}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}})}(k_{v},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1})

for all
v\neq{\mathdutchbcal{p}},{\mathdutchbcal{q}}
. In particular,

(B.11)

\langle c,d\rangle_{\mathdutchbcal p}\neq 0\iff\langle c,d\rangle_{\mathdutchbcal q}\neq 0.

Our next claim is that:

(B.12)

\langle c,d\rangle_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}\neq 0.

Indeed,
\Res_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}c

lies in
H^{1}_{{\operatorname{unr}}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1})+H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1})
; one can see this by comparing both
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

and
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}}{\mathdutchbcal{p}}}

to the representation
\tau_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}

in Definition
B.4.7
(
1
). Because
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}

is not
(n+1)
-admissible,
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}

is not ordinary at
{\mathdutchbcal{q}}

modulo
\varpi^{n+1}
, so

\Res_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}c\in\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},\ad^{0}\rho_{2m-1})+H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},\ad^{0}\rho_{2m-1})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},\ad^{0}\rho_{2m-1})}\approx O/\varpi^{2m-1}

is nonzero modulo
\varpi^{m}
.
On the other hand,

\begin{split}\Res_{\mathdutchbcal{q}}d\in\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))}\\
=\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))+H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))}{\approx}O/\varpi^{2m-1}\end{split}

is also nonzero modulo
\varpi^{m}
. Otherwise, the image of
d

modulo
\varpi^{m}

would lie in
{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m}(1))
, which contradicts the assumption that
\overline{{\operatorname{Sel}}}_{\mathcal{F}(\mathdutchbcal Q)^{\ast}}(k,{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{m}(1))=0.

Since local Poitou-Tate duality gives a perfect pairing

\begin{split}\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1})+H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1})}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1})}\times\frac{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))}{H^{1}_{\operatorname{ord}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))\cap H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{q}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}(1))}\to\\
O/\varpi^{2m-1},\end{split}

we indeed have (
B.12
). Then by (
B.11
), we conclude

\langle c,d\rangle_{\mathdutchbcal{p}}\neq 0.

Since
\operatorname{loc}_{\mathdutchbcal{p}}d

is unramified, we must have

\Res_{\mathdutchbcal p}c\not\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{unr}}(k_{\mathdutchbcal{p}},{\operatorname{ad}}^{0}\rho_{2m-1}).

Since
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}|_{G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{p}}}}

is unramified, and
\Res_{\mathdutchbcal{p}}c

measures the difference between
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q}|_{G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{p}}}}

and
\rho^{\mathdutchbcal Q{\mathdutchbcal{q}}{\mathdutchbcal{p}}}|_{G_{k_{\mathdutchbcal{p}}}}

modulo
\varpi^{n+m}
, this proves the lemma.
∎

Appendix C
Large image results

Throughout this appendix, let
E
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, with ring of integers
O_{E}\subset E
.

C.1.
Generalities on
p
-adic Lie groups

Lemma C.1.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a simple Lie algebra over
E
.

(1)

If
\mathfrak{g}\subset\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus n}

is a Lie subalgebra that surjects onto each factor, then
\mathfrak{g}
is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus m}
for some integer
m\leq n
. Up to an automorphism of
\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus n}
, the map
\mathfrak{g}\cong\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus m}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus n}
is given by

(h_{1},\ldots,h_{m})\mapsto(\underbrace{h_{1},\ldots,h_{1}}_{n_{1}\text{ times}},\underbrace{h_{2},\ldots,h_{2}}_{n_{2}\text{ times}},\ldots,\underbrace{h_{m},\cdots,h_{m}}_{n_{m}\text{ times}})

with
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{m}=n
.

(2)

The only ideal
I\subset\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus n}
that surjects onto each factor is
I=\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus n}
.

Proof.

We prove (
1
) by induction on
n
, with the case
n=1
being trivial. Supposing we know (
1
) for
n-1
, let
\mathfrak{g}\subset\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus n}
be a subalgebra surjective onto each factor, and let
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
be the image of
\mathfrak{g}
under the projection
\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus n}=\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus(n-1)}\oplus\mathfrak{h}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus(n-1)}
. Then by the inductive hypothesis,
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\cong\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus m}

for some integer
m\leq n-1
. Now,
\mathfrak{g}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\oplus\mathfrak{h}

is a subalgebra surjective onto each factor, so by Goursat’s Lemma for Lie algebras,
\mathfrak{g}
is either
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\oplus\mathfrak{h}
or the graph of isomorphism between
\mathfrak{h}
and a simple factor of
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
. In particular,
\mathfrak{g}
is isomorphic to either
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\cong\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus m}
or
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\oplus\mathfrak{h}\cong\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus(m+1)}
, and it is easy to check that the embedding
\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus n}
is of the desired form using that
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus(n-1)}
is.
For (
2
), it suffices to check that the subalgebras in (
1
) are never ideals unless
n=m
(and hence
n_{1}=n_{2}=\cdots=n_{m}=1
). Indeed, it suffices to check that the diagonal subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{h}
is not an ideal, but this is clear: since
\mathfrak{h}
is simple, it is not abelian, so for some
h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathfrak{h}
we have
[h_{1},h_{2}]\neq 0
. In particular, the bracket
[(h_{1},h_{1}),(h_{2},0)]
is not contained in the diagonal subalgebra, which witnesses that the latter is not an ideal.
∎

Corollary C.1.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be an absolutely simple Lie algebra over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then for any finite extension
E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
:

(1)

The base change
\mathfrak{h}_{E}\coloneqq\mathfrak{h}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}E
is simple as a Lie algebra over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

(2)

For any
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{E}
such that
E\cdot\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{h}_{E}
,
\mathfrak{g}
is simple.

Proof.

For any subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{E}
, consider the extension of scalars

\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\cong\mathfrak{h}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{[E:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]}.

For (
1
), suppose
\mathfrak{g}
is an ideal; then the image of
\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
in each factor of
\mathfrak{h}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{[E:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]}
is a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-stable ideal, hence either 0 or
\mathfrak{h}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
. Now,
\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is stable under the action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, which transitively permutes the factors of
\mathfrak{h}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{[E:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]}
. Hence if
\mathfrak{g}\neq 0
, then
\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
surjects onto each factor of
\mathfrak{h}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{[E:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]}
. Then by Lemma
C.1.1
(
2
),
\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}=\mathfrak{h}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, so
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{h}_{E}
. This proves (
1
).
For (
2
), if
\mathfrak{g}\cdot E=\mathfrak{h}_{E}
, then
\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
generates
\mathfrak{h}_{E}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\cong\mathfrak{h}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{[E:{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}]}

under the action of
E\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{[E:{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}]}
, so
\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
surjects onto each factor. By Lemma
C.1.1
(
1
), we conclude
\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\cong\mathfrak{h}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{\oplus m}
for some
m\leq[E:{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}]
.

If
I\subset\mathfrak{g}
is a nonzero ideal, then
I\cdot E
is a nonzero ideal of
\mathfrak{g}\cdot E=\mathfrak{h}_{E}
, so
I\cdot E=\mathfrak{h}_{E}
. But then
I\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

is an ideal of
\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
that surjects onto each factor of
\mathfrak{h}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{[E:{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}]}
, and, inspecting the possible embeddings

\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\cong\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus m}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{[E:{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}]}

from Lemma
C.1.1
(
1
), we conclude that
I\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
surjects onto each factor of
\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\cong\mathfrak{h}^{\oplus m}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
. But by Lemma
C.1.1
(
2
),
I\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}=\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, so then
I=\mathfrak{g}
. This proves (
2
).
∎

C.2.
Strongly irreducible representations

For the following definition only, we allow
E
to be an arbitrary algebraic extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Definition C.2.1
.

Suppose
V
is a finite-dimensional
E
-vector space,
G
is a group, and
\rho:G\to\GL_{E}(V)
is a representation.
Then
V
(or
\rho
) is said to be
strongly irreducible if, for any finite-index subgroup
H\subset G
,
(\rho,V)
is absolutely irreducible as a representation of
H
.

Lemma C.2.2
.

Let
V
be an
E
-vector space of finite dimension, and let
G\subset\GL_{E}(V)
be a compact
p
-adic Lie subgroup. If
V
is strongly irreducible as a representation of
G
, then:

(1)

No nontrivial element of
G/Z_{G}
is fixed under conjugation by an open subgroup
U\subset G
; in particular
G/Z_{G}
has trivial center.

(2)

The group
G/Z_{G}
contains no finite normal subgroup.

(3)

If
g\in G
acts unipotently on
\Lie G
, then
g

has only one eigenvalue on
V
.

(4)

The Lie algebra
\Lie G/Z_{G}
is semisimple.

(5)

The natural maps
G\cap\SL_{E}(V)\rightarrow G/Z_{G}
and
Z_{G}\to\det G
induce isomorphisms on Lie algebras.

(6)

V
is absolutely irreducible as a representation of
\Lie(G\cap\SL_{E}(V))
.

Proof.

(1)

Let
h\in G
be an element whose image in
G/Z_{G}
is invariant under conjugation by
U
. Then for all
g\in U
,
hgh^{-1}g^{-1}
lies in
Z_{G}
, so by Schur’s Lemma

(C.1)

hgh^{-1}=g\lambda_{h}(g)\;\;\text{for a scalar $\lambda_{h}(g)\in E^{\times}$}.

It is easy to check that
g\mapsto\lambda_{h}(g)
is a group homomorphism
U\rightarrow E^{\times}
. On the other hand, if
\dimension_{E}V=n
, then (
C.1
) implies that
\lambda_{h}(g)

lies in
\mu_{n}(E)
for all
g\in U
. In particular, the homomorphism
g\mapsto\lambda_{h}(g)
has open kernel, so
h
commutes with an open subgroup of
U
. By strong irreducibility and Schur’s Lemma again,
h
is scalar, so has trivial image in
G/Z_{G}
.

(2)

Let
H\subset G/Z_{G}
be a finite normal subgroup. Then the map
G\rightarrow\automorphisms(H)
has open kernel, so (
1
) implies that
H
is trivial.

(3)

Let
g=g^{ss}g^{u}
be the Jordan decomposition in
\GL_{E}(V)
. Then

{\operatorname{ad}}(g)-1=({\operatorname{ad}}(g^{ss})-1)({\operatorname{ad}}(g^{u})-1)+({\operatorname{ad}}(g^{u})-1)+({\operatorname{ad}}(g^{ss})-1)

as operators on
\mathfrak{gl}_{E}(V)
. In particular, if
{\operatorname{ad}}(g)-1

is nilpotent on
\mathfrak{g}\subset\mathfrak{gl}_{E}(V)
, then for
N
sufficiently large,
\mathfrak{g}
lies in the kernel of
({\operatorname{ad}}(g^{ss})-1)^{N}
.

But since
{\operatorname{ad}}g^{ss}-1
is diagonalizable over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
as an operator on
\mathfrak{gl}_{E}(V)
, we conclude
\mathfrak{g}
lies in the kernel of
{\operatorname{ad}}(g^{ss})-1
; hence
g^{ss}
commutes with an open subgroup of
G
, so by Schur’s Lemma
g^{ss}
is a scalar in
\GL_{E}(V)
. In particular,
g=g^{ss}g^{u}
has a single eigenvalue on
V
.

(4)

By
[
11
, §6, Proposition 5]
,
\mathfrak{g}
is a direct sum
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{s}
, with
\mathfrak{h}
semisimple and
\mathfrak{s}
abelian. Since
\Lie(G/Z_{G})
has trivial center by (
1
), it follows that the natural maps induce isomorphisms

\mathfrak{h}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lie(G/Z_{G})
and
\Lie Z_{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{s}
.
In particular,
\Lie(G/Z_{G})
is semisimple.

(5)

The map
\Lie(G\cap\SL_{E}(V))\to\Lie(G/Z_{G})=\mathfrak{h}
is injective with abelian cokernel; hence it is an isomorphism. Since
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{s}
, it follows that the determinant identifies
\Lie Z_{G}=\mathfrak{s}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lie(\det(G))
.

(6)

By (
5
),

\mathfrak{g}
is a direct sum
\mathfrak{g}=\Lie(Z_{G})\oplus\Lie(G\cap\SL_{E}(V))
. So if
\Lie(G\cap\SL_{E}(V))
stabilized any subspace of
V
after extending scalars,
\mathfrak{g}
would as well, which contradicts strong irreducibility.

∎

Lemma C.2.3
.

Let
V
be a symplectic
E
-vector space of dimension 2 or 4, and let
G\subset\GSP_{E}(V)
be a compact
p
-adic Lie subgroup. If
V
is strongly irreducible as a representation of
G
, then every nontrivial closed normal subgroup of
G/Z_{G}
has finite index.

Proof.

Abbreviate
\overline{G}=G/Z_{G}

and
\overline{\mathfrak{g}}=\Lie\overline{G}
. Then
\overline{\mathfrak{g}}

is a Lie subalgebra (over
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
) of
\mathfrak{sp}_{n,E}
, with
n=2
or 4. After replacing
E
with a finite extension, we may assume
E\cdot\overline{\mathfrak{g}}\subset\mathfrak{sp}_{n,E}
is split. It is also semisimple (by Lemma
C.2.2
(
4
)) of rank at most 2, so
E\cdot\overline{\mathfrak{g}}
is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}
,
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}\times\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}
, or
\mathfrak{sp}_{4,E}
. The second case is impossible by Lemma
C.2.2
(
5
,
6
), since
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}\times\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}
admits no faithful irreducible two- or four-dimensional symplectic representation. Hence
E\cdot\overline{\mathfrak{g}}
is simple, so
\overline{\mathfrak{g}}
is simple by Corollary
C.1.2
(
2
). Now if
H\subset\overline{G}
is any closed normal subgroup, it has the structure of a compact
p
-adic Lie subgroup by
[
60
, Ch. III, Théorème 3.2.3]
. In particular,
\mathfrak{h}\coloneqq\Lie H
is an ideal of
\overline{\mathfrak{g}}
, hence
\mathfrak{h}=0
or
\mathfrak{h}=\overline{\mathfrak{g}}
. By arguing with the exponential map, we see that
H
is either finite or has finite index; but if finite it is trivial by Lemma
C.2.2
(
2
), so the lemma is proved.
∎

In fact, we extract the following more precise statement in the four-dimensional case.

Proposition C.2.4
.

Let
G\subset\GSP_{4}(E)
be a compact
p
-adic Lie subgroup, such that the defining representation is strongly irreducible. Write
\mathfrak{h}\coloneqq\Lie(G/Z_{G})\subset\mathfrak{sp}_{4,E}
. Then after a finite extension of
E
:

(1)

E\cdot\mathfrak{h}
is isomorphic to either
\mathfrak{sp}_{4,E}
or
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}
.

(2)

In the latter case
G
is contained in the image of the symmetric cube representation
\symmetric^{3}:\GL_{2}(E)\rightarrow\GL_{4}(E)
up to
\GL_{4}(E)
-conjugacy.

Proof.

We have seen (
1
) in the proof of Lemma
C.2.3
, so we prove (
2
). Let
V
be the four-dimensional defining representation of
G
; as a representation of
E\cdot\mathfrak{h}\cong\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}
,
V
is isomorphic to the symmetric cube. In particular, after extending
E
if necessary, the embedding
E\cdot\mathfrak{h}\cong\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{sp}_{4,E}
is conjugate to
\Lie\symmetric^{3}
by some
g\in\GL_{4}(E)
. We may assume without loss of generality that
g=1
. Let
S\subset\GSP_{4}(E)
be the image of the symmetric cube embedding over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
; we first claim that
G
is contained in
S\cdot E^{\times}\subset\GL_{4}(E)
. Indeed, for any
g\in G
,
\Ad(g)
preserves
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}=E\cdot\mathfrak{h}
. Since the automorphism group of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}
is
\PGL_{2}(E)
, for each
g\in G
there exists
h\in S
such that
\Ad(h)=\Ad(g)
on
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,E}
. In particular
h^{-1}g\in\GL_{4}(E)
commutes with an open subgroup of
G
(by arguing with the exponential map), so by Schur’s Lemma and strong irreducibility
h^{-1}g
is scalar. So
G\subset S\cdot E^{\times}
, as desired.

If
E^{\prime}
denotes the compositum of the finitely many cubic extensions of
E
, then
S\cdot E^{\times}
is contained in the image of the symmetric cube map
\GL_{2}(E^{\prime})\to\GL_{4}(E^{\prime})
, and this completes the proof.
∎

The following lemma is a corollary of
[
16
, Lemma 4.3]
.

Lemma C.2.5
.

Fix a number field
F
, and let
\rho:G_{F}\to\GL_{E}(V)
be a continuous, absolutely irreducible representation of
G_{F}
. Assume there exists a place
\mathfrak{p}|p
of
F
such that
V|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
is Hodge-Tate with distinct weights. Then, after possibly replacing
E
by a finite extension, there exists a number field
K\supset F
and a strongly irreducible, continuous representation
\rho_{0}:G_{K}\to\GL_{E}(V_{0})
such that
\rho\cong\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{F}}\rho_{0}
.

Proof.

After taking a finite extension of
E
, there exists a Galois extension
K
of
F
such that each constituent of
\rho|_{G_{K}}
is strongly irreducible. Write

\rho|_{G_{K}}^{ss}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{j}\rho_{i}

for some
0\leq j<n
. Then the
\rho_{i}
are all distinct because
\rho
has distinct Hodge-Tate weights, so there is a well-defined action of
\Gal(K/F)
on the set of
\rho_{i}
’s. This action must be transitive or else
\rho
would be reducible; hence
\rho|_{G_{K}}
is semisimple and each
\rho_{i}
has the same dimension
m
. Replacing
K
by the fixed field of the stabilizer of
\rho_{0}
, it follows that
\rho=\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{F}}\rho_{0}
.
∎

Corollary C.2.6
.

Let
\pi
be a relevant, non-endoscopic automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\BC(\pi)
(Lemma
2.2.17
) is not an automorphic induction. Then for each isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
with
p>3
,
V_{\pi,\iota}

is strongly irreducible.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.2.12
,
V_{\pi,\iota}
is absolutely irreducible. Suppose for contradiction that it is not strongly irreducible. By Lemma
C.2.5
, we may assume that
V_{\pi,\iota}=\induction_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}\rho_{0}
, where
K/\mathbb{Q}
is either quartic or quadratic and
\rho_{0}
is strongly irreducible and Hodge-Tate. If
K
is quartic,
\rho_{0}
corresponds to Hecke character of
K
with algebraic infinity type via the usual recipe, and
\BC(\pi)
is the automorphic induction of
\chi|\cdot|^{-1/2}
, a contradiction.

If
K
is quadratic, then
V_{\pi,\iota}\cong V_{\pi,\iota}\otimes\omega_{K/\mathbb{Q}}
where
\omega_{K/\mathbb{Q}}
is the quadratic character of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
corresponding to
K
. Hence
\BC(\pi)\cong\BC(\pi)\otimes\omega_{K/\mathbb{Q}}
by strong multiplicity one for
\GL_{4}
, so by
[
3
, Theorem 4.2(b)]

\BC(\pi)
is an automorphic induction. This is a contradiction, so indeed
V_{\pi,\iota}
is strongly irreducible, as desired.
∎

C.2.7.

For the next corollary, we use the following notation. Let
\varpi\in O_{E}
be a uniformizer; then for an
O_{E}
-lattice
T
in an
E
-vector space
V
, we write
T_{n}\coloneqq T/\varpi^{n}T
for all
n\geq 1
.

Corollary C.2.8
.

Let
F
be a number field, and let
(\rho,V)

be as in Lemma
C.2.5
above. Assume, if
\dim V=1
, that there exists a place
\mathfrak{p}|p
of
F
such that the character
\rho|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
has nonzero Hodge-Tate weight. Then for any Galois-stable
O_{E}
-lattice
T\subset V
, there exists a constant
C\geq 0
such that

\varpi^{C}H^{1}(F(\rho)/F,T_{n})=0

for all
n\geq 1
.

Proof.
