Without loss of generality, we can extend
E
so that the conclusion of Lemma
C.2.5
holds, for some finite extension
K/F
and some
\rho_{0}:G_{K}\to\GL_{E}(V_{0})
. Let
K^{c}
be the Galois closure of
K
; then
by inflation-restriction, it suffices to show

(C.2)

H^{1}(K^{c}(\rho)/K^{c},T_{n})\text{ is uniformly bounded in $n$.}

We label the
\Gal(K^{c}/F)
-conjugates of
(\rho_{0},V_{0})
as
(\rho_{i},V_{i})
, for
0\leq i<\dim V/\dim V_{0},

and let

G\subset\prod_{i}\GL_{E}(V_{i})

be the image of
G_{K^{c}}
under
\rho
. We assume without loss of generality that
T=\oplus T_{i}
for Galois-stable
O_{E}
-lattices
T_{i}\subset V_{i}
. Hence to show (
C.2
), it suffices to show
H^{1}(G,T_{0,n})
is uniformly bounded in
n
.

Case 1
.

Z_{G}
contains an element
z
that acts nontrivially on
V_{0}
.

Then by inflation-restriction, we have an exact sequence

0\to H^{1}(G/\langle z\rangle,T_{0,n}^{z})\to H^{1}(G,T_{0,n})\to H^{1}(\langle z\rangle,T_{0,n})^{G/\langle z\rangle}.

The outer terms are clearly uniformly bounded, so we are done in this case.

Case 2
.

Z_{G}
acts trivially on
V_{0}
.

For this case, note that
G/Z_{G}
is a compact
p
-adic Lie group with semisimple Lie algebra;
indeed, if
G_{i}=\rho_{i}(G_{K^{c}})
with center
Z_{G_{i}}\subset G_{i}
, then we have an injection

(C.3)

\Lie(G/Z_{G})\hookrightarrow\prod_{i}\Lie(G_{i}/Z_{G_{i}}),

and the semisimplicity of
\Lie(G/Z_{G})
follows by Goursat’s Lemma and Lemma
C.2.2
(
4
).
In particular, by
[
30
, Lemma B.1]
,

H^{1}(G/Z_{G},T_{0,n})
is uniformly bounded in
n
.
By inflation-restriction again, it then suffices to show

H^{1}(Z_{G},T_{0,n})^{G/Z_{G}}=\Hom_{G}(Z_{G},T_{0,n})

is uniformly bounded. Since
G
acts trivially on
Z_{G}
and
V_{0}
is strongly irreducible, it suffices to ensure
V_{0}
is not the trivial representation of
G
. However, if this occurs then
\rho_{0}:G_{K}\to\GL_{E}(V_{0})
is a finite-order character, so all its Galois conjugates
\rho_{i}
also have finite order. This would mean that
\rho|_{G_{F_{v}}}
has trivial Hodge-Tate weights, which is ruled out by our assumptions on
\rho
.

∎

Lemma C.2.9
.

Let
F
be a number field, and let
\rho:G_{F}\to\GL_{E}(V)
be as in Lemma
C.2.5
above, so after an extension of scalars we can write

\rho\cong\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{F}}\rho_{0}

for a number field
K\supset F
and a strongly irreducible representation
\rho_{0}:G_{K}\to\GL_{E}(V_{0})
. Let
L
be an abelian Galois extension of
F
(possibly infinite) which is disjoint from
K
; then
\rho|_{G_{L}}
is absolutely irreducible.

Proof.

Let
K^{c}
be the Galois closure of
K
; then

\rho|_{G_{K^{c}}}=\bigoplus\rho_{0}^{\sigma}|_{G_{K^{c}}},

where
\rho_{0}^{\sigma}
runs over the distinct
\Gal(K^{c}/K)
-conjugates of
\rho_{0}
. In particular, if
v
is a prime of
K^{c}
lying over the
\mathfrak{p}
from Lemma
C.2.5
, then

(C.4)

\rho_{0}^{\sigma}|_{G_{K^{c}_{v}}}\text{ and }\rho_{0}|_{G_{K^{c}_{v}}}\text{ have distinct Hodge-Tate weights if }\rho_{0}^{\sigma}\not\cong\rho_{0}.

If
\rho|_{G_{L}}
is reducible (after replacing
E
by any finite extension), then

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle<\dim_{E}\Hom_{E[G_{L}]}\left((\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{F}}\rho_{0})|_{G_{L}},(\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{F}}\rho_{0})|_{G_{L}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dim_{E}\Hom_{E[G_{L}]}\left(\Ind_{G_{KL}}^{G_{L}}\rho_{0}|_{G_{KL}},\Ind_{G_{KL}}^{G_{L}}\rho_{0}|_{G_{KL}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dim_{E}\Hom_{E[G_{KL}]}\left(\rho_{0}|_{G_{KL}},\Res_{G_{KL}}^{G_{L}}\Ind_{G_{KL}}^{G_{L}}\rho_{0}|_{G_{KL}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dim_{E}\Hom_{E[G_{K^{c}L}]}\left(\rho_{0}|_{G_{K^{c}L}},\bigoplus\rho_{0}^{\sigma}|_{G_{K^{c}L}}\right).

In particular, we may fix
\sigma\in\Gal(K^{c}/K)
such that
\rho_{0}^{\sigma}\not\cong\rho_{0}
but

\Hom_{E[G_{K^{c}L}]}(\rho_{0}|_{G_{K^{c}L}},\rho_{0}^{\sigma}|_{G_{K^{c}L}})\neq 0.

We claim
\rho_{0}|_{G_{K^{c}L}}
is absolutely irreducible; indeed, if
G_{0}=\rho_{0}(G_{K^{c}})
, then
H=\rho_{0}(G_{K^{c}L})
is a normal subgroup of
G_{0}
with abelian cokernel. Then
\Lie(H\cap\SL_{E}(V_{0}))\subset\Lie(G_{0}\cap\SL_{E}(V_{0}))
has abelian cokernel, which implies
\Lie(H\cap\SL_{E}(V_{0}))=\Lie(G_{0}\cap\SL_{E}(V_{0}))
by Lemma
C.2.2
(
4
,
5
). Then
H\cap\SL_{E}(V_{0})
acts strongly irreducibly on
V_{0}
by Lemma
C.2.2
(
6
), so
a fortiori

\rho_{0}|_{G_{K^{c}L}}
is absolutely irreducible, as desired.

This implies that
\Hom_{E[G_{K^{c}L}]}(\rho_{0},\rho_{0}^{\sigma})
, which is nonzero by assumption, is in fact one-dimensional. It is also preserved by the natural action of
G_{K^{c}}
on
\Hom_{E}(\rho_{0},\rho_{0}^{\sigma})
, because
G_{K^{c}L}
is normal in
G_{K^{c}}
. Hence
\Gal(K^{c}L/K^{c})
acts on
\Hom_{E[G_{K^{c}L}]}(\rho_{0},\rho_{0}^{\sigma})
by scalars, and in particular we conclude that

(C.5)

\rho_{0}|_{G_{K^{c}}}\cong\rho_{0}^{\sigma}|_{G_{K^{c}}}\otimes\chi

for a character
\chi
of
\Gal(K^{c}L/K^{c})\subset\Gal(L/F)
. Because
\Gal(K^{c}/K)
acts trivially by conjugation on
\Gal(L/F)
and
\sigma\in\Gal(K^{c}/K)
has finite order, (
C.5
) implies

\rho_{0}\cong\rho_{0}\otimes\chi^{n}\text{ for some }n\geq 1,

hence
\chi
has finite order. But then (
C.5
) contradicts (
C.4
), so the lemma is proved.
∎

C.3.
Galois representations associated to Hilbert modular forms

C.3.1.

Fix a totally real field
F
.
The following result is due to Nekovar:

Theorem C.3.2
.

Let
\pi
be an automorphic representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
corresponding to a non-CM Hilbert modular form of weight
(2k_{v})_{v|\infty}
, with each
k_{v}\geq 1
. If
E_{0}
is a strong coefficient field for
\pi
, then there exists a subfield
E_{1}\subset E_{0}
and a quaternion algebra
D
over
E_{1}
, along with a finite abelian extension
K
of
F
, such that for all primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
, with residue characteristic
p
:

(1)

The image of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
contains an open subgroup of

H_{\mathfrak{p}}\coloneqq\left\{x\in\left(D\otimes_{E_{1}}E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\right)^{\times}\,:\,\Nm(x)\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\right\},

where the embedding
H_{\mathfrak{p}}\hookrightarrow\GL_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})

is induced by the natural embedding
D\otimes_{E_{1}}E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\hookrightarrow D\otimes_{E_{1}}E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}
and an isomorphism
D\otimes_{E_{1}}E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}\simeq M_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})
.

(2)

The image
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K})
is contained in
H_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Moreover, for any finite abelian extension
K^{\prime}/K
and all but finitely many
\mathfrak{p}
, the image of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}({G_{K^{\prime}}})
is a conjugate of

\left\{g\in\GL_{2}(O_{E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}})\,:\,\det g\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\right\}.

Proof.

Let
K
be the field written
F_{\Gamma}
in
[
81
, Theorem B.5.2]
. Then the first two claims, and the last part when
K^{\prime}=K
, follow from
loc. cit.

For the general case, note that
\det\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K}}=\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}
. If
K^{\prime}/K
is a finite abelian extension, restrict to those primes
\mathfrak{p}
such that
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}({G_{K}})
contains
\SL_{2}(O_{E_{1},\mathfrak{p}})
and
K^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})=K\cap\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p^{\infty}}).
Then

\det\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K})=\det\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K^{\prime}})
. On the other hand,

\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K^{\prime}})
is a normal subgroup of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K})
with abelian cokernel, which necessarily contains
\SL_{2}(O_{E_{1},\mathfrak{p}})
; it follows that
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K})=\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K^{\prime}})
, which completes the proof.
∎

C.3.3.

Now let
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
be two automorphic representations as in Theorem
C.3.2
, with
E_{0}
a common strong coefficient field. For the rest of this section we will always write
p
for the residue characteristic of a prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
. Also,
let
E_{1}
,
D_{1}
,
K_{1}
,
H_{1,\mathfrak{p}}
,
E_{2}
,
D_{2}
,
K_{2}
, and
H_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
be as in the conclusion of Theorem
C.3.2
applied to
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
, respectively; we can and do fix a finite abelian extension
K\supset K_{1}\cdot K_{2}
such that

(C.6)

\det\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K}}=\det\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K}}=\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}

for all
\mathfrak{p}
,
and the conclusions of Theorem
C.3.2
hold for this choice of
K
.

We will also consider the joint representation

(C.7)

\rho_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}:G_{F}\xrightarrow{\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}\times\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}}\GL_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})\times\GL_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}).

Lemma C.3.4
.

Suppose there exists a prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
and a finite extension
L/F
such that

\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{L}}\cong\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{L}}.

Then
\pi_{1}
is the twist of
\pi_{2}
by a finite-order automorphic character of
\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
.

Proof.

By
[
91
, Theorem 2]
, we have

\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}=\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\chi

for some character
\chi
of
G_{F}
, which is of finite order because it vanishes on
G_{L}
.
Viewing
\chi
as a finite-order character of
F^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
via class field theory, we conclude that
\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}=\rho_{\pi_{2}\otimes\chi,\mathfrak{p}},

and hence
\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}\otimes\chi
.
∎

C.3.5.

If
\pi
is as in Theorem
C.3.2
, corresponding to a holomorphic Hilbert modular form
f
, then for any
\sigma\in\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
we write
\pi^{\sigma}
for the automorphic representation corresponding to
f^{\sigma}
.
The following result generalizes

[
71
]
to the setting of Theorem
C.3.2
.

Theorem C.3.6
.

In the setting of (
C.3.3
), suppose
\pi_{1}\neq\pi_{2}^{\sigma}\otimes\chi
for any
\sigma\in\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
and any finite-order automorphic character
\chi
of
\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
.
Then:

(1)

For all primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
,
\rho_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K})
contains an open subgroup of
H_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}H_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
.

(2)

For all but finitely many
\mathfrak{p}
, the image of
\rho_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K})
contains a conjugate of

\left\{(g_{1},g_{2})\in\GL_{2}(O_{E_{1},\mathfrak{p}})\times\GL_{2}(O_{E_{2},\mathfrak{p}}):\,\det g=\det h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\right\}.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to
[
71
, Theorem 3.2.2, Proposition 3.3.2]
, where we replace Lemma 3.1.1 of
op. cit.
with Lemma
C.3.4
above. For completeness, we recall the argument.
Let
G_{\mathfrak{p}}=\rho_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K})
,
so that we have a natural embedding

G_{\mathfrak{p}}\hookrightarrow H_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}H_{2,\mathfrak{p}}.

It follows from Goursat’s Lemma for Lie algebras that
\Lie G_{\mathfrak{p}}
is either
\Lie\left(H_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}H_{2\mathfrak{p}}\right)
or
\Lie G_{1,\mathfrak{p}}=\Lie H_{1,\mathfrak{p}}
, diagonally embedded by an isomorphism
\Lie H_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lie H_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
that preserves the linearized determinant maps to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. To prove (
1
), we assume that we are in the latter case, and aim to show
\pi_{1}
is a conjugate twist of
\pi_{2}
.

By
[
71
, Lemma 1.1.4]
, any isomorphism
\Lie H_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lie H_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
is induced by an isomorphism
i:E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\xrightarrow{\sim}E_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
and an
i
-linear isomorphism
D_{1}\otimes_{E_{1}}E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{2}\otimes_{E_{2}}E_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
. In particular, assuming without loss of generality that
E_{0}
is Galois, there exists an automorphism
\sigma\in\Gal(E_{0}/\mathbb{Q})
that preserves
\mathfrak{p}
and induces
i:E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\xrightarrow{\sim}E_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
.
Since all automorphisms of
M_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})
are inner, it follows from the description of the embedding in Theorem
C.3.2
(
1
) that,
after conjugating
\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}
,
\Lie G_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})\times\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})
is contained in a subalgebra of the form

\left\{(X,\sigma X)\,:X\in\mathfrak{gl}_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})\right\}.

Exponentiating,
for some finite extension
L/K
we have

\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{L}}=\sigma\circ\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{L}}.

Since
\sigma\circ\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}=\rho_{\pi_{2}^{\sigma},\mathfrak{p}}
, Lemma
C.3.4
concludes the proof of (
1
).

For (
2
), we restrict our attention to those
\mathfrak{p}
such that
\rho_{\pi_{i},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K})=\GL_{2}(O_{E_{i},\mathfrak{p}})
(after conjugating) for
i=1,2
, which eliminates only finitely many primes of
E_{0}
by Theorem
C.3.2
.
Let
S
be the set of primes
\mathfrak{p}
as above such that the conclusion of (
2
) does
not
hold; we assume for contradiction that
S
is infinite.
By
[
71
, Proposition 3.2.1]
, for all
\mathfrak{p}\in S
, we have an element
\sigma\in\Gal(E_{0}/\mathbb{Q})
preserving
\mathfrak{p}
such that, after conjugating
\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}
:

(C.8)

\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}(g)=\pm\sigma\circ\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(g)\pmod{\mathfrak{p}},\;\;\forall g\in G_{K}.

If
S
is infinite, then there exists a single
\sigma\in\Gal(E_{0}/\mathbb{Q})
such that (
C.8
) holds for infinitely many of the
\mathfrak{p}\in S
fixed by
\sigma
.

Let
\Sigma
be the set of primes
v
of
F
such that either
\pi_{1,v}
or
\pi_{2,v}
is ramified, and for
v\not\in\Sigma
, let
a_{1,v}
and
a_{2,v}
be the eigenvalues of the standard Hecke operator at
v
on the spherical vectors of
\pi_{1,v}
and
\pi_{2,v}
, respectively. By (
C.8
), for all
v\not\in\Sigma
that split completely in
K/F
,
a_{1,v}^{2}-\sigma(a_{2,v})^{2}\in O_{E_{0}}
is divisible by infinitely many primes
\mathfrak{p}\in S
, hence vanishes. Now take a single
\mathfrak{p}\in S
fixed by
\sigma
such that (
C.8
) holds, and assume without loss of generality that
p\neq 2
.
Let
K^{\prime}
be the compositum of the fixed fields of
\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K}}
and
\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K}}
. Then for all
v\not\in\Sigma
that split completely in
K^{\prime}
,

a_{1,v}\equiv\sigma(a_{2,v})\not\equiv 0\pmod{\mathfrak{p}},

so the identity
a_{1,v}^{2}-\sigma(a_{2,v})^{2}=0
implies
a_{1,v}=\sigma(a_{2,v})
. In particular, the traces of
\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K^{\prime}}}
and
\sigma\circ\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K^{\prime}}}
coincide, so
we have

\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K^{\prime}}}\cong\sigma\circ\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K^{\prime}}}.
Now we can conclude by Lemma
C.3.4
.

∎

We also have the following complementary result.

Corollary C.3.7
.

In the setting of (
C.3.3
), there exists a finite abelian extension
L
of
K
with the following property:

(1)

For all primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
, either
\rho_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{L})
is an open subgroup of
H_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}H_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
, or there exists an isomorphism
\sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}:E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\xrightarrow{\sim}E_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
and a
\sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
-linear isomorphism
i_{\mathfrak{p}}:D_{1}\otimes_{E_{1}}E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{2}\otimes_{E_{2}}E_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
such that
\rho_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{L})
is an open subgroup of

H_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\xhookrightarrow{\operatorname{id},i_{\mathfrak{p}}}H_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}H_{2,\mathfrak{p}}.

(2)

For all but finitely many
\mathfrak{p}
,
either the image of
\rho_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{L})
is a conjugate
of

\left\{(g_{1},g_{2})\in\GL_{2}(O_{E_{1},\mathfrak{p}})\times\GL_{2}(O_{E_{2},\mathfrak{p}}):\,\det g=\det h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\right\},

or there exists an isomorphism
\sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}:E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}\xrightarrow{\sim}E_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
such that
\rho_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{L})
is a conjugate of

\left\{(g,\sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}(g))\in\GL_{2}(O_{E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}})\times\GL_{2}(O_{E_{2,\mathfrak{p}}})\,:\,g\in\GL_{2}(O_{E_{1,\mathfrak{p}}}),\,\det g\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\right\}.

Proof.

By Theorem
C.3.6
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\pi_{1}\cong\pi_{2}^{\sigma}\otimes\chi
for some
\sigma\in\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
and some finite-order automorphic character
\chi
of
\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
, which we also view as a character of
G_{F}
via class field theory.
Then for all primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
,

\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{E_{0,\mathfrak{p}},\sigma}E_{0,\sigma(\mathfrak{p})}\cong\rho_{\pi_{2}^{\sigma},\sigma(\mathfrak{p})}\cong\rho_{\pi_{1},\sigma(\mathfrak{p})}\otimes\chi.

By
[
81
, Theorem B.4.10]
, for all but finitely many
\mathfrak{p}
the image of
\rho_{\pi_{2}^{\sigma},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K_{0}})
is one of the groups listed in part (
2
), for a certain abelian extension
K_{0}
of
F
such that
\det_{\rho_{\pi_{2}},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K_{0}}}=\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}
. If

L
is the compositum of
K_{0}
with
K
and with the fixed field of the kernel of
\chi
,
it follows from the same argument as in Theorem
C.3.2
that the image of
\rho_{\pi_{2}^{\sigma},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{L})=\rho_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{L})
coincides with that of
\rho_{\pi_{2}^{\sigma},\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K_{0}})
for all but finitely many
\mathfrak{p}
, and this proves (
2
).
∎

C.4.
Large image for relevant representations

C.4.1.

Fix a relevant automorphic representation
\pi
of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, with trivial central character and with strong coefficient field
E_{0}
. In this subsection, we prove some results on the image of the Galois representation
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
associated to
\pi
, with an eye towards studying the existence of admissible elements (Definition
4.2.1
) and assumption
(R1)
from (
9.3.1
). Throughout this section we write
p
for the residue characteristic of a prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
.

Lemma C.4.2
.

Suppose
\pi
is not endoscopic, and
\BC(\pi)
is the symmetric cube lift of a non-CM automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Consider the map of algebraic groups

f=\Sym^{3}\otimes\debt^{-1}:\GL_{2}\to\GSP_{4}.

For all but finitely many primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
, the image of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
contains a conjugate of
f(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
. In particular, for all but finitely many
\mathfrak{p}
, admissible elements exist for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.2.18
,
\pi_{0,\infty}
is discrete series of weight 2.
Without loss of generality, extend
E_{0}
so that it is also a strong coefficient field for
\pi_{0}
.
Then for all primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
,

\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}=\Sym^{3}\rho_{\pi_{0},\mathfrak{p}}(-1)
. Comparing similitude characters, we see that the central character of
\pi_{0}
is cubic; by twisting, we may assume without loss of generality that it is trivial.
Then the claim about the image of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
follows from
[
95
, Theorem 3.1]
.
Restricting to these
\mathfrak{p}
, if
p
is sufficiently large we may fix
z\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
with

(C.9)

z\not\equiv\pm 1,\pm z^{3},z^{6},z^{-3}\pmod{p},\;\;z^{12}\not\equiv 1\pmod{p}.

Then applying
f
to the diagonal matrix
\begin{pmatrix}z&0\\
0&z^{2}\end{pmatrix}
, it follows that
the image of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
contains a matrix with eigenvalues
\left\{1,z,z^{2},z^{3}\right\}
; in particular, admissible elements exist for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.
∎

Lemma C.4.3
.

Suppose
\pi
is not endoscopic, and
\BC(\pi)
is neither a (weak) symmetric cube lift nor a (weak) automorphic induction. For all but finitely many primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
:

(1)

The image of
\overline{\rho}_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
contains a conjugate of
\SP_{4}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
.

(2)

If
E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, the image of
\overline{\rho}_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
is a conjugate of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
.

Moreover, for all primes
\mathfrak{p}
with
p>3
, the Zariski closure (over
E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}
) of the image of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
is equal to
\GSP_{4}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})
.

Proof.

Part (
1
) is
[
124
, Theorem 1.2(ii)]
. Part (
2
) follows immediately.
For the final claim, by Proposition
C.2.4
(
2
) and Corollary
C.2.6
, it suffices to rule out the case that
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
factors through the image of the symmetric cube representation
\GL_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})/\mu_{3}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})\hookrightarrow\GSP_{4}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})
. If so, then by a theorem of Tate
[
88
, Theorem 2.1.1]
, after extending
E_{0}
if necessary there exists a representation
\rho_{0}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\GL_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})
such that
\Sym^{3}\rho_{0}=\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(1)\otimes\tau
for a character
\tau:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
. Comparing the similitude factors,
(\det\rho_{0})^{3}=\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}^{3}\otimes\tau^{2}
; replacing
\rho_{0}
with
\rho_{0}\otimes(\tau^{-1}\det\rho_{0}/\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}})
, we can assume

\Sym^{3}\rho_{0}=\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(1)
.
Now it follows that

\det\rho_{0}/\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}
is cubic; so after twisting, we may assume without loss of generality that
\det\rho_{0}=\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}
.
For all but finitely many primes
\ell
, we have
\rho_{0}(I_{\ell})\subset\mu_{3}
, so the determinant condition implies
\rho_{0}
is unramified almost everywhere.
We also claim
\rho_{0}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is de Rham: indeed, by

[
88
, Corollary 3.2.13]
there exists a character
\tau
of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
such that
\rho_{0}\otimes\tau
is de Rham, so
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(1)\otimes\tau^{3}
is de Rham, hence
\tau^{3}
is de Rham; also
\det\rho_{0}\otimes\tau^{2}=\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}\otimes\tau^{2}
is de Rham, so
\tau^{2}
is de Rham. It follows that
\tau
is de Rham, so
\rho_{0}
is as well.
Since
\rho_{0}
is also clearly odd,
[
87
, Theorem 1.0.4]
implies
\rho_{0}
arises from a modular form, hence
\BC(\pi)
is a symmetric cube lift, and this concludes the proof of the final claim.
∎

C.4.4.

For an automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
as in Theorem
C.3.2
with
K/\mathbb{Q}
real quadratic, let
\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}
denote the
\Gal(K/\mathbb{Q})
-twist. We say
\pi_{0}
is
exceptional

if there exists
\sigma\in\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
and a finite-order automorphic character
\chi
of
\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\times}
such that

\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}\cong\pi_{0}^{\sigma}\otimes\chi.

Lemma C.4.5
.

Suppose
\pi
is not endoscopic, and
\BC(\pi)
is the automorphic induction of a non-CM automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
with
K/\mathbb{Q}
real quadratic. Then
for all but finitely many primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
, the following hold.

(1)

The image of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
contains a conjugate of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, embedded diagonally via

\GL_{2}\hookrightarrow\GL_{2}\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\GL_{2}\hookrightarrow\GSP_{4}.

(2)

If
p
splits in
K
or
\pi_{0}
is not exceptional,
the image of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
contains a conjugate of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Proof.

Recall from Lemma
2.2.19
that
\pi_{0}
is the automorphic representation associated to a Hilbert modular form of weights
(2,4)
. Assume without loss of generality that
E_{0}
is Galois and is also a strong coefficient field for
\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}
; then

\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K}}=\rho_{\pi_{0},\mathfrak{p}}\oplus\rho_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}},\mathfrak{p}}.

Hence part (
1
) follows from Corollary
C.3.7
(
2
). For part (
2
), the non-exceptional case is immediate from Theorem
C.3.6
(
2
), so suppose without loss of generality that
p
splits in
K
.
Then for any
\sigma\in\Gal(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and any fixed embedding
j:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, the Hodge-Tate weights of
\rho_{\pi_{0},\mathfrak{p}}
and
\sigma\circ\rho_{\pi_{0},\mathfrak{p}}
with respect to
j
coincide. This rules out that
\rho_{\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{L}}\cong\sigma\circ\rho_{\pi_{0},\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{L}}

for any finite extension
L/K
, so by Corollary
C.3.7
(
2
), we obtain (
2
).
∎

Lemma C.4.6
.

Suppose
\BC(\pi)
is the automorphic induction of an automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
with
K/\mathbb{Q}
imaginary quadratic, and
\pi_{0}
is not an automorphic induction. Then for all but finitely many primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
:

(1)

The image of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
contains a conjugate of

\SL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, where
\SL_{2}\hookrightarrow\SP_{4}
is embedded into the Levi factor of a Siegel parabolic.

(2)

If
p
splits in
K
, then admissible elements exist for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.2.20
,
after possibly extending
E_{0}
we can write

(C.10)

\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K}}=\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}\oplus\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}}

for all
\mathfrak{p}
, where:

•

\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}}
is the Galois representation attached to a classical modular form
f
of weight
k=2
or 3 with coefficients in
E_{0}
; here the normalization is as usual, i.e.
\det\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}}=\omega_{f}\cdot\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}^{k-1}
where
\omega_{f}
is the nebentype character of
f
, viewed as a character of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
via class field theory.

•

\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the
G_{K}
-representation attached to an algebraic Hecke character
\chi^{\prime}
of infinity type
(-1,3-k)
.

•

\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}}
is the
\Gal(K/\mathbb{Q})
-twist of
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
, which is also associated to the twist
(\chi^{\prime})^{\operatorname{tw}}
.

The symplectic form in (
C.10
) is given by the natural pairing

\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}}\to\det\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}}=\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}.

Let
L
be the fixed field of
\omega_{f}
, which is independent of
\mathfrak{p}
.
After discarding finitely many primes
\mathfrak{p}
and changing basis, we may assume by
[
95
, Theorem 3.1]
that

\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{L})=\left\{g\in\GL_{2}(O)\,:\,\det g\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{k-1}\right\},

where
O
is the ring of integers of
a subfield of
E_{0,\mathfrak{p}}
.
Then
the Galois group

\Gal(L(\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}})\cap LK(\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}},\chi_{\mathfrak{p}},\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}},\omega_{f})/L(\det(\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}})))

is a solvable quotient of
\SL_{2}(O)
, hence trivial if
p
is sufficiently large; so we have

(C.11)

L(\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}})\cap LK(\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}},\chi_{\mathfrak{p}},\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}},\omega_{f})=L(\det(\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}})).

In particular, this immediately implies (
1
).

For (
2
), we further restrict to those
\mathfrak{p}
such that
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is crystalline at all primes above
p
.
Fix a prime
v|p
of
K
, and let
\overline{v}
be its complex conjugate, with inertia subgroups
I_{v},I_{\overline{v}}\subset G_{K}^{\ab}
; these are disjoint and each naturally identified with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
since we are assuming
p
is split (and unramified) in
K
. When restricted to inertia, the characters
\chi_{p}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
,
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}}
have the form:

\displaystyle\chi_{p}^{\operatorname{cyc}}|_{I_{v}\times I_{\overline{v}}}\,:\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto z_{1}z_{2},

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}|_{I_{v}\times I_{\overline{v}}}\,:\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\subset O_{E,\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto z_{1}^{-1}z_{2}^{3-k}

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}}|_{I_{v}\times I_{\overline{v}}}\,:\,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\subset O_{E,\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto z_{1}^{3-k}z_{2}^{-1}.

In particular, one can calculate that, for
p
unramified in
L
, the image of

(\chi_{\mathfrak{p}},\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}},\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}):G_{L}\to O_{E_{0},\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}\times O_{E_{0},\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}

contains a subgroup of

\left\{(a,b,c)\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{3}\,:\,ab=c^{2-k}\right\}

with index at most 2.
Comparing with (
C.11
), we see that the image of

(C.12)

(\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}},\chi_{\mathfrak{p}},\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}}):G_{L}\to\GL_{2}(O)\times O_{E_{0},\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}\times O_{E_{0},\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}

contains a subgroup of

(C.13)

\left\{(g,x,y)\in\GL_{2}(O)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\,:\,\det g\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{k-1},\,(xy)^{(k-1)}=(\det g)^{2-k}\right\}

with index at most
2
.
Let
n\coloneqq 2(k-1)+2(k-2)=4k-6
, and set

S_{n}\coloneqq\left\{(g,c)\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\,:\,c,\det g\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{n}\right\}.

Then for any
(g,c)\in S_{n}
, there exists
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
satisfying

\lambda^{n}=(\det g)^{k-2}c^{1-k}.

It follows from (
C.13
) that
(g\lambda^{-1},\lambda,c\lambda)^{2}
lies in the image of (
C.12
); hence
(g,cg)^{2}
lies in the image of
(\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\chi_{\mathfrak{p}},\rho_{f,\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\operatorname{tw}}):G_{L}\to\GL_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})\times\GL_{2}(E_{0,\mathfrak{p}})
for all
(g,c)\in S_{n}
. If
p
is sufficiently large, this immediately implies that admissible elements exist for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.
∎

Lemma C.4.7
.

Suppose
\pi
is not endoscopic, and
\BC(\pi)
is the (weak) automorphic induction of a Hecke character
\chi_{0}
of a quartic field
K\subset\mathbb{C}
. Then there exists a constant
n
such that, for all but finitely many primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
, the following holds:

(1)

\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}})
contains the scalar subgroup
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{n}\subset\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

(2)

If
p
splits completely in the Galois closure
K^{c}
of
K
, then

\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}})
contains a conjugate of

\left\{\begin{pmatrix}x&&&\\
&y&&\\
&&z&\\
&&&xz/y\end{pmatrix}\,:\,x,y,z\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{n}\right\}\subset\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Proof.

Let
\chi\coloneqq\chi_{0}|\cdot|^{1/2}
. From Theorem
2.2.10
(
1
), we see that the local component
\chi_{v}
of
\chi
takes algebraic values on
K_{v}^{\times}
for cofinitely many primes
v
of
K
; hence
\chi_{\infty}
is algebraic
[
118
, Théorème 3.1]
. Extending
E_{0}
if necessary, for all primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
we have the
\mathfrak{p}
-adic character
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
associated to
\chi
, and
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}=\Ind_{G_{K}}^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
for all
\mathfrak{p}
. We restrict to those
\mathfrak{p}
such that
K^{c}/\mathbb{Q}
is unramified at
p
, and
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is crystalline at all primes
v|p
.
The Hodge-Tate weights of
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
with respect to the four embeddings
i:K\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
are
\left\{-1,0,1,2\right\}
in some order by Theorem
2.2.10
(
2
); hence on the subgroup

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\hookrightarrow(O_{K}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\hookrightarrow G_{K}^{\ab},

\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is given by
z\mapsto z^{-1+0+1+2}=z^{2}
.
In particular, on the subgroup

\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\hookrightarrow(O_{K^{c}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\hookrightarrow G_{K^{c}}^{\ab},

\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is given by
z\mapsto z^{2[K^{c}:K]}
. The same is true for all
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-conjugates of
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
, so the image of
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K^{c}}}
contains the scalar subgroup
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{2[K^{c}:K]}
, proving (
1
).

For (
2
),
we decompose

(C.14)

\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K^{c}}}=\chi_{1}\oplus\chi_{2}\oplus\chi_{3}\oplus\chi_{4},

where all of the characters
\chi_{j}
are Galois conjugates of
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}|_{G_{K^{c}}}
and

(C.15)

\chi_{1}\cdot\chi_{2}=\chi_{3}\cdot\chi_{4}=\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}.

For each
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K^{c}})

contains the image of

(C.16)

\begin{pmatrix}g\cdot\chi_{1}&&&\\
&g\cdot\chi_{2}&&\\
&&g\cdot\chi_{3}\\
&&&g\cdot\chi_{4}\end{pmatrix}.

Fix an embedding
i:K^{c}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and
for any Hodge-Tate character
\rho
of
G_{K^{c}}
, let
\HT(\rho)
denote the Hodge-Tate weight with respect to
i
. Let
I_{p}\subset G_{K^{c}}
be the inertia subgroup for the prime induced by
i
.
In particular, restricting (
C.16
) to
I_{p}
and using that each
\chi_{j}
is crystalline at primes above
p
,
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(G_{K^{c}})

contains the image of

\begin{split}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}&\to\GL_{4}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\\
z&\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}z^{\HT(g\cdot\chi_{1})}&&&\\
&z^{\HT(g\cdot\chi_{2})}&&\\
&&z^{\HT(g\cdot\chi_{3})}&\\
&&&z^{\HT(g\cdot\chi_{4})}\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

Let

(C.17)

L\subset\left\{(x,y,z,w)\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\,:\,x+y=z+w\right\}

be the sublattice spanned by the vectors
(\HT(g\cdot\chi_{1}),\HT(g\cdot\chi_{2}),\HT(g\cdot\chi_{3}),\HT(g\cdot\chi_{4}))
for
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Claim
.

For a constant
n\geq 1
independent of
\mathfrak{p}
, the lattice
L
contains

n\cdot\left\{(x,y,z,w)\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\,:\,x+y=z+w\right\}.

Note that the claim implies the lemma, because, as long as
p
is sufficiently large, there exists
z\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{n}

satisfying (
C.9
);
an element
h\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(h)
has eigenvalues
\left\{1,z^{3},z,z^{2}\right\}
is admissible for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
, and the claim implies such elements exist.

Now we prove the claim. Let
\pr:\mathbb{Z}^{4}\to\mathbb{Z}^{3}
be the projection onto the first three factors, and note that it suffices to show
\pr(L)
contains
n\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{3}
.
Without loss of generality, suppose the Hodge-Tate weights of
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
,
\chi_{3}
, and
\chi_{4}
are 1, 0, 2, and
-1
(in order).
Because the action of
G_{K^{c}}
on the set
\left\{\chi_{1},\chi_{2},\chi_{3},\chi_{4}\right\}
is transitive, for each
j\in 1,\ldots,4
we have some
g_{j}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
\HT(g_{j}\chi_{j})=1
. In particular, using (
C.15
),
\pr(L)
contains
(1,0,2)
; a vector
e=(0,1,2)
or
e=(0,1,-1)
; and a vector
f=(2,-1,1)
or
f=(-1,2,1)
. In particular, the set
\left\{(1,0,2),e,f\right\}
is always linearly independent; and, since there are only four total possibilities for this set, there exists
n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that the
\mathbb{Z}
-span of
(1,0,2)
,
e
, and
f
always contains
n\mathbb{Z}
.

∎

Now we are ready to consider assumption
(R1)
from the main text (see (
9.3.1
)).

Theorem C.4.8
.

Let
\pi
be a relevant, non-endoscopic automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, with strong coefficient field
E_{0}
. Then
(R1)
holds for all but finitely many primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
.

The theorem is also true in the endoscopic case, but not used in the main text; the proof uses Lemma
C.4.12
below.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of
Lemmas
C.4.2
through
C.4.7
.

∎

Proposition C.4.9
.

Suppose
\pi
is not endoscopic, and there exists a prime
\ell
such that
\pi_{\ell}
is of type IIa. Then for all but finitely many primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
, admissible elements exist for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.

Proof.

The Weil-Deligne representation
\rec_{\operatorname{GT}}(\pi_{\ell})
is tamely ramified; under the embedding
\GSP_{4}\hookrightarrow\GL_{4}
from (
1.1.4
), it is given by

\Frob_{\ell}=\begin{pmatrix}\pm\ell^{1/2}&&&\\
&\alpha&&\\
&&\pm\ell^{-1/2}&\\
&&&\ell/\alpha\end{pmatrix}\in\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{C}),\;\;N=\begin{pmatrix}&&1&\\
&&&\\
&&&\\
&&&\end{pmatrix}\in\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{C})

By the purity assertion in Theorem
2.2.10
(
1
) (for any prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
), we know
|\alpha|=1
. Extend
E_{0}
if necessary so that
\alpha^{2}\in E_{0}
. Then for all but finitely many primes
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
, we have:

\displaystyle\ell^{8}

\displaystyle\not\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}},

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\ell

\displaystyle\not\equiv\pm 1,\pm\ell^{2},\ell^{-2},\ell^{4}\pmod{\mathfrak{p}}.

Suppose
\mathfrak{p}
satisfies the above conditions, and let
\Frob_{\ell}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
be any lift of Frobenius.
By Theorem
2.2.10
(
1
),
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}(\Frob_{\ell}^{2})
has eigenvalues
\left\{\ell^{2},\alpha^{2}\ell,1,\ell^{2}/\alpha^{2}\right\}
, hence
\Frob_{\ell}^{2}
is an admissible element for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.
∎

Combining Lemmas
C.4.2
through
C.4.7
with Proposition
C.4.9
, we obtain:

Theorem C.4.10
.

Let
\pi
be a relevant, non-endoscopic automorphic representation of
\GSP_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, with strong coefficient field
E_{0}
.
There is a set
S
of rational primes of positive Dirichlet density such that for all
p\in S
and all
\mathfrak{p}|p
, admissible elements exist for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.
There exists such an
S
containing all but finitely many
p
if
\pi
satisfies any of the following:

(1)

There exists a prime
\ell
such that
\pi_{\ell}
is of type IIa.

(2)

\BC(\pi)
is a symmetric cube lift.

(3)

\BC(\pi)
is the automorphic induction of a non-CM automorphic representation
\pi_{0}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{K})
with
K
real quadratic, and
\pi_{0}^{\operatorname{tw}}\neq\pi_{0}^{\sigma}\otimes\chi
for all
\sigma\in\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
and all quadratic Hecke characters
\chi
of
K
.

∎

Finally, we handle the endoscopic case separately.

Proposition C.4.11
.

Suppose
\pi
is endoscopic, associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
(in any order). Then:

(1)

If
\pi_{1}
does not have CM, then for all but finitely many
p
and all
\mathfrak{p}|p
, there exist admissible primes for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
that are BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}
.

(2)

If
\pi_{1}
has CM by a field
K
and
\pi_{2}
does not have CM by
K
, then for all but finitely many
p
split in
K
and all
\mathfrak{p}|p
, there exist admissible primes for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
that are BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}
.

Proof.

Let
S
be the set of all rational primes in case (
1
) and all rational primes
p
split in
K
in case (
2
).
Then there exists a constant
n\geq 1
such that, for all but finitely many
p\in S
and all
\mathfrak{p}|p
,
\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}})
contains the diagonal subgroup

\left\{\begin{pmatrix}x&\\
&y\end{pmatrix}\,:\,x,y\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{n}\right\}.

In the non-CM case this follows from Theorem
C.3.2
(with
n=1
), and in the CM case it follows from either
[
81
, Proposition B.6.3]
or a similar argument to Lemma
C.4.7
.
On the other hand, there exists a constant
n\geq 1
such that, for all but finitely many
p
and all
\mathfrak{p}|p
,
\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}(G_{\mathbb{Q}})
contains the scalar subgroup
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{n}\subset\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

By Lemma
C.4.12
below, after enlarging
n
if necessary, for all but finitely many
p\in S
and all
\mathfrak{p}|p
,
\left(\rho_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}\times\rho_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}}\right)(G_{\mathbb{Q}})
contains

\left\{\begin{pmatrix}x&0\\
0&y\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}z&0\\
0&z\end{pmatrix}\,:\,x,y,z\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{n},z^{2}=xy\right\};

and this implies the proposition.
∎

Lemma C.4.12
.

Suppose
\pi
is endoscopic, associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
which do not both have CM by the same imaginary quadratic field. Then as
\mathfrak{p}
varies over primes of
E_{0}
,
\mathbb{Q}(\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mathbb{Q}(\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}})
has bounded degree over
\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p})
.

Proof.

If both
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are non-CM, then the lemma follows from
[
71
, Theorem 3.2.2]
, or equivalently from Theorem
C.3.6
(
2
) above.
Now suppose
\pi_{1}
is CM and
\pi_{2}
is not. Since
\SL_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is simple for
q
sufficiently large and
\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}}
is dihedral for all
\mathfrak{p}
, it follows from
[
95
, Theorem 3.1]
that
\mathbb{Q}(\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}})\cap\mathbb{Q}(\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}})=\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p})
for all but finitely many
\mathfrak{p}
.

If
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are CM with respect to two different imaginary quadratic fields
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
, fix elements
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
\tau_{i}
is a complex conjugation on
K_{i}
but acts trivially on
K_{j}
,
i\neq j
.
The abelian group
H\coloneqq\Gal(\mathbb{Q}(\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}})/K_{1})

is a subgroup of
k^{\times}
for a quadratic étale algebra
k
over the residue field of
\mathfrak{p}
; in particular,
H
is the product of at most two cyclic groups. Let
G\coloneqq\Gal(K_{1}K_{2}(\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{1},\mathfrak{p}})\cap K_{1}K_{2}(\overline{\rho}_{\pi_{2},\mathfrak{p}})/K_{1}K_{2})
. Because
G
is a subquotient of
H
, the
conjugation actions on
G
of both
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{1}\tau_{2}
are by inversion, so the conjugation action of
\tau_{2}
is trivial; arguing symmetrically, the conjugation action of
\tau_{1}
is also trivial, so
G
is 2-torsion and generated by at most two elements. We conclude that
|G|
is uniformly bounded, which implies the lemma.
∎

C.5.
Complements for the second reciprocity law

In this subsection, we prove some auxiliary results needed in §
11
. Let us fix a relevant
automorphic representation
\pi
of
\GSP_{4}
, and an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}
with
p>3
.

Lemma C.5.1
.

Let
\tau
be a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
whose archimedean component is discrete series of even weight
k\geq 2
. Let
F
be a number field such that
\tau
does not have CM by any quadratic imaginary subfield
K\subset F(\rho_{\pi,\iota})
.
If
F(\rho_{\pi,\iota})\cap F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})
is infinite,
then
F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})\subset F(\rho_{\pi,\iota})
,
and moreover one of
the following occurs:

(i)

\pi
is not endoscopic, and if
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
is admissible for
\rho_{\pi,\iota}
, then
\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g^{2})
has distinct eigenvalues.

(ii)

\pi
is endoscopic associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, and for
j=1
or 2,
\pi_{j}\cong\tau^{\sigma}\otimes\chi
for some finite-order Hecke character
\chi
and automorphism
\sigma\in\operatorname{Aut}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

First, we claim that
\tau
does not have CM. Indeed, if
\tau

has CM by a quadratic imaginary field
K
, then it is easy to check that any infinite subfield of
F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})
contains
K
; so if
F(\rho_{\pi,\iota})\cap F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})
is infinite then
K\subset F(\rho_{\pi,\iota})
, which contradicts the hypotheses of the lemma.

Since
\tau
does not have CM,
\rho_{\tau,\iota}
is strongly irreducible by Theorem
C.3.2
. Hence
by Lemma
C.2.3
, the normal, infinite-index subgroup

\Gal\left(F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})/F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})\cap F(\rho_{\pi,\iota})\right)\mathrel{\unlhd}\Gal\left(F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})/F\right)

must be trivial; equivalently, we have
F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})\subset F(\rho_{\pi,\iota})
.

Suppose first that
\pi
is not endoscopic, so
V_{\pi,\iota}
is absolutely irreducible by Lemma
2.2.12
.
Without loss of generality, we assume that
F
is Galois and that

(C.18)

V_{\pi,\iota}|_{G_{F}}=\bigoplus_{i}V_{i}

for some strongly irreducible representations
V_{i}
, all of the same dimension
n
(Lemma
C.2.5
). Let
G=\rho_{\pi,\iota}(G_{F})
, and let
H=\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota}(G_{F})
; then the inclusion
F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})\subset F(\rho_{\pi,\iota})
corresponds to a surjection
G/Z_{G}\twoheadrightarrow H
(recall here that
H
has trivial center by Lemma
C.2.2
(
1
)).
In particular,
n>1
.
Write
\mathfrak{g}=\Lie(G/Z_{G})
and
\mathfrak{h}=\Lie H
, and recall that
\mathfrak{h}
is simple by Theorem
C.3.2
and Corollary
C.1.2
(
2
). We have a surjection

(C.19)

\mathfrak{g}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{h}

which identifies
\mathfrak{h}
with a simple factor of
\mathfrak{g}
.

Suppose first that
n=2
.
Then the decomposition (
C.18
) has exactly two factors, and
\mathfrak{g}
is either simple, or isomorphic to
\mathfrak{g}_{0}\times\mathfrak{g}_{0}
, with
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
simple and the factors interchanged by the action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
. Since (
C.19
) is
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant,
\mathfrak{g}
is simple, and (
C.19
) is an isomorphism.
If
\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g^{2})
has only one eigenvalue for some
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
, then
g^{2}
acts unipotently on
\mathfrak{h}
, hence also on
\mathfrak{g}
.
Since
g^{2}
is a square, it preserves the decomposition (
C.18
), so by
Lemma
C.2.2
(
3
), we see that
g^{2}
has at most two eigenvalues on
V_{\pi,\iota}
, which contradicts
g
being admissible.

We are now reduced to the case
n=4
, i.e.
\BC(\pi)
is not an automorphic induction.
Hence
\mathfrak{g}
is simple by Proposition
C.2.4
(
1
) and Corollary
C.1.2
(
2
), and again (
C.19
) is an isomorphism.
If
\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g^{2})
had only one eigenvalue, then
g^{2}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
would act unipotently on
\mathfrak{h}
, hence also on
\mathfrak{g}
; but by Lemma
C.2.2
(
3
), this contradicts the admissibility of
g
.

It remains to consider the case when

\pi
is endoscopic associated to a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of cuspidal automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
. We let
G_{\pi}=\rho_{\pi,\iota}(G_{\mathbb{Q}}),

G_{\pi_{j}}=\rho_{\pi_{j},\iota}(G_{\mathbb{Q}})
,
\mathfrak{g}_{\pi}=\Lie(G_{\pi}/Z_{G_{\pi}})
,
\mathfrak{g}_{\pi_{j}}=\Lie(G_{\pi_{j}}/Z_{G_{\pi_{j}}})
, for
j=1,2
.
The assumption
F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})\subset F(\rho_{\pi,\iota})
implies that
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
does not have open image in the product
G_{\pi}/Z_{G_{\pi}}\times H
. By Goursat’s Lemma, the Lie algebra of the image of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
is the graph of an isomorphism between simple factors of
\mathfrak{g}_{\pi}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{\pi_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\pi_{2}}
and
\mathfrak{h}
. Since
\tau
is non-CM, we conclude that for
j=1
or 2,
\pi_{j}
is non-CM and
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
has non-open image in
G_{\pi_{j}}/Z_{\pi_{j}}\times H
.
Hence by
[
71
, Proposition 3.3.2]
, there exists an automorphism
\sigma\in\operatorname{Aut}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
and a Hecke character
\chi
such that
\tau\cong\pi_{j}^{\sigma}\otimes\chi
, for
j=1
or 2; this concludes the proof of the lemma.

∎

For the rest of the section, we fix a strong coefficient field
E_{0}
for
\pi
and let
\mathfrak{p}
be the prime of
E_{0}
induced by
\iota
.

Lemma C.5.2
.

Let
\tau
be a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
whose archimedean component is discrete series of even weight
k\geq 2
. If
\tau
does not have CM by any quadratic imaginary subfield
K\subset\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi,\iota})
, then for any number field
F
and any
O_{\mathfrak{p}}
-stable lattice
T_{\pi}\subset V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
,
H^{1}(\Gal(F(\rho_{\pi,\iota},\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})/\mathbb{Q}),T_{\pi})
is finite.

Proof.

By inflation-restriction, we may assume without loss of generality that
F=\mathbb{Q}
. Applying Corollary
C.2.8
and inflation-restriction again,
to prove the lemma it suffices to show

(C.20)

H^{1}(\Gal(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi,\iota},\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})/\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi,\iota}),T_{\pi})=\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\Gal(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi,\iota},\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})/\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi,\iota})),T_{\pi})=0.

By Lemma
C.5.1
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi,\iota})\cap\mathbb{Q}(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})
is finite. Since
\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}
has infinite order, there exists
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g)=1
and
\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}(g)
has infinite order, meaning in particular that
\rho_{\pi,\iota}(g)\neq 1
. Then

g
acts trivially by conjugation on
\Gal\left(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi,\iota},\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})/\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi,\iota})\right)\hookrightarrow\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota}(G_{\mathbb{Q}}).
In particular, any
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-invariant homomorphism
h:\Gal\left(\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi,\iota},\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})/\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi,\iota})\right)\to T_{\pi}
has image contained in
T_{\pi}^{g=1}\subsetneq T_{\pi}
. If
\pi
is non-endoscopic, this shows
h=0
by Lemma
2.2.12
; if
\pi
is endoscopic associated to
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
, the same argument applies because we cannot have
\rho_{\pi_{1},\iota}(g)=1
or
\rho_{\pi_{2},\iota}(g)=1
under the assumption that
\chi_{p,{\operatorname{cyc}}}(g)
has infinite order.
This shows (
C.20
).
∎

Proposition C.5.3
.

Let
\pi
,
\iota
,
E_{0}
, and
\mathfrak{p}

be as above with
\pi
non-endoscopic, and suppose admissible primes exist for
\rho_{\pi}=\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
.
Suppose given the following data:

\circ

A quadratic field
F\not\subset\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})
.

\circ

A cuspidal automorphic representation
\tau
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
whose archimedean component is discrete series of even weight
k\geq 2
, such that
\tau
does not have CM by any quadratic field
K\subset F(\rho_{\pi})
.

\circ

A
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable
O_{\mathfrak{p}}
-lattice
T_{\pi}\subset V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
, and a non-torsion cocycle
c\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi})
.

Then there exists an element
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that:

(1)

g
is is admissible for
\rho_{\pi}
and has nontrivial image in
\Gal(F/\mathbb{Q})
.

(2)

\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g^{2})
has distinct eigenvalues.

(3)

c(g)
has nonzero component in the 1-eigenspace for
g
.

Note the last condition is independent of the choice of cocycle representative for
c
.

Proof.

First choose
g
satisfying (
1
), and with the additional property that
g
has trivial image in
\Gal(K/\mathbb{Q})

if
\tau
has CM by a quadratic field
K
. (This choice is possible because we have
F\not\subset\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})
and
K\not\subset F(\rho_{\pi})
.)

Claim
.

There exists
h\in G_{F(\rho_{\pi})}
such that
hg
satisfies (
2
).

Proof of claim.

If
F(\rho_{\pi})\cap F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})
is infinite then taking
h=1
suffices by Lemma
C.5.1
, so we may assume without loss of generality that
F(\rho_{\pi})\cap F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})
is finite.
If
\tau
is non-CM, then because
F(\rho_{\pi})\cap F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})
is finite,
Theorem
C.3.2
implies that the image of
\rho_{\tau,\iota}|_{G_{F(\rho_{\pi})}}
contains a compact open subgroup of
\left\{x\in D^{\times}\,:\,\Nm(x)\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\right\}\hookrightarrow\GL_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, for a quaternion algebra
D
over a finite extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Since
x\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g)x\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g)
having distinct eigenvalues is an open condition on
x\in D^{\times}
, the claim follows when
\tau
is non-CM.

If on the other hand
\tau
has CM by an imaginary quadratic field
K
, then because
\rho_{\tau,\iota}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
has distinct Hodge-Tate weights, there exists
h_{0}\in G_{K}
such that
\rho_{\tau,\iota}(h_{0})
has eigenvalues whose ratio is of infinite order. After replacing
h_{0}
with a finite power, it acts trivially on
F(\rho_{\pi})\cap F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})
; thus there exists
h\in G_{K\cdot F(\rho_{\pi})}
such that
\rho_{\tau,\iota}(h^{2})
has distinct eigenvalues.
Since
g
has trivial image in
\Gal(K/\mathbb{Q})
,
\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g)
and
\rho_{\tau,\iota}(h)
commute; in particular, if
\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g^{2})
is scalar, then
\rho_{\tau,\iota}(hghg)=\rho_{\tau,\iota}(h^{2})\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g^{2})
has distinct eigenvalues. Hence either
g
or
hg
satisfies (
2
), which shows the claim.
∎

Replacing
g
with
hg
as in the claim, we may now assume
g
satisfies both (
1
) and (
2
).
By Lemma
C.5.2
and inflation-restriction,
c
has nonzero image in

H^{1}(F(\rho_{\pi},\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota}),T_{\pi})^{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}=\Hom_{G_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/F(\rho_{\pi},\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})),T_{\pi}),

and because
V_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
is absolutely irreducible,
there exists
h\in G_{F}
such that
\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota}(h)=\rho_{\pi}(h)=1
and
c(h)
has nonzero component in the 1-eigenspace for
g
. Then either
g
or
hg
satisfies (
1
), (
2
), and (
3
), which proves the proposition.

∎

Finally, we have the endoscopic analogue of Proposition
C.5.3
.

Proposition C.5.4
.

Let
\pi
,
\iota
,
E_{0}
, and
\mathfrak{p}
be as above, with
\pi
endoscopic associated to pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of automorphic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})
; and assume that
E_{0}
is a common strong coefficient field of

\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
. Let
j=1
or 2, and suppose there exist admissible primes for
\rho_{\pi,\mathfrak{p}}
which are BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{j}}=\rho_{\pi_{j},\mathfrak{p}}
.

Suppose given the following data:

\circ

A quadratic field
F\not\subset\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi})
.

\circ

A cuspidal automorphic representation
\tau
of
\GL_{2}
whose archimedean component is discrete series of weight at least 2, such that
\tau
does not have CM by any quadratic field
K\subset F(\rho_{\pi})
.

\circ

A
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable
O_{\mathfrak{p}}
lattice
T_{\pi_{j}}\subset V_{\pi_{j},\mathfrak{p}}
, and a non-torsion cocycle
c\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{\pi_{j}})
.

Then there exists an element
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that:

(1)

g
is is admissible for
\rho_{\pi}
and BD-admissible for
\rho_{\pi_{j}}
, and has nontrivial image in
\Gal(F/\mathbb{Q})
.

(2)

\rho_{\tau,\iota}(g^{2})
has distinct eigenvalues.

(3)

c(g)
has nonzero component in the 1-eigenspace for
g
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, suppose
j=1
. Clearly there exists
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
satisfying (
1
), such that, if
\tau
has CM by an imaginary quadratic field
K
,
g
has trivial image in
\Gal(K/\mathbb{Q})
.
We next claim:

Claim
.

There exists
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
satisfying (
1
) and (
2
).

Proof.

If
F(\rho_{\pi})\cap F(\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota})
is finite, then we conclude using the same argument as for the claim in the proof of Proposition
C.5.3
.
By Lemma
C.5.1
, we may therefore assume that, for
i=1
or 2,

\pi_{i}\cong\tau^{\sigma}\otimes\chi
for some finite-order Hecke character
\chi
and automorphism
\sigma\in\automorphisms(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. In this case,
\tau
is necessarily non-CM (because its CM field would be contained in
\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi_{i}})
), so
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
cannot both be CM; and it suffices to show there exists
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}

satisfying (
1
), such that
\rho_{\pi_{2}}(g^{2})
has distinct eigenvalues. Hence it suffices to show that
\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi_{1}})\cap\mathbb{Q}(\rho_{\pi_{2}})
is finite over
\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})
; and this follows from an argument very similar to Lemma
C.4.12
, using that
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are not both CM.
∎

Now take
g
as in the claim. By Lemma
C.5.2
,
c
has nonzero image in

H^{1}(F(\rho_{\pi},\ad^{0}\rho_{\tau,\iota}),T_{\pi_{1}}).

Arguing as in Proposition
C.5.3
and using the absolute irreducibility of
\rho_{\pi_{1}}
, the proposition follows.
∎
